\section{Scope and conventions}

This document catalogues the two hundred and forty-seven research packages stored
under \dir{docs/reports}.  The collection originally held one hundred and four reports; after two
consolidations it contributes one hundred and two maintained reports. The
other one hundred and forty-five came through ProveIt's incoming-reports procedure
(\dir{docs/incoming}) --- six in batch~36, one each in batches~40, 41
and~44, two in batch~60, one each in batches~62 and~70, three in
batch~72, sixteen in batch~73, two in batch~74, three in batch~75, two in
batch~76, thirty-four in batch~77, three in batch~78, two in
batch~79, two in batch~82, one in batch~83, five in batch~85, one each
in batches~86, 87, 89 and~91, four in batch~100, one in batch~101, three in
batch~102, four each in batches~103, 104 and~105, seven in batch~106,
five in batch~107, ten in batch~108, eight in batch~109 and five in
batch~114 --- whose
archives remain in the repository's history.  Batches~100--109 are clusters of one session bundle
of numbered Research Reports (arrival \texttt{60f54ea06}); batches~110--113,
the rest of that bundle, are not yet placed; batch~114 is the
seven other archives of two arrivals of the same day.  Later batches
also extended existing reports; their entries name the batch.  Batch~80
opened no report here: it extended one report and replaced the programs of
three Parts by corrected code editions; batches~88, 90, 92, 93, 96, 97
and~98 likewise only extended reports.  Batches~81, 84, 94, 95, 99
and~115--125 brought nothing here; the manuscripts of batches~81 and~84, like part of
batches~83, 86, 87, 89, 90, 92, 93, 95, 96 and~97, went to
the surreal-number collection, one batch-85 manuscript, the five of
batch~94 and two of batch~95 went to
\dir{Analysis/Transseries}, three manuscripts of batch~97 and the
thirteen archives of batch~99 went to the Fabius drafts tree in
\dir{Analysis/FabiusFunction}, and the fifty-nine Gowers--Szemer\'edi
manuscripts of batches~115--125 went, by Vladimir's direction, to one
report beside the Lean development of Gowers's proof,
\dir{local-quantitative-refinements} in
\dir{Combinatorics/Ramsey/Research/GowersSzemeredi}.
One archive of the batch-114 arrivals,
which continues the A082528 report, is held for batch~110.
The next paragraphs describe the historical collection before those two
consolidations. On 29 September 2026 the two Lipparini Problem~6.2 reports
were unified, and the finite-height transfinite-word report was integrated
with its guarded-block companion. Both proof routes and all distinct
extensions are retained; old directories contain redirects, not extra
catalogue entries.  The seven surreal-number reports that this catalogue
used to carry were moved out to a repository of their own, with their history,
and are no longer listed here.  They arrived as one hundred and twenty-five ZIP
archives, in seven deliveries, which have been unpacked and grouped into the
subject hierarchy described in Section~\ref{sec:tree}.  The archives themselves
are no longer kept in the repository.

One hundred and twenty-five archives yield one hundred and three reports for
two different reasons.  Twenty archives duplicated another: sixteen pairs, three
three-way clusters and one four-way cluster.  Two more were folded into a
neighbour not because they repeated it but because they shared a subject --- the
three reports on term counts in the derivatives of power towers are now one
article with a common framework and three parts.  That consolidation is
editorial, and the distinction matters: those three were put through the
duplicate sweep and cleared it, and their three theorems are three different
theorems.  The seventh delivery of eighteen contained no duplicate at all, which
is worth recording: a sweep that finds nothing is evidence about the collection,
not a sweep that failed.
Each pair has been merged into one report that proves the
shared theorem once and keeps everything both originals built on top of it.
Where the two reached a result by genuinely different routes, both proofs are
kept and marked as alternatives, with a sentence on what each one buys; the
merged shuffle report keeps three independent finite certifications of one
theorem, and the merged report on surreal option graphs kept two different
five-vertex counterexamples for the same reason before it moved out with the
other surreal-number reports.  A merged entry below names both of its
source archives and says what was doubled.

Not every pair aimed at one problem is a duplicate.  The two Gonshor
reports both prove his product-birthday bound, but on domains that are
incomparable in both directions, so neither theorem contains the other and they
are catalogued separately; the same holds for reports answering different numbered problems of
one paper, noted where it occurs.

Every package is self-contained and follows the same shape: a typeset article
(\texttt{*.pdf}) with its complete \LaTeX{} source, a \texttt{README.md}
summarising the result, usually a \texttt{code/} directory with exact-arithmetic
verification scripts, a \texttt{data/} or \texttt{results/} directory holding the
recorded output of those scripts, and often \texttt{STATUS.md},
\texttt{sources.md} or \texttt{notes/} files auditing the sources consulted and
the precise limits of the claim.

Two cautions apply uniformly and are therefore not repeated in the individual
entries.  First, these are AI-assisted research drafts: none has been
independently refereed, and none has been checked in a proof assistant.
Several continue a Lean or Rocq project elsewhere in ProveIt; that confers no
formal status on them, and their entries say which project they continue.  Second,
the descriptions below record \emph{what each report sets out to settle and what
it claims to prove}.  Compiling this manifest involved reading each package's
statement of its problem and result; it did not involve checking any proof.
Where a report itself flags a limitation --- a prior disproof, an imported
theorem, a correction to the source, or an exception left open --- that is noted,
because it changes what the report is.

The naming of the directories is editorial.  Several distinct reports attack the
same published problem, and some arrived under colliding archive names, so each
directory has been given a name describing its own angle on the problem; the
merged directories usually name their combined scope. The September 29
consolidations retain the base directory names to preserve existing links.  The
original archive names are recorded with every entry.

\clearpage

\medskip
\noindent
More than a quarter of the collection consists of counterexamples and
corrections rather than proofs.  Ordinal Chomp, open-query games, compositional
tree series, the secant numbers, the decimal-length bound for tetration
stabilization, power-tower stabilization, iterated-power digit stabilization,
cluster variables, chromatic coefficients of cycles, products of $q$-integers,
domination roots, Hardinian array constants, the mixed-base counterexample,
recursive exponents, Gregory sign thresholds and binary substitution discrepancy
all refute, or replace with a proved statement, an assertion published or
catalogued elsewhere.  Several reports whose main outcome is a proof still
correct the statement they prove: the Somos-8 certificates, the shifted Catalan
Hankel polynomials and the Rueppel determinants record a sign error, a missing
one-unit shift and a numbering ambiguity in their sources, and the
independence-system thresholds report refines its target after finding that the
original conjecture had already been disproved by someone else.

\section{Ordinals and order types}

\subsection{Friendly order types}

Three reports on Isa Vialard's \emph{friendly order type} $f(P)$, the rank of
the tree of open-ended bad sequences in which every chosen point has an
incomparable partner in the current residual.  All three prove the same finite
formula; they differ in what they build on top of it, and in how they prove it
--- one by a forest-rank monovariant that survives moves deleting a whole upset,
one through a lemma bounding the cut maxima.  A fourth report proved the formula
by the first report's argument almost verbatim and has been merged into it.

\entry{Friendly Order Types Through Incomparability Components}
{ordinals-and-order-types/friendly-order-types/incomparability-components-and-infinite-tails}
{friendly\_order\_types.pdf}{friendly\_order\_type\_research (1).zip}
{The same component formula, together with an exact friendly rank for connected
infinite well-partial-orders: writing $o(C)=\lambda+n$ with $\lambda$ the limit
part and $T(C)$ the finite set of points with finite principal upsets.
Includes an exhaustive finite verification and a proof audit.}

\entry{Friendly Order Type Through Incomparability Components}
{ordinals-and-order-types/friendly-order-types/matroid-rank-and-ordinal-sums}
{article.pdf}{friendly\_order\_type\_research (3).zip}
{Identifies $|P|-c(P)$ with the graphic-matroid rank and the incidence-matrix
rank of the incomparability graph.  Adds a substitution formula, a finite
Cartesian-product formula, and additivity over arbitrary well-ordered ordinal
sums; evaluates every well-partial-order whose incomparability components are
finite, and gives countable counterexamples delimiting stronger infinite
extrapolations.}

\entry{Friendly Order Types of Finite Posets and Ordinal Products}
{ordinals-and-order-types/friendly-order-types/components-forests-and-ordinal-products}
{friendly\_order\_types.pdf}{friendly\_order\_types\_research.zip + friendly\_order\_type\_research.zip}
{Proves $f(P)=|P|-c(\mathrm{Inc}(P))$ for a finite poset $P$, where $c$ counts
connected components of the incomparability graph (isolated vertices included),
and classifies the witnesses: every maximum friendly subset is $P$ minus one
minimal root per component, counted by $\prod|\mathrm{Min}(C_i)|$.  With
Vialard's width-transfer theorem this gives ordinal width $\omega^{f(P)}$ for
the Dershowitz--Manna multiset ordering.  On the transfinite side it proves
$f(\alpha_1\times\cdots\times\alpha_r\times B)=\Theta$, the natural product,
except that it is the predecessor of $\Theta$ when every $\alpha_i$ is a
successor and $B$ has a greatest element, and classifies arbitrary finite
ordinal boxes.  Also: spanning-forest certificates, exact monotonicity under
added comparisons, a bivariate generating function, and explicit four-point
factors with equal $(o,h,w,f)$ whose products differ.  \emph{Merged; the
Cartesian-product formula is proved twice, once by corner deletion and once by
a chain-grid spanning-subgraph argument.}}

\subsection{Well-quasi-ordered powersets and statures}

Four reports on the open questions in Section~6 of Abriola, Halfon, Lopez,
Schmitz, Schnoebelen and Vialard, \emph{Measuring well-quasi-ordered finitary
powersets} (arXiv:2312.14587v2), and on the corresponding topological invariant.

\entry{Cofinal strata and ordinal absorption}
{ordinals-and-order-types/wqo-powersets-and-statures/cofinal-strata-of-finitary-powersets}
{article.pdf}{cofinal\_strata\_ordinal\_powersets.zip; batch~42: ProveIt\_Ordinal\_Heights\_Matchings.zip; batch~36:
ProveIt\_Nonuniform\_Powerset\_Heights.zip, ProveIt\_Persistent\_Frontier\_Heights.zip,
persistent\_frontier\_heights.zip, ProveIt\_Ordinal\_Heights\_Research.zip}
{For $S_\alpha(Q)$, a finite poset $Q$ with each point replaced by a chain
$\alpha=\omega^\rho$, proves
$o(K(S_\alpha(Q)))=\sum_k \omega^{\rho\otimes k}c_k(Q)$ and
$h(K(S_\alpha(Q)))=\alpha\cdot\mathrm{height}(M(Q))$, where $c_k$ counts size-$k$
maximal antichains not dominated by a larger one.  The maximal-order-type
theorem allows nonuniform pure well-partial-order fibers and is proved by a
finite cofinal-stratification argument.  Parts~II and~III, merged in batch~36
from four manuscripts that each prove it, supply the nonuniform height formula
the original left open.  For positive pure fibers $\omega^{\rho_q}$, $h(K)$ is
the largest ordinary ordinal sum, along a chain of maximal frontiers, of the
largest fiber leaving the frontier at each step plus the largest fiber of the
last frontier; after Cantor-normal-form refinement the same formula covers
nonzero limit fibers.  For arbitrary ordinal fibers, finite and successor ones
included, the height is given instead by a finite dynamic program over
downsets with fallback weight~1, and the frontier compression is not valid
there.  Adds exact point ranks, NP membership of the threshold problem for the
explicit pure-block encoding, and a separate part for well-partial-order
fibers with $h(K(P_q))$ a positive pure power.  Part~IV, added in batch~42,
shows that for height-two skeletons with uniform fibers the height is
$\alpha(\nu_{\mathrm{ur}}+1)$, with $\nu_{\mathrm{ur}}$ the maximum uniquely
restricted matching number, so deciding the height is NP-complete already when
every fiber is $\omega$.}

\entry{Intrinsic ordinal approximations for finitary powersets}
{ordinals-and-order-types/wqo-powersets-and-statures/intrinsic-ordinal-approximations}
{article.pdf}{intrinsic\_ordinal\_approximations.zip}
{Studies the intrinsic invariant
$a_o(A)=\sup\{o(B)+1: \mathrm{Idl}(B)\hookrightarrow A\}$.  Proves the universal
bound $a_o(A)\le h(P_f(A))$ with equality exactly when every finitely generated
downset of $A$ is an algebraic dcpo, gives the closed formula
$h(P_f(\prod_i\alpha_i))=\sup(\bigotimes_i(1+a_i)+1)$, and shows the universal
identity fails on $\lambda+F$.}

\entry{Ordinal Order Maps and Finitary Powersets of Ordinal--Finite Grids}
{ordinals-and-order-types/wqo-powersets-and-statures/ordinal-order-maps-and-grids}
{ordinal\_order\_maps.pdf}{ordinal\_order\_maps\_research.zip}
{Exact maximal order types and heights for finite Hoare powersets of
$\beta\times Q$ with $Q$ a finite poset: for
$\beta=\omega^{\gamma_0}+\cdots+\omega^{\gamma_{m-1}}$,
$o(\mathrm{Mon}(Q,\beta))=\bigoplus_{s\,\text{isotone}}\omega^{\bigoplus_q\gamma_{s(q)}}$.}

\entry{Sharp ordinal bounds for Hoare powerspaces}
{ordinals-and-order-types/wqo-powersets-and-statures/hoare-powerspace-statures}
{article.pdf}{hoare\_statures\_research.zip}
{For all Noetherian spaces, $1+\|X\|\le\|HX\|\le 2^{\|X\|}$, with witnesses
attaining both endpoints at every ordinal and $T_1$ upper witnesses; the older
$\omega^\alpha$ bound is attainable at infinite input stature $\alpha$ exactly
when $\omega\cdot\alpha=\alpha$; the complete spectrum at $\omega+n$ is
$\omega\cdot j(U)+(j(Q)-j(U))$.  Extends the known well-quasi-order results of
Abriola et al.\ to arbitrary Noetherian spaces.}

\subsection{Transfinite words}

One unified report on Harry Altman's finite-alphabet sharpness question
(arXiv:2409.03199v2, Example~3.15), combining three source manuscripts.
The common height-two formula is stated once. The finite-height induction
and the more detailed guarded-tail investigation are retained as distinct routes.

\entry{Finite-alphabet transfinite words: exact order types and guarded periodic blocks}
{ordinals-and-order-types/transfinite-words/order-types-below-omega-squared}
{article.pdf}{omega2\_order\_types\_proposed\_solution.zip + ordinal\_words\_research.zip + finite\_alphabet\_transfinite\_words.zip}
{Claims $o(V_k(P))=\omega^{\omega^{N_k(P)-1}}$ for $|P|\ge2$ and each
positive finite $k$, using canonical finite atom posets with
$N_{k+1}=|P|+J(I_k(P))-1$. At height two this is
$\omega^{\omega^{n+j(P)-2}}$; empty and singleton alphabets are explicit
exceptions. Retains the selected-tail-family classification, its universal-only
exception, chain/antichain extremality, exact-length strata, and canonical
linear-time token comparison. The guarded proof has both natural-product and
elementary amplification routes; the finite-height proof uses protected
separators. Two independent implementations and their separate audit records
are preserved. Neither an all-height union formula nor a higher-cutoff
selected-family classification is claimed.}

\subsection{Ordinal arithmetic}

Three reports on ordinal and infinitary arithmetic, including a consolidation
of two independent proposed solutions to Problem~6.2 of Paolo Lipparini,
\emph{Monotone infinitary operations on ordinals} (arXiv:2505.00424v2): define
and study the least weakly monotone, $e$-special strictly increasing operation
on countable sequences of ordinals.

\entry{An Explicit Formula for the Minimal $e$-Monotone Infinitary Ordinal Sum}
{ordinals-and-order-types/ordinal-arithmetic/lipparini-minimal-infinitary-sum}
{article/explicit\_ordinal\_sum.pdf}{lipparini\_problem\_6\_2\_proposed\_solution.zip + ordinal\_operation\_research.zip}
{The same three-case formula for the least admissible operation is proved
by two retained routes: profile ranks with arithmetic absorption and
finite-product lower bounds, and corrected blocks with threshold interpolation
and finite-part restoration. The latter avoids the product-rank lemma;
both import Lipparini's characterization of the weaker $S$. Retains the
multiset quotient, bounded-poset heights, finite-cap ranks with alternative
proofs, equality on comparable inputs, cardinality preservation, and
counterexamples to grouping and continuity. The correction is zero, a finite
increment, or one \emph{natural} summand $\omega$. Both implementations and
their separate verification records are preserved.}

\entry{Jacobsthal Products All at Once}
{ordinals-and-order-types/ordinal-arithmetic/jacobsthal-products-all-at-once}
{article.pdf}{Jacobsthal\_Products\_All\_at\_Once.zip}
{Question~2.16 of Altman, \emph{Intermediate arithmetic operations on ordinal
numbers} (MLQ 63 (2017) 228--242), asks for an all-at-once order-theoretic model
of finite and infinitary Jacobsthal products, rather than one built from
parenthesized binary multiplication or a limit.  Gives a constructive
affirmative answer: a Cantor-normal-form comparison rule on actual tuples or
finite-support functions, invariant under order isomorphisms and extending the
componentwise order, whose definition never evaluates a Jacobsthal product.
Adds a restricted maximality characterisation and a finite-tail reduction.
The associativity identity and the binary normal-form formula are classical and
are not claimed here; the construction is not claimed to be preserved by
arbitrary order embeddings.}

\entry{Pairwise Coherence Does Not Guarantee Completion}
{ordinals-and-order-types/ordinal-arithmetic/pairwise-coherence-of-infinitary-products}
{ordinal\_product\_coherence.pdf}{ordinal\_product\_coherence.zip}
{Answers the pairwise-coherence sufficiency question of Lipparini,
\emph{Noncommutative infinitary semigroups} (arXiv:2605.28413v1, Remark~7.1(b)),
negatively.  On the two-element group $C_2$, retaining every finite product and
declaring every infinite all-$e$ product to be $e$ gives a partial system
satisfying full pairwise regrouping, even for arbitrary partitions, yet with no
injective extension defining all finite and $\omega$-indexed products; two
elements is minimal.  Adds: for any monoid $M$ the canonical partial domain
$P(M)$ is coherent, and for directly finite $M$ it completes exactly when the
unit group is trivial; and for every $r$ a system on $3r+4$ elements coherent
through nesting depth $r$ but conflicting at depth $r+1$, so no fixed finite
depth of the coherence test characterises completability.}

\subsection{Games on ordinals}

\entry{An ordinal Chomp transition at 2}
{ordinals-and-order-types/games-on-ordinals/ordinal-chomp-transition-at-two}
{ordinal\_chomp\_counterexample.pdf}{ordinal\_chomp\_transition\_counterexample.zip + ordinal\_chomp\_counterexample.zip}
{For a numerical semigroup $S$ ordered by $x\le_S y$ iff $y-x\in S$, and the
ordinal extension ordered by ordinary ordinal subtraction (so $S^2$ is the
\emph{lexicographic}, not coordinatewise, square), Chomp on $S$ is a
second-player win while Chomp on $S^\sigma$ is a first-player win for every
$\sigma\ge2$, and $\omega\cdot4+4$ is the \emph{least} winning opening.  Hence
$\mathrm{ch}(\langle4,5,6\rangle)=\mathrm{ch}(\langle4,6,9\rangle)=2$, a negative
answer to Question~4.6 of Rivero Herrera (arXiv:2504.07317v3) and a refutation of
Conjecture~4.1 of v1, already for three small natural-number generators.  The
proof reduces the infinite game to a finite spectral statement via a
finite-window recurrence and an exact finite-state certificate, then transfers
it by an explicit response procedure.  \emph{Merged; the shared
$\langle4,6,9\rangle$ certificates were verified to hold the same 253 rows
entry for entry, and the two reports' poisoned and unpoisoned conventions are
reconciled by an explicit dictionary.}}

\entry{Every ordinal as a point-separating game value}
{ordinals-and-order-types/games-on-ordinals/point-separating-game-values}
{ordinal\_separation.pdf}{ordinal\_separation\_research.zip}
{For every ordinal $\lambda$, orders $\{0,1\}^\lambda$ lexicographically and
gives it the topology of all downward-closed sets, and the refinement generated
by downward-closed sets minus finite sets.  The point-separating game value is
exactly $\lambda$ for both: the first space is compact $T_0$, the second compact
$T_1$, and for infinite $\lambda$ neither is Hausdorff.  Taking
$\lambda=\omega^2+1$ supplies a compact $T_1$ witness of cardinality continuum
for Problem~1.2 of Chiozini, Csern\'ak and Soukup (arXiv:2510.05754v3), with no
CH, forcing or large-cardinal hypothesis.  Hausdorff witnesses are not claimed.
This is a different problem of that paper from the one the open-query games
report answers.}

\entry{Open-query games on ordinal spaces}
{ordinals-and-order-types/games-on-ordinals/open-query-membership-games}
{article.pdf}{ordinal\_membership\_games.zip; batch~52: ProveIt\_Finite\_Output\_Open\_Query\_Games.zip; batch~56: ProveIt\_Filter\_Profiles\_Open\_Query\_Complexity.zip; batch~65: ProveIt\_Closure\_Word\_Duality.zip; batch~68: ProveIt\_Universal\_Word\_Profiles.zip}
{A negative answer to Problem~1.3 of Chiozini, Csern\'ak and Soukup
(arXiv:2510.05754v3): two copies of $[0,\omega]$ each have set-membership
number~1, but their topological sum has value~2.  The general theorem computes
the invariant for every nonempty Hausdorff space of finite Cantor--Bendixson
height~$h$ as $\lceil\log_2(h+1)\rceil$ when the last nonempty derivative is a
singleton and $\lceil\log_2(h+2)\rceil$ otherwise.  Part~II, added in
batch~52, replaces the membership bit by one of $q$ output labels: the optimal
depth is $\lceil\log_2 L\rceil$ for the least number $L$ of ordered layers with
open suffixes; on Hausdorff spaces of finite height $L\le(q-1)h+s$, sharply,
with $s$ the number of labels on the last derivative; a topological sum of
components of depth at most $d$ has depth at most $d+\lceil\log_2 q\rceil$,
sharply; and computing the optimal depth is polynomial for two labels but
NP-complete for three, even on disjoint chains.  At $q=2$ it reproduces Part~I.
Part~III, added in batch~56, answers Part~II's question of when that bound is
attained, for spaces whose derived set is finite and nonempty: a coloring with
$q_c$ colors needs exactly $q_c+\tau(G_c)$ layers, $\tau$ the directed
feedback vertex number of a color digraph; the bound is attained exactly
under a bin-partition condition on the partition capacities of the
neighborhood filters; and at three labels a sum of three one-point
free-ultrafilter spaces has value~$2$ while a sum of three convergent
sequences has value~$3$.  Part~IV, added in batch~65, drops the separation
and finiteness hypotheses for a fixed coloring: a layer word is admissible
exactly when it contains as subsequences all reduced words whose nested color
closure is nonempty.  It classifies the finite-depth behaviors, shows that the
longest such word has the length of the least Cantor--Bendixson height of a
Hausdorff representative, gives $q_c+\tau(G_c)$ layers whenever the second
derivative is empty, bounds finite-poset obstructions by $mq(q-1)^{m-1}$
points, and proves the product law $\rho(X\times Y)=\rho(X)+\rho(Y)-1$ for
that longest length~$\rho$.  Part~V, added in batch~68, computes the worst
case over colorings at every finite height on locally compact or sequential
(more generally rank-accessible) Hausdorff spaces: $\max_cL=h(q-1)+\min(q,t)$
with $t$ the size of the last nonempty derivative, also when $t$ is
infinite, attained by one coloring constant off a countable set; it adds
product and sum laws and an inverse theory of layer and query-depth spectra.
Local data determining an arbitrary fixed coloring at height two, and a
characterization of the spaces attaining that worst case (rank accessibility
is sufficient, not necessary), remain open.}

\subsection{Order-theoretic invariants without a subcategory}

Thirteen reports whose objects sit outside every subcategory above: the minor order
on finite graphs, two on sites of filters on $\omega$, congruences of finite
lattices, a translation-invariant ideal, the lexicographic order of the
well-orderings of the reals, measurable prediction in Glazer's box games and
hat guessing (batches~87, 90, 92 and~96), named elementary embeddings in
urelement set theory (batches~89, 93 and~96), and five reports of batch~114:
random bits, finite catalogues and arbitrary cuts of models of arithmetic,
noisy parity on finite and infinite cubes, robust neutral choice rules,
Freiling symmetry from finite blacklists, and bounded-width compression of
power sets.  They share a template but no main argument.

\entry{Ordinal complexity of graph minors}
{ordinals-and-order-types/graph-minor-ordinals}
{graph\_minor\_ordinals.pdf}{graph\_minor\_ordinals.zip}
{For finite simple graphs of maximum degree at most two under minor
containment, computes maximal order type, ordinal antichain-tree width and
descending-tree height: $o(D)=w(D)=\omega^{\omega\cdot2}$ and $h(D)=\omega$,
with exact values $\omega^{b+k}$ and $\omega^{b+k-1}$ for the subfamilies
bounded by path size $b$ and cyclic-component count $k$.  The general theorem is
that a wqo $C$ of connected graphs with $o(C)=\alpha<\varepsilon_0$ has finite
disjoint-union closure of type $\omega^\alpha$, proved by a component-splitting
argument, since minor containment is not multiset embedding.  A corollary is
that continuity fails for increasing unions of minor-closed classes.  The
report is explicit that it does not compute the maximal order type of the minor
order on all finite graphs and settles no named conjecture.}

\entry{A two-valued obstruction to gluing Fr\'echet powers}
{ordinals-and-order-types/frechet-two-valued-obstruction}
{article.pdf}{frechet\_two\_valued\_obstruction.zip}
{For the presheaf $F\mapsto A^\omega/{\sim_F}$ on proper filters on $\omega$
containing the cofinite filter, with the dense Grothendieck topology, proves in
ZF that it is a sheaf if and only if $|A|\le1$.  The counterexample is an
explicit matching family of constant sections using two values, on a covering
sieve with countably many generators each of countable base; the distinction
between the number of sieve generators and the size of the full sieve is
essential to it.  The question is Massas, \emph{A Semi-Constructive Approach to
the Hyperreal Line} (2023), p.~517 after Fact~5.10, repeated in the 2024
dissertation \emph{Duality and Infinity}, p.~249.}

\entry{Bounded-character Fr\'echet filter sites}
{ordinals-and-order-types/frechet-bounded-character-sites}
{article.pdf}{filter\_complexity\_research.zip, Frechet\_Cardinal\_Boundaries.zip}
{Continues the previous entry's non-claim about other filter sites.  For the
site $P_\kappa$ of free filters on $\omega$ generated by fewer than $\kappa$
sets modulo the cofinite filter, represents the Booleanization $C_\kappa$ as a
regular-open algebra on the free ultrafilters and proves that
$P(\omega)/\mathrm{Fin}$ is order dense in it exactly for $\kappa\le
\mathfrak p$; above $\mathfrak p$ no complete Boolean homomorphism preserving
the modulo-finite events exists in either direction.  The atoms are the
ultrafilters of character below $\kappa$, so $C_\kappa$ is atomless exactly for
$\kappa\le\mathfrak u$; $\mathfrak h$ is the first size of a family of events
that cannot be decided jointly on a dense set of regions.  Computes the
associated sheaf by Boolean mixtures, with full Boolean-valued transfer,
countable saturation of ultrafilter specializations, and sizes
$\mathfrak c$, $2^{\mathfrak c}$, $2^{2^{\mathfrak c}}$ for the raw power and
the two sheafifications.  A three-stage example uses Mill\'an's forcing model
as an imported input.  Merged from two manuscripts whose indexings differ by a
successor shift; the second one's closure-based results are stated at
successor stages only.}

\entry{A sharp cover bound for congruences of finite lattices}
{ordinals-and-order-types/lattice-congruence-cover-bound}
{sharp\_cover\_bound.pdf}{lattice\_cover\_bound\_research.zip}
{If a finite $n$-element lattice has an element with at least $k\ge3$ upper
covers then $|\mathrm{Con}(L)|\le2^{\,n-k-1}$, with equality exactly for the
glued sums $C_s\oplus M_k\oplus C_t$.  This replaces the multicolour Ramsey
threshold $R_{k-1}(k)$ of the source's Lemma~4.4 (arXiv:2603.11454) by $k$
itself.  Adds a sharp nonextremal gap, a fan-label ideal refinement, and a
bound on the size of Cz\'edli's skeleton in terms of congruence density.  All
congruence machinery, including Freese's maximum, is proved from scratch.  The
target is an informal suggestion in the source, not a numbered conjecture.}

\entry{A non-Baire translation-invariant ideal}
{ordinals-and-order-types/non-baire-translation-invariant-ideal}
{article.pdf}{non\_baire\_ideal\_research.zip}
{An affirmative construction for Question~1 of Filipów and Tryba,
\emph{Densities for sets of natural numbers vanishing on a given family}
(J.\ Number Theory 211 (2020) 371--382; arXiv:2309.00982, p.~7): a
translation-invariant ideal that is not Baire and carries a perfect
translation-almost-disjoint family.}

\entry{Lexicographic orders of the well-orderings of the reals}
{ordinals-and-order-types/lexicographic-well-orderings-of-reals}
{article.pdf}{batch~76: lexicographic\_well\_orderings\_of\_reals.zip + Lexicographic\_Well\_Orderings\_of\_Reals (1).zip + lexicographic\_well\_orderings\_research.zip}
{With $\kappa=2^{\aleph_0}$ and $\mathcal W$ the order of all well-orderings
of $\R$, as enumerations compared at their first unequal real entry, proves
in ZFC that $|\mathcal W|=2^\kappa$, that every linear order of size at most
$\kappa$ embeds, even into each fixed-type slice, and that an ordinal embeds
exactly when it is below $\kappa^+$; yet the binary lexicographic coding
length and the real representation rank of $\mathcal W$ are both $\kappa^+$,
so $\mathcal W$ embeds into no bounded union of strata, in particular not
into the slice $\mathcal W_\kappa$, which is equimorphic with the cube
$2^\kappa$ in lexicographic order and with the Kanovei--Shelah
ultrafilter index.  Also: each stratum $\mathcal W_{\lambda+n}$, $\lambda$ a
limit of cardinality $\kappa$, is equimorphic with $2^\lambda\times n!$;
$\mathcal W^\gamma_{\mathrm{lex}}$ is equimorphic with $\mathcal W$
exactly for $0<\gamma<\kappa^+$; adjacency and the $n!$ blocks; the exact point-character and gap spectra (every gap has a
countable side; exactly $2^\kappa$ gaps), a
$(\mathrm{cf}\,\kappa,\mathrm{cf}\,\kappa)$ gap of $\mathcal W_\kappa$, a
dense finite-condensation quotient equimorphic with $\mathcal W$, bounded
surreal sign orders and an ultrafilter index built from well-orderings.  The
cube hierarchy re-proves a corollary of \dir{point-separating-game-values},
cited first; real-power strictness is Kuhlmann's theorem; no priority is
claimed, and no intrinsic embedding criterion or classification of the gaps
of the longer strata.  \emph{Merged from three independent manuscripts
proving the same core: each shared theorem printed once, with the other
arguments as second routes.  Each answers a question of another: the ZFC
non-embedding into $\mathcal W_\kappa$, which one had only under
$2^{<\kappa}<2^\kappa$, and the cut and character spectra and the quotient
another had asked about.}}

\entry{Countable information, uncountably many boxes: measurable prediction, hat-guessing surplus, adaptive extensions, query and randomness thresholds}
{ordinals-and-order-types/measurable-box-games}
{article.pdf}{batch~87: glazer\_box\_games\_research.zip (Part~I); batch~90: hat\_guessing\_research.zip (Part~II); batch~92: adaptive\_box\_games\_research.zip (Part~III), hat\_query\_thresholds\_package.zip (base) + hat\_inspection\_frontier.zip (Part~IV); batch~96: hat\_randomness\_frontier.zip (Part~V)}
{Part~I takes up the question that ends Section~5 of Glazer's
\emph{A choiceless box game paradox} (arXiv:2211.10474): does ZF prove that
three players facing continuum many boxes of bits can guarantee that at most
one of them guesses wrong?  It separates a measurable choice of box and
guess, a measurable success event and actual executability, and answers the
question for the first class only.  For a standard Borel set of boxes,
alphabet size $q$ and $m$ blind output maps measurable for the cylinder
$\sigma$-algebra, the guaranteed number of correct guesses is exactly
$\lfloor m/q\rfloor$, without assuming the success events measurable; a
countable team has a fair simultaneous measure extension; measurable
success forces a countable target range; a continuous selector has a success
event of inner measure~$0$ and outer measure~$1$.  Blind binary maps on $n$
boxes are labelled perfect matchings of the cube, executable maps exactly
the recursively sliceable ones (the two classes first differ at four
boxes); their number $F_n=1,2,9,232,206065,\dots$ satisfies an exact
recurrence and the all-orders expansion
$F_n=e^{\gamma2^n}n^{-1}(1-\tfrac3{2n}+\tfrac{17}{4n^2}-\tfrac{12}{n^3}+O(n^{-4}))$
with a growth constant $\gamma\approx0.438158$, not Euler's; a doubly
exponential rarity bound, information inequalities, an oracle extraction of
finite losing prefixes, and a Busy Beaver comparison that is conditional on
an encoding and uses a program-length running-time function, not Rado's
score function.  Neither counting sequence was in the OEIS on 3~October
2026.  Part~II (batch~90) answers both questions of Eldredge's Remark~6.8
(arXiv:2508.02828) on the surplus $D_n=S_n-n/2$ of correct guesses in the
infinite binary hat game: Hamming-syndrome blocks give a computable
strategy with $D_n=\log_2n+O(1)$ almost surely, and for every positive
$g=o(n)$ a continuous strategy with $D_n/g(n)\to\infty$ almost surely, so no sublinear
envelope is universal; the coding mechanism is credited to
Ebert--Merkle--Vollmer.  Its finite corollary settles Part~I's
question~Q9 for $N=2a(2^r-1)$ binary players and threshold $a2^r$: the
optimum is exactly $1-2^{-r}$, over legal trees and blind maps alike.
Part~III (batch~92) classifies every extension of the product measure to
the success events of adaptive players by measurable densities on a random
set of feasible success patterns, which answers Part~I's question~Q5, with
an atomic-mass law, a Hall polytope of joint laws and kernels for countable
teams; the minimax $\lfloor m/q\rfloor$ holds for full Borel and
universally measurable blind outputs, two of the three clauses of Q2,
while for Baire-property outputs an invariant comeagre null set blocks the
averaging proof and that clause stays open.  Part~IV (batch~92) merges two
independent manuscripts that answer Part~II's question on the cost of
inspecting hats by the same construction: a common bound on adaptive
queries makes divergence of the surplus null and meagre, and the exact
expected-query threshold for deterministic trees is~2, attained by a
computable strategy with fewer than two expected queries per player.  The
second source adds a lower bound for every nondecreasing cost, a central
limit theorem and a classification at minimum depth that settles Q9 for
$(m(2^r-1),2,m(2^r-1))$ at threshold $m2^{r-1}$ for every $m\ge1$.  Two
sentences calling the remaining cases of Q9 open are retained and refuted:
the batch-90 one by Part~I's own three-box team (Remark~15.1), and the
batch-92 one on every $q\ge3$ case above $\lfloor m/q\rfloor$ by
zero-query constant guesses at a shared target, which attain $m/(qt)$
whenever $t$ divides~$m$ (Remark~15.2).
Part~V (batch~96) answers the almost-sure clauses of Part~IV's
question~57.2, on that cost when random bits are free: with independent
private coins, or with a public seed that is almost surely finitely
valued, the admissible uniform expected inspection caps are $(1,\infty)$,
the endpoint excluded (at cap~1 private coins succeed with probability at
most $1-1/5184$), whereas one shared random integer of countably infinite
support attains cap~1 with every individual expected cost below~1 and
arbitrarily small entropy.  A prescribed public seed with values in a
standard Borel space permits cap~1 iff it is not almost surely finitely
valued, and if the cap must hold conditionally on the public seed the
caps are $(1,\infty)$ with private coins and $[2,\infty)$ without.  The
tools are a sign bound by projection onto the players who inspect
nothing, owner-indexed Walsh accounting and a stopped supermartingale
lemma; Part~IV's thresholds and Bonami's lemma are re-proved with credit.
The private positive-probability threshold at cap~1 stays open.  The
manuscript's hypothesis ``finite positive-probability support'' makes its
finite-public theorem false as worded, since a seed with one atom and a
diffuse part permits the endpoint (Remark~75.6); the write prints the
hypothesis as probability-one concentration on finitely many values, with
the delivered wording on record, corrects the abstract's ``countably
supported'' seed to countably infinite support, and moves three unproved
statements, among them the unsupported statement that its proofs were
independently reviewed, to the questions.  The publication review \texttt{47ab39154} then refuted two
sentences of the write itself, retained in Remark~75.9: the guide's
extension of the gap $1/5184$ to finite public seeds (a two-valued public
gate succeeds with probability at least $9999/10000$ below cap~1) and an
equality in the proof of Corollary~75.7, now an inequality; neither
theorem changes.
Glazer's question itself is not answered; Part~I's Q1 is not the
``Question~1'' of the surreal report
\dir{polish-models-of-omnific-arithmetic}.  The Hilbert's-tenth programme
reviewed Part~I's computability claims (\texttt{f15962acd}, not its
probability, enumeration or asymptotic claims), routed Part~II's
archive from its READMEs only (\texttt{bcc1a4438}) and reviewed the
publication of Parts~III--IV within a stated scope (\texttt{42b49fdbd},
the source of Remark~15.2); for Part~V it read the manuscript in two
bounded passes (\texttt{18825ee16}, \texttt{1eab36bb3}, both reading the
seed hypothesis as atomic), authenticated the placement
(\texttt{608e06daa}), confirmed the hypothesis correction independently
(\texttt{c52f7d055}) and reviewed the publication (\texttt{47ab39154}).
No priority; not formalized.}

\entry{Naming elementary embeddings: atom components, Replacement, reflection, commuting actions and relations among injections}
{ordinals-and-order-types/naming-elementary-embeddings}
{article.pdf}{batch~89: Naming\_Elementary\_Embeddings\_Research.zip (Part~I); batch~93: commuting\_actions\_replacement.zip (Part~II); batch~96: atom\_actions\_research.zip (base) + commuting\_injections\_research.zip (Part~III)}
{In Yao's kernel-ideal models $M_\kappa(A)$ of urelement set theory with
Replacement and choice (objects whose transitive closures involve fewer
than $\kappa$ atoms), every injection $f$ of the atoms lifts to a canonical
amenable elementary self-embedding $j_f$ fixing every pure set, and the
initial elementary self-embeddings are exactly these lifts.  When finitely
many lifts are named, so that the schemes range over formulas containing
them, Replacement holds exactly when the small type blocks of the atom
graph have fewer than $\kappa$ atoms (for $\kappa=\omega$: finite components
and a finite small part).  Hence every one-name expansion keeps Replacement
iff $\mathrm{cf}\,\kappa>\omega$, every expansion by two or more names iff
$\mathrm{cf}\,\kappa>2^{\aleph_0}$, and at $\aleph_2$ universal two-name
preservation is equivalent, externally, to CH (a characterization, not a
proof or refutation of CH).  Collection fails at limit cutoffs already for
$\Delta_0$ formulas of the base language and holds in full exactly at an
uncountable successor $\kappa$ with fewer than $\kappa$ component types;
full transitive reflection has the same criterion; two individually safe
automorphisms can be jointly unsafe.  The kernel-ideal construction is
Yao's and the lifting idea is not claimed new.  The same Collection spectrum
is proved independently in Part~VI of the surreal report
\dir{birthday-cutoffs-and-hereditary-sets}, whose Part~VIII (batch~90)
contains the case $\kappa=\omega$ of the criterion for finitely many named
permutations, proved independently.  Part~II (batch~93) names commuting
maps: every tuple of commuting permutations keeps expanded Replacement iff
$\mathrm{cf}\,\kappa>\omega$, which answers Part~I's question on commuting
automorphisms in full; with commuting injections added the threshold is
$\mathrm{cf}\,\kappa>\omega$ for at most one injection and
$\mathrm{cf}\,\kappa>2^{\aleph_0}$ for two or more, and two commuting
proper injections, each harmless alone, can jointly destroy Replacement;
for commuting permutations the atom kernel of definable closure is computed
exactly (Part~I's Question~10.1 in that case); for a finitely generated
group named by generators every action keeps Replacement iff the group is
finite ($\kappa=\omega$) or $\mathrm{cf}\,\kappa$ exceeds its number of
conjugacy classes of subgroups; one uniform evaluator for an action of
$\bigoplus_\omega C_2$ with orbits of at most two atoms destroys
Replacement whenever $\mathrm{cf}\,\kappa\le2^{\aleph_0}$ while separate
names keep it; and set forcing that keeps $\kappa$ a cardinal preserves the
verdict for a fixed action (the fixed-action part of Question~10.10).  Part~VIII
of the surreal report is credited as prior at $\kappa=\omega$; one imported
group-existence theorem (Ivanov--Ol'shanskii, read through Smith) is not
proved, and its primary check is an open question.  Part~III (batch~96)
merges two independent manuscripts on relations among named injections,
which answer Part~II's Question~23.3 by the same main theorem: for a finitely
generated commutative monoid acting by injections, with $G$ the group
completion of its cancellative image and $d$ the rank of $G$ modulo the
units, the connected labelled types number $|\mathrm{Sub}(G)|$ if $G$ is
finite, $\aleph_0$ if $G$ is infinite and $d\le1$, and $2^{\aleph_0}$ (with
a rigid Cantor family) if $d\ge2$; $d$ is decidable from a finite
commutative presentation; every such action keeps Replacement for all
$\kappa$, iff $\mathrm{cf}\,\kappa>\aleph_0$, iff
$\mathrm{cf}\,\kappa>2^{\aleph_0}$ in the three cases; the relation
$f^m=g^n$ is enumerated by necklace counts, with rational Catalan numbers
in the coprime case; and forcing adds a connected type for $d\ge2$ iff it
adds a real.  The base adds pure-real rooted codes of components, a
centralizer-orbit count that generalizes a lemma of Part~VIII of the
surreal report to injections and infinite components, and the exact atom
kernel of the objects definable from given atoms and finitely many pure
reals, for any finite tuple of injections; so Part~II's Questions~23.1
and~23.2 and Part~I's Question~10.1 are answered only with pure real
parameters, and ordinary definability stays open.  Results proved by both
sources are printed once, with the second proof as a marked route.
Re-scoped at the write: the second source's ``commutative answer'' to
Question~23.5 decides only the commutative-monoid analogue, and the base's
countability argument for the parameter-free definable kernel is
metatheoretic, kept as prose and asked as Question~37.17.  The
Hilbert's-tenth research tree read the second manuscript in full and
selected interfaces of the base before placement (\texttt{18825ee16}),
authenticated the placement (\texttt{b16786caf}) and reviewed the write
within a bounded scope without finding a mathematical defect
(\texttt{6d78c238d}); \texttt{6d06f8952} corrected the README's sentence
that no review of Part~III existed, retaining it as a review remark.  It continues
the formal projects \dir{SetTheory/ZF} and \dir{SetTheory/BoundedConsistency}
semantically and proposes a formalization route; their formula type has no
atom predicate, and nothing here is formalized.  Unrefereed; priority not
established.}

\entry{Random bits, finite catalogs and arbitrary cuts of arithmetic: exact noise thresholds, Boolean universality and the strength of induction}
{ordinals-and-order-types/random-bits-arithmetic-cuts}
{article.pdf}{batch~114: Random\_Bits\_Arithmetic\_Cuts.zip}
{For a countable model $M$ of PA and an external random predicate
$P\subseteq M$, the coding cut $\mathrm{Cut}(P)$ is the initial segment of
those $a$ for which $P\cap[0,a)$ has an internal binary code.  For
pairwise-independent bits with biases $p_t$, modal bit $b$ and
$\varepsilon_t=\min(p_t,1-p_t)$, almost surely
$\mathrm{Cut}(P)=\mathrm{Cut}(b)\cap\{a:\sum_{t<a}\varepsilon_t<\infty\}$
(external sums).  Fair bits give the standard cut, announced by Elliot
Glazer (2023, for PA ``or even Q''); the PA case is reconstructed with the
report's own formula, and neither the Q case nor uncountable models are
claimed.  Every cut is the almost-sure coding cut of a full-support
nonatomic product law; for independent arrays a family of conjunction cuts
is realizable iff it is inclusion monotone, and every finitary Boolean
observable has the intersection of the cuts of its minimal sensitive
supports.  A proper cut refutes $\mathsf{I}\Sigma_1(P)$;
$\mathrm{Cut}(P)=M$ iff $(M,P)$ satisfies $\mathsf{I}\Delta_0(P,\mathrm{E})$;
for zero-modal noise, $W=\sum_ap_a$ separates three regimes (full
induction; $\mathsf{I}\Delta_0(P,\mathrm{E})$ without $\mathsf{I}\Sigma_1(P)$;
neither).  Also category and Baire-class results, the sharp pairwise atom
bound $1/(N+1)$ or $1/(N+2)$ (Benjamini--Gurel-Gurevich--Peled, credited),
and finite catalogs: bounded budgets iff $\sum\varepsilon_i<\infty$, an exact
Hamming-layer formula, the normal transition (Kontoyiannis--Verd\'u,
Chen--Effros--Kostina, credited) and a Poisson staircase for
$p_N=\lambda/N$.  The write proves Remark~13.1: $\mathrm{Cut}(P)=M$ makes
$P$ a class, hence not strongly CP-generic (Abdul-Quader--Schmerl), partly
settling Question~5; all fourteen questions stay open and priority is not
established.  Only the passing remark that PA has no finite models is
formalized (\dir{Logic/PeanoArithmetic/NoFiniteModel}; the independent
check added same-named copies in an unimported optimization file); beta
coding and a definable-cut induction failure of
\dir{Logic/PeanoArithmetic/NotFinitelyAxiomatizable} are ingredients, and
the route of Section~11 is a proposal.  Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{Noisy parity on finite and infinite cubes: exact coloring, inspection budgets, asymptotic laws and measurable choice}
{ordinals-and-order-types/noisy-parity-cubes}
{article.pdf}{batch~114: Noisy\_Parity\_Finite\_and\_Infinite\_Cubes.zip}
{A uniform configuration $X$ of finitely or countably many bits is
compared with a copy $Y$ in which one coordinate, drawn with ordered
weights $p_1\ge p_2\ge\cdots$, is flipped and every bit is flipped
independently with probability $\eta$; one measurable label $f$ succeeds
when $f(X)\ne f(Y)$.  For $0<\eta<1/2$ the optimum is
$\max_k[1+(1-2\eta)^k(2P_k-1)]/2$, $P_k=p_1+\dots+p_k$, attained by a
prefix parity on $K$ coordinates, $K$ finite also on the countable cube,
with at most one adjacent tie and a first-sign-change certificate; under
strictly decreasing weights the optima are exactly the signed prefix
parities, with a quantitative stability gap.  The exact expected-inspection
frontier is linear between the points $(k,w_k)$, $k\le K$, and constant
beyond, attained by shared mixtures of adjacent prefix parities; a spectral
simulation replaces every measurable rule by a random parity $\chi_S$ with
$\Pr(S=T)=\hat f(T)^2$, at mean cost equal to the total influence.  At
zero noise the measurable supremum~1 is not attained.  A total parity label
exists under AC, and over ZF exactly under a restricted choice of
$E_{\mathrm{ev}}$-classes; it is measurable for a biased product measure
iff $\sum\min(q_i,1-q_i)<\infty$; for summable heterogeneous noise the
optimization over all subsets of $\mathbb N$ is compact and, in ZFC, a label
with a measurable success event attains its value.  Small-noise laws for
uniform weights (saturation at $\eta n=1$), geometric weights (a
logarithmically periodic term), regularly varying and Zipf weights.  The
write adds, with proofs, Remark~5.2 (no deterministic decision tree attains
the frontier at a non-integer budget $0<c<K$) and Remark~6.4 (on the
countable cube the stability gap tends to~0 as $\eta\to0$).  Two classical
lemmas of Part~IV of \dir{measurable-box-games} (total influence at most
the expected number of inspections; the Walsh degree of a depth-$d$ tree)
are re-proved and printed as second routes.  The machinery is classical
(O'Donnell; Geschke--Lubarsky--Rahn and Serafin; Conley--Miller), priority
is not established, and fifteen further questions stay open.  The
independent check found nothing to correct.  Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{Robust choices that never settle: an exact finite--infinite puzzle about resilience, choice and randomness}
{ordinals-and-order-types/robust-neutral-choice}
{article.pdf}{batch~114: robust\_choices.zip}
{A neutral rule $f:\{-1,1\}^n\to\{-1,1\}$ ($f(-x)=-f(x)$) is robust to $r$
edits at $x$ when no $r$ coordinate changes flip its output.  The largest
probability of robustness is $2^{1-d}\sum_{j\le(d-1)/2-r}\binom dj$, $d$
the largest odd integer $\le n$, attained for every $r$ at once by majority
of $d$ voters, so an even committee does no better than one fewer member: a
corollary of Kleitman's diameter theorem, credited to Kleitman and to
Huang--Klurman--Pohoata, with the reduction and the constructions proved.
A sharp prefix inequality bounds $\|\mathbb E[f\mid\mathcal F_m]\|_\infty$
by $2^m/V(m,r)$ times the vulnerability, $V$ the Hamming-ball volume, with
the best dimension-free constant.  If one-edit vulnerability tends to~0 the
decisions are mixing and change sign infinitely often on a conull comeagre
set; a Hamming-code construction separates one-edit from two-edit
resilience; a subsequence makes the decision map a continuous open
surjection with density within $e^{\pm\eta}$ of fair Bernoulli measure.  In
ZFC a neutral rule invariant under finite changes exists on all infinite
streams, none is measurable and neither fibre has the Baire property; for a
product of Bernoulli$(p_i)$ laws a completed-measurable one exists iff
$\sum(p_i-1/2)^2=\infty$, a deduction from Kakutani's dichotomy.  The write
proves Proposition~11.1 (for several labels and a group with no global
fixed label the fibres are not all measurable and not all Baire), the
regularity half of Question~6; re-scopes Questions~1 and~8 (existence of
the rule is equivalent over ZF to choosing one of each complementary pair
of $E_0$-classes); and in Remark~10.1 derives the category half of the ZFC
theorem from proved declarations of
\dir{SetTheory/Cardinals/Cardinals/Countable/GenericErgodicity.lean}, given
complementation as a homeomorphism, which the library does not define.
The finite statements were checked exhaustively in dimensions 1--5
(65{,}814 neutral rules); ten questions stay open; no priority is claimed.
The independent check found nothing to correct.  Unrefereed; not
formalized.}

\entry{From finite blacklists to Freiling symmetry: exact avoidance thresholds, critical tournaments, choice-sensitive colorings and perfect safe sets}
{ordinals-and-order-types/freiling-symmetry-blacklists}
{article.pdf}{batch~114: From\_Finite\_Blacklists\_to\_Freiling\_Symmetry.zip}
{In a $k$-bounded blacklist system each point refuses at most $k$ others;
a set is safe if no two members refuse each other.  Every system on $n$
points has a safe set of size $\lceil n/(2k+1)\rceil$, which is sharp; at
the critical size $n=(r-1)(2k+1)$ every system without a safe $r$-set has
conflict graph $(r-1)K_{2k+1}$ with each block a regular tournament, and
these systems are counted exactly.  The least number of safe pairs is
$\max\{0,\binom n2-kn\}$,
$A_r(n,k)=\binom nr-kn\binom{n-2}{r-2}+O(n^{r-2})$, and $A_3(n,1)$ is
exact.  The partition of an arbitrary system into $2k+1$ safe sets is
Theorem~3 of de Bruijn and Erd\H{o}s (1951), credited; over ZF the report
proves $\mathrm{BPI}\Rightarrow\mathrm{SAP}_k\Rightarrow
C_2\wedge\dots\wedge C_{k+1}$ and $\mathrm{SAP}_k$ on well-orderable sets.
A Borel relation with countable sections on a perfect Polish space has a
perfect safe set, a definable strengthening of the conclusion of Freiling's
axiom of symmetry (the axiom itself is not proved), and almost every
i.i.d.\ sample from an atomless measure is an infinite safe set.  The write
proves Propositions~12.16--12.18: the pseudoforest Conjecture~12.5 for
$n\le9$ (an unshipped exact computation reaches $n\le18$) and
Conjecture~12.6 for $n\in\{2k+1,2(2k+1)\}$; $\mathrm{SAP}_k\Rightarrow C_3$,
new for $k=1$; and
$\mathrm{BPI}\Rightarrow\mathrm{SAP}^+_{\mathrm{fin}}\Rightarrow
\mathrm{SAP}_{\mathrm{fin}}\Rightarrow C_{\mathrm{fin}}$, with
$\mathrm{SAP}_{\mathrm{fin}}$ de Bruijn--Erd\H{o}s's countable-colour
theorem; it identifies two of the counts as OEIS A133686 and A007079.  The
independent check showed, with Jech's Theorem~7.16, that $C_2$ does not
imply $C_3$ over ZF, so $\mathrm{SAP}_1$ is strictly stronger than choice
for pairs; its exact strength stays open, and all seventeen further items
are stated with what is missing.  No priority is claimed for the finite
refinements, and the free-set literature was not surveyed.  Unrefereed;
not formalized.}

\entry{Bounded-width power-set compression: exact finite thresholds, choiceless infinite obstructions and a defect extension of Glazer's chain-fiber theorem}
{ordinals-and-order-types/bounded-width-power-set-compression}
{article.pdf}{batch~114: Bounded\_Width\_Power\_Set\_Compression.zip}
{Studies maps $f:\mathcal P(X)\to X$ whose fibers contain no antichain of
$r+1$ sets, the property $\mathrm{SC}_r(X)$, extending Glazer's $r=1$
theorem (his accepted MathOverflow answer, imported).  With
$W_n=\binom n{\lfloor n/2\rfloor}$, a finite poset of width $w$ needs
exactly $\lceil w/r\rceil$ labels (Dilworth), so
$q_r(n)=\lceil W_n/r\rceil$, with middle-layer and grouped symmetric-chain
certificates; $\mathcal P([n])\to[n]$ exists iff $W_n\le rn$, so the least
admissible width is $\delta(n)=\lceil W_n/n\rceil=1,1,1,2,2,4,5,9,14,26,\dots$,
$\delta(n)=\sqrt{2/\pi}\,2^nn^{-3/2}(1+O(1/n))$, and the largest admissible
$n$ is $N(r)=L+\tfrac32\log_2L+\tfrac12\log_2(\pi/2)+O(1)$, $L=\log_2r$.
Over ZF an infinite set with $\mathrm{SC}_r$ is Dedekind-infinite, with an
injection of $\mathbb N$ definable from $f$ and finitely many points, so no
Dedekind-finite or amorphous set has it; through Forster's theorem no
infinite $X$ with $X\times2\preccurlyeq X$ and no infinite well-orderable
$X$ has it, so ZFC proves $\mathrm{SC}_r(X)$ iff $X$ is finite, nonempty
and $W_{|X|}\le r|X|$.  The ZF case $r\ge2$ is posed as open, with the
profile of a counterexample.  Wrong as printed, and kept with a numbered
Remark~9.10 of the write: the definition of an antichain code does not
require injectivity, and then the antichain-transfer lemma and theorem are
false ($X=\{0,1,2\}$, $r=1$, $\Theta$ constantly $\emptyset$, where
$\mathrm{SC}_1(X)$ holds); requiring injectivity repairs them, and the only
code the source constructs is injective, so the ZFC classification stands,
as the independent check confirmed.  That code is Pruss's for $r=1$ in the
same MathOverflow thread, credited by the write.  The source's statement
that ProveIt already has finite-poset and Boolean-lattice work is scoped:
no Lean or Rocq file treats Dilworth, Sperner or poset width.  Seventeen
further items (F1--F17) are stated with what is missing; priority is not
established.  Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\section{Automata and formal languages}

Eight reports on state complexity, synchronization, language hierarchies,
crossover closures, additive complexity and the asymptotic enumeration of
automata.
Three are merges: the shuffle bound arrived four times, the DFAO reversal
bound twice, and the last report joins a manuscript on accessible automata
with one on strongly connected automata that adapts its method.

\entry{The six-state shuffle bound}
{automata-and-formal-languages/shuffle-six-state-bound}
{shuffle\_six\_state.pdf}{shuffle\_six\_state\_research (1).zip + shuffle\_six\_state\_research.zip + Shuffle\_Six\_State\_Theorem.zip + six\_state\_shuffle\_research.zip}
{For complete DFAs with $m,n\ge2$ and $\min(m,n)\le6$, the worst-case
deterministic state complexity of the shuffle is
$F(m,n)=2^{mn-1}+2^{(m-1)(n-1)}(2^{m-1}-1)(2^{n-1}-1)$, over a finite alphabet
allowed to depend on $m$ and $n$; the unrestricted conjecture stays open.  The
route is a reachability theorem in the universal shuffle automaton --- a subset
is reachable exactly when it meets the initial row and the initial column ---
proved by induction on $(m+n,|S|)$ down to a finite certificate obligation.
\emph{Merged from four reports.  Two shipped the same 10{,}642 witnesses; a
third supplies an independent database of 10{,}619 witnesses sharing no witness
with them, so the finite step is certified twice over; a fourth adds a fifth
analytic reduction that cuts the obligation to 453 certificates and replaces
the solver search with a deterministic enumeration.  All three routes are
kept.}}

\entry{A coloring obstruction to maximal binary DFAO reversal}
{automata-and-formal-languages/dfao-reversal-coloring-obstruction}
{article.pdf}{dfao\_reversal\_research.zip + binary\_dfao\_reversal\_research.zip; batch~39: ProveIt\_Three\_Output\_Reversal.zip; batches~40--41: ProveIt\_DFAO\_Eventual\_Optimality.zip, ProveIt\_Binary\_DFAO\_Reversal.zip, ProveIt\_Binary\_Reversal\_Exactness.zip; batch~43: ProveIt\_Four\_Output\_Reversal.zip, ProveIt\_Four\_Output\_Reversal~(1).zip; batch~67: ProveIt\_Near\_Linear\_DFAO\_Reversal.zip, ProveIt\_DFAO\_Spectral\_Cancellation.zip}
{For transformations $a,b$ on an $n$-element set and an output map to $k$
labels, proves $|\tau\langle a,b\rangle|\le k^n-k!+g(k)<k^n$ for $3\le k\le n$,
with $g(k)$ the largest order of a permutation of $k$ symbols --- the
binary-alphabet nonattainment question of Davies, arXiv:1705.07150v2,
Section~5.  The mechanism is a collision-orbit graph, a rightmost singular
factorization, and a cyclic relabeling bound.  Adds an abelian-permutation
extension, a structural universal bound matching the published lower
construction on all 372 pairs with $7\le n\le30$, and polynomial-time
missing-coloring certificates.  \emph{Merged; the two originals ran the same
three-case argument, and both a qualitative short proof and the counting proof
are kept.}  Part~II, added in batch~39, settles three outputs exactly:
$R_2(n,3)=3^n-3(2^A+2^B-2)+B$ for every $n\ge7$, with $(A,B)$ the closest
coprime split of $n$, together with rigidity of the extremizers; the formula
matches all twenty-four $k=3$ rows of the finite table.  Part~III, merged in
batches~40--41 from three manuscripts, settles every $k\ge4$ past an explicit
threshold: $R_2(n,k)=k^n-\mathrm{Bip}_k(a_n,b_n)+a_nb_n$ once $n\ge N(k)\le3k^4$,
and for all $n\ge\max(7,k+1)$ when $k=4,\dots,7$ (computer-assisted below
$N(k)$), with the lower construction imported from Davies; a non-effective
eventual route for every $k\ge3$, 919 finite certificates and a second proof of
the $k=3$ formula are kept.  Explicit exactness for $k\ge8$ below
$\min(N(k),T(k))$, with $T(k)$ the threshold of Part~V, stays open.  Part~IV,
merged in batch~43 from two manuscripts on $k=4$, re-derives that case with an
analytic threshold $n\ge26$ (against $212$), and adds stability, the
missing-orbit inventory, the extremal output maps and a minimal order-15
recurrence.  Part~V, added in batch~67, proves the formula for every $k\ge4$
once $n\ge T(k)=4k\log k+O(k)$ instead of $n\ge3k^4$ (so for all $k$ up to
$cn/\log n$, $c<1/4$), with a theta-function limit of the optimal deficit when
$n/k^2$ tends to a constant and a Gaussian law in between.  Part~VI, added in
batch~67, determines the minimal recurrences of the explicit witness deficit
for every fixed $k$: order~9 at three outputs (against Part~II's~12), a
product-12 cancellation on the odd residues but not in the full sequence at
nineteen outputs, the only product-12 cancellation at any $k$, and explicit
tail numerators.}

\entry{Synchronizing D\.zyga's automata by a cyclic retract}
{automata-and-formal-languages/dzyga-synchronizing-retract}
{article.pdf}{Dzyga\_Automata\_Cyclic\_Retract\_Research.zip}
{For the Figure-3-complete automaton $A_k$ of Conjecture~8 in Szyku\l{}a's 2026
survey, exhibits an explicit word $R_k=p(ec^{r-1})^{r-2}e$ with $r=k+4$ that
sends every state to $q_2$ for every $k\ge1$, of expanded length
$8k^2+54k+92=\tfrac89n^2+\tfrac{22}3n+16$ for $n=3k+6$.  The proof is a cyclic
retraction with two projected reflections whose discrepancy supplies one
adjacent merger, and does not depend on the finite computations; shortening the
reflected tail by one state gives a nonsynchronizing automaton with a dihedral
permutation quotient.  This settles the synchronization clause of the stated
model, not the numerical threshold conjecture, and the universal lower bound
$\tau(q_0)\ge4k+8$ is not proved.}

\entry{Gaps in bounded-shuffle hierarchies}
{automata-and-formal-languages/insertion-degree-spectra}
{article.pdf}{shuffle\_spectra\_research.zip; batch~41: ProveIt\_Binary\_Insertion\_Spectra.zip; batch~66: ProveIt\_Sharp\_Insertion\_Isolation.zip}
{A counterexample to the insertion-degree No-Gap Conjecture of Hughes,
arXiv:2608.27755v1, Section~11, followed by positive results: every finite set
of positive integers containing~1 is the insertion-degree spectrum of two
nonempty finite languages over a suitable alphabet, and any prescribed finite
list of degrees above~1 is realizable with every other output of degree~1.  A
ternary pair has spectrum $\{1,3\}$ with sole difficult output \texttt{abcba},
whose length~5 is the minimum possible for a gap witness; a binary pair has
spectrum $\{1,2,4\}$ while all 18 singleton source-pair spectra are initial
intervals, so source-pair ambiguity can erase a degree.  The separate
iteration-depth no-gap conjecture is proved for arbitrary languages.  Part~II,
added in batch~41, fixes the alphabet: every finite set containing~1 is the
spectrum of finite \emph{binary} languages with the single inserted word $a^m$,
so two letters always suffice; its $m^2$ length bound holds in its
full-coverage class only.  Part~III, added in batch~66, determines that class
exactly: an isolated degree-$m$ output with $g$ gaps between its $a$'s
(the two end gaps included) and $T$ letters~$a$ exists exactly when $g\ge m$
and $T\ge\max(g(m-1)+1,\tau_m)$, with $\tau_3=12$ and $\tau_m=5m-2$ for
$m\ge4$, so the minimum lengths for $m=3,4,5$ are $14$, $21$ and~$27$.  It
characterizes the extremal centers and the mandatory targets, and at $m=3$
classifies the shortest target languages: $3^9=19{,}683$ of them, of minimum
size~37, attained by~512.}

\entry{Constrained crossover closure}
{automata-and-formal-languages/constrained-crossover-closure}
{article.pdf}{ProveIt\_Constrained\_Crossover.zip (batch~44); batch~66: ProveIt\_Crossover\_Stabilization.zip, ProveIt\_Crossover\_Classification.zip; batch~71: quadratic-finite-rank-package.zip; batch~73: rational-rank-slopes-package.zip, dfa-crossover-package.zip, common-path-rank-package.zip (the earlier editions dfa-crossover-package~(1).zip and common-path-rank-package~(1).zip superseded, not printed)}
{Answers the question Hughes (arXiv:2608.27755v1, Section~4) leaves open on
the complexity of deciding $L\otimes_c L=L$ for the constrained crossover of a
context-free language: it is co-r.e.-complete.  Adds an aligned-rank no-gap
theorem for iterated crossover and effectivity results in which Presburger
quantifier elimination is used only as an algorithmic step.  Related to, but
using nothing from, the insertion-degree report above.  Part~II, added in
batch~66, shows finite stabilization of regular seeds PSPACE-complete for NFA
input, already over two letters; the upper bound also covers DFA input,
improving Part~I's EXPSPACE bound (Part~VI settles DFA input);
in the stabilizing case the rank is at most quadratic in the number of
states.  Part~III, added in batch~66, decides regularity, inclusion and
equality of the full closures of context-free seeds, classifies the finitely
stabilizing sparse one-marker seeds into regular, linear non-regular and
non-context-free closures, and shows that thresholding the exact
stabilization depth is coNP-complete for succinct ray input.  Part~IV, added
in batch~71, shows that the quadratic order is attained for NFAs: the
largest finite rank $F(s)$ of a binary language recognized by an $s$-state
NFA satisfies $(s-3)^2\le F(s)\le(2s+5)^2$ for $s\ge4$, the lower bound
replacing Part~II's linear one.  Parts~V--VII, added in batch~73, answer the
questions Parts~II and~IV left.  Part~V: on every live residue the largest
rank of a length-$n$ hull word is $\sigma_rn+O(1)$ with a rational,
computable slope $\sigma_r$, and $F(s)\le2(s-1)^2+3$.  Part~VI: finite
stabilization of an explicit complete binary DFA is coNP-complete, and the
largest finite rank of an $s$-state complete binary DFA satisfies
$(s-7)^2+2\le F_{\mathrm{DFA}}(s)\le s^2+3$ for $s\ge8$.  Part~VII:
$F(s)\le s^2+8s$ for binary NFAs ($s^2+\max(8,2q-4)s$ over $q$ letters), so
$F(s)/s^2\to1$ and $F_{\mathrm{DFA}}(s)/s^2\to1$, with an exact algorithm
and $F(1),\dots,F(4)=1,2,5,7$, the last three computer-assisted.  The exact
values beyond four states and the lower-order terms stay open.  Lemmas the
new Parts re-prove are cited, not reprinted, and two lemmas shared by
Parts~VI and~VII are printed once.}

\entry{A cut-sensitive attack on the first open MIX grammar case}
{automata-and-formal-languages/mix-grammar-cut-sensitive}
{mix\_grammar\_report.pdf}{mix\_grammar\_research.zip}
{The selected question --- whether $L_3=\mathrm{MIX}$ for the canonical tuple
grammars of Yuyama, \emph{On the Kanazawa--Salvati Conjecture}
(arXiv:2609.13871v1) --- is \emph{not} settled here, and the report says so at
the front.  What it does contain, with complete written proofs, is a
cut-sensitive membership recurrence, a broad three-component positive family,
an infinite family in $L_3\setminus L_2$, and an exhaustive computation through
length~21.}

\entry{Natural densities of the Tribonacci word's additive complexities}
{automata-and-formal-languages/tribonacci-additive-complexity}
{article.pdf}{tribonacci\_additive\_density\_research.zip}
{Answers Remark~19 of Popoli, Shallit and Stipulanti (arXiv:2410.02409).  With
$\beta$ the Tribonacci constant, the additive complexity of the Tribonacci word
takes values in $\{3,4,5\}$ with natural densities
$d_3=(21\beta^2-38\beta+5)/22$, $d_4=(-55\beta^2+72\beta+67)/22$ and
$d_5=(17\beta^2-17\beta-25)/11$, all positive and summing to~1, with a
power-saving error $O(N^\theta)$, $\theta=\log(7/5)/\log\beta$, at
\emph{every} integer cutoff rather than only along Tribonacci cutoffs.  The
76-state output automaton is reconstructed independently rather than copied,
and certified by evaluating its degree-76 characteristic polynomial at the
adjacency matrix.}

\entry{Accessible and strongly connected deterministic automata, to every fixed order}
{automata-and-formal-languages/accessible-and-strong-automata}
{article.pdf}{batch~77: accessible-automata-reproducibility.zip + strong-automata-reproducibility.zip}
{For a fixed alphabet of $k\ge2$ letters, with $t=k(1-e^{-t})$,
$\rho=e^{-t}$, $v=t/k$ and $c=1-k\rho$, Part~I proves that the number $a_n$
of rooted accessible complete deterministic transition structures on $n$
states (OEIS A006689, A006690) satisfies
$a_n/S(kn+1,n)=\sum_{j\le J}c_jn^{-j}+O(n^{-J-1})$ for every $J$, with
$c_0=c$, $c_1=k\rho v/(2c)$ and the small-state counts entering first at
$c_k$.  The route is an exact first-failure renewal with two boundaries and
a finite coefficient algorithm; $c_1,c_2,c_3$ are tabulated.  Part~II adapts
the method, with a strict checkpoint, to strongly connected structures
(A027834/A027835, A006691/A006692, A304312/A304313), transfers the expansion
through the classical formal logarithm, confirms the identification that the
A304312/A304313 records leave conjectural, and gives exact cyclic
automorphism sectors with a uniform divisor-tail bound and a self-contained
proof of Liskovets's existing unrooted formula.  The leading equivalents
(Korshunov, Bassino--Nicaud, Lebensztayn) are classical, and priority of the
refinement is left unresolved: Korshunov's 1978 survey was not obtained.
The Lambert-$W$ inverses and threshold brackets are instances of the
transseries volume.  \emph{Merged from two manuscripts; the passages Part~II
repeats are printed once, in Part~I.}  Minimal DFAs of finite languages, a
different object, are counted in the report \dir{a082161-airy-amplitudes}.}

\section{Hankel determinants and continued fractions}

\subsection{Somos sequences and elliptic curves}

\entry{A proof of Barry's Hankel--Somos-6 conjecture}
{hankel-determinants/somos-and-elliptic/barry-somos-6-hankel-recurrence}
{article.pdf}{barry\_somos6\_research.zip}
{Conjecture~7 of Paul Barry, arXiv:2211.12637: for the Hankel determinants
$H_N$ of a four-parameter Jacobi-type generating function,
$H_NH_{N-6}=\alpha H_{N-1}H_{N-5}+\gamma H_{N-3}^2$ with
$\alpha=t^2(r+2)^2$ and
$\gamma=t^3((s+t)(r+s+t+2)^2+t(r+2)^3)$.  Also proves a homogeneous
four-parameter form and a seven-parameter polynomial Hankel theorem making
exceptional specialisations rigorous; the verifier never divides by a
determinant.}

\entry{Finite Hankel Certificates for Three Somos-8 Conjectures}
{hankel-determinants/somos-and-elliptic/somos-8-hankel-certificates}
{article.pdf}{somos\_hankel\_proofs.zip}
{Conjectures~11, 12 and~13 of the same Barry paper, by importing Hone's
genus-two Hankel theorem (arXiv:1907.05204v3, Thm.~5.4) and supplying a
binomial translation, a polynomial-minor specialisation valid on the degenerate
locus, and a rank-bound lemma that turns four symbolic rows into an all-index
identity.  Identifies a sign error in the source's Catalan expression for
Conjecture~13.}

\entry{Elliptic addition and Jacobi continued fractions}
{hankel-determinants/somos-and-elliptic/elliptic-addition-and-jacobi-fractions}
{article.pdf}{elliptic\_continued\_fraction\_research.zip}
{A consistently indexed, non-torsion version of Barry's 2023 Conjecture~4, with
the coordinate formulas of Conjecture~3: every continued-fraction tail is given
explicitly and removing one layer is shown to be elliptic translation.
Hypotheses: characteristic zero, $Y^2+aXY+bY=X^3+cX^2+dX$ nonsingular,
$b\ne 0$, and $P=(0,0)$ of infinite order; order-three and order-four examples
show the regular fraction genuinely needs the last one.}

\subsection{Catalan and ballot moments}

\entry{Shifted Catalan Hankel Polynomials}
{hankel-determinants/catalan-and-ballot/shifted-catalan-hankel-polynomials}
{article.pdf}{catalan\_hankel\_conjecture\_solution.zip; batch~39: ProveIt\_Catalan\_Root\_Collisions.zip; batch~42: ProveIt\_Cyclotomic\_Catalan\_Recurrences.zip; batch~53: Two\_Endpoint\_Catalan\_Confluence.zip; batch~55: ProveIt\_Interior\_Collision\_Research.zip; batch~57: ProveIt\_Catalan\_Growing\_Shifts.zip}
{For $D_N^{(m)}(a,b)=\det(aC_{i+j+m}+bC_{i+j+m+1})$, proves the
denominator-exponent assertion on page~4 of Barry, arXiv:2011.10827v1, and
shows the separately printed Conjecture~2 needs an explicit one-unit shift
correction.  The minimal characteristic polynomial is
$(X^2-(a+2b)X+b^2)^{m(m-1)/2+1}$, so the minimal order is $m(m-1)+2$; all
exceptional reduced denominators, the exact coefficient formula, numerator
degree and reciprocal symmetry are supplied.  Part~II, added in batch~39,
replaces $aC+bC'$ by an arbitrary polynomial multiplier and computes the root
collisions: exact sector degrees with nonzero leading constants, the minimal
recurrence order off resonance, a secondary-Hankel factorization and a
certified root-free algorithm for the recurrence order.  Part~III, added in
batch~42, gives every characteristic multiplicity for one repeated root at a
cyclotomic resonance, and shows that the question it answers needed one more
parameter: the sign of $z^L$, not only $t$ and $\operatorname{ord}(z^2)$.
Part~IV, added in batch~53, answers the endpoint half of Part~II's question on
uniform limits: for fixed-size clusters of roots approaching the endpoints $0$
and $4$ ($d$ roots in all) the normalized determinant is a convergent series
in even powers of $1/(N+d/2)$, locally uniform through every collision inside
a cluster, with a factorized leading profile, an explicit first interaction
term, and ratio to the fixed-endpoint determinant tending to $1$ exactly when
the outward displacements are $o(N^{-2})$.  Part~V, added in batch~55,
answers the other half: for fixed-size clusters of roots colliding on the
scale $1/N$ at fixed bases other than $0$ and $4$, the determinant is an exact
finite sum of exponential sectors whose amplitudes are convergent series in
$1/(N+d/2)$, uniform through every collision, with an explicit first
correction (this expansion is not even), an explicit remainder bound, and
factorized amplitudes for several separated clusters.  For two
equal-multiplicity roots inside the spectrum the limit is an oscillatory
Jacobi Hankel determinant, the first correction oscillates as
$\sin(2(N+t)\theta)$, and simple zeros move by a convergent phase-dependent
expansion.  Limits uniform up to the endpoints remain open.  Part~VI, added
in batch~57, lets the shift grow with the determinant size: for real $a\ge0$
the ratio $D_N^{(m)}(a,1)/D_N^{(m)}(0,1)$ tends to $1$ exactly when
$aN(N+m+1)/(4m+2)\to0$, at any relative growth of $N$ and $m$; the
size-biased inverse Jacobi zeros follow a shifted Marchenko--Pastur
(Catalan-moment) law, and three critical scales, an algebraic free energy
and a uniform saddle prefactor describe the crossover.}

\entry{Product formulas for ballot-polynomial Hankel determinants}
{hankel-determinants/catalan-and-ballot/ballot-polynomial-hankel-determinants}
{article.pdf}{Cigler\_Hankel\_Conjectures\_13\_15.zip}
{Conjectures 13, 14 and 15 in Section~5 of Johann Cigler, arXiv:2111.14492v3.
An explicit coefficient product gives the sign and exact support of the
normalised shift-$k$ Hankel determinant of the ballot moments and positivity of
every residual coefficient for $n\ge1$; the same products give the conjectured
generating-function denominators, exact numerator degrees and reciprocal
numerator identities.  Adds strict log-concavity of the residual coefficients
and their limiting binomial profile.}

\entry{Rectangular Schur polynomials and Cigler's Conjecture 8}
{hankel-determinants/catalan-and-ballot/cigler-conjecture-8-schur}
{article.pdf}{cigler\_conjecture8\_research.zip}
{A complete proof of Conjecture~8 in Cigler, arXiv:2111.14492v3, Section~4
(printed pp.~16--17), for the Hankel determinants of
$b_m(t)=\sum_r\binom{\lfloor m/2\rfloor}{r}\binom{\lceil m/2\rceil}{r}t^r$,
including the numerator-polynomial degree and reciprocity assertions.  A
different conjecture of that paper from the ballot-polynomial entry
(Conjectures 13--15) and from the parity entry (Conjecture~16).}

\entry{A parity factorization for Cigler's Conjecture 16}
{hankel-determinants/catalan-and-ballot/cigler-conjecture-16-parity}
{article.pdf}{cigler\_conjecture16\_research.zip; batch~56: ProveIt\_Cigler\_Schur\_Recurrences.zip; batch~66: ProveIt\_Cyclotomic\_Pole\_Collapse.zip; batch~70: ProveIt\_Cigler\_Conjecture17\_Resolution.zip}
{A complete proof of Conjecture~16 in Cigler, arXiv:2111.14492v3, Section~6
(pp.~21--22), on the Hankel polynomials, together with a sharp stabilization
statement: the report also determines the first coefficient at which the
conjectured stabilization fails.  Part~II, added in batch~56, proves the
report's experimental rectangular-Schur formula: with $a=\lfloor k/2\rfloor$
and $b=\lfloor(k-1)/2\rfloor$ the normalized determinant is
$s_{(n^a)}(1^a,t,(t^2)^b)$ for all $k\ge1$ and $n\ge0$.  It determines the
exact minimal recurrence at each shift, with reduced denominator
$\prod_{q=0}^{k-1}(1-t^qz)^{1+\lfloor q(k-1-q)/2\rfloor}$ over $\Q(t)$ and at
every real $t\ne0,\pm1$, proves Cigler's denominators (84) and (86) minimal,
and shows that the positive reciprocity sign printed in his (85), part of
Conjecture~18, is false when $k=2a$ with $a$ even.
Part~III, added in batch~66, determines the reduced denominator and the
minimal recurrence at every complex parameter: at a primitive root of unity
of order $\ell\ge3$ at most one residue class loses pole order, decided by an
explicit parity test, and it loses one order except for a loss of three at
primitive fourth roots when $k\equiv1\pmod4$.  Part~IV, added in batch~70,
proves Cigler's Conjecture~17 in sign-corrected form: once his denominator
$(1-t)^a(1-t^2)^{ab}$ is cleared, the determinant splits into $k$ blocks,
polynomials in $t$ whose coefficients are integer-valued polynomials in $n$,
of exact
degrees $q(k-1-q)$ in $t$ and $\lfloor q(k-1-q)/2\rfloor$ in $n$, with
explicit leading terms in $n$; their reciprocity sign is $-1$ exactly when
$4\mid k$.  The sign printed in Cigler's (83) fails for every
$k\equiv1\pmod4$ from $5$ on (his displayed block for $k=5$ is
palindromic), so the conjecture as printed is false.
Its own README explains why this is a different problem from the other
Cigler entries in this collection.}

\subsection{Arithmetic Hankel determinants}

The numerator formula recorded as a conjecture in OEIS A122251, with A122249 as
its binary special case.  Two reports attacked it and proved it the same way,
sharing even their worked counterexamples; they are merged here into one.  The
merged report sits at the category's top level.

\entry{A Proof of an OEIS Hankel-Numerator Conjecture}
{hankel-determinants/a122251-numerators-and-denominators}
{report.pdf}{hankel\_numerator\_research.zip + hankel\_oeis\_conjecture\_proof.zip}
{For the Hankel determinant
\[H_n(m,a)=\det(1/(a+m(i+j)))_{0\le i,j\le n},\]
the reduced numerator is
$\prod_{p\mid m}p^{\,n(n+1)v_p(m)+2\sum_{k\le n}v_p(k!)}$: independent of the
coprime offset $a$, a perfect square, and supported on the primes dividing $m$.
Taking $m=p$ and $a=1$ settles the OEIS A122251 conjecture, with A122249 as the
binary case.  Also gives a closed form for the reduced denominator and all its
local valuations, a sharp gcd theorem over the allowed offsets realised at just
two explicitly constructed shifts, the extension to unequal row and column steps
$\det(1/(a+m_1i+m_2j))$, recurrences, digit-sum asymptotics, and a Lambert-series
generating function with a natural boundary.  \emph{Merged; the determinant is
evaluated three independent ways, and a second proof of the numerator formula
runs through an explicit inverse matrix and MacMahon's boxed plane partitions.}}

\subsection{Growth laws and run statistics}

\entry{A proof of the Ap\'ery Hankel growth conjecture}
