answer, independently, the universal-rationality part of Richard Stanley's
MathOverflow question 431075 (``A conjectured rational generating function'',
2022), which had no posted answer when checked on 19 September 2026.  A third
answer used the same mixed-base construction and the same enumerative argument
as the first, and has been merged into it.

\entry{A Mixed-Base Counterexample to Stanley's Rationality Question}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/binary-product-coefficient-counts/mixed-base-counterexample}
{article.pdf}{stanley\_mixed\_base\_counterexample.zip + Stanley\_Rationality\_Counterexample.zip}
{With $G_{2j+1}=2^j$ and $G_{2j+2}=3^j$ the exponent generating function is
rational, while $\nu(2n)=2^{\,n-\lfloor(n+1)\alpha\rfloor}$ for
$\alpha=\log_3 2$ counts the coefficients equal to one in
$\prod_{i\le N}(1+x^{G_i})$ and its generating function is not rational, not
D-finite, not P-recursive, and has a natural boundary.  Classifies rationality
in the family interleaving $a^j$ and $q^j$ with digit polynomial
$1+y+\cdots+y^{a-1}$ for all $2\le a<q$ --- Stanley's general $r=a-1$ --- as
holding exactly when $q^u=a^v$.  Adds a finite-quotient rigidity theorem, an
abstract natural-boundary theorem for $\sum q^{\lfloor n\theta\rfloor}t^n$ at
every irrational $\theta$, the exact limit set of $a_n/\lambda^n$, and a
streaming recurrence making the point that non-P-recursive does not mean hard to
compute.  \emph{Merged; the enumerative half was the same argument in both
originals, but the two routes to non-rationality differ and both are kept ---
an elementary finite-state reduction mod $p$, and the rigidity route that
reaches the stronger transcendence conclusions.}}

\entry{Rational exponents, nonrational coefficient counts}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/binary-product-coefficient-counts/recursive-exponent-construction}
{research.pdf}{stanley\_counterexample.zip}
{A different negative answer, from the recursion $a_0=2$, $a_1=1$,
$a_n=a_{n-1}-2a_{n-2}$, with $b_n=T_{n-1}+2^n-1+a_n$, $T_n=T_{n-1}+b_n$,
$G_{2n-1}=2^{n-1}$ and $G_{2n}=b_n$: the counting series has a natural boundary
on the unit circle, hence is neither rational, algebraic nor D-finite.}

\entry{Coefficient residues in generalized Fibonacci products}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/binary-product-coefficient-counts/kbonacci-coefficient-residues}
{article.pdf}{stanley\_kbonacci\_research.zip + quasifibonacci\_parity\_research.zip}
{Settles Conjectures 6.2 and 6.3 of Stanley, \emph{Theorems and Conjectures on
Some Rational Generating Functions} (arXiv:2101.02131v3, pp.~23--24).  For
$k$-bonacci seeds each exceeding the sum of their predecessors, the number of
odd coefficients of $\prod_{i\le n}(1+x^{w_i})$ has generating function
$(1+2z^k)/(1-2z+2z^k-2z^{k+1})$, independently of the seeds; the proof builds
periodic signs of block length $k+1$ and block product $-1$ and shows every
signed finite product is flat.  \emph{Merged; the Conjecture 6.3 halves proved
the same statement on the same domain, and the surviving report adds
Conjecture~6.2 and the asymptotic analysis.}}

\subsection{Single-sequence OEIS studies}

One hundred and fifteen reports on the growth or the closed form of a single OEIS sequence.  One
article counts the terms in the derivatives of three power towers --- $x^{x^x}$,
$x^{x^2}$ and $x^x$ --- whose three parts reach three different answers by three
different recurrences, and two reports added in batch~72 sit beside it: one
proves the leading constant $\tfrac12$ for $x^{x^a}$ at every integer $a$, hence
for $x^x$ and $x^{x^2}$, and the other studies the $x^{x^2}$ coefficients at
unbounded logarithmic deficit.  Two more reports share a Bell-scale
saddle-point method on unrelated sequences.  Shared technique is not shared
theorem, and none of these duplicates another.  Thirteen reports came in
batch~73, one in batch~74, one in batch~75, thirty-three in batch~77,
five in batch~85, one in batch~91 and fifty in batches~100--109; they
are the last one hundred and four entries below, in order of placement, the batch-77 ones grouped by
the cluster that placed them (ascent sequences; iterated Bell numbers,
cone walks and divisor weights; trees and networks; words, permutations,
partitions and placements; moments, renewals and moving parameters).  Four
batch-73 reports merge two manuscripts that proved the same theorems
independently on the same day (A139217, A022629, A215561, A279619): the
shared theorems are printed once and the other proofs kept as marked second
routes.  Batch~75 brought a third and a fourth independent proof of the
A022629 theorems, and a manuscript about two thirds of which re-proves
results of the A215561 report; those duplicates are tabulated in the
reports, not reprinted, and only their new results were added.  Batch~77
did the same with a fifth independent proof of the A022629 theorems, a
fourth of the A215561 ones, a third of the A279619 constant and a second
of the A126764 corrections.  Several of its new reports join a manuscript
with addenda that nest it byte for byte; each paper is printed once.  The four
partition reports of batches~73--74 (A022629, A033552, A097356, A238016)
study different products in different saddle regimes and share no
proposition, nor do the batch-77 partition reports (A174065, A291698,
A292507, A301746, A301981), which share at most a method with them; the
A189281 and A330266 reports share a method but no theorem.  The two
batch-85 manuscripts on matrix compositions proved the same core theorems
independently and are one report, printed in one notation with a
dictionary; batches~85 and~86 also added Parts to the A189281, A000571,
A088714 and A301981 reports.  Batches~100--109 are clusters of one session
bundle of numbered Research Reports (arrival \texttt{60f54ea06}): four new
reports in batch~100, one in 101, three in 102, four each in 103, 104 and
105, seven in 106, five in 107, ten in 108 and eight in 109;
many bundle Reports instead became Parts of reports above (batches~100--103
extended fifteen of them and batch~108 the A261781 report, after
batches~97 and~98 had extended ten), and their re-proofs are printed as
marked second routes.  Where
several bundle Reports on one sequence were merged, the entry names the
base and every archive.
Many of these manuscripts invert their asymptotics by Lambert~$W$ and
rounding; those steps are instances of the inversion theorems of the
transseries volume in \dir{Analysis/Transseries}, which the reports cite
rather than claim.

\entry{Bell-scale growth of OEIS A088714}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a088714-bell-scale-growth}
{article.pdf}{A088714\_asymptotic\_growth.zip; batch~73: golden\_moment\_laws.zip; batch~76: A088714\_Bell\_Local\_Estimates.zip; batch~86: oeis\_advances.zip (its Sections 3--6), oeis\_research\_bundle.zip (its Part~II)}
{For the unique formal series with $A(x)=1+xA(x)^2A(xA(x))$, proves
$\log a_n=n\log n-n\log\log n-n+O(n\log\log n/\log n)$, so
$(e\log n/n)\,a_n^{1/n}\to1$ and the ordinary generating function has radius of
convergence zero while the exponential one is entire.  The lower bound comes
from a single explicit path through an exact triangular recurrence, the upper
from a Stirling-positivity comparison with derivatives of Touchard
polynomials.  The multiplicative equivalent is \emph{not} claimed, and the
finer Bell-number form is labelled a conjecture with a sufficient ratio
condition isolated.  Part~II, added in batch~73, treats A088714 and its
companion A088713 as moment sequences: determinate moment measures, strict
Hankel positivity, golden-ratio endpoint laws with exponents
$(3-\sqrt5)/2$ and $(\sqrt5-1)/2$, the ratio law
$a_{n+1}/a_n\sim n/\log n$ and the far tail.  Its re-proof of Part~I's
theorem is replaced by a pointer; the finer Bell conjecture stayed open there.
Part~III, added in batch~76 from a continuation manuscript: Part~I's formal
endpoint coefficient expansion, proved uniformly for $m\le C\log n$; the
one-sided correction $\liminf(r_n-n/W(n))\ge3/2$ for $r_n=a_n/a_{n-1}$
(the conjecture needs $2$); and a reduction of the finer Bell conjecture to
one scalar defect estimate $E_n=O(W(n)^q/n^2)$, sufficient and in some form
necessary, with a tilted-variance criterion, the complementary-endpoint
bound $5/(r_nr_{n-1})$ and exact numerics to $n=1600$.  Its re-derivation
of Part~I's summability step is a pointer; $W(n)$ enters only as the Bell
saddle of the transseries volume, which it cites.  Parts~IV and~V, added in
batch~86 from two manuscripts whose other subjects are the new report
\dir{a343093-bridgeless-toroidal-maps} and Parts~II of the A301981 and
A321941 reports.  Part~IV: both moment
measures have strictly positive real-analytic densities on $(0,\infty)$
with full support and exact density and derivative equivalents at zero;
the correction to $F(s)\sim s^{-\alpha}$ is a nonzero real-analytic
antiperiodic profile in $\log_\varphi\log s$, certified nonzero by an
interval computation, with a convergent expansion in $1/\log s$; and every
fixed-shift expansion $c_n=\sum_{j\le K}\gamma_ja_{n-1-j}+O(a_{n-K-2})$ of
the companion A088713.  Part~V: the complementary endpoint to every fixed
order, which proves Part~III's missing estimate; bounded factors and
$r_n=n/W(n)+O(1)$; and the finer Bell conjecture (Conjecture~8.1),
$a_n=C_*\mathcal N_n(1+O(W(n)^5/n))$ with $0<C_*<\infty$ unidentified, so
$a_n\sim C_*B_ne^{W(n)^2+3W(n)}$ --- proved there, not yet independently
reviewed (the other batch-86 manuscript still calls it open).  Its proof
of the companion expansion is a second route to Part~IV's, and its
normalization of $c_n$ by the same $C_*$ is new.  Part~I's pointwise ratio
expansion (8.7) stays open.}

\entry{The exponential growth constant of OEIS A158415}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a158415-growth-constant}
{article.pdf}{A158415\_asymptotic\_growth.zip}
{For the number of distinct values expressible with $n$ symbols from
$E::=1\mid\sqrt E\mid(E+E)$, proves a computable $\gamma>1$ exists with
$a(n)^{1/n}\to\gamma$ and $1.86204<\gamma<1.9208$, gives an effective
characterisation as a supremum of algebraic finite-data bounds, and proves the
boundary identity $\sum a(n)\gamma^{-n}=\gamma^2$, which rules out a pure
$C\gamma^n$ prefactor.  The rigorous lower bounds rest on a uniform injectivity
theorem: any finite alphabet of representable values can serve as nested-radical
digits, via an odd valuation at a prime above~2.  No closed form for $\gamma$ is
claimed, and terms 22--28 get certified intervals rather than exact values.}

\entry{Dyadic scaling rigidity of 3AP-free permutations}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a003407-dyadic-scaling-rigidity}
{article.pdf}{ProveIt\_Dyadic\_Scaling\_Rigidity.zip (batch~40); batch~43: ProveIt\_3AP\_Nonholonomicity.zip}
{For $\theta(n)$, the number of permutations of $[n]$ with no three-term
arithmetic progression (OEIS A003407), studies the dyadic scaling of
$\theta(n)$: a continuous log-periodic growth profile with its exact
fundamental period, rigidity of the profile under real rescaling, explicit
losses and gains of the 2:1 gluing, and the empirical and sampling laws; the
adjacent ratios $\theta(n+1)/\theta(n)$ have no limit.  The parity recurrences and Sharma's
Theorem~2.8, which the manuscript imports, are already proved in Lean in
\dir{Combinatorics/Ramsey} (the table $\theta(1..20)$ there rests on a
\texttt{native\_decide} certificate); the report's own theorems are not
formalized.  Part~II, added in batch~43, proves $\theta$ is not P-recursive and
its generating functions are not D-finite (the ordinary one is transcendental),
using the published counts $\theta(64)$, $\theta(75)$, which nothing in ProveIt
certifies; whether the generating function has a natural boundary stays open.}

\entry{An exact coefficient formula for Ramanujan's theta function}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a260306-formula-proof}
{a260306\_proof.pdf}{A260306\_formula\_proof.zip}
{Proves both finite formulas recorded in OEIS A260306, for every coefficient
index, for the expansion $\theta(N)\sim\sum c_nN^{-n}$ defined by
$\sum_{k<N}N^k/k!+\theta(N)N^N/N!=e^N/2$.  The route is an exact Laplace-integral
difference, an analytic change of variable with $F(\psi(w))=w^2/2$, and Lagrange
inversion reducing each $c_n$ to one coefficient extraction, then a finite
change of basis into the entry's double and triple sums.  Includes the analytic
error estimate the identification needs, rather than only checking the two sums
agree, and identifies A065973 as the reduced denominators.}

\entry{The maximal positive coefficient of a Hermite polynomial}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a277280-hermite-maximum}
{article.pdf}{A277280\_asymptotics.zip}
{An exact closed form for OEIS A277280, the largest coefficient of the
physicists' Hermite polynomial $H_n$ with signs retained: with
$k_n=\lfloor(2n+6-\lfloor\sqrt{8n+21}\rfloor)/8\rfloor$ and $d_n=n-4k_n$, the
exponent $d_n$ is the unique maximizer and $a(n)=n!\,2^{d_n}/((2k_n)!\,d_n!)$.
The distinction from the largest coefficient in absolute value is kept explicit
throughout.}

\entry{Asymptotics of OEIS A277364}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a277364-bell-asymptotics}
{A277364\_asymptotics.pdf}{A277364\_asymptotics.zip}
{For $a(n)=\sum_{k\le n/2}S(n,k)$, partitions of an $n$-set into at most
$\lfloor n/2\rfloor$ blocks, proves $a(n)\sim B_n$ with an explicit first
correction: writing $r=W_0(n)$,
$a(n)=\exp\{n(r-1+1/r)-1\}(1+r)^{-1/2}\,(1-P(r)/n+O(r^2/n^2))$ for an explicit
rational $P$.  Also gives a parity-split equivalent for the omitted tail
$B_n-a(n)$.}

\entry{Term counts in the derivatives of power towers}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/power-tower-derivative-term-counts}
{article.pdf}{A293239\_research\_report.zip, A290268\_partial\_results.zip,
A281434\_exact\_algorithm\_and\_cubic\_growth.zip; batch~42: ProveIt\_A290268\_Finite\_Certificates.zip;
batch~70: ProveIt\_A290268\_Depth3\_Article.zip; batch~72: ProveIt\_Lehmer\_Comtet\_Prime\_Multiple\_Nonvanishing.zip,
ProveIt\_Lehmer\_Comtet\_Stirling\_Residues\_and\_Candidate\_Lists.zip, ProveIt\_Lehmer\_Comtet\_Uniform\_Padic\_Nonvanishing.zip,
ProveIt\_A290268\_Depths\_Five\_Six.zip, ProveIt\_A290268\_Count\_Bounds.zip; batch~73:
ProveIt\_A290268\_Eight\_Depths.zip, ProveIt\_A290268\_Depth7.zip}
{One article on three OEIS sequences that ask the same question of three
functions: how many distinct monomials are in the fully collected $n$th
derivative of $x^x$ (A293239), $x^{x^2}$ (A290268) and $x^{x^x}$ (A281434)?
Part~I proves once the apparatus all three had established separately --- the
canonical differential-polynomial form $f^{(n)}=fP_n$ or $fx^{-n}P_n$ with its
first-order integer recurrence, the independence of logarithmic monomials that
makes the count an invariant of $f$ rather than of a differentiation algorithm,
the identity $a(n)=|\operatorname{supp}P_n|$, and the envelope bound.  Two
normalizations are needed, because neither clause covers all three in the frames
the parts use.  \textbf{None of the three headline questions is fully settled,
and the article says so in its abstract}: for $x^x$ the count
reduces exactly to zeros of the first-kind Lehmer--Comtet triangle A008296 and
$a(n)=\Theta(n^2)$, but the closed formula needs an isolated nonvanishing step
--- and separately the recurrence printed in the entry for $n>6$ is
\emph{disproved} at $n=8$; for $x^{x^2}$ the conjectured expression is proved to
be an upper bound and $a(n)=\Theta(n^2)$, but the article does not prove the
constant $\tfrac12$ (since 1~October 2026 the sibling report
\emph{power-tower-exponent-supports} does, for $x^x$ as well); for
$x^{x^x}$ explicit bounds give $a(n)=\Theta(n^3)$ but not $a(n)\sim\tfrac23n^3$,
and there the obstruction is not a nonvanishing statement but a density one,
since further zeros provably exist.  A closing part sets the three side by side
and records what they share beyond the framework: every cancellation any of them
can prove is a parity zero of the same falling factorial.  This is a thematic consolidation,
not a deduplication: the three were checked and are not duplicates, and all
fifty numbered results of the three originals are preserved unchanged.
Batch~42 added to the $x^{(x^2)}$ part depth certificates that close the
nonvanishing problem at logarithmic deficits $3$ and~$4$ for every $n$ and
improve its lower bound on the count; the open region was then deficit at least
$5$.  Where that manuscript re-derived results already in the report, it is
printed as a second route.  Batch~70 added a second proof of the
deficit-three case (no zeros beyond the forced holes), by a
harmonic sign law and positivity propagation in $k$ with its own finite
certificate; that sign law proves a sign change the batch-42 data had only
observed.  Its manuscript's claim that deficit three was still open is
corrected in the text.  Batch~72 added three manuscripts to the $x^x$ part:
Lehmer--Comtet nonvanishing on the prime-multiple offsets $\mu(p-1)$, $p>\mu$,
under explicit conditions (in full for $\mu=2,4$, and for $\mu=3,5$ at odd
$p$), Stirling residues that leave one candidate zero on $(p-1)^2$, and exact
$p$-adic valuations on two families; the closed formula stays open.  It added
two to the $x^{x^2}$ part: deficits five and six are classified by exact
moment seeds, so the open region was then deficit at least~$7$, every fixed
deficit reduces to a singly exponential rectangle, and
$a(n)\ge\frac38n^2+\frac n2\log n-\frac n2\log\log n-\frac54n$ for
$n\ge e^{10}$, still the best explicit bound.  Re-derivations of earlier
results are printed once and credited.  The two sibling reports of the same
batch, \emph{power-tower-exponent-supports} and
\emph{a290268-unbounded-deficits}, are not part of this article; dated notes
in it point to them.  Batch~73 added two independent manuscripts to the
$x^{x^2}$ part: deficit seven is classified twice, by three-anchor
certificates of $51{,}699{,}091$ and $48{,}113{,}025$ cells, and deficit
eight once ($552{,}465{,}284$ cells); at both only the reflection holes
vanish, so the open region is now deficit at least~$9$.  Their re-derivations
of the deficit-five and -six classification, the largest-root minorant and
weaker cutoffs and count bounds are printed as second routes or remarks.}

\entry{Derivative supports of $x^{x^a}$}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/power-tower-exponent-supports}
{article.pdf}{batch~72: ProveIt\_All\_Integer\_Exponent\_Derivative\_Supports.zip, ProveIt\_A293239\_Leading\_Asymptotic.zip, ProveIt\_A290268\_Leading\_Asymptotic.zip, ProveIt\_Half\_Exponent\_Derivative\_Supports.zip, ProveIt\_Algebraic\_Exponents\_and\_Cancellation\_Frequencies.zip (with a second manuscript nested inside it), ProveIt\_Irrational\_Exponents\_Effective\_Cancellation\_Bounds.zip; batch~100: ProveIt\_Half\_Exponent\_Hermite\_and\_Finiteness.zip (bundle Report~59)}
{Let $A_a(N)$ count the nonzero monomials $x^{a\ell-N}(\log x)^k$ in the $N$th
derivative of $x^{x^a}$ divided by $x^{x^a}$.  For every fixed positive
integer $a$, proves $A_a(N)=N^2/2+o(N^2)$, by saddle contours in the
coordinate $\tanh(t/2)$, a convex-envelope coverage argument and an
integer-triangle estimate; for $a=1$ this is $a(N)\sim N^2/2$ for OEIS
A293239 (density zero of the zeros of the Lehmer--Comtet triangle), and for
$a=2$ it is $\lim a(N)/N^2=\tfrac12$ for OEIS A290268, the constant the
power-tower term-count report leaves open.  The same leading asymptotic holds
for $a=1/2$, with two infinite families of cancellations off the reflection
line.  On a fixed factor defect $r$ it proves finiteness of the cancellations
off an explicit reflection line for rational $a\ne1$ ($r\ge4$) and for
algebraic $a$ of degree $D$ ($r\ge4D+2$), at most $(2\lfloor r/2\rfloor)^2-1$
cancellation cells, effectively, for irrational $a$ and none for
transcendental $a$, and exact square-root counting laws for rational
$1/2<a<1$ on defects two and three.  Every asymptotic statement is
ineffective.  Parts~I--III are merged from seven manuscripts: the integer
theorem was proved three times, and the separate proofs at $a=1$ and $a=2$,
more explicit at those exponents, are printed as second routes.
Part~IV (batch~100), an eighth manuscript, shows for $x^{\sqrt x}$ that on
each fixed defect $r$ the scaled residual tends to the Hermite polynomial
$\mathrm{He}_r$ with an explicit first correction of relative order
$N^{-1/2}$; for large $N$ it has $r$ simple real roots with explicit shifts,
so at most $\lfloor r/2\rfloor$ cells vanish per large order and
$O_r(\sqrt X)$ through order $X$.  Also a Weierstrass model
$V^2=U^3-122400U+9415000$ of the defect-four quartic and the count
$X+(1+1/\sqrt3)\sqrt X+O_R(1)$ of cancellations through order $X$ on the
defects $0\le r\le R$.  Its residual, odd factor, Laguerre edges, defect-two
and -three counts and both finiteness theorems re-derive Parts~I--III at
$a=1/2$ by the same route and are printed as second routes; its contribution
sentence, fair at its pin, is kept with a dated correction, and its README's
``integrality preserved'' for the elliptic model is false read literally.
No effective threshold or finite zero list on any defect $r\ge4$.  The
write's outlook that the leading Hermite term holds for every $0<a<1$ was
qualified by the independent check (\texttt{0c81c51e2}): its window
$k=(2-1/a)N+O(\sqrt N)$ meets the physical cells only for $1/2\le a<1$.
Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{OEIS A290268 at unbounded logarithmic deficit}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a290268-unbounded-deficits}
{article.pdf}{batch~72: ProveIt\_A290268\_Explicit\_Finite\_Ratio.zip, ProveIt\_A290268\_Airy\_Fold.zip, ProveIt\_A290268\_Arithmetic\_Phase.zip, ProveIt\_A290268\_Sparse\_Cancellations.zip, ProveIt\_A290268\_Pointwise\_Three\_Halves.zip, ProveIt\_A290268\_Strict\_Quadratic\_Gain.zip, ProveIt\_A290268\_Direct\_Contour.zip, ProveIt\_A290268\_Global\_049\_Bound.zip, ProveIt\_A290268\_Growing\_Logarithmic\_Degrees.zip (superseded, not printed)}
{Studies the integer coefficients $H_{d,k,q}$ behind the term count of the
$N$th derivative of $x^{x^2}$ when the logarithmic deficit $d$ grows with $N$.
A regular saddle branch ending at a fold $\kappa_c\approx0.0281461$ carries a
uniform phase law that proves eventual nonvanishing at every algebraic
limiting ratio $k/d\to\kappa<\kappa_c$, with possible zeros only near explicit
transcendental rays; an additive Airy law holds in the window
$|k-\kappa_cd|=O(d^{1/3})$.  Two-dimensional cosine laws with explicit phase
curvatures give density one with $O(N^{5/3})$ exceptions on tail patches and
positivity on the triangle $40k+420q\le91d$; block congruences modulo primes,
prime-unit families with exact valuations, all-depth central strips and an
example showing that root geometry alone cannot prove the support conjecture
complete it.  The manuscripts' headline lower bounds ($(3/8+c)N^2$,
$3N^2/8+CN^{3/2}$, $\liminf a(N)/N^2\ge28176/57247$) are kept in a table as
superseded by $a(N)=N^2/2+o(N^2)$ from the companion report on derivative
supports; the phase laws behind them are not implied by it.  Merged from eight
manuscripts with shared material printed once; a ninth is wholly implied by
the base and is described, not printed.  The support conjecture stays open.}

\entry{Iterated sums and products from one}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a352969-correctness-and-growth}
{article.pdf}{A352969\_correctness\_and\_growth.zip}
{For $S_0=\{1\}$, $S_{n+1}=(S_n+S_n)\cup(S_n\cdot S_n)$ and $a_n=|S_n|$ (OEIS
A352969), proves correctness of the listed terms and the doubly exponential law
$2^{2^{\,n-r_n-4}}\le a_n\le 2^{2^{\,n-1}}$, hence
$\log\log a_n=n+O(\sqrt{\log n})$.  The step is simultaneous, not repeated
closure --- a distinction the report is careful about.}

\entry{Distinct third derivatives of a power tower}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a199085-exponentiation-derivative-values}
{article.pdf}{A199085\_generating\_function.zip}
{For OEIS A199085, the number of distinct values taken by the third derivative
at $x=1$ over all fully parenthesized exponentiations of $n$ copies of $x$,
proves $a(1)=a(2)=1$ and $a(n)=\lfloor(n^2-2)/3\rfloor$ for $n\ge3$, with the
rational generating function $z/(1-z)+z^3/(1-z)^3-z^7/((1-z)^2(1-z^3))$.  The
proof is for all $n$, not an extrapolation: every attainable pair of second and
third derivatives is classified and a witness constructed for each admissible
pair.  Despite the shared vocabulary this is a different question from
\emph{power-tower-derivative-term-counts}, which fixes the function and varies
the derivative order; here the order is fixed at three and the parenthesization
varies.}

\entry{Finite-fringe rigidity in congruence-restricted greedy dissociated sequences}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a139217-greedy-dissociated-fringes}
{article.pdf}{batch~73: finite\_fringe\_oeis\_A139217\_A139218.zip + ProveIt\_OEIS\_A139217\_A139218.zip}
{OEIS A139217 and A139218 choose greedily the least positive integer
congruent to $1$, respectively $2$, modulo~3 that keeps the set dissociated
(all subset sums distinct).  After three terms the signed subset-sum
difference set is an interval with a fixed finite fringe of holes,
$\{3\}$ for A139217 and $\{1,3,6,11\}$ for A139218, and a general
propagation theorem keeps it so forever.  Hence exact closed forms, for
instance $a(n)=\tfrac34\,2^n+1$ for $n\equiv0,1$ and $\tfrac34\,2^n-2$ for
$n\equiv2\pmod3$ for A139217 ($n\ge3$),
rational generating functions and inverse expansions; both satisfy
$a(n)=a(n-1)+a(n-2)+2a(n-3)$, for A139217 only from $n=6$, so the entry's
range ``$n>4$'' fails at $n=5$.  \emph{Merged from two manuscripts that
proved the same theorems independently on the same day; each shared theorem
is printed once and credited to both, the second proof kept as a second
route, and the cross-identities, remainder bound and tables that only one
has are added.}}

\entry{Five OEIS asymptotic conjectures for distinct-partition norms}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a022629-distinct-partition-norms}
{article.pdf}{batch~73: OEIS\_Distinct\_Partition\_Norms.zip + A022629\_Research\_Package.zip; batch~75: A022629\_research\_package.zip, OEIS\_Norm\_Weighted\_Partitions.zip; batch~77: oeis-fixed-power-asymptotics-result.zip; batch~100: ProveIt\_Power\_Weighted\_Distinct\_Partitions\_Relative\_Expansions.zip (bundle Report~89, base of Section~21) + ProveIt\_OEIS\_A022629\_Asymptotics\_and\_Inversion.zip (bundle Report~82), ProveIt\_Critical\_Theta\_Power\_Weighted\_Distinct\_Partitions.zip (bundle Report~93)}
{For $a_\alpha(n)=\sum N(\lambda)^\alpha$ over partitions $\lambda$ of $n$
into distinct parts, $N(\lambda)$ the product of the parts, proves the logarithmic
growth formulas marked conjectural in A022629, A092484 and A265840--A265842,
and with $K=\sqrt{2n}$, $r=\log K$, $c=\pi^2/(6\alpha^2)$ the expansion
$\log a_\alpha(n)=\alpha K(r-1+c/r+c/r^2+\cdots)$ to every order; a separate
local-limit argument gives multiplicatively accurate saddle asymptotics with
all Edgeworth orders, since exponentiating a truncated logarithm does not.
Also a Lambert-$W$ inverse, a Gumbel law for the largest part, asymptotic to
$(1+1/\alpha)\sqrt{2n}$, and the modulus of the first Fourier resonances.
\emph{Merged from two independent manuscripts with identical coefficients
(one treats A022629, the other's $\alpha=1$): shared results printed once,
with the second proofs, an explicit differential-operator derivation and the
results only the A022629 manuscript has.}  Two batch-75 manuscripts, the
third and fourth independent proofs of these theorems with identical
coefficients, are recorded in one table rather than reprinted; their new
results form Sections~15--19: sharp signed cumulant scales, a saddle
remainder of every fixed order with first correction
$\sim-3/(8\alpha Kr)$, localization of the first index attaining a
large value to within $1+o(1)$ of an explicit inverse, eventual strict log-concavity and
fixed-degree Jensen hyperbolicity; and the convolution powers
$\prod(1+k^\alpha q^k)^\mu$ (A297321, A022630, A022631) with
$\mu$-independent coefficients, two further inverse coefficients and an
exact check of the eighth forward coefficient.
A batch-77 manuscript is a fifth independent proof of the core theorems
(all logarithmic orders for $\alpha=1$), again tabulated, not reprinted.
Its new results form Section~20: the slot family
$\prod(1+k^\alpha q^k)^{k^\beta}$, $\alpha>0$, $\beta\ge0$ an integer, with
two logarithmic orders, an exact-saddle expansion to every fixed order, a
threshold inverse and strict monotonicity, which for $\alpha=\beta=1$ proves
Kot\v{e}\v{s}ovec's conjectured leading term for OEIS A266891; and resummed
``thermal'' forms of the logarithm and the inverse, no sharper than the
two-term truncations.
Sections~21--22 (batch~100) come from three manuscripts of one author series.
Two of them, for every real $\alpha>0$ (the base) and for A022629, are the
sixth and seventh proofs of the core theorems, again tabulated; Section~21
adds the forward and inverse coefficients $P_9$, $P_{10}$, $\Delta_9$,
$\Delta_{10}$, the smooth extension to $t=0$ of the lattice-minus-integral
difference with coefficients $\xi_r(\alpha)$ holomorphic on $\Re\alpha>0$
(for instance $\xi_0(1)=\frac12\log2\pi-1$, $\xi_1(1)=7/12-\gamma$), an
all-orders relative expansion keeping the exact integral, and an exact-range
inverse that recovers the index by rounding at sequence values.  The third,
Section~22, lets $\alpha$ grow so that $\Lambda=\alpha(s-1)/M^{1/3}$ stays in
a compact subset of $(0,\infty)$ ($t$ the exact saddle, $M=e^s$ its
transition site; $\alpha\asymp(2n)^{1/6}/\log n$) and proves
$a_\alpha(n)=e^{F(t)+nt}(2\pi B)^{-1/2}\{\Theta(\mu_R,Q_\perp)+o(1)\}$ with
a theta multiplier, $\mu_R$ the mean number of parts at least two and
$Q_\perp\to\pi^2/(3\Lambda^3)$ its variance after regression on the size, so
that single-saddle dominance fails there; also a theta-adjusted inverse with
error $o(M^{2/3})$.  It answers in part the report's projects on multi-saddle
expansions and growing powers; it is the Fourier-dual form of the lattice-peak
transfer theorem of \dir{a357825-theta-ballot-power-sums}, not a corollary
(two of its hypotheses are unproved for this row), and it does not explain
the fixed-$\alpha$ finite-size errors, which an uncertified cell
decomposition places in the Fourier cells near $\pm2\pi/M$.  No effective
onset, convergence, optimal truncation or uniformity as $\Lambda\to0$ or
$\infty$; the two re-proofs' claims to settle or strengthen the posted
conjectures are read as further proofs.  The write completed two proofs; the independent check
(\texttt{91ef1ef6f}) found its five additions valid and corrected one order
claim: the theta shift of the threshold is of order at most $1/t$, and of
exact order $1/t$ only away from two phases per period.  Unrefereed; not
formalized.}

\entry{Three exceptions before every square}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a097356-sqrt-restricted-partitions}
{article.pdf}{batch~73: OEIS\_SquareRoot\_Partition\_Research.zip; batch~74: A097356\_Sharp\_Envelopes\_Transseries\_Inversion.zip}
{OEIS A097356 counts partitions of $N$ into parts at most
$\lfloor\sqrt N\rfloor$.  Proves that the posted lower constant
$0.0881548837\ldots$ is exactly the limit inferior, while the literal
eventual lower bound fails at precisely three indices in every square
block, the three integers before the next square.  The engine is a uniform
all-orders expansion of $p_m(\alpha m^2+\sigma m+\tau)$ with a finite
Gaussian coefficient operator; for A097356 it carries a phase factor
$\rho^{\{\sqrt N\}}$, giving the cluster set, limiting distribution,
summatory law and an inverse with $O(1)$ index error once a piecewise-linear
phase map is added to the Lambert-$W_{-1}$ core.  A206227 and A206240 are
covered too; the leading asymptotic is Szekeres's.  A second derivation
from batch~74, written independently with equal constants, is merged: its
proofs are second routes, and its own results (exact shell-polynomial
degrees, second-order envelope constants, the exact phase loss, a Fourier
identity and an $O(N^{-1/2})$ rate for the limiting law) form an added
section.}

\entry{Partitions into Catalan numbers}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a033552-catalan-partitions}
{article.pdf}{batch~73: Catalan\_Partitions\_Research.zip; batch~98: catalan\_partition\_laws.zip (base) + Catalan\_Partition\_Statistics\_Research.zip (Part~II)}
{For OEIS A033552, partitions whose parts are distinct Catalan numbers, a
complete expansion in inverse powers of a logarithmic size parameter with a
nonconstant periodic amplitude, an explicit first correction and an inverse
expansion for the threshold at which $p(n)$ reaches a given value, from a
contour-controlled saddle estimate, a Barnes $G$ product identity and a
Fourier series in $\Gamma(s)\zeta(1+s)$; also a phase-dependent limit law
for the largest part index.  The entry poses no asymptotic conjecture, and
the methods are classical.  Part~II (batch~98) merges two independent
manuscripts answering Part~I's questions 10.4 and 10.5, with the more
general one as base: the exact total-variation threshold for independence
of the upper multiplicities from geometric variables; a corrected edge law
$P(J_n-M\le h)=G_h(1+C_h/m)+O(m^{-2})$ for the largest index, proved in
both and printed once, with growing offsets, two-sided tails, all moments
and periodic first corrections; for the number of parts, $t_nN_n$ tends to
an explicit series $W$ of scaled exponential variables with no $1/m$ term
in the mean ($S_1=1+4\pi/(9\sqrt3)$); joint limits with the upper process;
the law of $W$, whose tail series has the unit circle as natural boundary,
with an inverse tail; a Brownian-bridge mass profile and a lacunary
universality class.  The write proved one manuscript's unproved covariance
candidate, $m\,\mathrm{Cov}(t_nN_n,J_n)\to-S_1\Xi(\theta)$; the
independent check found it valid and sketched a sharper remainder.  No
claim of either manuscript was found wrong; the conditioning framework is
classical (Arratia--Tavar\'e, Fristedt), and no exponentially small
sectors or interval certificates are claimed.  Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{Central Poupard numbers}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a125054-central-poupard-numbers}
{article.pdf}{batch~73: Central\_Poupard\_OEIS.zip}
{For $A_n(c)=\sum_k\binom nk c^{n-k}T_k$ with $T_k$ the tangent numbers,
$A_n(1)$ is OEIS A125054.  Proves the continued fraction recorded there as
a conjecture in December 2025 and $A_n(1)\equiv3\pmod9$ for $n\ge1$, by
formal continued fractions.  Analytically: an exact convergent
decomposition into odd-pole exponential sectors, a uniform all-order
endpoint--saddle expansion for $c=\tau n^2$ with a first-order transition at
$\tau_*\approx0.2624665$ and a displaced finite-size window, mixture and
Gaussian laws, located polynomial zeros, the same transition for moments of
any density with tail $Cy^\beta e^{-\alpha y}$, and a Lambert-$W$ inverse.}

\entry{Theta laws for high-power ballot sums}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a357825-theta-ballot-power-sums}
{article.pdf}{batch~73: OEIS\_Theta\_Ballot\_Sums.zip; batch~98: ProveIt\_OEIS\_Lattice\_Peak\_Transfer\_2026-10-05.zip (Part~II)}
{For $a_n=\sum_hB(n,h)^n$, with $B(n,h)$ the ballot paths of length $n$
ending at height $h$ (OEIS A357825), the normalized ratio whose
non-convergence the entry records is a Jacobi theta function at a drifting
parity-sensitive phase times $e^{-5/6}$.  Proves a uniform all-orders
expansion in theta derivatives, the exact lower and upper limiting
constants, the cluster interval and phase law, ratio and Tur\'an
asymptotics and eventual strict log-convexity; the multiset counterpart
differs by an explicit exponential sector of relative order $n^32^{-n}$, and
for $\sum_hB(n,h)^k$ transitions occur at $k\asymp n$ and $k\asymp n^{3/2}$.
Bala's supercongruence stays open.  Part~II (batch~98, a late arrival)
answers the report's question Q6 in a sufficient-hypothesis form: a general
lattice-peak transfer theorem gives, under a local quadratic law, a
Gaussian majorant and interior completeness,
$S_n(r_n)/\Omega_n^{r_n}=\vartheta(q_n,\delta_n)+o(1)$ for power sums of a
positive array on a moving lattice, with cluster sets, every fixed order, a
multidimensional version and a first-order inverse.  The write weakened the
majorant to an exponential one that log-concavity supplies, completed two
proofs, and proved that the ballot rows and the Mahonian rows of
\dir{a380274-mahonian-growing-powers} are instances, the latter by
reorganizing that report's proof.  The manuscript's identity for
$\sum u^3e^{-qu^2/2}$ drops a term and is corrected on record, and its
claim that Part~I's all-orders coefficients are ``precisely'' its own holds
at first order only; the independent check fixed two wordings.  The
hypotheses are sufficient, not necessary, and nothing arithmetic is
claimed.  Unrefereed; not formalized; Part~II does not say whether an AI
assistant was used.}

\entry{Fixed-composition excursions}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a215561-fixed-composition-excursions}
{article.pdf}{batch~73: oeis\_balanced\_excursions.zip + late\_growing\_excursions.zip; batch~75: OEIS\_Fixed\_Content\_Ballot\_Words.zip; batch~77: oeis-ballot-asymptotics-20261001.zip; batch~101: A215561\_Fixed\_Alphabet\_Asymptotics.zip (bundle Report~90, base) + A215570\_Balanced\_Ballot\_Asymptotics.zip (bundle Report~86) (Part~VI)}
{For words with $n$ copies of each of $r$ letters whose every prefix sum is
at most its average (OEIS A215561), proves the fixed-row conjecture
$A_r(n)\sim\kappa_r(rn)!/(rn\,(n!)^r)$ with $\kappa_r$ a positive algebraic
number given by a finite kernel-root product, a complete expansion in
inverse powers of $n$, D-finiteness of every row with a transcendental
ordinary generating function for $r\ge3$ (the specific A215570 recurrence
is proved in Part~IV, batch~75), a contact law and a Lambert-$W$ inversion.  Part~II holds
the second manuscript's own results: the leading term for every finite
labelled step set, a second all-orders route, second-order terms for
$r=2,3$, a giant primitive component, eventual log-convexity and a rounding
bound.  \emph{Merged from two manuscripts that proved the main theorems
independently, with constants agreeing to the last digit; shared results
printed once and credited to both, the different routes kept as second
proofs.}  Part~IV, added in batch~75 from a third manuscript, proves
Kauers--Koutschan's Conjecture~15, the order-three recurrence for A215570,
by a positive Legendre-coefficient formula and a rational telescoping
certificate printed in full, and computes the coefficients
$\alpha_{5,2},\ldots,\alpha_{5,8}$ of the five-letter row exactly.  About two
thirds of that manuscript re-proves results of Parts~I--II, its row theorem
only with relative error $O(n^{-1/5})$; those results are listed in one
comparison table, not reprinted.
Part~V, added in batch~77 from a fourth manuscript written without knowledge
of this report, re-proves the main theorems with the same constants, sextic,
Hessian and $\alpha_{5,1}$; these are tabulated, not reprinted.
It adds the first correction
$\alpha_{4,1}=-\frac{37}{200}\varrho^3+\frac{11}{40}\varrho^2+\frac{13}{40}\varrho-\frac{259}{400}\approx-0.5097846849$
of the four-letter row A215562, with $\varrho=\varphi-\sqrt\varphi$ and
$\varphi$ the golden ratio, the Hessian $H_4$, an operator-norm second
route to the global-arc bound and a nested-logarithm inverse for every row;
$\alpha_{4,2}$, $\alpha_{4,3}$ and the rows $r=6,7,8$ stay open.
Part~VI, added in batch~101 from two bundle reports also written without
knowledge of this report, proves Theorems~3.1--3.3 a fifth and a sixth time
(every fixed alphabet with the two extreme steps unmarked; five letters
with the zero steps deleted); these are tabulated as second routes.  New in
them: $\alpha_{5,2}=\frac{36}{25}-\frac{63\sqrt5}{125}$ computed without the
recurrence by the Gaussian-moment rule on the zero-free walk, agreeing with
Part~IV; the leading constant of the zero-free count $A^\circ_5(n)$; $A_5(n+1)\ge35A_5(n)$,
which the write extends to $A_r(m+n)\ge A_r(m)A_r(n)$, so every row with
$r\ge3$ increases strictly at every index; for odd $r$ a zero-step identity
$A_r(n)=\binom{rn}{n}A^\circ_r(n)$; and a closed Hessian determinant.  Report~86's
``lattice factor~4'' is shown not to be an instance of the
lattice-peak transfer theorem of \dir{a357825-theta-ballot-power-sums} (no
growing exponent; it depends on the coordinates), and the two sources' ``no proof
found'' and ``still unproved'' are corrected by dated notes; nothing in them
was found wrong.  The independent check of the write found every item valid
and refined three wordings.  Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{An exact gamma constant for an Ap\'ery-like sequence}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a279619-level-seven-gamma-constant}
{article.pdf}{batch~73: A279619\_Gamma\_Asymptotics.zip + A279619\_exact\_asymptotics.zip; batch~77: level7-asymptotics-replay.zip; batch~97: A279619\_Hankel\_Positivity.zip (Section~17)}
{Proves the asymptotic displayed in OEIS A279619 with the constant
identified:
$a_n\sim\sqrt{3\pi}\,27^n/(\Gamma(\tfrac17)\Gamma(\tfrac27)\Gamma(\tfrac47)\,n^{3/2})$,
with $\sum a_n/27^n=3\Gamma(\tfrac17)\Gamma(\tfrac27)\Gamma(\tfrac47)/(8\pi^2)$,
a second exact sum at $z=1/125$, rational corrections to every fixed order
with error bounds, ratio, root and critical-tail expansions, and a
continued-fraction companion with a recessive alternating solution.  The
Lucas-congruence conjecture of the entry is not established.
\emph{Merged from two manuscripts that proved the constant independently
on the same day by different routes --- a hypergeometric pullback with
Ramanujan's level-seven series, and an eta product at $i/\sqrt7$ evaluated by
Chowla--Selberg --- both kept.}  A third manuscript, added in batch~77,
proves the constant once more; its route is printed as Section~16: the
Fricke involution of the weight-one theta series gives the singular
coefficient directly at $\tau=i/\sqrt7$, the level-seven case of
Guillera--Zudilin's modular limit formula, and an Epstein zeta function
gives the eta value.  It adds no theorem, but an explicit generator for
the corrections of every convolution power, with coefficients in a
polynomial ring over~$\Q$, and rounding brackets for every fixed weight,
whose inversion cites the transseries volume; its claim to answer
O'Brien's Question~10.2 is not carried.  A fourth manuscript, written
with the report in hand and added in batch~97 as Section~17, answers its
research question on log-convexity and Hankel positivity: $a_n$ is
strictly log-convex for every $n\ge1$, by elementary ratio barriers; the
Hankel matrix $(a_{i+j})$ is strictly totally positive of order~5 but not
totally nonnegative of order~6, with
$D_6(1)=-26875777009408537346562624$, the positive part certified by 21
rational polynomials with positive coefficients and Fekete's finite-order
criterion; $a_0,\dots,a_{10}$ are the moments of a positive measure on
$[0,\infty)$, the least $a_{11}$ such a measure admits is attained by a
unique six-point measure, and the actual $a_{11}$ falls below it; every
fixed-size shifted Hankel determinant has a full asymptotic expansion with
an explicit generator, and a large enough shift restores any fixed order of
total positivity.  Its recurrence-only proof of the expansion is printed as
a second route, and its Lambert-$W$ inverse for the determinants is an
instance of the transseries volume's theorem.  The write added
Subsection~17.10: the generating function has a square-root singularity at
$z=-1$ with coefficient $B_-\in(-0.2075,-0.1754)$, so no tail of $(a_n)$
is a Stieltjes moment sequence, the order of first failure of total
positivity is finite for every shift and tends to infinity with the shift,
and a positive measure on $\mathbb{R}$ with moments $a_n$, if one exists,
is unique, supported in $[-1,27]$ and charges every neighbourhood of~$-1$;
an independent check of these statements found no gap and added one
sentence on an atom at~$0$.  Not established: such a Hamburger measure,
the optimal shifts for orders $\ge6$ (finite data only), and the closed
forms $B_-=-2\pi/(\Gamma(\tfrac17)\Gamma(\tfrac27)\Gamma(\tfrac47))$ and
its companion, observed to 200 digits.  No claim of the manuscript was
found wrong; the Hilbert's-tenth research tree triaged its archive within a
bounded scope (\texttt{37af2f0d6}).  Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{Spectral collisions and boundary asymptotics for zig-zag Eulerian numbers}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a205497-zigzag-eulerian-spectra}
{article.pdf}{batch~73: zigzag\_oeis\_research.zip}
{For the descent counts $z(n,k)$ of linear extensions of a fence (OEIS
A205497), determines the exact reduced denominator of every fixed-column
generating function; its degree, the minimal recurrence order, is
$r_k=\frac{40}{27\pi^2}(k+1)^3+O(k^2\log k)$, about $80/(9\pi^2)$ of the usual
unreduced order.  Also a uniform relative-error bound, a Gumbel crossover
$\exp(-e^{-s})$ when $n/(k+\tfrac32)-\log n\to s$, and strict log-concavity
near both edges for $k\le cn/\log n$; full-row log-concavity and
real-rootedness are not proved.  Two OEIS presentation corrections.}

\entry{Winding sectors and the four-displacement restricted-permutation problem}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a000382-winding-correction}
{article.pdf}{batch~73: ProveIt\_A000382\_winding\_correction.zip}
{For the permutations of $\mathbb{Z}/n$ with every displacement in
$\{0,1,2,3\}$, a bijective proof by winding number of $\Phi_n=2(L_n+1)$,
$L_n$ the Tribonacci--Lucas numbers; the formula itself is already printed in
OEIS A004306.  OEIS identifies $\Phi_n$ with A000496 and with four times
A000382, which first disagree at $n=8$: the recurrence marked conjectural in
A000382 is proved, but for the sequence produced by Mendelsohn's 1961
formula, not for the permutation count, and the exact difference has
generating function $z^4/(1-2z+z^4)$.  Also an inverse transseries.}

\entry{Fixed-multiplicity shuffles and balanced Smirnov words}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a330266-balanced-smirnov-poisson}
{article.pdf}{batch~73: ProveIt\_Balanced\_Smirnov\_OEIS.zip; batch~77: balanced-smirnov-repair-reproducibility.zip; batch~101: A330266\_Uniform\_Tail\_Repair.zip (bundle Report~98, Part~III)}
{Proves Kirgizov's conjecture in OEIS A330266 for every fixed $k\ge2$: the
fraction of shuffles of $n$ ranks of $k$ cards with no two equal neighbours
tends to $e^{-(k-1)}$, and the number of equal adjacencies is asymptotically
Poisson$(k-1)$, with an exact Laguerre-kernel generating integral and
explicit corrections, for $k=4$
$e^{-3}(1-\frac9{8n}-\frac3{128n^2}+\frac{189}{1024n^3}+O(n^{-4}))$.  Part~I
marks one tail estimate, on which the uniform expansion and the
corrections rest, as a sketch; the limit law does not depend on it.
Part~II, added in batch~77 from a second manuscript, proves it for every
fixed $k$: the factorial-moment domination $E(X)_M\le(k-1)^M$, an explicit
uniform remainder with a Touchard-polynomial constant, an all-orders
expansion in integer powers, a degree bound giving factorial cumulants
$O(n^{1-r})$, and a sharper inverse along the count sequence, which cites
the transseries volume.  Every coefficient Part~I displays is re-derived
unchanged and cited, not reprinted; the second manuscript's contraction,
ratio and local derivations are kept as second proofs.
Part~III, added in batch~101 from a third manuscript written from the same
pin as Part~II without knowledge of it, repairs the same step a second time;
the steps that agree with Part~II are mapped and cited, and two proofs are
kept as further routes.  New in it: the fourth-order terms for every $k$,
for A330266 the term $+\frac{7395}{32768n^4}$; a remainder constant sharper
by $2^{-(J+1)}$; and for unequal rank sizes an exact factorial-moment
identity with the bound $E(X)_M\le(k_{\max}-1)^M$.  The write adds a Poisson
limit for bounded multiplicities and the local probabilities $P(X=j)$ to
every fixed order.  The fourth-order coefficients are an exact
computer-algebra identity, not a hand derivation; growing $k$, divergence
and optimal truncation, and a certified integer inverse stay open.  The
independent check found every item valid and corrected the wording of one
proposition on unequal ranks (its degenerate cases).  Unrefereed; not
formalized.}

\entry{Unconditional all-orders asymptotics for two OEIS permutation sequences}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a189281-path-forest-expansions}
{article.pdf}{batch~73: oeis\_path\_forests.zip; batch~74: ProveIt\_Fixed\_Gap\_Asymptotics.zip; batch~85: OEIS\_Rational\_Collapse\_and\_Integrality.zip; batch~97: ProveIt\_Moving\_Gap\_Permutations.zip (Part~IV), ProveIt\_A189281\_Borel\_Completion.zip (Part~V); batch~101: Fixed\_Displacement\_Permutation\_Asymptotics\_Source.zip (bundle Report~83, Part~VI), Report232.zip (Part~VII)}
{OEIS A189281 and A110128 count permutations avoiding a prescribed signed
or absolute difference between entries two positions apart.  Proves the
displayed higher expansions without the conjectured recurrences, through a
stabilization theorem for tilings of long path forests: for any fixed
offsets the violation count has an all-orders expansion $e^{\theta u}$ times
polynomials, with a finite exact formula for every coefficient, extended
through order sixteen.  The conjectured recurrences are not claimed;
Part~I proposes a rational-function conjecture, which Part~III proves.
Part~II, added in batch~74 from a manuscript that re-derives Part~I's
theorems independently (its coefficients through order twelve agree and are
printed only as a check), adds shape-uniform remainders, comparison bounds
with an $n=12$ example, a closed $c_3(r,s)$ and integrality for oriented
one-path families.  Part~III, added in batch~85, proves that conjecture for
every order as the case $(2,2)$ of a closed form, valid for every pair of
directed offsets, of the stable moments (via a single-sum moment formula),
and deduces that every oriented correction polynomial has integer
coefficients, with an exact degree law; it adds an inverse-factorial
${}_2F_0$ kernel, an entire Borel transform of the coefficients (no
summability claim), A189282--A189284 through $n^{-6}$ and certificates at
$n=64,128,256$.  Its special cases of Part~II's one-path results are
credited to Part~II.  Part~III does not cover the absolute case, and no
Part proves the guessed order-8 recurrence of A189281.
Part~IV (batch~97) proves the expansion uniformly over every pair of path
forests, in normalized subpath counts and with no hypothesis on the path
lengths: the order-$n^{-J}$ term depends only on subpaths of at most $J+1$
edges, sharply; one short path of $L$ vertices shifts the absolute-model
avoidance probability by $-2e^{-2}n^{-L-1}$; and for moving offsets
$r/n\to\alpha$, $s/n\to\beta$ the limit is Poisson$(\theta(1-\alpha)(1-\beta))$,
with correction boundaries at $r/n=1/q$.  Part~V (batch~97), for A189281,
gives an explicit Dawson-function Borel kernel and the positive-ray sum
$C_*(n)$, summable in no open sector, the sharp growth of the coefficients
(the series has radius zero and they are not P-recursive), a truncation
rule for $C_*$, the OEIS order-13 operator annihilating the analytic
completion (not the counts), and a flat remainder $e\,a_n/n!-C_*(n)$ that
is nonzero at least once in every four consecutive indices; two numerical
statements of its source (a truncation index, and one sign change that is
in fact fourteen) are corrected on record.  Part~VI (batch~101, bundle
Report~83), a same-day third derivation of Part~I's theorem that predates
Parts~I--II, prints only its additions: exact Bonferroni enclosures at
$n=100,200,1000$, the threshold ceiling bracket and an exact-digamma
inverse (instances of the transseries volume, proofs completed by the
write) and four literature items.  Part~VII (batch~101, bundle
Report~232) treats two reciprocal absolute gaps at once, answering the
several-gaps question of the earlier Parts: a joint two-mark expansion
uniform on compact sets of $\mathbb{C}^2$, in which cycles enter first at
order $n^{-2}$; A189358 (camel) $=e^{-4}n!(1+8/n+48/n^2+O(n^{-3}))$ and
A137774 (knight) $=e^{-4}n!(1+4/n+28/n^2+O(n^{-3}))$, where multiplying the
marginal laws would predict 36 and~8; and threshold inverses.  Its
single-gap marginals are Part~I's, and one misstated H\"older step is
corrected in place.  The independent check of the batch-101 write found
everything valid and narrowed one sentence (the $n=1000$ enclosure checks
Part~I's $c_{11}$, not $c_{12}$).  Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{Two Fourier peaks and a moving lattice maximum}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a039831-two-fourier-peaks}
{article.pdf}{batch~73: A039831\_Two\_Fourier\_Peaks.zip}
{Proves Kote\v{s}ovec's 2023 conjecture $M_n\sim3(n/e)^n$ in OEIS A039831, for
the largest coefficient of $\prod_{j\le n}(1+q+q^3+\cdots+q^{2j-1})$, with
explicit corrections through relative order $n^{-4}$: rounding produces a
log-periodic term at order $n^{-3}$, and a second Fourier neighbourhood at
$q=-1$ a parity term at order $n^{-4}$, which moves the maximizing exponent
infinitely often.  Also a zero-multiplicity law under Bernoulli smoothing and
a Lambert-$W$ inverse.  A039824 is not resolved.}

\entry{The cubic boundary for restricted partitions}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a238016-restricted-partitions-cubic-boundary}
{article.pdf}{batch~74: OEIS\_Restricted\_Partitions\_Cubic\_Boundary.zip; batch~98: OEIS\_Restricted\_Partitions\_Crossover.zip (Part~II)}
{For partitions of $N$ into parts at most $m$, proves
$p_m(N)\sim N^{m-1}/(m!(m-1)!)$ if and only if $N/m^3\to\infty$, a condition
still called conjectural in OEIS A238016 and A258670 (sufficiency is
Erd\H{o}s--Lehner's; necessity is new here), with ratio $e^{1/(4c)}$ at
$N\sim cm^3$, a uniform centred expansion for $N\ge c_0m^3$, five
corrections for A238608, exponential sectors at $N=q^m$, inverse charts and
a Poisson law for equal adjacent parts.  The cubic scale is the
$\alpha\to\infty$ end of the A097356 report's saddle question, not an
answer to it.  Part~II (batch~98) answers Part~I's question on a uniform
bridge below the cubic scale, and with it that end of the A097356 report's
question: with $X=N+m(m+1)/4$, $\varepsilon=m^2/X$ and
$V_m(X)=X^{m-1}/(m!(m-1)!)$, $\log(p_m(N)/V_m(X))$ has an expansion
$m\mathcal D(\varepsilon)+E_0(\varepsilon)+\sum_jE_j(\varepsilon)m^{-j}$ to
every fixed order, uniform for all $N\ge am^2$; the centred volume law
holds iff $X/m^{5/2}\to\infty$, with ratio $\exp(-1/(72\lambda^2))$ at
$X\sim\lambda m^{5/2}$; transitions at the scales $m^{2+1/(2r)}$ have
rational constants $d_r$, infinitely many nonzero; and an analytic inverse
chart.  Several Part~I results and Szekeres's constants are re-proved as
second routes.  Part~II is not uniform as $N/m^2\to0$, and negativity of
every $d_r$ is not claimed ($d_1,\ldots,d_{16}<0$ is evidence only); the
expansions are fixed-order.  The independent check corrected one tabulated
decimal and the order of a step in the write's all-orders inverse, and
recorded floating evidence that the second-transition sign changes between
$m=1373$ and $1374$.  Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{L-convex polyominoes and a colored-partition comparison}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a126764-lconvex-polyominoes}
{article.pdf}{batch~75: A126764\_Lconvex\_Research.zip; batch~77: lconvex-asymptotics-reproducibility.zip; batch~101: L\_Convex\_Polyomino\_Area\_Asymptotics\_Source.zip (bundle Report~95, Part~III)}
{Proves the Guttmann--Kot\v{e}\v{s}ovec asymptotic recorded as a conjecture
in OEIS A126764 for fixed L-convex polyominoes of area~$n$,
$a_n\sim\tfrac{13\sqrt2}{768}\,n^{-3/2}e^{\pi\sqrt{13n/6}}$, with three
correction terms in closed form and relative error $O(n^{-7/4})$.  The leading term
and first correction already follow from a squeeze
$0\le p_n-4a_n\le3(p_n-2p_{n-1}+p_{n-2})$ ($n\ge3$) against the
coefficients $p_n$ of an eta quotient counting coloured partitions; a
finite-cut identity and a full-circle estimate give the other two.  Also an
all-orders radial expansion of the generating function at $q=e^{-t}$ (not a
coefficient expansion) and a $W_{-1}$ inverse, an instance of the
transseries volume's Lambert and staircase theorems.  The area generating
function is a cited input; Part~I's manuscript metadata says it was
prepared with ChatGPT.  Part~II, added in batch~77 from an independent
second manuscript, proves the all-orders coefficient expansion that
Part~I left open: from an exact decomposition $A=PB^2+R$, with $P$
Part~I's eta quotient, $B$ a partial theta function and $R$ exponentially
smaller,
$a_n=\tfrac{13\sqrt2}{768}N^{-3/2}e^{\pi\sqrt{13N/6}}(\sum_{r<M}c_rN^{-r/2}+O(N^{-M/2}))$
with $N=n-1/6$, for every $M$, re-proving Part~I's three corrections.
Also eventual strict log-concavity, monotonicity by an injection, and a
shifted $W_{-1}$ inverse with integer brackets of every order, again an
instance of the transseries volume; Part~I's boundary series equals~$B$.
Part~II states that Guttmann and Kot\v{e}\v{s}ovec print the reciprocal of
the amplitude; checked in batch~101 against their arXiv version~3, the
display has the reciprocal while the quoted decimal is correct.
Part~III, added in batch~101 from a third manuscript that knew Part~I but
not Part~II, proves the all-orders coefficient theorem a third time, in
the unshifted index, by a route with every estimate written out
(positivity, the partition factor $(q;q)_N$ common to all later terms, and
Chebyshev interpolation into the saddle window), where Part~II sketches its
circle estimates; it gives a duality proof of the decomposition, with a
positive series and explicit bounds for the remainder, strict increase
$a_{n+1}\ge a_n+1$ for every $n\ge1$, the limit
$n-n_0(a_n)\to\eta=1/6+36/(13\pi^2)$ for the leading Lambert root, so that
rounding that model is eventually right (wrong for $2\le n\le555$ and right
for $556\le n\le8000$ by computation; no onset is proved), and exact terms
to $n=8000$, agreeing with all 2001 b-file terms.  The write completes its
saddle extraction and identifies its auxiliary polynomials as
Al-Salam--Chihara polynomials; its novelty sentence, stale against Part~II,
is corrected.  The independent check found every item valid and sharpened
one constant.  Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{The logarithmic deficit of 120-avoiding ascent sequences}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a202061-ascent-120-deficit}
{article.pdf}{batch~77: oeis-a202061-report.zip + oeis-a202061-sharp-deficit.zip + oeis-a202061-second-order-report.zip + oeis-a202061-third-order-report.zip + oeis-a202061-allorders-report.zip}
{For ascent sequences avoiding 120 (OEIS A202061), with $\mu\approx7.2959$
the largest root of $\mu^3-8\mu^2+5\mu+1$, $D_n=n\log\mu-\log a_n$,
$F=n^{1/3}(\log n)^{2/3}$ and $L=\log n$, five Parts prove, each refining
the last: $D_n=\Theta(F)$ from an exact positive height-walk
representation, which excludes every equivalent
$C_0\mu^ne^{-dn^\gamma}n^g$ with $\gamma\ge1/3$, among them the stretch
$n^{3/8}$ that Conway, Conway, Elvey Price and Guttmann estimated
numerically, and that the generating function is not D-finite (Part~I);
$D_n/F\to C_\star\approx2.2326$, explicit (II); the term
$(7/3)C_\star F\log L/L$ (III); the next constant
$\kappa\approx-1.9401$, which proves Part~III's conjecture (IV); and, for
every fixed $J$, an expansion of $D_n/(C_\star F)$ in powers of $1/L$
with universal polynomials $P_j(\kappa+(7/3)\log L)$ (V); each with its
inverse.  No multiplicative equivalent, amplitude or effective threshold
is claimed, priority is not established, and the inverses are instances
of the transseries volume.  The A202062 report gives a second route to
the growth rate~$\mu$.  \emph{Merged from five manuscripts whose archives
nest their predecessors byte for byte; each paper is printed once, in
chain order, and one manuscript's restatements of Part~I are cited.}}

\entry{Ascent sequences avoiding 000}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a202058-ascent-000-growth}
{article.pdf}{batch~77: oeis-a202058-report.zip + oeis-a202058-fine-report.zip + a202058-lognormal-report.zip + a202058-ratio-report.zip; batch~102: A202058\_Ascent\_Sequence\_Growth\_Source.zip (bundle Report~97)}
{For ascent sequences avoiding 000 (OEIS A202058), $T=3\pi^2/8$ and
$c_n=a_nT^n/n!$, Part~I proves the factorial growth constant
$(a_n/n!)^{1/n}\to8/(3\pi^2)$ conjectured by Conway, Conway, Elvey Price
and Guttmann, through the exact radius $T$ of the exponential generating
function, with $\log c_n=O(n^{9/13}(\log n)^3)$.  Part~II proves
$-O(n^{2/3}(\log n)^{5/3})\le\log c_n\le O((\log n)^2)$, which excludes a
factor $e^{cn^\sigma}$ with $c,\sigma>0$, gives exact counts through 400,
shows that $a_n/n!$ is not PF$_3$, and conjectures
$\log c_n\sim(2/3)(\log n)^2$.  Part~III proves that law in limsup and
cumulative form, with $\log A(t)\sim(2/3)\log^2(1/(T-t))$; Part~IV
proves the ratio limit $a_{n+1}/((n+1)a_n)\to8/(3\pi^2)$ with error
$O(n^{-1/4}(\log n)^{3/4})$ and the lower bound
$\log c_n\ge-O(\sqrt n(\log n)^{3/2})$.  The pointwise law,
log-concavity and a multiplicative equivalent stay open; the Lambert-$W$
inverses are instances of the transseries volume.  \emph{Merged from four
manuscripts in chain order; Part~III's re-proof of Part~II's bounds is
kept as a second route, Part~IV's uncredited restatement of a Part~II
identity is credited, and statements a later Part supersedes carry dated
notes.}  Part~V (batch~102), bundle Report~97, written without knowledge
of Parts~I--IV, gives a third proof of the root limit (error $o(n)$ only),
by a finite Perron bound with buffered Eulerian blocks, and a second proof
of the ratio limit, without a rate.  New: the two-catalytic functional
equation $H(x;s+x(t-1),t)=tH(x;s,t-xs(t-1))-xst(t-1)$ with its
coefficient recurrence, a weighted upper bound, and endpoint laws: the
proportions of values used once, of unused available values, of distinct
values and of ascents tend to $1-8/(3\pi)$, $8(\pi+1)/(3\pi^2)-1$,
$1-4/(3\pi)$ and $4/(3\pi)+8/(3\pi^2)$, with exponentially small
deviations; strict monotonicity $a_{n+1}>a_n$; exact counts through 395,
equal to Part~II's.  Its inverse is weaker than those of Parts~II and~IV.
Part~V is the cap-two case of Part~III of
\dir{a294220-ascent-multiplicity-caps}, whose Corollary~18.3 proves
Report~97's operation count.  Report~97's ``unresolved'' stretched factor
is excluded by Parts~II--III (dated notes); its pointwise law and its
formal eikonal and Borel reading are further questions.}

\entry{Exact enumeration of 201-avoiding ascent sequences}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a202062-ascent-201-enumeration}
{article.pdf}{batch~77: oeis-a202062-report.zip}
{For ascent sequences avoiding 201 (OEIS A202062), proves the cubic
generating function conjectured by Guttmann and Kot\v{e}\v{s}ovec, by an
exact two-label generating tree whose two-catalytic kernel equation is
solved by two rational invariants, one from an order-seven kernel orbit;
the report finds their conjectured cubic equivalent to its own and a sign
error in their printed final combination.  For every $M$,
$a_n=C\mu^nn^{-9/2}(\sum_{m\le M}c_mn^{-m}+O(n^{-M-1}))$, with
$\mu\approx7.2959$ the largest root of $\mu^3-8\mu^2+5\mu+1$,
$C\approx13.4300$ in closed form and $c_m\in\Q(\mu)$, the first three
exact; also a $W_{-1}$ inverse with ceiling brackets, an instance of the
transseries volume's Lambert theorems.  With Conway, Conway, Elvey Price
and Guttmann's equal-growth theorem it gives $\lim b_n^{1/n}=\mu$ for
A202061, a second route to Part~I of the A202061 report.  Novelty rests
on a scoped literature check, and the envelope constants are not
explicit.}

\entry{Asymptotic enumeration and inversion for A122399}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a122399-surjection-diagonal}
{article.pdf}{batch~77: oeis-a122399-research.zip; batch~98: A122399\_Boundary\_Transitions\_and\_Inversion.zip (Part~II)}
{For $a_n=\sum_kk^nk!\,S(n,k)$ (OEIS A122399), the diagonal of
$1/(1-ue^y(e^x-1))$: an exact, absolutely convergent vertical-contour
formula for the two-size numbers $A_{m,n}$ with a nonasymptotic tail
bound; an all-orders saddle expansion uniform for $m/n$ in compact
subsets of $(0,\infty)$; and
$a_n=CD^n(n!)^2n^{-1/2}(1-0.0600744\ldots/n+\cdots)$ through $n^{-4}$,
with $D\approx3.1610887$ (A317855) and the first correction in closed
form.  Also a controlled inverse with integer-threshold envelopes, whose
initializer is an instance of the transseries volume's inverse-Gamma
core; a central limit theorem for the number of occupied blocks; and
$a_{n+\varphi(p^r)}\equiv a_n\pmod{p^r}$ for $n\ge r$, which proves Bala's
conjecture that $p-1$ is a period modulo~$p$; no least period is claimed.
The leading equivalent is Kot\v{e}\v{s}ovec's (OEIS, 2018), the methods
are standard saddle methods, and no global novelty is claimed.
Part~II (batch~98) studies the defect $D=m-K$ of the number $K$ of blocks
of a uniform structure counted by $A_{m,n}$: $D$ is exactly a sum of $m-1$
independent Bernoulli variables (real zeros of the block polynomials, by
Harper's method; the case $n=0$ is Frobenius's), with explicit Poisson
bounds for all $(m,n)$; a degenerate, Poisson or Gaussian regime at
$n=m(\log m+c)$ as $c\to+\infty$, fixed or $-\infty$, with
$D\to\mathrm{Poisson}(e^{-c}/2)$; uniform lattice Edgeworth expansions to
every fixed order; a complete expansion in the critical window with a
rational coefficient algorithm; doubleton defects; an all-orders inverse
for the all-singleton probability with the exact certificate
$N_{1/e}(1000)=6207$; and a diagonal local limit theorem with Part~I's
constants.  It answers Part~I's Questions 2 and 4 in part.  Its
``transseries'' are depth-one power--log expansions, as a dated note
records; the write proved a sketched degree bound and the sharpness of the
count half of the growing Poisson window, and the independent check
corrected that proof's closing sentence, which named an impossible regime.
Large deviations, count asymptotics for $m/n\to\infty$ and a bridge to the
saddle range stay open.  Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{Asymptotics of iterated Bell diagonals (OEIS A139383, A261280)}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a139383-iterated-bell-diagonals}
{article.pdf}{batch~77: oeis-iterated-bell-report-attribution-revised.zip + iterated-bell-proportional-addendum.zip (the first edition oeis-iterated-bell-report.zip superseded, not printed)}
{For the iterated Bell numbers $H(n,m)=n![z^n](e^z-1)^{\circ m}$, with
OEIS A139383 $=H(n,n)$ and A261280 $=H(n,n+1)$, Part~I proves the leading
equivalents those entries label conjectural, and for each fixed integer $k$
an expansion
$H(n,n+k)=e^kC\,n^{2n-5/6}2^{1-n}e^{-n}\bigl(1+\sum_jP_{j,k}(\log n)n^{-j}\bigr)$
to every fixed order, with an outward-rounded interval certificate that
$2<C<4$ (the digits $C\approx2.86540798$ are not certified), an exact
coefficient generator, and smooth inverses with a safe integer bracket.
Part~II, from an addendum conditional on Part~I, extends the expansion to
proportional depth $m=\lambda n+\delta$, uniformly for $\lambda$ in compact
subsets of $(0,\infty)$, with an amplitude $D(\lambda)$, $D(1)=2C$, proved
strictly positive for every $\lambda>0$, a floor-depth phase
$e^{-\{\lambda n\}/\lambda}$, eventual monotonicity and inverses; the
endpoint regimes stay open.  Prellberg's 2002 bivariate formula and
parabolic-coordinate method already give the leading equivalent: no priority
is claimed for it, for the method or for the coefficients.  The inverses are
instances of the transseries volume.  The superseded first edition differs
only in that attribution; the check that Prellberg's broad printed theorem
fails for $f(z)=z/(1-z)$ is shared with the Takeuchi report.}

\entry{Corner polyhedra and Schnyder labelings: polynomial exponents and limiting amplitudes}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a377922-corner-polyhedra-schnyder}
{article.pdf}{batch~77: oeis-bimodal-cone-research.zip + oeis-bimodal-full-asymptotics.zip; batch~103: Geometric\_OEIS\_Sequences\_Cone\_Exponents\_and\_Inverses\_Source.zip (bundle Report~112; Part~III)}
{Fusy, Narmanli and Schaeffer encode corner polyhedra (OEIS A377922) and
3-connected Schnyder labelings (A377920) as quadrant walks with a two-state
parity and conjecture equivalents $\kappa\mu^nn^{-\alpha}$ with irrational
exponents (their Conjecture~25).  Part~I proves
$p_n=\Theta((9/2)^nn^{-\alpha_P})$ and $s_n=\Theta((16/3)^nn^{-\alpha_S})$
with the conjectured $\alpha_P=1+\pi/\arccos(9/16)$ and
$\alpha_S=1+\pi/\arccos(22/27)$, by a one-unit cycle buffer, a random-clock
transfer and a Fourier local limit; since both exponents are irrational, the
generating functions of A377922, A377920 and A377921 are not D-finite; and
first-threshold inverses with $O(1)$ error.  Part~II, from an addendum,
proves the full equivalents with finite positive amplitudes $\kappa_P$,
$\kappa_S$ (completing Conjecture~25), the coefficientwise equivalent
$\tilde s_n\sim(16/19)^3\kappa_S(16/3)^nn^{-\alpha_S}$ for A377921, and a
Lambert-$W_{-1}$ inverse with a vanishing-width ceiling bracket, an instance
of the transseries volume.  No digits of $\kappa_P$, $\kappa_S$, no rates and
no correction terms are claimed; A377923 is outside both theorems; the iid
cone theorems are Denisov--Wachtel's, and neither manuscript claims
priority.  Part~III (batch~103), from a manuscript written without
knowledge of Parts~I--II, proves a cone theorem at the logarithmic scale for
Markov-additive walks on $\mathbb Z^2\times E$ with a finite phase set $E$
and unbounded steps with a uniform exponential moment, killed on leaving the
quadrant: under hypotheses (H1)--(H5) the exact-time kernel is
$n^{-1-\pi/\theta+o(1)}$, with a survival bound under (H1)--(H4).  Also new: a
six-face sleeve injection giving $s_{n-6}\le\tilde s_n\le s_n$, hence (at the
write, with Part~I) $\tilde s_n=\Theta(\mu_S^nn^{-\alpha_S})$; non-D-finiteness
from the logarithmic law alone (stated in general at the write); the
exponential-moment certificate $189/13$; $4<\alpha_P<5$, $6<\alpha_S<7$; three
further references.  What it says about the three sequences is a weaker
second route, and its open amplitudes, ratio limit and $O(1)$ inverse are
settled by Parts~I--II (dated notes).  \dir{a348351-one-sided-rectangulations}
proves its exponent by the same method; its walk satisfies (H1)--(H5).  The
independent check found the write's remarks valid and corrected a scale label
and a coefficient in one proof.  An amplitude or $\Theta$ form of the cone
theorem stays open.  Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{Precise asymptotics of the powered Catalan numbers (OEIS A113227)}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a113227-powered-catalan}
{article.pdf}{batch~77: oeis-powered-catalan-report.zip + oeis-powered-catalan-fourier-addendum.zip}
{The powered Catalan numbers $a_n$ (OEIS A113227) count permutations
avoiding the vincular pattern $1$-$23$-$4$.  From the known continued
fraction Part~I derives a positive discrete moment representation
$a_n=\sum_\lambda\omega_\lambda\lambda^n$ whose transform is a Bessel
quotient, locates its large spectral nodes, and proves
$a_n\sim e^{e-2}(2\pi)^{-1/2}r^{-3/2}B_n(e)$, with $B_n$ the Touchard
polynomial and $r=n/W(n/e)$, answering the question of precise asymptotics
that Elizalde left open in 2006; also an expansion to every fixed order with
explicit $c_1$, $c_2$, a canonical continuous inverse and an
exponential-generating-function corollary.  Part~II, from an addendum,
proves a zero-free complex wedge for the Bessel weight, replaces the
spectral sum by a lattice sum with relative error $O(e^{-dn})$ for every
$d<\log2$, and splits $a_n$, for each fixed $J$, into $J+1$ exact Fourier
sectors, each with an all-orders expansion.  No convergence, growing number
of sectors, Borel sum or transseries is claimed, and the inverse is an
instance of the transseries volume.  The recurrence, models and continued
fraction are prior art (Callan, Beaton et al., Gladkovskii); the sibling
pattern $12$-$34$ (A113226) has its own report.}

\entry{Growing powers of Mahonian coefficients}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a380274-mahonian-growing-powers}
{article.pdf}{batch~77: oeis-mahonian-crossover-result.zip}
{For $S_n(r)=\sum_kM_n(k)^r$, with $M_n(k)$ the number of permutations of
$n$ with $k$ inversions, proves that for every $r_n\to\infty$ the ratio
$S_n(r_n)/C_n^{r_n}$, $C_n$ the central Mahonian number, is asymptotic to a
theta function $\Theta_\delta(r_n/V_n)$, where $V_n=n(n-1)(2n+5)/72$ is the
inversion variance and $\delta\in\{0,\tfrac12\}$ depends on $n\bmod4$: a
Gaussian integral below the critical scale $r\asymp n^3$, a discrete Gaussian
partition function on it and $1+2\delta$ above it, with the matching limit
laws for the common inversion count of colliding random permutations.  Also
an explicit fourth-order correction, uniform expansions to every fixed order
on the transition scale, an unnormalized multiplicative equivalent, and a
Lambert-$W_0$ index inverse on a fixed critical path, an instance of the
transseries volume.  The fixed-power equivalent with three corrections,
including OEIS A380274 ($r=3$) and A380275 ($r=4$), is in Wang's 2026
manuscript and is not claimed; priority for the growing-power extension is
not established.  A dated note relates the crossover to the critical theorem
of the theta-ballot report; that report's general lattice-peak question is
answered in a sufficient-hypothesis form by its Part~II (batch~98), whose
Proposition~21.1 makes this report's critical window an instance by
reorganizing its proof.}

\entry{Divisor-weighted partition products and assemblies (OEIS A301746 and A294363)}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a301746-divisor-weighted-asymptotics}
{article.pdf}{batch~77: oeis-divisor-square-asymptotics-result.zip + oeis-divisor-assembly-result.zip}
{Two manuscripts on generating functions weighted by the divisor function
$d(k)$.  Part~I treats OEIS A301746, the coefficients of
$\prod_k(1+q^k)^{d(k)^2}$, and proves the conjecture recorded there.
From the fourth-order Mellin pole at $s=1$ it obtains
$\log a_n\sim\sqrt n(\log n)^{3/2}/(2\sqrt6)$, Kot\v{e}\v{s}ovec's 2018
conjecture, with three further inverse-logarithmic corrections and the
matching inverse.  Part~I also gives a relative expansion to every order in
exact saddle cumulants, strict monotonicity and shrinking brackets for the
integer threshold; the contributions of zeta zeros are left open.
Part~II treats A294363, $n![z^n]\exp(\sum_kd(k)z^k)$, whose Mellin kernel has
a double pole: an explicit multiplicative equivalent through a shifted
Lambert-$W$ saddle, an all-orders expansion, the OEIS logarithmic statement,
and a factorial-core inverse.
Its coefficients are rational in the Lambert parameter, and the inverse's
absolute error tends to zero.
The two Parts share a method template, also used by the batch-77 source of
the A022629 report, but no theorem.  No priority is claimed for the methods,
and the Lambert, reversion and bracket steps are instances of the
transseries volume.}

\entry{Ascent sequences with bounded multiplicity (OEIS A294220)}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a294220-ascent-multiplicity-caps}
{article.pdf}{batch~77: oeis-bounded-multiplicity-family-report.zip + oeis-fixed-sector-addendum.zip; batch~102: Ascent\_Sequences\_with\_Bounded\_Multiplicities\_Source.zip (bundle Report~99)}
{For ascent sequences of length $n$ in which every value occurs at most $b$
times (the array OEIS A294220; cap three is A317784), Part~I proves for every
fixed $b\ge3$ that $(a^{(b)}_n/n!)^{1/n}\to\mu_b=1/T_b$, with
$T_b=\int_0^\infty\log E_b(v)/(E_b(v)-1)\,dv$ and $E_b$ the truncated
exponential, with error $O_b(n/\log n)$ in $\log a^{(b)}_n$; also a
Lambert-$W$ inverse in the length, the large-cap law
$T_b=\pi^2/6+(b+1)(\zeta(b+2)-1)+O(b^{5/2}(4/9)^b)$ with explicit constants,
and a $W_{-1}$ inverse in the cap with counterexamples to naive rounding.
The cap-two value $T_2=3\pi^2/8$, the constant $8/(3\pi^2)$ conjectured by
Conway, Conway, Elvey Price and Guttmann, is taken from the A202058 report,
not re-proved.  Part~II, from an addendum, writes $T_b-\pi^2/6$ as a
convergent sum of positive Taylor sectors, proves that the remainder after
any fixed number of sectors is asymptotic to the next sector, and expands
each fixed sector to all orders in $1/b$; the remainder above is
$\sim\frac8{9\sqrt\pi}\sqrt b\,(4/9)^b$.  No prefactor or all-orders
expansion in $n$, no joint regime $b=b(n)$ and no priority is claimed; the
inverses are instances of the transseries volume.  Part~III (batch~102),
bundle Report~99, which writes the cap as $r$ and knew neither earlier
Part, proves for every fixed $r\ge2$ the root and ratio limits $\mu_r$: the
ratio limit is new for $r\ge3$ and answers Part~I's Question~2, while the
root half and the case $r=2$ are second routes.  New also: endpoint laws
for the proportions of values used $k$ times, of distinct values and of
ascents, with exponential tails; the weighted growth formula
$\lim N^{-1}\log(H_N(u)/N!)=-\log\chi(u)$ and a full large-deviation
principle; and a histogram-preserving sorted recurrence counting in
$O_r(N^{r+2})$ operations.  Its Section~24 re-proves the large-cap results
in $R=b+1$ (its $-31/8$ is Part~II's $-27/8$ in $b$) and adds a continuous
cap, exact-sector envelopes and a two-candidate cap selection; its length
inverse is weaker than Part~I's.  The write proves Report~99's remark that
for $K=1$ the derivative bracket of its envelopes improves to
$1+O((2/3)^R)$.  Part~V of
\dir{a202058-ascent-000-growth} is the case $r=2$.  No prefactor, central
or local limit theorem, or classification of the finite-rate endpoint
region is claimed.}

\entry{Galled trees by gall count: the cube-root crossover, positive density and the near-maximal endpoint}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a399421-galled-trees}
{article.pdf}{batch~77: galled-density-reproducibility.zip + galled-crossover-reproducibility.zip + galled-endpoint-report.zip (the earlier edition galled-crossover-reproducibility (1).zip superseded, not printed)}
{For $g(n,k)$, the rooted binary unlabeled simplex time-consistent galled
trees with $n$ leaves and $k$ galls (OEIS A399421, row sums A397952), proves
expansions to every fixed order in three disjoint regimes.  Part~I: for
$k\asymp n^{1/3}$ the fixed-$k$ asymptotic acquires the factor
$\exp(-2\gamma_0k^{3/2}/\sqrt n)$, $\gamma_0=1.1300337163\ldots$, with a
series in $n^{-1/3}$ and an explicit first correction.  Part~II: for $k/n$ in
