compact subsets of $(0,1/2)$, a saddle expansion in $1/n$, a central limit
theorem with mean $0.19741431\,n$ and variance $0.05548588\,n$, local and
large-deviation laws and integer inverse brackets; it records that the
inspected versions of Agranat-Tamir et al.\ print $\gamma\approx0.3846$ where
their defining equation gives $0.3835758866\ldots$, the value credited on
OEIS to Kot\v{e}\v{s}ovec.  Part~III: for $n-2k-1\asymp\sqrt n$, an
expansion in $n^{-1/2}$ whose factor two comes from the parity of two moving
square-root singularities.  The generating function and the fixed-$k$ and
scalar leading terms are prior work, the cube-root mechanism is Fuchs and
Hao~Yu's for leaf-labeled classes, matching the regimes stays open, and the
inverses are instances of the transseries volume.  \emph{Merged from three
manuscripts of one workspace, one per regime, with no theorem proved twice;
the common model is printed once.}  Five heavy triangle files are not
shipped; the README regenerates them.}

\entry{Airy amplitudes for relaxed trees, compacted trees and finite-language automata}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a082161-airy-amplitudes}
{article.pdf}{batch~77: relaxed-binary-trees-reproducibility.zip + compacted-binary-trees-reproducibility.zip + fixed-arity-airy-package.zip + ternary-airy-amplitudes-result.zip + larger-alphabet-automata-report.zip + oeis-dfa-finite-language-report.zip; batch~102: A082161\_Positive\_Airy\_Amplitude\_and\_All\_Orders\_Source.zip (bundle Report~100) + A254789\_Positive\_Airy\_Amplitude\_and\_All\_Orders\_Source.zip (bundle Report~101) + A331120\_Minimal\_Binary\_Automata\_All\_Orders\_Source.zip (bundle Report~103)}
{Relaxed trees, compacted trees and minimal automata of finite languages
satisfy one triangular recurrence family indexed by the arity $k$; their
growth orders are due to Elvey Price, Fang and Wallner and to Ghosh Dastidar
and Wallner, and the existence of a limiting amplitude was open.  Proves
positive amplitudes and Poincar\'e expansions to every fixed order in
$n^{-1/3}$.  Part~I (A082161):
$a_n=\gamma\,n!\,4^ne^{3zn^{1/3}}n\,(1+\sum_kc_kn^{-k/3})$, $z$ the largest
Airy zero, with three explicit logarithmic corrections.  Part~II (A254789):
the same with $n^{3/4}$, exact positive renewal models, and cancellation of
the $n^{-1/3}$ term in the logarithm of the compacted-to-relaxed ratio.
Part~III: every fixed $k\ge3$ and all three models at scale
$(n!)^{k-1}(k^k/(k-1)^{k-1})^ne^{\gamma n^{1/3}}n^{(2k-1)/3}$, with common
first two corrections, first model-dependent defects of order $n^{-(k-2)}$,
eventual decrease of the normalized ratios and a cancellation identity; its
ternary section, the base proof, adds the third coefficient, and its
comparison section bounds the automaton-to-relaxed ratio below by
$\prod_{j=2}^n(1-1/(2j^{k-1}))\ge(2\sqrt2/\pi)\sin(\pi/\sqrt2)$, uniformly
in $k$ and $n$.  Part~IV (A331120): binary automata, with $8^n$ and
$n^{7/8}$.  No amplitude is evaluated, the expansions are not uniform in $k$, the
Lambert-$W$ inverses are instances of the transseries volume, and the
sources' claim that Ghosh Dastidar--Wallner use a false bound $U\le q$ was
not checked against the printed paper.  \emph{Merged from six manuscripts:
the ternary one is the $k=3$ case and base proof of the arity one, the
comparison one the earliest form of its comparison step, and the automaton
one repeats the compacted one's method and Jacobi appendix, printed once.}
Part~V (batch~102), from bundle Reports~100, 101 and~103 (relaxed,
compacted, automata), which knew none of Parts~I--IV, proves their binary
theorems a second time in even time, by a positive semidefinite two-step
Jacobi product, lazy-walk smoothing and three-lag signed memory.  New:
Report~100's Lemma~72.1 proves the relaxed amplitude positive from the
exact recurrence alone, without the published lower bound every earlier
Part uses (the write's Remark~66.1 applies it to Part~I); the endpoint
error $O(n^{-2/3})$; the fourth to sixth relaxed and compacted
coefficients, with $r_n/(4nr_{n-1})$ and, by the write's Corollary~66.3,
$c_n/(4nc_{n-1})$ through $n^{-3}$; for automata $d_4$, $c_4$,
$\sigma_7$, the ratio through $n^{-7/3}$ and an explicit $X^{-1/3}$
inverse term.  The independent check stated two dependencies of
Corollary~66.3: Report~100's exact certificate suite and, through Part~II,
the published compacted lower bound.  The amplitude values
$166.952\ldots$, $173.127\ldots$ and $76.438$ are uncertified, Report~103's
remark on a normalization in the AofA~2020 paper was not checked, and
Reports~100 and~101 carry the author line ``Research prepared for private
review''.  Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{Airy asymptotics for tree-child networks}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a213863-tree-child-networks}
{article.pdf}{batch~77: d-combining-asymptotics-reproducibility.zip + tree-child-asymptotics-reproducibility.zip; batch~102: Fixed\_Degree\_Tree\_Child\_Networks\_All\_Orders\_Source.zip (bundle Report~104) + A213863\_Tableaux\_and\_Tree\_Child\_Networks\_Source.zip (bundle Report~102)}
{For maximally reticulated $d$-combining tree-child networks and their word
diagonal $a_n$ (OEIS A213863 at $d=2$), proves for every fixed $d\ge2$ that
$a_n=\gamma_d(n!)^{d-1}\Gamma^ne^{Bn^{1/3}}n^{\rho}(1+\sum_rC_{d,r}n^{-r/3})$
to every finite order, with $\gamma_d>0$ defined by convergent limits,
$\Gamma=4(d+1)^{d-1}/(d-1)!$, $B=3z((d-1)/(d+1))^{2/3}$, $z$ the largest
Airy zero, and $\rho$ explicit, by a contractive Jacobi reduction and
polynomial--Airy profiles; ternary coefficients are given.  Part~II, for
$d=2$: explicit coefficients; every order for the total number of binary
tree-child networks, $TC_n\sim(\sqrt e\,\gamma/12)(n!)^212^ne^{Bn^{1/3}}n^{-5/3}$,
through the Pons--Batle identity proved by Lin, Liu, Liu, Liu and Xin;
weighted all-orders expansions of the reticulation deficit around
Poisson$(1/2)$; an exact eventual total-variation identity with sharp rate
$|B|n^{-2/3}/(48\sqrt e)$; explicit inverses with integer brackets, instances
of the transseries volume.  In Parts~I--II the total count for $d\ge3$ has
only its leading term, and no amplitude is evaluated or certified.  \emph{Merged from two
manuscripts with one proof skeleton: the binary manuscript's own proof of
the case $d=2$ is kept as a second route, and four identical displays are
printed once.}  Parts~III and~IV (batch~102), from bundle Reports~104
(every fixed $d$) and~102 ($d=2$), closed before the sources of Parts~I--II
arrived, re-prove the main theorem independently, in an exact gamma-product
gauge with a new convergent amplitude formula and in the factorial gauge
$3^pp!$; they are credited as third and fourth routes, and their priority
sentences are qualified.  New for every $d$: $C_{d,1}=B^2(8(d+1)^2-27(d-1)^2)/(810(d-1)^2)$,
negative exactly for $d\ge4$, and $C_{d,2}$ in closed form; at $d=3$ the
coefficient $C_{3,3}$ and, resting on Report~104's reading of a published
proof (Chang et al., Lemma~3.24 and Theorem~1.9(ii)) that is not shipped
and was not checked, the total-to-maximal ratio $1+O(n^{-1/2})$ and the
first total-count correction; the $d=4$ diagonal $1,1,91,51821$ and
the trap that A213864 is not the $d=3$ sequence.  New for $d=2$: the fifth
and sixth logarithmic coefficients $L_5=B(108-B^3)/8748$ and
$L_6=2B^3/2187-1/162$, confirmed by Part~II's generator at order nine and,
at the independent check, by exact counts to $n=7000$; the ratio
$a_n/(12na_{n-1})$ through $n^{-3}$; and an $O(n^{-2/3})$ ratio and
total-variation rate from published lemmas alone, without the Pons--Batle
identity, weaker than Part~II and kept as a second route.  Fitted
amplitudes $\gamma_2,\ldots,\gamma_5$ are uncertified diagnostics.}

\entry{Historic tree asymptotics and a high-order obstruction}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a333497-historic-trees}
{article.pdf}{batch~77: historic-tree-report.zip; batch~103: A333497\_B\_Tree\_Histories\_All\_Orders\_and\_Inversion\_Source.zip (bundle Report~113; Part~II) + A336009\_B\_Tree\_Histories\_Transseries\_and\_Non\_D\_Finiteness\_Source.zip (bundle Report~115; Part~III) + B\_Tree\_Order\_59\_Counterexample\_to\_the\_Universal\_Asymptotic\_Conjecture\_Source.zip (bundle Report~117; Part~IV)}
{For reduced 5-historic trees (OEIS A333497), the exponential coefficients
$h_n$ of the solution of $H'''=H^2$, $H(0)=H'(0)=H''(0)=1$, proves a unique
positive blowup point $\rho$, $180^{1/4}\le\rho\le60^{1/3}$, and an
expansion of $h_n/(30(n+2)!\rho^{-n-3})$ to every fixed grade with exact
Gamma ratios, oscillating in $\log n$ through the exponent
$\lambda=(13+i\sqrt{71})/2$: the relative error is
$2|K|n^{-13/2}\cos(\arg K-\tfrac12\sqrt{71}\log n)+O(n^{-15/2})$ with
$K\ne0$, so it changes sign infinitely often.  The same for A336009
($G''''=G^2$), with the exact real-mode coefficient $-1/18052070400$ from a
conserved energy.  Main result: the factorial-pole asymptotic of Burghart
and Wagner's Conjecture~1 fails for every ODE order $r\ge30$ (conjecture
parameter $m=r-1\ge29$), through an unstable conjugate pair (an exact
rational bridge for $30\le r\le37$), a sign-variation cone, strict
sign-regularity and a generalized stable manifold.  The orders
$4\le m\le28$ and Conjecture~2 stay open, no replacement asymptotic is
given, and the fitted constants are not certified; the manuscript also
reads the journal's Table~1 as listing the radius under its reciprocal's
label.  The Lambert-$W$ start and two-ceiling window of the inverse are
instances of the transseries volume.  Parts~II--IV (batch~103), three
manuscripts written without sight of Part~I, prove its order-five
($m=2$), order-seven ($m=3$) and order-59 ($m=29$, $r=30$) results again by
other routes (an explicit convergent invariant surface; a scalar majorant
reaching $z=0$; a cyclic sign-change cone invariant under Euler steps and the
complete spectrum at $r=30$ certified by exact inequalities), printed as
marked second routes, their abstracts' novelty claims with dated notes.  New:
Part~III proves that the exponential generating functions of A333497 and
A336009 are not D-finite, so neither sequence is P-recursive (windings about
the dominant singularity give infinitely many independent branches); the
bounds $16800^{1/6}\le\rho_4\le840^{1/4}$; a blowup enclosure for every
order; explicit polydisk radii $1183/960$ and $121/6720$ of the local series
(a radius in $t$ needs a bound on $|C|$, which none gives).  The write
credits Mallet-Paret--Smith (1990) for the cyclic sign-change count and
proves that the cyclic cone equals Part~I's, so Part~IV's lemma replaces
one of Part~I's two uses of the strong-$k$-positivity theorem; the
independent check narrowed both statements (the other use, and signed
couplings).  Nothing was found wrong.  Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{Asymptotic expansions of the Takeuchi numbers}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a000651-takeuchi-numbers}
{article.pdf}{batch~77: takeuchi-asymptotics-research.zip}
{For the Takeuchi numbers $T_n$ (OEIS A000651) and $w=W(n)$, proves
$\log T_n=e^w(w^2-w+1)+w^2/2-\tfrac12\log(1+w)+\log C+p_1(w)/n+p_2(w)/n^2
+O((1+w)^8/n^3)$ with explicit rational $p_1,p_2$ and $0<C<\infty$, without
assuming the leading equivalent, which is Prellberg's (announced 2002);
then every fixed order, each $p_j$ by a finite exact algorithm and one
convergent quadrature, and an explicit $p_3\sim w^4/72$, so $p_j=O(w^j)$
fails at $j=3$.  The transfer is a quantitative stability lemma for
descending Markov chains with jumps close to $1+\mathrm{Pois}(W(r))$.  Also
the Takeuchi-minus-Bell coefficients, and real and integer inverses that
are instances of the transseries volume.  The manuscript states that the
general theorem printed in Prellberg's 2002 slides omits a restriction
($f=z/(1-z)$, $a=z$, $b=1$ gives $B_{n-1}$, not a multiple of $B_n$),
without effect on the Takeuchi case; \dir{a139383-iterated-bell-diagonals}
makes the same check, printed only here.  $C$ is not enclosed, and
effective constants and optimal truncation are open.}

\entry{Rooted identity trees with fixed maximum outdegree}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a116379-bounded-identity-trees}
{article.pdf}{batch~77: rooted-identity-trees-source.zip; batch~103: A116379\_A116380\_All\_Orders\_Identity\_Trees\_and\_Inversion\_Source.zip (bundle Report~130; Part~II)}
{For rooted unlabeled identity trees with at most $d$ children at each
vertex (OEIS A116379 for $d=3$, A116380 for $d=4$, whose leading asymptotic
OEIS records as unknown), proves for every fixed $d\ge2$ the square-root law
$a_{d,n}=C\rho^{-n}n^{-3/2}(\sum_{j\le R}D_jn^{-j}+O(n^{-R-1}))$ to every
fixed order, with $C>0$ and explicit $D_1,D_2$, from the signed root
equation: $1/4\le\rho\le9/20$, a transversality bound with the constant
$1-810/(319\sqrt{19})$ independent of $d$, a characteristic point and a
unique dominant singularity.  Tabulates $\rho,C,D_1,D_2$ for $d=3,4$ to 30
decimals, uncertified as delivered and certified in Part~II
($\rho_3=0.40077450047\ldots$,
$C_3=0.41431687066\ldots$) and inverts a specified finite model by
$W_{-1}$, an instance of the transseries volume.  Nothing is uniform in
$d$; the derivative cancellation is Bell, Burris and Yeats's, and no
priority is claimed, since Meir--Moon--Mycielski (1983) and
Haigh--Kennedy--Quintas (1984) were not read in full.  Part~II
(batch~103), from a manuscript written without knowledge of Part~I, encloses
$\rho$, $\tau$, $C$, $D_1$, $D_2$, $\lambda=\log(1/\rho)$,
$b=-\frac32\log\lambda-\log C$ and the Puiseux coefficients $\beta_1,\ldots,\beta_5$ for $d=3,4$ by a
rational-interval computation (outward rounding at denominator $10^{90}$;
radius brackets of width $10^{-45}$, the others at most $1.4\cdot10^{-41}$),
so every entry of Part~I's table is the correctly rounded value; this
answers half of Part~I's first further question ($D_j$ for $j\ge3$, other
caps and effective thresholds stay open).  The certificate is
computer-assisted, specialized to $d=3,4$, and uses the qualitative theorem
to identify the radius.  Also a second proof of Part~I's theorem, an
explicit binary family giving $\rho_d<9/20$ strictly, a rational recurrence
for the Gamma-ratio coefficients (proved equal to Part~I's at the write) and
an exact threshold algorithm.  The manuscript credits Haigh--Kennedy--Quintas
1984 to ``G. W. Furnas'' (corrected at the write); the independent check
withdrew a criticism the write had made of one of its proofs and corrected
one digit of the write.  Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{Tournament score sequences and weighted component asymptotics}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a000571-tournament-score-sequences}
{article.pdf}{batch~77: tournament-score-sequences-source.zip; batch~85: tournament\_coexistence\_research.zip (Part~II); batch~98: tournament\_supercritical.zip (Part~III)}
{Refines Kolesnik's leading asymptotics for the score sequences of
tournaments (OEIS A000571) and of strong tournaments (A351822), from the
generating function $\exp A(z)$ of
Claesson--Dukes--Frankl\'{\i}n--Stef\'ansson: to every fixed order,
$T_n^\pm=(e^{\pm\lambda}/(2\sqrt\pi))\,4^nn^{-5/2}(1+c_1^\pm/n+\cdots)$ with
$\lambda=A(1/4)=0.33023754398\ldots$, $c_1^\pm=-1/8\pm5\mu/2$,
$\mu=A'(1/4)/4=0.67395260185\ldots$ and explicit $c_2^\pm$.  For the count
weighted by $u^m$, $m$ the number of strong blocks: fixed subcritical
weights; at the critical weight an expansion in $n^{-1/2}$ with no $n^{-1}$
term; uniformly on a logarithmic window above it a half-power expansion
with Kummer-function coefficients; and at
$\tau=\tfrac12\log n+2\log\log n+\log(\mu\sqrt\pi/2)+s$ a coexistence
profile proportional to $1+e^{-s}$.  Inverses of specified models are
instances of the transseries volume.  Part~I claims nothing for $\tau<0$ or
for growing orders, and the renewal mechanisms are credited to Blanchet
and Glynn.  Part~II (batch~85) answers the report's first open question,
uniformly for $s$ in compact sets: at that weight the score sequence is asymptotically a logistic mixture,
with probability $1/(1+e^{-s})$ of one block of size $n-O_P(n/\log n)$
(residual strings within total variation $O(1/\log n)$ of two geometric
renewal strings, Gamma$(2,1)$ count and deficit, stable residual
fluctuations, a mixed Fr\'echet second maximum, every fixed
inverse-logarithmic correction) and otherwise $n/a_c+O_P(n^{2/3})$ blocks
with a $3/2$-stable count and a Fr\'echet largest block; the giant
probability converges at rate $O(1/\log n)$ around a Lambert-$W_{-1}$
centre, an instance of the transseries volume.  Its coefficient input
re-proves Part~I's coexistence theorem (printed as a note); the
Gibbs-partition and big-jump mechanisms are credited to Stufler and
others.  Part~III (batch~98) answers Part~I's second question, the
supercritical crossover, on a fixed small interval of weights $u\ge u_c$
with excess growth rate $0\le t\le t_0$ ($t_0$ not effective): an exact
change of measure to a tilted renewal, a pole--branch expansion of the
weighted count to every fixed order uniformly on that interval, through the
coalescence $nt=O(1)$ of the moving pole with the branch point, with a new
$n^{-3/2}$ coefficient at the critical weight; tempered $3/2$-stable block
counts on the scale $tn^{2/3}$ and Gaussian counts beyond it, tempered
Poisson large blocks, a Gumbel maximum with a Lambert-$W_0$ centre (an
instance of the transseries volume) and a convergent Puiseux inverse.  The
write proved the manuscript's unproved claim that no continuous Gumbel
limit exists at fixed $t>0$.  The $t=0$ stable count law is the
critical-composition (map-Airy) law of Banderier, Flajolet, Schaeffer and
Soria and is not claimed new; expansions are fixed-order, the count and
maximum theorems marginal, two sketched lemmas are further questions, and
the joint theory across $\tau=0$ stays open.  Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{Fixed forbidden distances in involutions}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a239144-forbidden-distance-involutions}
{article.pdf}{batch~77: fixed-forbidden-distance-involutions.zip + involution-refinements-attribution-revision.zip (its earlier edition involution-refinements-reproducibility.zip superseded, not printed)}
{For involutions of $[n]$ avoiding the transpositions of a forbidden graph
of fixed maximum degree, proves a uniform complex probability-generating
function expansion to every fixed order in $n^{-1/2}$, with coefficients
evaluated at the graph's own matching-root moments, and an exact split of
the avoidance count into two Gaussian sectors of relative weights
$e^{\pm\sqrt n}$, each with its own expansion.  For a fixed finite set of
forbidden distances the local statistics are eventually exactly affine in
$1/n$, so the columns of OEIS A239144 have constant coefficients:
$A_{n,r}/I_n=e^{-r}(1+r/\sqrt n-r/(2n)-r(8r^2-12r+3)/(24n^{3/2})+O(n^{-2}))$.
Section~9 holds the second manuscript's case $r=1$: the all-orders law of
the number of adjacent transpositions (A217876; zero column A170941), its
total-variation distance from Poisson(1), a second proof by growing moments
and a Touchard transform, a Chebyshev sector decomposition with sector ratio
$(-1)^ne^{-2\sqrt n}(1-31/(12\sqrt n)+O(1/n))$, and coefficients through
order eight.  \emph{Merged: the second manuscript proves the case $r=1$
with the same coefficients $31/24$, $-55/48$, $1717/1920$; its inverse
statements are given by pointer.}  The Gaussian matching identity and the
root-moment method are prior work (Godsil, Godsil--McKay, McLeod), the
zero-count limit is Ganjtabesh--Steyaert's, the Poisson law Li's and $31/24$
Kot\v{e}\v{s}ovec's.  The inverses are instances of the transseries volumes.}

\entry{All fixed order asymptotics for distinct parabolic double cosets}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a260700-parabolic-double-cosets}
{article.pdf}{batch~77: double-coset-report.zip}
{Let $p_n$ count the distinct subsets of $S_n$ that are double cosets of
standard parabolic subgroups (OEIS A260700).  Starting from Browning's exact
enumeration, proves
$p_n=Kn!\,\rho^{-2n}(\sum_{m\le M}B_m(\rho)n^{-m}+O(n^{-M-1}))$ for every
fixed $M$, with $\rho=\log2$, $K=e^{-\rho^2/2}/(4\rho^2)$ and
$B_1(r)=r^2/2-r^3-11r^4/12+r^5/4$; $B_2$, $B_3$, $B_4$ are explicit.  The
first correction proves Browning's higher-order conjecture with
$c=-KB_1(\rho)=0.10819705289\ldots>0$, the sign proved without a numerical
test.  The tools are uniform generalized-Fubini pole extraction, summable
bounds on the signed inner defects and a joint bound for the omitted
long-cycle configurations of the outer Stirling transform, with a finite
exact coefficient algorithm.  The leading equivalent is Browning's theorem,
novelty rests on a bounded search, and five questions stay open.  Smooth
Lambert-$W$ inverses and an eventual two-ceiling bracket for the integer
threshold are instances of the transseries volume; the report keeps the
counterexample $y=p_{25}+1$ to the naive rounding
$N(y)=\lceil x_1(y)\rceil$.  A companion of the A260952 report below;
neither uses the other.}

\entry{Full support signed permutations and their asymptotic coefficients}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a260952-full-support-signed-permutations}
{article.pdf}{batch~77: Coxeter\_Full\_Support\_Asymptotics.zip}
{Proves Kot\v{e}\v{s}ovec's 2015 conjecture recorded in OEIS A260952,
$a_m\sim-2^{m+1}m!/(9(\log3)^{m+1})$, with the corrections
$1-2\tau/m-9\tau^2/(4(m)_2)-27\tau^3/(2(m)_3)-1755\tau^4/(16(m)_4)+O(m^{-5})$,
$\tau=\log3$, every further fixed order by a finite formula, and $a_m<0$
for all $m\ge1$.  The $a_m$ are the asymptotic coefficients of the
full-support elements of the Weyl groups of types~$B$ and~$D$ (A109253,
A112225); they are written as a positive Stirling transform whose late
expansion holds to every fixed order on the scale $\log(1+q)$, uniformly in
$q$.  The two exact endpoint sums of the signed-permutation model have
separate all-order expansions for $1/4<|q|<4$ (not extended to $q\ge4$),
which give an all-order sparse-sign crossover: the number of negative
entries tends to a Poisson law conditioned to be positive.  The models are
Bergeron--Hohlweg--Zabrocki's and the Stirling-transform construction
Martin--Kearney's.  Lambert-$W$ growth-index inverses with a stated rounding
width are instances of the transseries volume.  A companion of the A260700
report above; neither uses the other.}

\entry{Ordered long-jump rook paths}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a227578-ordered-rook-paths}
{article.pdf}{batch~77: ordered-rook-reproducibility.zip}
{Let $A(n,k)$ count the paths from $(n,\dots,n)$ to the origin that lower
one coordinate by any positive amount at each step and stay weakly ordered
(OEIS A227578; columns A059231, A227580, A227583, A227596).  For every
$k\ge2$ the report proves an exact coefficient identity with a squared
Vandermonde numerator, so each column is D-finite (no posted recurrence is
certified), and, by the Raichev--Wilson smooth-point theorem,
$A(n,k)=C_k(k+1)^{kn}n^{-(k^2-1)/2}(\sum_{j<M}d_j(k)n^{-j}+O(n^{-M}))$ for
every $M$, with $C_k$ in closed form through a Mehta-type Gaussian integral,
$d_1(k)=-(k-1)(k+1)(2k^4+8k^3+9k^2+6k+12)/(12k(k+2)^2)$ and $d_2(k)$ in
closed form; every $d_j(k)$ comes from a finite generator and is a rational
function of $k$ with poles only at $0,-1,-2$.  This proves the general
fixed-$k$ growth that OEIS records as a conjecture; the leading constants
for $k\le5$ were already recorded there by Kot\v{e}\v{s}ovec, and the
methods are classical.  The $W_{-1}$ inverse and the two-ceiling threshold
enclosure are instances of the transseries volume.  ``Rook'' means a move
here, not a rook polynomial.}

\entry{Regular cyclic word covers}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a108242-regular-cyclic-word-covers}
{article.pdf}{batch~77: regular-cyclic-word-covers-reproducibility.zip}
{Counts sets and multisets of rotation classes of words of fixed length
$\ell\ge3$ over an $n$-letter alphabet in which every letter occurs exactly
$r$ times.  An exact slot-partition identity gives, for fixed degree, the
ratio to the carrier $(rn)!/(\ell^{rn/\ell}(rn/\ell)!\,(r!)^n)$ to every
order in $1/n$, with rational coefficients and absolute remainder control.
For $\ell=3$ it identifies OEIS A108242, A110105, A110104 and A110106
(following the examples and Mishna's source where OEIS titles disagree with
the parameters), with corrections through $n^{-5}$, such as
$1+8/(9n)+104/(81n^2)+\cdots$, and the corrections each posted carrier
induces.  At the critical degree $r=\lambda n^{(\ell-2)/2}$ it proves an
expansion to every fixed order in $n^{-1/2}$, uniform on compact positive
$\lambda$-intervals, with multiplier
$\exp(s\lambda^2/(2\ell)+\mathbf{1}_{2\mid\ell}\,\lambda/\ell)$, $s=\pm1$;
the even-length term comes from half-turn symmetry.  The leading
equivalents were already posted by Kot\v{e}\v{s}ovec, and the literature
comparison is an applicability screen, not a priority claim.  A $W_0$
inverse with a lattice threshold enclosure is an instance of the
transseries volume.  The open cyclic-words question of the A330266 report
concerns other objects and is not answered.}

\entry{Unitary divisor sum colored partitions and their asymptotics}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a301981-unitary-divisor-partitions}
{article.pdf}{batch~77: unitary-partition-report.zip; batch~86: oeis\_advances.zip (its Section~7)}
{For the products $\prod(1-z^m)^{-\sigma^*(m)}$ (OEIS A301981) and
$\prod(1+z^m)^{\sigma^*(m)}$ (A301982), $\sigma^*$ the unitary divisor sum,
proves that the stretched-exponential equivalents conjectured in both OEIS
entries are false: the ratio of coefficient to model does not eventually
stay in any compact subinterval of $(0,\infty)$, by a least-height
zeta-zero noncancellation (the Mellin kernel has denominator
$\zeta(2s-1)$) and an Abelian transfer.  Unconditionally
$\log a_n=c\,n^{2/3}+o(n^{1/3})$ with $c=3(A/4)^{1/3}$, $A=\pi^2/6$ for
A301981 and $\pi^2/8$ for A301982, and the error is not
$O(n^{1/4-\varepsilon})$; for each sequence separately, an error
$O_\varepsilon(n^{1/4+\varepsilon})$ for every $\varepsilon>0$ is
equivalent to the Riemann hypothesis, which is neither proved nor
disproved.  Also a corrected equivalent at the explicit point
$(2A/n)^{1/3}$ (without a rate), all-fixed-order exact-saddle and
off-center Hermite expansions, and two-ceiling threshold brackets whose
integer step is an instance of the transseries volume.  Part~I left open
which one-sided divergence occurs; Part~II (batch~86, from one section of
a three-subject manuscript) proves that both do: by Landau's positivity
principle with the surviving pole's order $m$ and leading Laurent
coefficient, the ratio to the refuted model has $\liminf a_n/m_n=0$ and $\limsup a_n/m_n=\infty$ for both
products, with $\log(a_n/m_n)$ beyond $\pm K_\pm n^{\sigma_*/3}(\log n)^{m-1}$
infinitely often, hence $\Omega_\pm(n^{1/4})$ (exact under the Riemann
hypothesis, with Part~I), without the Riemann hypothesis or simple zeros;
the inverse of the pure model errs by $\Omega_\pm((\log y)^{7/8})$.  The
refutation concerns OEIS text, not a repository claim, and no OEIS
submission is implied.}

\entry{Log-periodic factors in radix-layer partition asymptotics}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a174065-radix-layer-partitions}
{article.pdf}{batch~77: radix-partition-reproducibility.zip}
{For $b\ge2$ let $a_b(n)=[z^n]\prod_{j\ge0,\,i\ge1}(1+z^{ib^j})$; for
$b=2^m$ it counts partitions into parts $k$ with $v_2(k)\equiv0\pmod m$:
ordinary partitions at $b=2$, OEIS A174065 at $b=4$, A393565 at $b=8$.  The
report proves an expansion of $a_b(n)$ to every fixed order against
$KN^{-\kappa}e^{2\sqrt{AN}}$, with explicit differential operators acting
on a positive analytic $1$-periodic function $H$ of $\log_b\sqrt{N/A}$, and
shows by the zero-free line of $\zeta$ that $H$ is nonconstant for every
$b>2$.  Hence the equivalents recorded in A174065 and A393565 have correct
smooth constants but omit a persistent log-periodic factor; a separate
Abelian argument excludes every constant-only equivalent, and the
subsequential ratios fill exactly $[\min H,\max H]$, with
$(\min H)(\max H)=1$ from an exact radial reciprocity.  Also eventual
strict increase and log-concavity, and a two-ceiling threshold enclosure
whose integer step is an instance of the transseries volume.  The methods
are classical Mellin analysis (Flajolet--Gourdon--Dumas, Madritsch--Wagner).
The correction concerns OEIS text, not a repository claim, and no OEIS edit
was made.  The mechanism, but no theorem, is shared with the A033552
report.}

\entry{Full asymptotic expansions for partition sums of acyclic orientations}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a372395-acyclic-orientation-partitions}
{article.pdf}{batch~77: orientation-partition-asymptotics.zip}
{OEIS A372395 and A370613 sum the numbers of acyclic orientations of the
complete multipartite graphs $K_\lambda$ over the partitions $\lambda$ of
$n$, unrestricted or into distinct parts.  The report proves
$B_\pm(n)=A_\pm n!\,n^{-p_\pm}e^{C_\pm\sqrt n}(\sum_{j\le J}a_{\pm,j}n^{-j/2}+O(n^{-(J+1)/2}))$
for every $J$, with $p_+=1$, $p_-=3/4$, explicit amplitudes including a
tilted Gamma-integral factor, and $a_{+,1}=-0.30756533\ldots$,
$a_{-,1}=0.23095021\ldots$; this answers the question of Sun
(arXiv:2605.04006), to whom the exact Gamma representation and the
constants $C_\pm$ are due.  A uniform quadratic majorant controls the
signed representation; a finite Gaussian-expectation algorithm gives every
coefficient, and a second short route gives $a_{\pm,1}$.  A corollary
extends the expansion to the sums of $(-1)^{|V|}\chi_{K_\lambda}(-v)$ for
fixed $v>0$, with $\Gamma(n+v)/\Gamma(v)$ in place of $n!$.  The expansions
are Poincar\'e expansions, not transseries, and four questions stay open.
The Lambert-$W$ inverse with its integer-threshold envelope is an instance
of the transseries volumes.}

\entry{Sparse nonattacking chess placements}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a201513-sparse-chess-placements}
{article.pdf}{batch~77: Sparse\_Chess\_Asymptotics.zip}
{For $n$ nonattacking kings (OEIS A201513) or knights (A201540) on an
$n\times n$ board, measured against Kot\v{e}\v{s}ovec's common leading
equivalent $B_n=e^{-9/2}n^{2n}/n!$, proves rational expansions to every
fixed order, four corrections explicit:
$K_n/B_n=1+7/(3n)+119/(9n^2)+11327/(810n^3)+90475/(1944n^4)+O(n^{-5})$ and
$N_n/B_n=1+37/(3n)+914/(9n^2)+330917/(810n^3)+966583/(1944n^4)+O(n^{-5})$.
For any fixed finite symmetric move set $S$ the count is
$(n^{2n}/n!)\,e^{-(|S|+1)/2}$ times an all-orders series, also uniformly
for $k$ pieces with $k/n$ in a compact positive interval, and the first
correction is $c_1=B+1/3-\tau$ (boundary loss $B$, triangle density
$\tau$), which is why kings and knights share the leading term but not the
next; wazir (A201511) and fers (A201861) coefficients through order four
follow.  The cluster-expansion method is credited to
Davies--Jenssen--Perkins and Pulvirenti--Tsagkarogiannis.  The coefficients
depend on the carrier: against the Stirling-expanded carrier the first
corrections are $9/4$ and $49/4$.  The Lambert-$W$ growth-index inverse and
its rounding qualification are instances of the transseries volume.}

\entry{Asymptotic enumeration of tied football seasons}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a380592-tied-football-seasons}
{article.pdf}{batch~77: tied-football-report.zip}
{In a double round-robin league of $n$ labelled teams with $3/1/0$ scoring,
$a(n)$ (OEIS A380592) counts the seasons in which every team finishes on
the same score.  The report proves $a(n)\sim B_n\Theta(c_n)$ with
$B_n=3^{n(n-1)}n^{-1/2}(9/(56\pi n))^{(n-1)/2}e^{-9829/65856}$,
$c_n=8(n-1)/3-13/84$ and the positive $1$-periodic theta function
$\Theta(c)=\sum_je^{-4\pi^2j^2/9}e^{2\pi ijc}$, so the multiplier depends
on $n\bmod3$ and cannot be replaced by~$1$.  The proof uses mean-matching
exponential tilts and a sharp global Fourier localization: a negative
quartic gives an exact Gaussian majorant and an outlier bound handles the
complement.  On each residue class the conditioned common score tends to a
discrete Gaussian centred at $c_n$, which is not its conditional mean.
After checking the hypotheses of Isaev's complex cumulant theorem
(arXiv:2508.16952), the external input, it gives every fixed order in $1/n$
with a finite exact algorithm and the first correction.  The
residue-aware inverse threshold enclosures need no Lambert function, the
phase being quadratic, and are instances of the transseries volume's
reversion and staircase theorems.  The model and its exact counts are
Jehn--Habermann--Lavrov's.}

\entry{All-orders asymptotics and asymptotic inversion for A292507}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a292507-binomial-partition-transform}
{article.pdf}{batch~77: A292507\_report\_and\_code.zip}
{For $a_n=\sum_k\binom nk b(k)$, where $b(k)=p(k)-p(k-1)$ counts the
partitions of $k$ without parts~$1$ (OEIS A292507), proves an all-orders
Poincar\'e expansion
$a_n=C\,2^nn^{-3/2}e^{\beta\sqrt n}\bigl(\sum_{j\le M}c_jn^{-j/2}+O(n^{-(M+1)/2})\bigr)$
with $\beta=\pi/\sqrt3$ and $C=(\pi/6)e^{\pi^2/24}$, an additive form of
Kot\v{e}\v{s}ovec's logarithmic conjecture in the entry; $c_1,c_2,c_3$ are in
closed form, and a finite Gaussian-moment algorithm gives every $c_j$
($c_1,\ldots,c_6$ computed).  Also an all-orders reversion of the real model
in $X^{-1/2}$ and $\log X$ with a residual bound, and shrinking
integer-threshold brackets with existence constants only, not certified
thresholds; these are instances of the transseries volume's Lambert,
perturbed-inversion and staircase theorems and are cited, not claimed.  The
only analytic input is Nemes' explicit partition-remainder estimate.  The
leading binomial-smoothing mechanism is prior art (A218481), the coefficient
formula is Gutkovskiy's, and no publication priority is claimed; nor is
convergence of the correction series or a complete transseries.}

\entry{Permutations avoiding the vincular pattern 12--34 (A113226)}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a113226-vincular-avoiders}
{article.pdf}{batch~77: a113226-asymptotics.zip + a113226-refined-addendum.zip; batch~101: Exact\_Vincular\_Avoidance\_Asymptotics\_Source.zip (bundle Report~87, Part~III), Canonical\_Vincular\_Exponential\_Sectors\_Source.zip (bundle Report~94, Part~IV)}
{For the permutations avoiding the vincular pattern $12$--$34$ (OEIS
A113226), equivalently Bevan--Cheon--Kitaev's $\{3,2+2\}$-free naturally
labelled posets, Part~I derives the closed EGF
$H(z)=\exp\{z/2+q(3\arcsin q-\pi/2)/\sqrt{1-q^2}\}$, $q=e^{z/2}/2$,
identifies the cumulants as A136127 shifted, and proves the
stretched-exponential form of Bevan--Cheon--Kitaev's Conjectures~13 and~14
with exact constants and to all orders:
$A_n\sim D\,n!\,\rho^{-n}e^{3cn^{1/3}}n^{-5/6}(1+\sum_ja_jn^{-j/3})$ with
$\rho=\log4$, $c=(\pi^2/(4\rho))^{1/3}$ and
$D=2e^{-3}\sqrt{c/(3\pi)}\approx0.0357$, where their fit gave about $0.032$;
$a_1,\ldots,a_4$ are exact.  Its Lambert-$W$ inverse is an instance of the
transseries volume.  Part~II, an addendum, marks the distinct strict
downsets and the isolated elements: a record-to-cycle bijection explaining
the exponential transform, a complex-uniform saddle expansion, all-orders
local large deviations for interior densities, Gaussian and local limit
laws, and a conditional Poisson law of isolated elements with
total-variation error $O(n^{-2/3})$.  Endpoint densities and certified
constants are not claimed; Part~II answers Part~I's questions~1--2 in part,
question~3 stays open, and question~4 is answered for every fixed sector by
Part~IV.  The two heavy exact tables are not shipped; the README
regenerates them.  Part~III (batch~101), a same-day independent answer to
Part~I's assignment written against an earlier pin, re-derives the EGF, the
recurrences, the A136127 identification and $a_1,a_2,a_3$ from the
permutations themselves by a diagonal-state route (printed as second
routes), gives integral forms of Elizalde's block functions, and proves
that the EGF is differentially algebraic but not D-finite, so A113226 is
not P-recursive.  Part~IV (batch~101), which continues Part~III, gives a
single-valued branch of period $4\pi i$ cut horizontally from every
$\zeta_k=\log4+2\pi ik$, the exact decomposition
$A_n/n!=\sum_kH_k(n)$ for $n\ge1$ into absolutely convergent full-cut
sectors, and a complete expansion
$H_k(n)=C_k\zeta_k^{-n}e^{3\kappa_kn^{1/3}}n^{-5/6}(\sum_jc_{k,j}n^{-j/3})$ of
each fixed sector, whose $k=0$ case is Part~I's theorem; nothing is uniform
in a growing $k$, and no transseries, resummation or certified sector value
is claimed.  The write proves four steps the sources assert, among them the
displayed two-term reversion of Part~III's inverse; the independent check
found one of its sentences false (successive reversion terms, unlike their
bounds, need not gain $X^{-2/3}/\log X$: the third term is of order
$X^{-1}(\log X)^{-4}$ and the fourth of order $X^{-5/3}(\log X)^{-4}$), corrected
with that counterexample in a dated note.  Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{Asymptotic expansions and inversion for A124380}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a124380-signed-moment-asymptotics}
{article.pdf}{batch~77: a124380-reproducibility.zip}
{For $\sum_na_nz^n=\sum_kz^k\prod_{j\le k}(1+jz)$ (OEIS A124380), proves an
exact decomposition $a_n=I_n+(-1)^nJ_n$ into a positive and a signed moment
integral, the multiplicative equivalent
$a_n\sim\tfrac12e^{1/2}x\exp\{x^2(2L-1)+x(L+1)+L^2/8\}$ with $x=\sqrt{n/2}$
and $L=\log x$, which is more than Kot\v{e}\v{s}ovec's logarithmic
conjecture in the entry, and every fixed-order relative expansion with
coefficients polynomial in~$L$.  The signed sector is exponentially smaller
and has its own all-orders oscillatory expansion, with the error measured
against its amplitude so that it stays valid near zeros of the sine, and
coefficients $q_j^-(L)=(-1)^jq_j(L-i\pi)$.  Also eventual strict
log-convexity, an all-orders inverse in $X=\sqrt{y/W(y/e)}$, an instance of
the transseries volume's inverse-Gamma core with no novelty claimed, and a
conditional certification rule for the integer threshold;
$T(y)=\lceil\nu(y)\rceil$ is not asserted.  The combinatorial models are
Chen--Fan--Zhao's and Cerbai--Claesson--Sagan's, no priority is claimed,
and four research questions stay open.}

\entry{Complete increasing subsequences in multiset permutations (A047909 and A268485)}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a047909-beta-renewal-subsequences}
{article.pdf}{batch~77: beta-renewal-asymptotics-reproducibility.zip + beta-renewal-uniform-reproducibility.zip}
{Studies the probability $p_m(k)=H(m,k)(m!)^k/(mk)!$ that a word with $m$
copies of each of $1,\ldots,k$ contains $12\cdots k$ (OEIS A047909, diagonal
A268485).  Its representation as the probability that $k$ independent
$\mathrm{Beta}(1,m)$ increments sum to at most~$1$, and the Gaussian limit,
are El Maazouz--Pitman's and are credited, not claimed.  Part~I proves an
expansion to every fixed order in the window $k=m+x\sqrt m$ with
$P_1,\ldots,P_4$ explicit ($P_1=(x^2+8)/6$); on the diagonal only odd
half-integer powers occur beyond the leading~$1/2$, with four corrections
computed.  It adds smooth quantile and Lambert-$W$ count inverses with
qualified integer brackets and all-orders rare-tail expansions for $k/m$ in compact subsets of $(0,1)$ or
$(1,\infty)$.  Part~II, an addendum, joins the regimes: relative error
$O(m^{-(R+1)/2})$ for both tails, uniformly on compact ranges of $k/m$ and
through $k=m+1$, recovering Part~I's coefficients by a second route, and
both real Lambert-$W$ branches for tail thresholds.  The inverses are instances of the transseries volume and the
tilting method is classical (Esscher, Cram\'er, Daniels).  Effective
constants, growing orders, ratios tending to $0$ or $\infty$, and certified
rounding remain open.}

\entry{Uniform asymptotics and moving zeros for the level-15 family of Cooper}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/cooper-level-15-moving-zeros}
{article.pdf}{batch~77: modular-crossover-replay.zip}
{For Cooper's level-15 polynomials $T_\varepsilon(n)$, of degree $n$ in the
parameter~$\varepsilon$, proves a two-singularity expansion with rates
$\varepsilon+11$ and $\varepsilon-1$, uniform to every fixed order on the
disc $|\varepsilon+5|\le1/10$, with an additive error that stays valid
where the two terms cancel; in the window $\varepsilon=-5+u/n$ the profile
is $e^{u/6}+10(-1)^ne^{-u/6}$.  Hence for every large odd $n$ there is
exactly one zero with $|u-3\log10|<1$, real and simple, at
$\varepsilon_n=-5+3\log(10)/n+(162/125-\tfrac92\log10)/n^2+O(n^{-3})$, and
no real zero in a bounded window for large even~$n$.  Explicit path, sheet
and connection proofs give all ten constants of Cooper's Table~8 (levels
11, 14, 15 and 24), labelled conjectured and determined numerically there,
including two negative-axis connections with exact eta multipliers.  The
formulas are Cooper's, the level-11 equivalent is Kot\v{e}\v{s}ovec's
(A284756) and the positive modular radial principle Guillera--Zudilin's.
Also a finite generator for every correction order and a qualified,
non-effective threshold inverse, an instance of the transseries volume.  No
historical priority and no global location of the zeros are claimed; the
OEIS numbers of five of the sequences were not verified.}

\entry{Moving fugacity asymptotics for distinct partitions}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a291698-moving-fugacity-partitions}
{article.pdf}{batch~77: moving-fugacity-reproducibility.zip}
{For $a_n(\alpha)=[q^n]\prod_{k\ge1}(1+n^\alpha q^k)$, distinct partitions
with one size-dependent weight $u=n^\alpha$ per part, proves, uniformly for
$\alpha$ in compact subsets of $(0,\infty)$, an all-orders principal Bessel
expansion with a finite generator for its coefficients and, through an
exact Jacobi decomposition, finitely many conjugate resonance sectors with
separated local integrals and sectorwise remainders.  This yields
the equivalents conjectured by Kot\v{e}\v{s}ovec for A291698 ($\alpha=1$)
and A292304 ($\alpha=2$), and shows that the
elementary Sommerfeld form is relatively valid exactly when
$\alpha\ge1/2$.  Also a specified monotone real continuation with a
Lambert-$W$ inverse and its first correction, a formal reversion generator
and an integer-threshold statement, instances of the transseries volume
with no novelty claimed.  The onset is slow: at $n=20{,}000$ the principal
term for $\alpha=2$ is still off by about $5.6\%$.  The sector count and
depth are fixed; no growing-sector theorem, convergence or explicit
constants are claimed, nor priority, and four questions stay open.}

\entry{Gamma constants and scaling limits for rounding extinction}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a082528-rounding-extinction}
{article.pdf}{batch~85: Rounding\_Extinction\_OEIS.zip}
{Starting from $x_1=n$ and rounding down to a multiple of $k^m$ at stages
$k=2,3,\ldots$, the first zero satisfies
$\tau_m(n)\sim(c_mn)^{1/(m+1)}$ with $c_m=m\Gamma(m/(m+1))^{m+1}$ for every
real $m>0$: Cloitre's conjecture in OEIS A082528, with the constants
$2\Gamma(2/3)^3$ and $3\Gamma(3/4)^4$ conjectured numerically for A082527
and A082528.  The case $m=1$ ($c_1=\pi$, the Tchoukaillon sieve A002491)
is Erd\H{o}s and Jabotinsky's and is credited, not claimed.  Also the
uniform limit of the whole extinction trajectory, the same law for weights
with $k(w_k/w_{k-1}-1)\to m$, rational product certificates for $c_m$, the
limiting distribution of rounding losses, the dependence on $m$, and a
block construction showing that regular variation alone does not fix the
constant.  No error term (the $O(1)$ recorded in A073047 is not proved), no
rate, nothing uniform in a moving $m$; Appendix~B is an OEIS update draft
that was not posted, and the OEIS terms the package uses are
CC~BY-SA~4.0 data.}

\entry{Matrix compositions: exact spectra, Hankel products and uniform asymptotics}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a261781-matrix-compositions}
{article.pdf}{batch~85: Matrix\_Compositions\_Uniform\_Asymptotics.zip (base) + OEIS\_Matrix\_Compositions\_Research.zip; batch~98: a261781\_hankel\_zero\_laws.zip (Part~III); batch~108: Packed\_Matrices\_Asymptotics\_Inverses\_and\_Joint\_Limits\_Source.zip (bundle Report~169, Part~IV)}
{For matrix compositions with $k$ rows (OEIS A261780, A261781) and the
diagonal A261784, Part~I proves, for every column weight $u>0$, that the
reduced denominator of column $k$ is $\prod_{j\le k}((1+u)(1-z)^j-u)$, so the
minimal eventual constant-coefficient recurrence has order exactly
$k(k+1)/2$; the recurrence itself, with that denominator, is Munarini,
Poneti and Rinaldi's (2009), and only minimality is new, which narrows one
manuscript's claim to prove both posted conjectures.  Also the maximal
Hankel determinants as explicit products in $\mathbb Z[u]$ with their other
zeros on $\Re u=-1/2$; a pole bound uniform in $n$ and $k$; the coverage
window $n=k(\log k+s)$ with the sharp total-variation rate and a joint
Gaussian--Poisson law of column and zero-row counts; uniform saddle
expansions to every order on compact rays $n/k\in K\Subset(1,\infty)$, with
the closed amplitude and two corrections for A261784; and a
prescribed-column threshold.  Part~II, at $u=1$, adds strict monotonicity of
the growth-constant increments, every fixed order of the coverage
probability, the entire Stirling kernel, the Poisson law of a Stirling
defect, a third diagonal correction and a Lambert-$W$ index inversion with
integer brackets, an instance of the transseries volume.  The two
manuscripts proved the same core theorems independently and permute their
Greek letters, so the second is printed in the first's notation with a
dictionary, its re-proofs tabulated.  The Fubini pole and the positive
Stirling transform are those of \dir{a260700-parabolic-double-cosets}; the
only formalized ingredient is the Fubini generating function in the Lean
\dir{Analysis/FabiusFunction} tree.  No uniform statement for $T(n,k)$, no
$n/k\to1$, no exponentially small sectors; OEIS fixtures are
CC~BY-SA~4.0 and the OEIS draft is not submitted.  Part~III (batch~98)
answers Part~I's question on the limiting distribution of the determinant
zeros: the zero-counting measure of the reduced Hankel determinant,
normalized by its degree, tends to the law of $-1/2+iY$ with $Y$ Cauchy of
scale $1/2$, at Kolmogorov distance $\sim(9/\pi^2)(\log k)/k$ (a sharp
constant); cyclotomic multiplicities
$E_m(k)=k^2\log k/(\zeta(2)\psi(m))+O(k^2)$ uniformly in $m$ ($\psi$
Dedekind's function); a first correction for smooth statistics, a
divisor-sum edge law with the exact extreme zeros, a transition formula for
every height, the moment transition at $p=1$ and the full-degree limit in
$s/k^2$.  Its re-proof of the determinant product is printed as a pointer.
The multiplicity estimate has an absolute error only, the sharp constant
comes with no quantitative error, and the edge law concerns zeros of order
$k^2$, not the largest few.  Part~IV (batch~108, bundle Report~169,
written without knowledge of Parts~I--III, Sections~34--44)
reaches the diagonal A261784 through the Stirling transform and a single
saddle: its amplitude (its $\beta_1$ is Corollary~7.4's $\beta$ as the same
rational function), corrections $\beta_2,\beta_3$, column mean and variance,
first inverse correction and integer brackets re-prove results of Parts~I
and~II and are printed in full as marked second routes, a table renaming
about forty of its letters, while the inverse terms in $x_0^{-2}$ and
$x_0^{-3}$ (shown to hold for Part~II's inverse too), an explicit pole-tail
bound, a complex-uniform expansion in a column marker and a marker for
cells with entry at least~$2$, and an independent joint limit of the
column count (Gaussian) and the number of such cells (Poisson with mean
$Lr=2v=1.1046\ldots$), transferred to the total excess, are new.  It
answers no question of the host; it bears on Part~II's latent-defect
question and answers an analogue, at the single ray $n/k=2$, of its
joint-laws question (the write's ``settles one instance'' was corrected by
the independent check: the repeated-cell count, Poisson$(2v)$, is not a
realization of the defect, Poisson$(v)$).  Its questions~1--5 become
Research questions 17--21 (the second re-scoped) and its sixth is printed
once in Part~II; fixed orders only, no local limit
for the column count, and its $N=800$ diagnostics stay uncertified.
Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{The source boundary of extensional acyclic digraphs}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a182220-source-boundary}
{article.pdf}{batch~85: OEIS\_A182220\_Source\_Boundary\_Research.zip; batch~98: OEIS\_Source\_Boundaries.zip (base) + OEIS\_EAD\_Natural\_Boundaries.zip (Part~II)}
{An extensional acyclic digraph on $n$ vertices has at most
$n-\lceil\log_2n\rceil$ sources, and every smaller positive count occurs:
the formula still labelled conjectural in OEIS A182220, whose upper half is
Tomescu's (2011, credited).  The extremal classes are classified, so the
boundary diagonals $b_n^{(d)}=u(n,n-\lceil\log_2n\rceil-d)$ of A182162 have
$b_n^{(0)}=u_q\binom{2^q-q}{n-q}$ ($u_m=$ A001192$(m)$) and exact
$(d+1)$-term formulas; a uniform covering bound gives a dyadic entropy law,
the subsequential growth rates fill $[e^{\lambda_d},e^{\lambda_{d+1}}]$
($[1,4]$ for $d=0$), and the jumps at powers of two make every $b^{(d)}$ and
$n!\,b^{(d)}$ non-P-recursive, a counterpart of a lemma of
\dir{a003407-dyadic-scaling-rigidity}.  Also an all-orders expansion with
$u_m$ kept exact and a total-variation binomial law for arc deficits.  The
source sieve and the collapse to transitive sets are known; no priority,
no natural-boundary claim for the diagonal series in Part~I, no formal
proof; the OEIS-facing appendix is an unsubmitted draft, and the code's OEIS
fixtures are CC~BY-SA~4.0 data.  Part~II (batch~98) merges two independent
answers to Part~I's first question, with the one stronger on every shared
theorem as base: for every fixed $d$ the circle $|z|=R_d=e^{-\lambda_{d+1}}$
is a natural boundary of the diagonal series $B_d$; at every root of unity
$\zeta$ of power-of-two order the radial ratio is
$\zeta(1-aR_d)/(1-aR_d\zeta)+O(t)$ at radius $R_de^{-t}$, $a=2^{d+1}-1$;
$\log B_d(R_de^{-t})=L^2/(2\log2)+L\log L/\log2+O(L)$, $L=\log(1/t)$;
two-sided $L^p$ bounds on every arc; and no bidisk.  Also an abstract
transfer lemma for coherent dyadic blocks, natural boundaries for every
residue class of power-of-two step, a bijective proof of Tomescu's deletion
recurrence and first block corrections.  The write added, with proofs, that
every arithmetic section has a natural boundary, and bounds for the $O(L)$
term; the independent check found them valid, derived when the radial ratio
of a section has a limit, and corrected two values in a numerical note.  One
manuscript's mention of ``independent internal reviews'' could not be
verified and is not evidence of review.  Nothing is claimed at non-dyadic
angles or for growing defects.  Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{Finite Fredholm truncation for long increasing subsequences}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a047874-long-increasing-subsequences}
{article.pdf}{batch~85: LIS\_fixed\_sector\_research\_package.zip}
{For the permutations of $N$ letters with longest increasing subsequence of
length exactly $k$ (OEIS A047874) or at least $k$ (A214152), truncating a
Bonferroni expansion over shifted rows of the Robinson--Schensted shape at
$R$ terms gives globally holonomic arrays that agree with the true ones for
$N<(R+1)(k+R)$, a cutoff shown sharp, so both arrays are D-finite in every
fixed sector $N\le rk$: the sector question that Kauers and Wang
(arXiv:2609.02220) raise informally, whose case $r=2$ is their theorem.
Hence P-recursiveness along every rational ray; single sums when
$N\le2k+1$; a uniform all-orders expansion of the tail on compact ranges
of $(N-k)/k$, with polynomial coefficients; and five correction terms for
A269021 (whose leading term is Kot\v{e}\v{s}ovec's) and three for A267433.
The holonomic-closure step is flagged for independent review; the guessed
operators of the one-third sector, minimal orders and global
holonomicity are not claimed, and the discrete Bessel kernel and
holonomic summation are imported.  Not formalized; OEIS fixtures are
CC~BY-SA~4.0 data.}

\entry{Fixed-height shifted rectangles}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a181199-shifted-rectangles}
{article.pdf}{batch~85: Shifted\_Rectangle\_Asymptotics.zip (Part~I); batch~98: Shifted\_Rectangle\_Boundary\_Research.zip (Part~II); batch~101: Report231.zip (Part~III), Report233.zip (Part~IV), ProveIt\_Fixed\_Height\_Shifted\_Strips\_Asymptotics.zip (bundle Report~85) and ProveIt\_Shifted\_Strips\_Rational\_Diagonals\_and\_Transcendence.zip (bundle Report~91) (Part~V)}
{For $T_m(n)$, the $m\times n$ arrays of $1,\ldots,mn$ increasing along
rows, columns, diagonals and downward antidiagonals (OEIS A181196; $m=4,5$
are A181198, A181199), five Parts.  Part~I proves an all-orders expansion at
every fixed height $m$ with rational coefficients from a finite moment
formula, via a random-threshold cut and a Gaussian--Vandermonde ensemble; for
$m=5$ this is Kot\v{e}\v{s}ovec's conjectured
$T_5(n)\sim9\cdot5^{5n+1/2}/(2^{17}\pi^2n^{12})$, proved without the guessed
recurrence, with five corrections.  The generating function is algebraic
exactly for $m=1,2$.  Also the ratio to ordinary rectangular standard Young
tableaux, $2^{-m(m-1)}(1+m(m^2-1)/(4n)+\cdots)$, whose normalizer is the
transseries volume's rectangle-tableau asymptotic; GUE-type limit laws for
the intermediate row lengths; eventual monotonicity and log-convexity; and a
$W_{-1}$ inversion, an instance of the transseries volume.  Part~II
(batch~98) proves that the generating function is the diagonal of a rational
series, hence D-finite, at every fixed height (event-word binomial sums and
Bostan--Lairez--Salvy), and gives the sharp all-order expansion of the rare
count $R_m(n)$ of arrays whose first row ends before the last row starts,
with $R_m(n)/T_m(n)\asymp n^{2m-5/2}4^{-n}$.  The write adds the leading
term of Part~I's boundary error and narrows the source's framing: $R_m$ is a
much smaller sector of that error, so the source does not answer Part~I's
question on it (its all-order expansion stays open), and of Part~I's question~7 only D-finiteness is, not the growth
of the minimal order.  Parts~III and~IV (batch~101, Research
Reports~231 and~233) prove, from the array definition and for every
$n\ge1$, the order-two, degree-nine A181198 recurrence and the order-three,
degree-24 A181199 recurrence that OEIS displays as conjectural, and
Kauers--Koutschan's Conjectures~18 and~19, the printed finite sums (the
first for $n\ge2$), through Pfaffian formulas for a Volterra transfer, one
or two period integrals and explicit rational certificates; each adds a
sixth correction, and Part~IV evaluates effective error constants.  The
write shows that the A181198 operator has minimal order and that A181199
obeys no recurrence of order one; the independent check found and proved
recurrences of lower degree at higher order (degrees 8 and 7 at orders 3
and 4 for A181198, 18 and 16 at orders 4 and 5 for A181199).  Part~V prints
Reports~85 and~91 (1--2 October 2026, before Parts~I--II and without
knowledge of them), earlier independent routes to the all-orders
expansion, the algebraicity dichotomy and the rational-diagonal theorem;
the report credits that chronology.  Open: whether A181199 has a
recurrence of order two, everything at $m\ge6$ and any growing-height
statement.  The shifted hook formula and Sun's order-polytope reading are
credited; the two OEIS drafts, which say not to mark the recurrences
proved, are unchanged and not submitted.  Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{Zero auxiliary clipping tables and A196460}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a196460-clipping-tables}
{article.pdf}{batch~91: A196460\_asymptotics\_and\_inversion\_sources.zip,
A196460\_sharp\_uniform\_truncations\_sources.zip}
{For each fixed Boolean table $S\subseteq\{1,\ldots,K\}^2$ read on positive
integers by clipping its two coordinates, one integer polynomial equation needs no auxiliary exactly
when the table is closed under coordinate-flat replacement, and one strictly
positive auxiliary otherwise.  The closed tables of side $n+1$ number
$C_n=1+a_n$, where $a_n$ is A196460.  Part~I gives exact counts and
fixed-order forward, logarithmic and inverse expansions.  Part~II bounds
all truncations of the exact finite sector expansion for $n\ge32$, keeping
the distinct terminal-sector convention for $C_n$.  This is not a uniform
growing-order logarithmic or inverse theorem: an exact integer threshold
still needs an exact comparison.  Coefficients and degree may depend on
the whole finite table; no uniform paid integer compiler or universal
operation bound follows.  Part~I's classification theorem is that of
Research Report~69, source~38 of
\dir{signal-machine-collision-certificates}, restated with proof (dated
notes in both reports); its inversion sections redo the companion
transseries volume's connected-graph method, and its constants $b_k$, $c_k$
are A047863 and A002027, credited at the write.  Two manuscripts, integrated and reviewed within
the publication review's stated scope at \texttt{9f924ac47}; that review did
not certify every analytic proof.  Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{Strict twice partitions}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a271619-strict-twice-partitions}
{article.pdf}{batch~100: Strict\_Twice\_Partitions\_Phase\_Asymptotics\_Source.zip (bundle Report~182, base) + Nested\_Partitions\_Asymptotics\_and\_Inverses\_Source.zip (bundle Report~181)}
{Twice partitions with a strict outer level.  Part~I treats OEIS A271619,
$a(n)=[q^n]\prod_{k\ge1}(1+p(k)q^k)$, blocks of distinct sizes: an expansion
to every fixed relative order in $M^{-1/2}$,
$M=\lfloor(\sqrt{8n+1}-1)/2\rfloor$, uniform through triangular boundaries,
through two effective charges $M$, $M-1$ that exchange dominance near the
triangular phase $9/16$ over a persistent low-hole cloud
($C_0=\prod(1+1/p(k))=7.6015\ldots$, constants enclosed in integers), with
explicit first and second corrections, integer inverse enclosures,
$\log a(n)=(2b/3)(2n)^{3/4}+O(\sqrt n\log n)$ for $b=\pi\sqrt{2/3}$, the
failure of a single central minor-arc bound, a tame edge expansion through
Bloch--Okounkov brackets, the crossover centre and a total-variation law for
the outer count.  It is an eventual theorem with no effective onset: at
$n=5000$ the leading formula is about 562 times too small.  Part~II treats
A358836, $\prod(1+q^k/(q;q)_k)$, blocks of distinct lengths: an exact Jacobi,
eta and MacMahon reduction, every fixed order of the free energy and of the
exact-saddle Edgeworth expansion,
$\log a_n=\frac{4\pi}{3\cdot24^{1/4}}n^{3/4}-\frac{\sqrt6}{24}\sqrt n\log
n+O(\sqrt n)$ with an elementary relative equivalent, controlled inversion,
a Gaussian number of blocks and a Gumbel largest length independent of it.
Two manuscripts, printed whole, sharing no theorem.  Part~II's expansion of
the moving Jacobi product is identity~(11) of
\dir{a291698-moving-fugacity-partitions} at a complex fugacity, derived
independently; neither Part answers that report's Section~10.3 or question~7
of \dir{a022629-distinct-partition-norms}, and the weight $p(k)$ is not
regularly varying.  The write proved two steps Part~II gives in outline (the
sharpened mean shift and the marker-derivative bounds); the independent check
(\texttt{342a4583e}) found both valid.  Further questions: the $\alpha=1/3$
stretched-amplitude modulus law (an observation), a certified constant $C_P$,
Part~II's full marked expansion.  Part~I calls itself ``rigorous''; no
priority claim.  Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{Asymptotics and inverses for a stable Hilbert series}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a126348-stable-hilbert-series}
{article.pdf}{batch~100: ProveIt\_A126348\_Hilbert\_Asymptotics\_and\_Inverses.zip (bundle Report~84)}
{For $F(q)=\prod_{k\ge1}(1+q^k/(1-q))=\sum a_nq^n$, OEIS A126348, a stable
Hilbert series of top-degree Grothendieck and Lascoux polynomials, proves a
relative all-orders expansion at the quadratic logarithmic saddle
$n=e^{2L}(L^2/2+L+\pi^2/6)$: the resummed leading term
$\exp\{e^LB-1\}/\sqrt{2\pi e^{3L}V}$, with $B=L^2+L+\pi^2/3$ and
$V=L^2+3L+\pi^2/3+1$, times rational corrections $C_j(L)=O((1+L)^j)$, with
$C_1$, $C_2$ explicit and a finite rule for every order.  Also an exact Jacobi
and eta factorization of $F(e^{-t})$ with a convergent sector expansion of its
theta quotient, a uniform power-log expansion of $\log F$, a minor-arc bound
with no resonance sectors (the fugacity $1/(1-q)$ is large only near $q=1$),
an explicit range inverse with $\widehat N(a_n)=n+O(e^{-L}(1+L)^3)$, so that
rounding recovers $n$, strict increase for $n\ge2$ and a two-candidate
ceiling bracket for integer thresholds.  The factorization is not new: it is
identity~(11) of \dir{a291698-moving-fugacity-partitions} at the
$q$-dependent fugacity $u=1/(1-q)$, which the manuscript, pinned before that
report existed, does not cite; the credit is the write's.  The Lambert start,
the reversion and the threshold bracket are instances of the transseries
volume, with no novelty claimed for the method.  Everything is eventual: no
effective onset or explicit constants, no exponentially small coefficient
sectors or Stokes data, no exact interpolation, no priority claim.  The
manuscript's unproved statements (deep truncations of the formal inverse,
the next correction $B_1$) are further questions; the write proved that the
carrier root $L=L_0+\sum d_kL_0^{-k}$, $L_0=2W(\sqrt y/2)$, is a convergent
series ($d_1=-1$, $d_2=5/2-\pi^2/3$) and completed a sketched step of the
complex-neighbourhood lemma.  The independent check (\texttt{f8accf37c})
corrected one figure in that proof: the five-term sum leaves an error of
$4.3\cdot10^{-6}$ at $y=10^{12}$, not $3\cdot10^{-7}$.  One manuscript; no
claim found wrong.  Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{Unlabeled Bipartite Multigraphs: Asymptotic Expansions, Rare Symmetries and Connectivity}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a007716-bipartite-multigraphs}
{article.pdf}{batch~100: ProveIt\_A007716\_Asymptotics\_and\_Inverses.zip (bundle Report~88, base) + ProveIt\_A007716\_Symmetry\_and\_A007718\_Connectivity.zip (bundle Report~92)}
{OEIS A007716 counts bipartite multigraphs with $n$ edges, no isolated
vertices and two distinguished shores up to shore-preserving isomorphism.
With $w=W(n)$ and $B_n$ the Bell numbers, Part~I proves the relative
equivalent $a_n\sim B_n^2e^{w^2/2}/n!$, whose divergent factor comes from
parallel edges and which strengthens Pavlichin, Jiao and Weissman's
logarithmic estimate, through an exact invariant-partition Burnside formula
and a positive weighted count $Z_n$ with $a_n/Z_n=1+O(\log^3n/n)$; a complete
fixed-order expansion with rational coefficients $Q_j(w)$ ($Q_1$, $Q_2$
explicit) and a finite coefficient algorithm; uniform Bell shifts; smooth
inverse models with integer-threshold brackets; non-P-recursiveness; and,
for $r\ge3$ shores, the identity term as a relative equivalent.  Part~II, a
sequel, shows for the uniform measure on classes that a nontrivial vertex
automorphism has probability $2w^3/n+O((1+w)^7/n^2)$, almost always a single
leaf transposition, which answers Part~I's question on rare symmetries at
leading order; that disconnection has probability $\sim w^2/n$, usually
through one isolated edge, with an exact fragment law; and, for the connected
counts A007718, every fixed order, inverse brackets and
non-P-recursiveness.  Merged from two manuscripts, base Report~88; Part~II's
restated inputs are printed once, in Part~I, and its letters are renamed to
avoid clashes.  The order-one Bell expansion is that of
\dir{a277364-bell-asymptotics}, credited; A120733 of
\dir{a260700-parabolic-double-cosets} is the labelled analogue.  No claim was
found wrong; further questions record the one-paragraph all-orders Bell-saddle
argument, existential exponents, an asserted order-one remainder, $Q_2$
printed without derivation (the write checked its unmarked part numerically
only) and Part~II's reliance on Part~I's sketches.  No convergence, effective
constants, Stokes data or priority is claimed.  Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{Leafless loopless multigraphs by number of edges}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a307316-leafless-multigraphs}
{article.pdf}{batch~100: A307316\_A307317\_Refined\_Asymptotics\_Connectedness\_and\_Inversion\_Source.zip (bundle Report~131)}
{$A_m$ (OEIS A307316) and $C_m$ (A307317) count isomorphism classes of
loopless multigraphs with $m$ edges and minimum degree at least two ($C_m$:
connected), vertices and edges unlabelled.  With $w=W(2m)$,
$F(m)=m(w-\log w-1+2/w)$ and
$H_m=\exp\{F(m)+w^2/4-3w/2-1\}/\sqrt{2\pi m(w+1)}$, proves the polynomial
sandwich $(1-o(1))H_m\le C_m\le A_m\le(1+o(1))e^wH_m$, hence
$\log a_m=F(m)+w^2/4+O(\log m)$ for either sequence with every fixed
inverse-logarithmic order, $C_m/A_m=1-O(w^3/m)$ by a component convolution on
isomorphism classes, and a first-threshold inverse $m_0+O(1)$ that assumes no
monotonicity; also an exact star-conditioning sandwich and a relative
minimum-degree probability in the vertex-labelled composition model.
\emph{Trust boundary:} the upper half rests on Wright's 1972 theorem on simple
graphs counted by edges, which the source checked only in an OCR'd
reproduction and a contemporary abstract and the intake not at all; the lower
bound, the labelled-model results and the Euler-product identities need no
Wright input.  Not claimed: a relative equivalent $A_m\sim H_m$ (the sandwich
leaves the factor $e^w$), historical priority (Wright's and Lupanov's work
must be credited), any transfer from labelled counts; the identification with
symmetric kinematic-polynomial counts and A226919 is conjectural.  These, the
leading disconnected correction, minimum degree above two and full expansions
are further questions; the exact data for $m\le50$ are evidence only.  The
write proves $A_{m-2}\le A_m-C_m$ (adding a doubled edge is injective on
classes) and that $(A_m-C_m)/A_m\sim w^4/(4m^2)$ would follow from any
$A_m\sim\kappa H_m$; the independent check (\texttt{f8accf37c}) found this
valid.  The neighbour \dir{a007716-bipartite-multigraphs} works on the scale
$W(n)$, not $W(2m)$.  One manuscript; no claim found wrong.  Unrefereed; not
formalized.}

\entry{Exact asymptotics and inversion for column convex permutominoes}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a196275-column-convex-permutominoes}
{article.pdf}{batch~101: A196275\_Exact\_Asymptotics\_and\_Inversion\_Source.zip (bundle Report~96)}
{For $a_n$, the column-convex permutominoes with $n$ rows and $n$ columns
(OEIS A196275), a Bernstein-basis embedding turns Tom\'as's triangular
recurrence (an external input, checked here only against the 200 b-file
terms) into $b_n=a_n/(n+1)!=\tfrac12\langle1,T^n1\rangle$ for a positive
self-adjoint operator $T$ on $L^2[0,1]$ with an elementary resolvent.
Hence the closed form $B(z)=-(2-z)\log(1-z)/(2z(2-z+\log(1-z)))$, an exact
spectral measure (one atom at $h=1/(1-W_0(1/e))\approx1.38593$ off $[0,1]$
plus a positive density on $[0,1]$), and $a_n\sim k\,(n+1)!\,h^n$ with
$k=(2h-1)(h-1)/2\approx0.34191$; the source presents this as a proof of the
equivalent conjectured numerically by Beaton, Disanto, Guttmann and
Rinaldi (2011), a literature status the intake did not check.  The
secondary term $r_n=b_n-kh^n\sim1/(2n\log^2n)$ has an inverse-logarithmic
expansion to every order and a mixed inverse-power/inverse-logarithmic
expansion with explicit coefficient functions and sector-level remainders;
for a specified spectral interpolation the inverse is a convergent series
of exponentially small sectors about the exact Gamma-envelope inverse; with
$\mathcal E(x)=k\,\Gamma(x+2)h^x$, $\mathcal E(n)<a_n<\mathcal E(n+\tfrac12)$
for every $n\ge1$, so rounding $\mathcal E^{-1}(a_n)$ recovers every index;
and $B$ is not D-finite, so $(a_n)$ is not P-recursive.  The write proves
the disk estimate behind the convergent inverse series (Proposition~8.2)
and an $L^2$ statement on the Bernstein-smoothed profile: its distance from
the projection on the eigenvector at $h$ is exactly $\sqrt{2r_{2n}}\to0$
(the write's bound $\le1$, sharpened by the independent check).
Not claimed: local asymptotics of the profile coefficients, optimal
truncation, convergence of the logarithmic series, interval-certified
inversion or global priority; the tail estimates of Theorems~5.1 and~6.1
are written in condensed form.  The inversion steps are instances of the
transseries volume's factorial-core and staircase theorems.  Single source;
the intake found no false claim.  Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{Ascent Sequences Avoiding 100 or 110}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a202059-ascent-100-110-growth}
{article.pdf}{batch~102: Report243.zip (bundle Report~243, base) + Report241.zip (bundle Report~241)}
{Ordinary ascent sequences avoiding the pattern 100 ($a_n$, OEIS A202059),
110 ($d_n$, A202060), both 000 and 100 ($e_n$), or all three ($c_n$,
Callan and Mansour's Class~36).  Part~I (bundle Report~243, the base)
proves $\log u_n=n(\log n-2\log\log n+\log2-1)+O(n(\log\log n)^2/\log n)$
for $u=a,e$, so $(\log n)^2(u_n/n!)^{1/n}\to2$, and brackets the linear
constant of $d_n$ and $c_n$ in $[-\log2-1,\log2-1]$, through the triangular
comparison count $b_n=\mathrm{A098569}(n-1)$, a run-indexed
marked-occurrence encoding, positive-row words with a stars-and-bars rank
bijection and a repair of empty rows; also two-term first-crossing
inverses.  Part~II (Report~241, written first) proves
$\log u_n=n\log n-2n\log\log n+O(n)$ for $c,a,d$ with an exactly counted
fresh-label family, and keeps priority for the encoding, for
Corollary~20.1, which refutes the scale $\Gamma(3n/4+1)\mu^nn^g$
conjectured by Conway, Conway, Elvey Price and Guttmann (EJC 2022) for $a$
and $d$, and every fractional factorial $\Gamma(\alpha n+1)$, $0<\alpha<1$,
even with a factor $e^{o(n\log n)}$, and for Corollary~21.2: $c$, $a$, $d$
are not P-recursive.  Every main estimate of Part~II follows from Part~I;
the implied results are kept as second routes and mapped in a table.  The
write proves Part~I's aside on the leading order of its error (sharpened at
the independent check to $L^2/4-L+1+o(1)$), shows that $e_n$ is not
P-recursive, bounds $d_n/a_n$ and $c_n/a_n$ on the exponential scale, and
asks whether $d_n\le b_n$ always (true for $n\le26$).  The independent
check corrected the write's claim that Theorem~6.1 of
\dir{a098569-self-modified-ascents} implies Part~I's triangular lemma: it
gives the sum estimate, not the single-term estimate.  No relative
equivalent, no constant for $d$ or $c$, no rounding rule for the inverses;
nothing in either manuscript was found wrong.  Unrefereed; not
formalized.}

\entry{Self Modified Ascent Sequences and Positive Diagonal Tables}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a098569-self-modified-ascents}
{article.pdf}{batch~102: Report242.zip (bundle Report~242)}
{Let $b_N$ count nonnegative upper-triangular integer tables with positive
diagonal and entry sum $N$, equivalently self-modified ascent sequences of
length $N$ ($b_N=\mathrm{A098569}(N-1)$), and $c_N$ their binary members
($c_N=\mathrm{A121690}(N-1)$); OEIS A098568 is the table by dimension.
Proves an expansion to every fixed order at the exact gamma saddle,
uniformly in bounded real dimension marks; a full-span local limit theorem for the
dimension, with corrected mean $\sim2N/\log N$ and variance
$\sim2N/(\log N)^2$, which answers the A098568 question on how the row
terms are distributed; the elementary carrier
$b_N=\sqrt{2/P(u)}\exp(Nu(u+1)/(u+2)+u/2+u^2/4)(1+O((\log N)^4/N))$ with
$u(u+2)e^u=2N$ and $P(u)=u^2+4u+2$; the binary probability
$c_N/b_N=e^{-u^2/2-u}(1+\mathcal R(u)/N+O((\log N)^8/N^2))$ with an explicit
rational $\mathcal R$ and an all-fixed-order quotient algorithm; and
two-ceiling brackets for the inverse thresholds, with a counterexample at
$b_{1000}$ to an exact-ceiling rule.  The write proves the moderate
deviations of the dimension that the source asserts, and records that the
first saddle correction $C_1\sim1/(24N)$ stays negative until its sign
change between $N=15{,}864$ and $15{,}865$ (located by the independent
check; the write had said between $10^4$ and $10^6$; uncertified
numerics).  The exact
enumeration, the word/table bridge, the binary subclass and the $N\log N$
scale are prior (Bayoumi, El-Zahar and Khamis 1989 and later papers;
Kote\v{s}ovec's OEIS formulas), credited as the source credits them and
not checked against the papers.  Constants are non-effective; difference
parameters $d\ge2$, complex marks, higher moments, growing order and the
binary collision structure are further questions.  The same triangular sum
is the comparison count of \dir{a202059-ascent-100-110-growth}; its
single-term estimate there follows from Section~3 here, not from
Theorem~6.1 (an overclaim corrected in both reports at the independent
checks).  Single manuscript; unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{Weak and difference ascent sequences (OEIS A336070)}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a336070-weak-ascents}
{article.pdf}{batch~102: A336070\_Weak\_Ascent\_Logarithmic\_Correction\_Source.zip (bundle Report~105, base) + A336070\_Weighted\_Levels\_and\_Large\_Deviations\_Source.zip (bundle Report~107) + Ascent\_Sequences\_Sharp\_Remainders\_and\_Bounded\_Inverses\_Source.zip (bundle Report~108)}
{Weak ascent sequences (B\'enyi, Claesson and Dukes; OEIS A336070) and
difference ascent sequences with a fixed parameter $d$ (Dukes and Sagan;
$d=0$ gives the Fishburn numbers, $d=1$ A336070); $\mu=6/\pi^2$.  Part~I
(bundle Report~105, the base) proves for every fixed $d\ge0$ that
$(a^{(d)}_n/n!)^{1/n}\to\mu$ and $a^{(d)}_{n+1}/((n+1)a^{(d)}_n)\to\mu$,
with exponential concentration of the ascent count, and for $d=1$ that
$\log(a_n/(n!\mu^n))=\frac12(\log n)^2+O((\log n)^2/\log\log n)$, with a
signed Lambert-$W$ inverse; the Fishburn asymptotic of Hwang and Jin is its
external lower bound.  Part~II (Report~107) weights each level by $w>0$:
$\log H_n(w)=\log n!+n\log\mu+\frac w2(\log n)^2+O((\log n)^2/\log\log n)$
compact-uniformly in $w$, a full large-deviation principle for the number
of levels at speed $(\log n)^2$ with rate $x\log(2x/w)-x+w/2$, and an
inverse.  Part~III (Report~108) reduces both
remainders to $O(\log n)$, proves the correction $\frac d2(\log n)^2$ with
remainder $O_d(\log n)$ for every fixed integer $d\ge1$, threshold
inverses with bounded additive error, and the moment bounds
$\mathbb E E_n=\frac w2(\log n)^2+O((\log n)^{3/2})$,
$\operatorname{Var}E_n=O((\log n)^3)$.  The write adds the zero-level atom
with error $O(\log n)$ and the case $d=0$ (coefficient~0) from the
Fishburn asymptotic; the independent check restricted a doubling
inequality in that proof to $i\ge1$ and confirmed that a real $d>0$ acts
as $\lceil d\rceil$ because the $d$-ascent inequality is strict, so the
coefficient is $\lceil d\rceil/2$.  Report~105's binomial transform is
Jel\'{\i}nek's Fact~5.1, credited at the write; the three manuscripts'
citation of Mansour (2026) was not verified.  No relative-error
equivalent, amplitude, coefficient of a $\log n$ term, central limit
theorem or uniformity in $d=d(n)$.  \emph{Merged from three manuscripts in chain
order, superseded statements with dated notes; Report~108's
Sections~3--4 repeat Report~107's and are printed once.}  Unrefereed; not
formalized.}

\entry{Closed lambda terms (OEIS A135501 and A220894)}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a135501-closed-lambda-terms}
{article.pdf}{batch~103: Closed\_Lambda\_Terms\_All\_Logarithmic\_Orders\_Source.zip (bundle Report~110, base) + Closed\_Lambda\_Terms\_Growth\_and\_Binding\_Geometry\_Source.zip (bundle Report~109)}
{Counts closed lambda terms of size $n$ up to alpha-equivalence, with
abstractions and applications of size one and variables of size
$s\in\{0,1\}$ (OEIS A135501 for $s=1$, A220894 for $s=0$); $r=s+1$,
$N=n+1$.  Part~I proves
$M_r(N)-O(\log N)\le\log A_s(n)\le M_r(N)+O(R_r(N))$ with
$M_r(N)=(N/r)(t-2+1/t)+N/2$, $t=W(4eNe^{-r/2})$ and
$R_r(N)=N^{1-1/(3r)}(\log N)^{-2/3+1/(3r)}$, hence every fixed
inverse-logarithmic order (polynomials $P_1,\ldots,P_6$ and a recurrence;
the $n/\log n$ term is $(n/r)(\log\log n-\log4+r/2)/\log n$), and a
controlled threshold inverse with an asymmetric bracket.  Part~II, written
first, proves the weaker four-scale form and inverse (printed as a
superseded second route; its undetermined $n/\log n$ coefficient is
determined by Part~I, and its inverse omits a nonzero constant), the
skeleton sandwich, and a joint large-deviation principle at speed $n$ for
the scaled numbers of abstractions and the maximum unary height of a uniform
closed term, rate $(t-1-\log x)/r$ on $0<x\le t$.  The coarse majorant,
proved by both manuscripts the same way, is printed once in Part~I.  Proved at the write: the open
ratio $R(b,u)$ of Part~II satisfies $\log R(b,u)/u\to\frac12$ at the moving
saddle, and, after the independent check (which proposed the exponential
tilt and corrected three sentences), Proposition~18.2 extends this to
Part~I's whole saddle window for large $N$ (a second check fixed that
range).  No equivalent, amplitude or fluctuation law; the exponent of $R_r$
is an upper-bound exponent; the literature statements were not checked
against the papers.  The Lean type \texttt{Term 0} of
\dir{Computability/CombinatoryLogic} is exactly the set counted; nothing is
formalized.  Merged from two manuscripts.  Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{One-sided rectangulations: logarithmic power and non-D-finiteness}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a348351-one-sided-rectangulations}
{article.pdf}{batch~103: A348351\_Rectangulations\_Exponent\_and\_Non\_D\_Finiteness\_Source.zip (bundle Report~111)}
{One-sided, equivalently area-universal, rectangulations with $n$
rectangles (OEIS A348351) are counted, through the
Asinowski--Cardinal--Felsner--Fusy bijection (used, not re-proved), by
four-colour quadrant walks with bounded steps.  With
$\Gamma=(7+\sqrt{17})/2$, $\rho=(29-7\sqrt{17})/4$ and
$\alpha=1+\pi/\arccos(-\rho)=2.9569294595\ldots$, the report proves
$\log a(n)=n\log\Gamma-\alpha\log n+o(\log n)$, the exponent half of the
conjecture $a(n)\sim c\,\Gamma^nn^{-\alpha}$ stated after their
Proposition~4.23; the generating function is not D-finite ($\alpha$ is
irrational, $2<\alpha<3$, by a conjugate of $2\cos\theta$ below $-2$); and
$N(y)=\log y/\log\Gamma+(\alpha/\log\Gamma)\log\log y+o(\log\log y)$.  The
method: a Perron tilt with a bounded Poisson corrector, Whitt's martingale
