functional limit theorem, a lattice local limit theorem, exact-time interior
bridges, wedge harmonic measure and annular transfer.  Proved at the write:
the walk satisfies hypotheses (H1)--(H5) of the cone theorem of
\dir{a377922-corner-polyhedra-schnyder}, Part~III, so the exponent is also an
instance of it (this report is the earlier, model-specific proof; neither
source cites the other); the general criterion that nonnegative integers
$\mu^nn^{-\alpha+o(1)}$ with irrational $\alpha$ have a non-D-finite
generating function; $a(n+1)\ge2a(n)$ for $n\ge1$; and two of four condensed
steps written out.  The other two (the bridge limit, the uniformity of the
annular transfer) are listed as an open question, as are the amplitude, an
error term, corrections and a bounded-term inverse; non-D-finiteness does not
exclude the exact multistate recurrence.  Priority is not established.  The
independent check found every item of the write valid (cosmetic fixes only).
Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{The second logarithmic term for regular Tesler matrices}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a008608-tesler-matrices}
{article.pdf}{batch~103: A008608\_Tesler\_Second\_Logarithmic\_Term\_Source.zip (bundle Report~106)}
{For the number $a_n$ of $n\times n$ regular Tesler matrices (OEIS A008608),
the leading term $\log a_n\sim Cn^{3/2}$, $C=\pi\sqrt{8/27}$, is
Balashov--Bulavenko--Molybog's (EJC 2026, Corollary~37).  The report proves
the second term: $Cn^{3/2}-\frac34n\log n-2n\log\log n-O(n)\le\log a_n\le
Cn^{3/2}-\frac34n\log n+B_Un+O(\sqrt n)$ with
$B_U=0.2043366936\ldots$, so
$\log a_n=Cn^{3/2}-\frac34n\log n+O(n\log\log n)$.  The upper bound is
elementary (an eta identity and singleton elimination); the lower bound
builds a feasible geometric cut profile and integer throughput vectors and
applies the capacity inequality of Br\"and\'en--Leake--Pak (an external
input whose proof ProveIt has not checked), with the assembly
$-\frac14-1+\frac12=-\frac34$.  Also the threshold inverse
$N(y)=x+(2C)^{-1}\sqrt x\log x+O(\sqrt x\log\log x)$, $x=(\log y/C)^{2/3}$,
with a non-effective integer bracket.  No constant is effective; no $O(n)$
remainder, linear term, equivalent or amplitude is claimed, and the novelty
of $-3/4$ is uncertified.  Added at the write: $B_U$ is only an upper-bound
constant (for $9\le n\le26$ the linear quotient lies in
$[-1.443,-1.421]$, uncertified), and a proof that the $2n\log\log n$ margin
penalty is sharp for this family of margin vectors.  The manuscript omits
O'Neill (2018), whose bounds are $e^{O(n^2)}$; the write cites it with Pak's
question whether $a_n=e^{\Theta(n^2)}$, which Corollary~37 and, alone, the
report's elementary upper bound answer negatively.  The independent
check completed the sharpness proof (two upper bounds it presupposed), gave
O'Neill's journal and arXiv numberings and credited the $n^{1.6}$
observation to Pantone.  Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{Self-complementary tournament score sequences (OEIS A345470 and A351869)}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a345470-self-complementary-scores}
{article.pdf}{batch~103: Self\_Complementary\_Tournament\_Scores\_Leading\_Constants\_and\_Inverses\_Source.zip (bundle Report~116, base) + Self\_Complementary\_Tournament\_Scores\_Growth\_and\_Inverse\_Source.zip (bundle Report~114)}
{Counts the self-complementary score sequences $C_n$ (OEIS A345470) and
the strong ones $D_n$ (A351869), as sorted sequences, by exit times of
integrated random walks.  Part~I proves $C_n\sim A\,2^nn^{-3/4}$ and
$D_n\sim e^{-\lambda}A\,2^nn^{-3/4}$ with one amplitude $A>0$ for both
parities, where $\lambda=0.33023754\ldots$ is the constant of
\dir{a000571-tournament-score-sequences}, so the strong fraction is
$e^{-\lambda}=0.71875297\ldots$ (certified enclosure); a harmonic identity, a
Lambert-$W_{-1}$ inverse with vanishing-width brackets and an eventual gap
$N_C\le N_D\le N_C+1$.  Part~II, written first, gives the first proof of
$\Theta(2^nn^{-3/4})$ (Stockmeyer's conjectures), an $O(1)$ inverse and the
monotonicity of $C_n$ (all $n$) and $D_n$ ($n\ge2$); its other results are
second routes.  Wrong claim on record: both manuscripts copy the limit
density printed in Denisov--Wachtel's Theorem~1 (AIHP 2015, journal version
only), which has mass $0.1154552960\ldots$, not~$1$.  The write proves that
the density of Groeneboom--Jongbloed--Wellner's (2.24), cited in the same
paper, has mass~$1$ and velocity marginal
$f_\star(0)=\sqrt{2/3}\,\Gamma(3/4)^2/\pi=0.3902762188\ldots$, about ten times
Part~I's printed $0.0377\ldots$; so
$A=\sqrt6\,\Gamma(3/4)\pi^{-3/2}V_{DW}(1/\sqrt2,1/\sqrt2)$, and the lemma that
used the printed shape is repaired.  The independent check confirmed the
corrected density by a dynamic programme of the conditioned walk to $n=600$
(floating point, extrapolated: evidence, not a certificate) and corrected
the write's supporting text (the printed density predicts $A\approx0.0374$;
Monte Carlo values replaced).  $V_{DW}$ is not evaluated: counts suggest
$A\approx0.387$, uncertified.  The $\lambda$ enclosure rests on Kolesnik's
tail theorem; no rates or corrections.  Merged from two manuscripts; re-proofs
printed as marked second routes.  Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{Inversion sequences solved by kernel orbits (OEIS A279544, A279567, A279569, A279558)}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a279544-inversion-kernel-classes}
{article.pdf}{batch~104: Inversion\_Sequences\_A279544\_A279567\_Asymptotics\_and\_Inverses\_Source.zip (bundle Report~118) + A279569\_Two\_Kernel\_Asymptotics\_and\_Inverses\_Source.zip (bundle Report~119) + A279558\_Asymptotic\_Expansion\_and\_Inverse\_Thresholds\_Source.zip (bundle Report~122)}
{Britt and Beaton (arXiv:2512.21943v3) gave generating trees and catalytic
equations for the inversion sequences avoiding a triple of relations and
stated, explicitly as non-rigorous, laws $a_n\sim C\mu^nn^{-3/2}$ for four
classes.  Three manuscripts prove them by one method: a kernel substitution
reduces the catalytic equation to a scalar affine functional equation, solved
along contracting M\"obius-type orbits seeded at an algebraic kernel root; a
global continuation argument excludes competing singularities, and an exact
rational certificate shows that the square-root amplitude does not vanish.
Part~I treats class~214 (OEIS A279544, $\mu=4$, $C$ to 52 decimals) and
class~1509 (A279567, $\mu=3+2\sqrt2$); Part~II class~1953A (A279569,
$\mu=27/4$) and, through Martinez and Savage's Wilf-equivalence (their
Theorem~60), the counts of class~1953B; Part~III class~830 (A279558,
$\mu=27/4$).  For each class: coefficient asymptotics to every fixed order with
$C$, $c_1$, $c_2$ in certified intervals, strict monotonicity (for class~830
completed by a remark of the write), and a threshold inverse inside two
ceilings, centred at a Lambert-$W_{-1}$ root in Parts~I--II and expanded in
powers of $1/\log M$ with coefficients polynomial in $\log\log M$ in Part~III;
the inverses are instances of the transseries volume.  Kot\v{e}\v{s}ovec's
OEIS amplitudes agree with every certified interval, so no digits are new.
Nonalgebraicity and $D$-finiteness (which Britt and Beaton believe fail), the
corrections beyond $c_2$, effective constants (the inverse constants are
existential) and earlier rigorous literature (not searched) stay open.  Nothing
in the manuscripts was found wrong; the independent check corrected the
write's statement that every Part proves a Lambert-$W_{-1}$ inverse.  Siblings
from the same paper, by unrelated methods: \dir{a279571-inversion-cone-walk}
and \dir{a279551-inversion-log-deficit}.  \emph{Merged from three manuscripts
that share no passage; every Part is printed in full.}  Unrefereed; not
formalized.}

\entry{Inversion sequences avoiding 100, 101, 201 (OEIS A279571)}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a279571-inversion-cone-walk}
{article.pdf}{batch~104: A279571\_Leading\_Equivalent\_and\_Harmonic\_Constant\_Source.zip (bundle Report~125) + A279571\_Exact\_Asymptotic\_Exponent\_and\_Non\_D\_Finiteness\_Source.zip (bundle Report~123)}
{A positive change of weights turns Britt and Beaton's generating tree for
class~2106 (inversion sequences avoiding 100, 101 and 201, OEIS A279571) into a
centred two-colour Markov-additive walk with unbounded steps in the quadrant,
with wedge angle $\theta=\arccos(2/\sqrt7)$ and $p=\pi/\theta\in(4,5)$.
Part~I proves $a_n\sim C_A9^nn^{-\kappa}$, $\kappa=1+\pi/\arccos(2/\sqrt7)
\approx5.4017$, with $0<C_A<\infty$ characterized by forward and reversed
killed harmonic functions and a positive endpoint series; a killed local limit
theorem at every fixed pair of states; a threshold inverse with explicit centre
and a ceiling bracket of vanishing width; that $1/9$ is the only singularity on
the circle of convergence; and, $\kappa$ being irrational, that the generating
function is not $D$-finite.  Part~II, the earlier manuscript, proves the weaker
$a_n=9^nn^{-\kappa+o(1)}$ by geometric annuli and exact-time gluing, and alone
derives the functional equations and kernel.  The write checks Part~II's walk
against the log-scale cone theorem of \dir{a377922-corner-polyhedra-schnyder}
(its Part~III): (H1)--(H4) hold, but (H5) fails at the origin endpoints the
theorem states, so the exponent is an instance of that theorem's proof, stated
as a proposition at fixed endpoint states, not of its statement; the
independent check confirmed this and needed no correction.  No digits of $C_A$
(an uncertified $a_n9^{-n}n^\kappa=87.65$ at $n=1000$), no rate or correction
terms, no local $\Delta$-domain expansion, no unconditional ceiling rule for
the inverse and no general amplitude cone theorem are claimed; prior numerical
estimates (Kot\v{e}\v{s}ovec, Britt--Beaton) are credited.  Nothing in the
manuscripts was found wrong.  \emph{Merged from two manuscripts; the six blocks
they share word for word are printed once, in Part~I.}  Unrefereed; not
formalized.}

\entry{The logarithmic deficit of two inversion sequence classes (OEIS A279551 and A279556)}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a279551-inversion-log-deficit}
{article.pdf}{batch~104: A279551\_A279556\_Logarithmic\_Deficit\_and\_Inverse\_Source.zip (bundle Report~126) + A279551\_A279556\_Sharp\_Deficit\_Constants\_and\_Inversion\_Source.zip (bundle Report~127) + A279551\_A279556\_Loglog\_Correction\_and\_Refined\_Inversion\_Source.zip (bundle Report~129)}
{For Britt and Beaton's inversion-sequence classes 247 and 759 (OEIS A279551
and A279556, growth constants $\mu=8$ and $9$), with deficit
$\mathcal D(n)=n\log\mu-\log a(n)$, $S=n^{1/3}(\log n)^{2/3}$, $N=\log n$ and
$J=\log\log n$, three manuscripts on one commitment-tree spine prove:
$\mathcal D(n)=\Theta(S)$, strict monotonicity, an inverse threshold, smooth
boundary values of sharp Gevrey order~3, a genuine singularity at $1/\mu$ and
that neither generating function is $D$-finite (Part~I); the sharp constant,
$\mathcal D(n)=\sigma_DS+o(S)$ with $\sigma_D=(3\pi^2/(2D))^{1/3}$, $D=1/2$ and
$1$ (Part~II); and the next term,
$\mathcal D(n)=\sigma_DS+(7\sigma_D/3)SJ/N+O(S/N)$, with refined inverse and
derivative-growth consequences (Part~III, the base).  The write proves that no
form $Cn^g\mu^n\exp(-bn^\beta)$ is an asymptotic equivalent, which refutes as
equivalents Britt and Beaton's numerical $n^{3/8}$ forms (their
(4.12)--(4.13), which they call non-rigorous); the independent check extended
this to a factor $(\log n)^\gamma$, restricted one sentence to $b>0$ and
corrected two twice-rounded entries of a residual table.  No coefficient
equivalent or amplitude, no coefficient of $S/\log n$, no ratio theorem, no
effective constants and no unique dominant singularity are claimed.  The method
parallels \dir{a202061-ascent-120-deficit} (the same scale, critical local
root, potential, $7/3$ term and excluded $n^{3/8}$; different sequences and
normalizations, no cross-citation), whose Parts~IV--V model the first open
question.  Nothing in the manuscripts was found wrong.  \emph{Merged from three
manuscripts printed in dependency order (Reports 126, 127, 129); Report~127's
repetition of Report~126's Sections~2--4 is printed once.}  Unrefereed; not
formalized.}

\entry{Diagonally symmetric alternating sign matrices (OEIS A005163)}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a005163-diagonally-symmetric-asms}
{article.pdf}{batch~104: A005163\_Full\_Leading\_Equivalent\_and\_Individual\_Amplitude\_Source.zip (bundle Report~128) + A005163\_Exact\_Logarithmic\_Scale\_and\_Inverse\_Thresholds\_Source.zip (bundle Report~124) + A005163\_Linear\_Growth\_Fugacity\_Pressure\_and\_Limit\_Laws\_Source.zip (bundle Report~121) + A005163\_Diagonal\_Roots\_Growth\_Bounds\_and\_Inverse\_Brackets\_Source.zip (bundle Report~120)}
{Let $a_n$ count the diagonally symmetric alternating sign matrices of
order~$n$ (OEIS A005163) and let $Z_n(s)$ weight each by $s$ to the number of
its nonzero diagonal entries; $\alpha=\frac12\log(3\sqrt3/4)$,
$\beta=\frac14\log3$, $\kappa=5/72$.  Part~I (the base) proves
$a_n\sim C_*e^{\alpha n^2+\beta n}n^{-5/72}$ along all $n$, and
$Z_n(s)\sim C(s)e^{\alpha n^2+nB(s)}n^{-\kappa}$ locally uniformly on
$\Re s>0$, the amplitude characterized by Fredholm determinants and a Fourier
series but not evaluated; this is the leading term of Behrend, Fischer and
Koutschan's Conjecture~11.1 (arXiv:2309.08446v3), whose $n^{-2}$ and
parity-dependent $n^{-5/2}$ corrections are not proved.  Part~II, on which
Part~I rests, proves stability of the fugacity polynomials, weak cross-size
interlacing, an exact calibration at $s=\sqrt3$,
$\log a_n=\alpha n^2+\beta n-\kappa\log n+O(1)$, a holomorphic pressure with
bounded error and a central limit theorem; Part~III a second route to the
pressure, the limiting root measure (half of its mass escapes to infinity),
Hoeffding concentration, a local limit theorem and a large-deviation principle;
Part~IV elementary envelopes with certified $p<\beta<q$; each with an inverse.
No digits or closed form of $C_*$ (an uncertified $C_*\approx0.7235287$ from
exact terms to $n=177$ agrees with Behrend--Fischer--Koutschan's estimate), no
rate, no effective constants, no root simplicity or strict interlacing are
claimed; Part~I's operator-theoretic sections were read but not re-derived line
by line at intake.  Nothing in the manuscripts was found wrong; the independent
check made the interlacing of two notes weak and corrected three statements of
uncertified evidence (among them the centre $0.7235287$, first printed
$0.7235288$).  \emph{Merged from four manuscripts; Report~124 is printed in
full because Part~I depends on it, and the sections that Reports 120, 121 and
124 share almost word for word are printed once.}  Unrefereed; not
formalized.}

\entry{Permutations with exactly one occurrence of 1234, 1243 or 12345 (OEIS A217057, A224179, A224248)}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a217057-unique-pattern-occurrences}
{article.pdf}{batch~105: A217057\_Unique\_1234\_Asymptotics\_and\_Conjecture\_Refutation\_Source.zip (bundle Report~134) + A217057\_Rational\_Structure\_Logarithmic\_Asymptotics\_and\_Inversion\_Source.zip (bundle Report~135) + A217057\_All\_Orders\_Logarithmic\_Asymptotics\_and\_Inversion\_Source.zip (bundle Report~137) + A224179\_Leading\_Amplitude\_Diffusive\_Cancellation\_and\_Inversion\_Source.zip (bundle Report~136) + A224248\_Exact\_Formulas\_Leading\_Asymptotics\_and\_Inversion\_Source.zip (bundle Report~138) + A224248\_All\_Orders\_Asymptotics\_and\_Inverse\_Thresholds\_Source.zip (bundle Report~140)}
{Counts the permutations with exactly one occurrence of 1234 ($u_n$, OEIS
A217057), of 1243 (A224179) and of 12345 (A224248), normalized by
$A_n=|\mathrm{Av}_n(1234)|\sim C_09^nn^{-4}$ and
$|\mathrm{Av}_n(12345)|\sim C_A16^nn^{-15/2}$, through exact half
decompositions glued by binomials.  Part~I proves $u_n/A_n\to R$, a series of
positive terms, so $u_n\sim C_0R\,9^nn^{-4}$, and a rational certificate
$R>0.50009$ that refutes Conway and Guttmann's conjecture $R=1/2$ (EJC 32(1)
P1.3, Section~5.1); the write sharpens the bound to $R>0.50153$ by an exact
partial sum and explains why their fits pointed to $1/2$.  Part~II shows that
the generating function is the diagonal of a rational series, hence
$D$-finite (through Bostan, Lairez and Salvy, whose hypotheses the write
checked), with first logarithmic term $(43020\sqrt3/\pi)\log n/n^3$;
Part~III (the base) gives every fixed order, with
$\kappa_4=-5041845\sqrt3/\pi$, and inverses.  Part~IV proves
$b_n/A_n\to R_{1243}$, a rational diagonal, non-algebraicity and a
square-root cancellation; Conway and Guttmann's suggested $149/160$ stays open.
Parts~V--VI give exact formulas for one 12345,
$U_n\sim C_AR_5\,16^nn^{-15/2}$ with $R_5\ge15/32768$, and every fixed order
with no logarithms: the same method with the opposite outcome.  No certified
decimals of $R$, $R_{1243}$, $R_5$ or the corrections, no effective constants,
no explicit annihilating equation and no algebraicity statement for 12345 are
claimed.  Nothing in the six manuscripts was found wrong; Report~138's uncited
literature claims are pinned to B\'ona--Burstein and Waite, and the
independent check reran the refutation independently and corrected two
wordings.  The 1432 sibling is \dir{a224182-unique-1432-order}.  \emph{Merged
from six manuscripts in dependency order; Report~136's word-for-word repetition (up
to one letter) of Report~134's Proposition~4 and Lemma~5 is printed once.}  Unrefereed; not
formalized.}

\entry{Polynomial order for permutations containing exactly one 1432}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a224182-unique-1432-order}
{article.pdf}{batch~105: A224182\_Polynomial\_Growth\_Order\_and\_Inverse\_Bounds\_Source.zip (bundle Report~139)}
{For the number $b_n$ of permutations of $[n]$ with exactly one occurrence of
1432 (OEIS A224182, by reversal) and $A_n=|\mathrm{Av}_n(1432)|=
|\mathrm{Av}_n(1234)|$, proves without a computer
$M_{n-3}\le b_n\le(n+2)A_{n+2}$, hence $b_n=\Theta(9^n/n^3)$ with
$1/2187\le\liminf b_n/(nA_n)\le\limsup b_n/(nA_n)\le81$, by minima skeletons
read as Ferrers-board matchings, a shape-Wilf step, an entropy estimate, an
inflation injection and a Burstein-type marked split; and an inverse bracket
whose unrounded endpoints differ by $11/2$.  The exact image of the split is
computer-assisted: a reduction to at most eight points proved by hand and an
exhaustive certificate of 2,074 cores.  The write adds that the generating
function is not algebraic (Lemma~6.3 of B\'ona and Burstein) and that the
inverse is an instance of the transseries volume, and finds that the one-step
bound $f_m(n)\le n\sum_{r<m}f_r(n-1)$ displayed in the proof of Mansour,
Rastegar and Roitershtein is justified by their argument only with the factor
$n^2$ (an avoider in $S_7$ has nine preimages), which gives one power less; so,
among the sources read, the marked split is the only complete proof of the
upper order, which the source and the placement record had treated as known.
No limit of $b_n/(nA_n)$, no proof of Conway and Guttmann's $3/80$ (the exact
ratio rises to $0.03829$ at $n=17$ and first falls at $n=18$), no leading
equivalent and no $D$-finiteness are claimed; Regev's three-row asymptotic and
Krattenthaler's growth diagrams are cited, not re-derived.  Nothing in the
source was found wrong; the independent check corrected three wordings.  The
sibling \dir{a217057-unique-pattern-occurrences} uses the same normalizer by
unrelated methods.  Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{The weighted Dyck Newton triangle (OEIS A258219, A258220 and A292692)}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a292692-weighted-dyck-newton-diagonal}
{article.pdf}{batch~105: A292692\_Weighted\_Dyck\_Diagonal\_Asymptotics\_and\_Inversion\_Source.zip (bundle Report~141) + A292692\_All\_Orders\_Interior\_Asymptotics\_and\_Inversion\_Source.zip (bundle Report~142) + Weighted\_Dyck\_Upper\_Edge\_Transition\_All\_Orders\_and\_Inverse\_Source.zip (bundle Report~143)}
{Weight a peak at $(a,b)$ of a Dyck path by $k+a/b$ and let $p_N(k)$ be the
total weight of the paths of semilength~$N$ (OEIS A258219); its Newton
coefficients $P_{N,m}$ form A258220, whose diagonal $a_n=P_{2n,n}$ is A292692.
Part~I proves Peter Bala's conjecture on A258219, the Riccati identity
$F-1=kxF+xF^2+2x^2F_x$ and its Stieltjes continued fraction, through an exact
identity for all weighted prefixes, and $a_n\sim Cd^nn!/\sqrt n$ with
$d=(51\sqrt{17}-107)/4$ and $C=\sqrt{2+2/\sqrt{17}}/\pi^{3/2}$, which
establishes Kot\v{e}\v{s}ovec's numerical asymptotic form (2017) and growth-rate
conjecture (2024) on A292692 with the amplitude in closed form; also the first
relative correction and threshold brackets.  Part~II (the base) proves every
fixed order of the normalized ratio $R_{N,m}$ uniformly on compact subsets of
$0<m/N<1$, with every diagonal coefficient in $\mathbb Q(\sqrt{17})$, and an
inverse at every fixed order.  Part~III treats the upper edge $m=N-q$: exact
two-sided bounds for $q\le N/2$, the profile $\sqrt NR_{N,N-q}\sim F_q$ for
$q=o(\sqrt N)$, built from the four-regular-map sequence A292186 (whose Riccati
equation and leading asymptotic are credited as prior art), its expansion to
every fixed order, its strict monotonicity and an inverse by polynomial
reversion.  The leading inverses reduce to the transseries volume's factorial
core.  The range $\sqrt N\lesssim q=o(N)$, the lower edge $m=o(N)$, effective
constants and convergence of the expansions stay open.  Nothing in the
manuscripts was found wrong; the independent check corrected a false note of
the write (positivity of $R_{N,m}$ at $m=0$ comes from $p_N(0)>0$, not from a
Stirling number, which vanishes there) and made the transseries citations
precise.  \emph{Merged from three manuscripts in dependency order (Reports
141, 142, 143); embedded copies and restatements are printed once.}
Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{The proportional placement game (OEIS A398540)}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a398540-proportional-placement-game}
{article.pdf}{batch~105: A398540\_Placement\_Game\_Leading\_Asymptotics\_and\_Inverse\_Source.zip (bundle Report~144) + A398540\_Placement\_Game\_Logarithmic\_Corrections\_and\_Inverse\_Source.zip (bundle Report~145) + A398540\_All\_Orders\_Logarithmic\_Asymptotics\_and\_Inverses\_Source.zip (bundle Report~146)}
{In A.~Serra's continuous placement game, $n$ uniform values arrive one at a
time and must be placed at once into $n$ ordered slots; the winning
probabilities $W_n$ of the proportional policy satisfy an exact recurrence with
beta-binomial weights, and OEIS A398540 records digits of
$r_*=\lim W_{n+1}/W_n\approx0.5754724$.  Part~I proves that $r_*$ exists and
is computable, $527/1000<r_*<79/125$ by exact rational arithmetic, and
$W_n\sim r_*\sqrt{2\pi n}\,r_*^n$, Serra's conjecture with a corrected
constant; Part~II the pointwise expansion
$W_n/(r_*\sqrt{2\pi n}\,r_*^n)=1+1/(4n)-(7/540)\log n/n^2+B_2/n^2+o(n^{-2})$,
eventual strict decrease of the ratios, and that no pure inverse-power ratio
expansion holds through $n^{-3}$; Part~III (the base) every fixed order, with
polynomials in $\log n$ of degree at most $j-1$, the explicit third order,
inverses at every fixed order and nested rational rate enclosures with a proved
contraction (not run).  With proofs, the write shows that Serra's prefactor
$\sqrt{2\pi}$ and its approach from below are wrong for the game (his
Proposition~6.17 is a correct computation for an integral model that does not
model the recurrence); that his integral equation of Open Problem~3, read
through an ansatz of amplitude $C$, has relative error tending to
$1-C^2/(2\pi r_*)$, which is $1-r_*$ at the true amplitude, not zero (as
restated by the independent check); that his pure-power ratio expansion fails
at every order $p\ge2$; and that his inequality (198) expresses
log-concavity, not log-convexity.  Effective constants, ratio monotonicity at
every index, exact logarithm degrees, certified digits, other policies and
whether Report~146's constant $S_A$ equals $1/12$ (uncertified fits agree to
$10^{-23}$) stay open.  Report~146 was corrected in place before delivery and
its earlier version was never received.  Nothing in the manuscripts was found
wrong.  \emph{Merged from three manuscripts in dependency order (Reports 144,
145, 146).}  Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{Alternating Baxter involutions (OEIS A347546): the corrected count and its fixed-point refinement}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a347546-alternating-baxter-involutions}
{article.pdf}{batch~106: Corrected\_Baxter\_Involutions\_Asymptotics\_and\_Inverses\_Source.zip (bundle Report~149, base) + Baxter\_Fixed\_Point\_Crossover\_Profiles\_and\_Inverses\_Source.zip (bundle Report~151)}
{OEIS A347546 counts the involutions among the doubly alternating Baxter
permutations of length $n$.  Part~I shows that the recurrence of Min
(J.~Chungcheong Math.\ Soc.\ 34 (2021), Theorem~2.7), from which the OEIS
data were computed, miscounts this class: in an even-length involution the
two outer blocks are mutual inverses, not involutions, so the outer factor
is the Catalan number $C_j$, not the involution count $o_j$, and
$C_4=14>12=o_4$.  The class has 2168 members of length~20, not 2166; two
omitted involutions are printed.  From three published necessity lemmas of
Min and Park (2006), accepted rather than re-proved, Part~I proves the
corrected recurrences, algebraic generating functions with dominant
singularity $\rho=(\sqrt5-1)/4$, equivalents
$\mathcal A_s(1+\sqrt5)^mm^{-3/2}$ for the even and odd subsequences with
every fixed order, and Lambert-$W_{-1}$ length inverses with ceiling
brackets leaving at most two adjacent candidates.  Its Catalan count of
doubly alternating Baxter permutations is Guibert and Linusson's theorem
(2000), re-derived and credited at the write.  Part~II refines the count by
pairs of fixed points ($q=1$ gives Part~I back): every fixed order
uniformly on compact complex windows of the sparse weight $q=w/m$ with
entire profiles, an exact criterion for inverting in the weight, interior
inverses, and Poisson-type laws with total-variation rate $O(m^{-1/2})$.
The write proves that Min's odd formula is right and her even formula fails
exactly from half-index~10 on, so the recurrence-defined sequence lies
strictly below the class at $n=20$ and every $n\ge22$: Min's printed values
at $n=20,22,\ldots,26$ and 21 of the 42 terms of A347546 ($n=20$ and
$22\le n\le41$) are wrong, while its definition is right; nothing was sent
to the OEIS or the journal.  The independent check confirmed the six
corrected values of Min's list as direct counts (literal brute force
through $n=26$).  The leading Lambert centres are instances of the
transseries volume.  No effective constants, all-index monotonicity or
fixed-weight central limit theorem.  Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{Unlabeled circle graphs (OEIS A156808 and A156809)}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a156808-circle-graphs}
{article.pdf}{batch~106: Circle\_Graph\_Enumeration\_Error\_Bounds\_and\_Inverses\_Source.zip (bundle Report~150, base) + Circle\_Graph\_First\_Corrections\_and\_Refined\_Inverses\_Source.zip (bundle Report~152)}
{Let $c_n$ and $g_n$ count connected and all unlabeled circle graphs on $n$
vertices (OEIS A156808, A156809) and $h_n=(2n-1)!!/(4n)$.  Part~I proves
$c_n\sim g_n\sim e^{-3/2}h_n$ from chord matchings under the dihedral
group of order $4n$ and asymmetric split-prime cores with recoverable
decorations; relative errors $O(1/n)$ through a self-contained Poisson
approximation of local patterns, the connected fraction
$1-1/(2n)+O(n^{-2})$, a labeled equivalent, a Lambert-$W$ inverse with
ceiling-safe brackets, and $\mathrm{A156808}(13)=21{,}593{,}488{,}017$.  Its prime
probability $e^{-3}+o(1)$ improves the bound $e^{-4}$ stated by Bassino,
Bouvel, F\'eray, Gerin and Pierrot (arXiv, 2024) but follows from their own
lemmas, and is not claimed as new; the write credits their Proposition~5.14
for the method of Part~I's symmetric-matching bound, cited by neither
manuscript.  Part~II, a sequel, proves
$c_n=e^{-3/2}h_n(1-\frac{15}{4n}+O(n^{-2}))$,
$g_n=e^{-3/2}h_n(1-\frac{13}{4n}+O(n^{-2}))$ and
$c_n/g_n=1-\frac1{2n}-\frac1{n^2}+O(n^{-3})$, from a prime probability
$e^{-3}(1-5/n+O(n^{-2}))$ and a canonical split-tree transfer, with first
corrections for automorphism-weighted and labeled counts and a refined
inverse.  Its transfer to every fixed order is conditional on the matching
prime expansion, and the second coefficients $\beta-277/32$ and
$\beta-297/32$ depend on an unknown prime constant $\beta$.  Open: $\beta$,
an unconditional expansion beyond the first correction, effective constants
and certified thresholds, exact counts beyond order~13.  The 2026 journal
version of Bassino et al.\ was not read, so priority is bounded.  The
independent check found no error and corrected one side condition
($\log(2u)>1$ holds for every $L>0$).  The leading inverses are instances
of the transseries volume's factorial core; the brackets are not instances
of its staircase theorem.  Siblings sharing a pipeline:
\dir{a123448-permutation-graphs}, \dir{a005975-interval-graphs}.
Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{All orders refinement for unlabeled permutation graphs: factorial expansion, inverse thresholds, and sampling in OEIS A123448}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a123448-permutation-graphs}
{article.pdf}{batch~106: Permutation\_Graph\_All\_Orders\_Expansion\_Inverses\_and\_Sampling\_Source.zip (bundle Report~153)}
{Let $a_n$ count unlabeled permutation graphs on $n$ vertices (OEIS
A123448).  For every fixed $R$ the report proves
$a_n=\frac14\sum_{k<R}h_k(n-k)!+O_R((n-R)!)$, with a coefficient generator
that uses only finite unrooted and vertex-rooted counts, $k!h_k\in\mathbb Z$
and $h_0,\ldots,h_7=1,-4,-1,-94/3,\ldots,-40114096/315$.  The main step is a
finite-polynomial approximation (almost every permutation realizer has a
recoverable large prime quotient with bounded decorations), after which
Borinsky's factorial composition theorem transfers every fixed order.
It adds strict monotonicity,
threshold windows for every fixed order that keep the rounding, an explicit
second Lambert-$W$ shift, and the all-orders total-variation distance
$4/n-15/n^2+\cdots$ between the graph of a uniform permutation and a
uniform graph class.  The write adds $h_8=-3687969659/2520$ from the OEIS
term $a_{10}=524572$, which the independent check confirmed by its own
exhaustive enumeration, and classifies the inverses against the transseries
volume (the centre an instance of its factorial core, the two shifts of its
core reversion; the windows proved directly).  Two statements of the
placement record are corrected: the phrase ``prepared for private review''
occurs in no delivered file, only packaging negations; and Johnston's 2020
question whether $a_n=o(n!)$ was already answered in the negative by
Bassino, Bouvel, F\'eray, Gerin and Pierrot (arXiv, 2024), whose proof gives
$a_n\ge n!/30$ for large $n$, so the report adds the constant $1/4$ and
every fixed order, not the dichotomy.  Priority for the leading term is not
claimed: El-Zahar and Sauer's $n!/2$ for two-dimensional posets (1988) is a
close antecedent, and whether it implies $1/4$ is open.  Not claimed:
effective constants, certified thresholds, uniformity in $R$; the 1990
table values for $n=15,\ldots,20$ are quoted, not verified (the
independent check corrected a note on their floating-point format).
Siblings: \dir{a156808-circle-graphs}, \dir{a005975-interval-graphs}.
Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{Asymptotics and inversion for unlabeled interval graphs: leading equivalent and second order corrections}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a005975-interval-graphs}
{article.pdf}{batch~106: A005975\_A005976\_Interval\_Graph\_Asymptotics\_and\_Inversion\_Source.zip (bundle Report~133)}
{Let $I_n$ and $C_n$ count all and connected unlabeled interval graphs on
$n$ vertices (OEIS A005975, A005976) and $F_n$ the Fishburn numbers
(A022493).  From Hwang and Jin's Fishburn asymptotics (their (1.1),
Theorem~25(ii) and Corollary~29, located at the write) and Gallai's
orientation theorems, the report proves
$C_n=F_n/2-F_{n-1}-F_{n-2}\log n+O(F_{n-2})$ and
$I_n=F_n/2-F_{n-1}/2-F_{n-2}\log n+O(F_{n-2})$, then the constant:
$-(\log n+b_0)F_{n-2}+o(F_{n-2})$ with $b_0=\log(6/\pi^2)+\gamma+1\approx
1.0795$.  It adds the factorial form
$K\,n!\sqrt n\,(6/\pi^2)^n(1+\alpha/n-\beta\log n/n^2+O(n^{-2}))$ and an
inverse threshold kept between two ceilings, with constants and onset not
effective.  The proof locates the fibres behind the logarithm: simplicial
vertices are the diagonal cells of the Fishburn matrix, and the dominant
fibres are exactly two-to-one.  The write read Hanlon (1982), who lists the
asymptotic number of interval graphs as an open problem, Acan, and Bukh and
Jeffs: none states $I_n\sim F_n/2$.  This settles the placement record's
caveat that the leading term might be classical, without establishing
priority.  Bukh and Jeffs's
$\log I_n=n\log n-(1+\log(\pi^2/6))n+O(\log n)$ is sharpened.  With the data
extended to $n=30$ from Hanlon's tables, the normalized remainder for $C_n$
crosses $b_0$ between $n=26$ and~27, so thirty terms neither confirm nor
refute $b_0$; the independent check corrected the quoted range of
extrapolated limits.  The Lambert start is an instance of the transseries
volume's factorial core; the Newton steps and the bracket are analogues.
Open: the remainder beyond $b_0$, a full power-log expansion, effective
constants, the $n^{-2}$ coefficient of the factorial form.  Shares the
Hwang--Jin input with \dir{a336070-weak-ascents}; siblings
\dir{a156808-circle-graphs}, \dir{a123448-permutation-graphs}.  Unrefereed;
not formalized.}

\entry{Diagonal Euler transforms (OEIS A252782 and A270917)}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a252782-diagonal-euler-transforms}
{article.pdf}{batch~106: Diagonal\_Euler\_Transforms\_Corrected\_Growth\_Crossover\_and\_Inverses\_Source.zip (bundle Report~147, base) + Growing\_Euler\_Crossover\_and\_Attenuation\_Inverse\_Source.zip (bundle Report~148)}
{Let $A_\varepsilon(n)=[x^n]\prod_{j\ge1}(1-\varepsilon x^j)^{-\varepsilon j^n}$:
the Euler transform of the $n$th powers, OEIS A252782 ($\varepsilon=1$),
and its distinct-colour analogue A270917 ($\varepsilon=-1$).  Both entries
state Kot\v{e}\v{s}ovec's conjecture (2016) that
$A_\varepsilon(n)^{1/n^2}\to e^{1/e}$.  Part~I proves that the limit is
$3^{1/3}$: $\log x/x$ is maximal at $x=e$ over the reals but at $j=3$ over
the integers.  The write adds
$3^{mn}/m^m\le A_\varepsilon(n)\le2^{n-1}3^{n^2/3}$ ($n=3m+r$) with an
elementary proof, so $A_\varepsilon(n)^{1/n^2}<e^{1/e}$ for every
$n\ge414$, and by exact computation for every $n\ge8$: the conjecture is
refuted, not merely sharpened, while the listed terms are right; nothing
was sent to the OEIS.  Part~I then gives an exact expansion over
logarithmic atoms, the three residue amplitudes with every exponentially
small sector and a uniform tail bound, the first unrestricted-minus-distinct
difference, power-log inverses to every order, convergent inverses of
finite sector models, asymptotic residue-aware integer brackets, strict
increase for every $n\ge1$, and a crossover for a separately varying
exponent $\tau$ with bounded $z=m^2(8/9)^\tau$, including a term of
non-integer order $m^{-\delta}$, $\delta=2.0959\ldots$, from parts of size
five.  Part~II answers in part Part~I's question on growing $z$: for
$z\le Sm^{3/4}$ the coefficient acquires the attenuation
$\exp(-8s^{4/3}/9)$, $s=z/m^{3/4}$, with quarter-power corrections and a
term of order $m^{-0.6849\ldots}$, and the attenuation of a smooth model is
inverted on compact windows; exact crossings are localized, uniqueness is
not claimed.  The write corrected one printed decimal of Part~II's
exponent; the independent check reclassified Part~I's power-log inverse as
an instance, not an analogue, of the transseries volume's core reversion.
No Lambert $W$ occurs.  Open: effective onsets and certified thresholds,
all orders in the crossover regimes, $s\to\infty$, integer exponents,
priority for $3^{1/3}$.  Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{Iterated Euler diagonals (OEIS A290353 and A290354)}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a290354-iterated-euler-diagonals}
{article.pdf}{batch~106: Report225.zip (bundle Report~225, base) + Report226.zip (bundle Report~226) + Report228.zip (bundle Report~228)}
{Let $F_0(z)=z$ and $1+F_{m+1}(z)=\prod_{d\ge1}(1-z^d)^{-[z^d]F_m(z)}$, so
that $A(n,m)=[z^n]F_m(z)$ counts unlabeled rooted trees with $n$ leaves,
all at level $m$ (OEIS A290353), and $a_n=A(n,n)$ is A290354.  Part~I
proves $a_n\sim c\,(2n/\pi)^nn^{-4/3}$ with $c=T^{1/3}e^{-B}h(1)>0$,
$T=\pi/2$, where $h$ is the smooth, strictly positive density of a positive
measure whose Laplace transform is the entire parametrization of
$w\mapsto e^w-1$ by its normalized Fatou coordinate: the form
Kot\v{e}\v{s}ovec conjectured on A290354 (2017), the digits
$c\approx4.4923$ remaining uncertified.  It adds proportional-height
asymptotics for $n/m$ in compact subsets of $(0,\infty)$, fixed and
square-root height shifts with a floor phase, and a Lambert-$W$ inverse at
exact values.  Part~II proves the first correction
$1+(-\frac1{18}\log^2n+A\log n+C)/n+O(n^{-7/5})$, with $A$ and $C$ exact in
$h(1),h'(1),h''(1)$ and a clock constant $\kappa$ in closed form in $\pi$,
$\log2$ and Catalan's constant.  Part~III proves
$A(n,m)/(2T^{-n}m^{n-1})\to e^{-1/(3\lambda)}$ when
$m/(n\log n)\to\lambda\in(0,\infty)$, to every fixed inverse-logarithm
order with rational-plus-zeta coefficients, through $h(0)=2$, a Hankel
representation of $h$ near zero and a smooth germ $t^{t/3}h(t)$.  Part~III
conjectures $I(\beta)=\beta2^{\beta/3}h(\beta)$ for the amplitude $I$ of
the labelled analogue \dir{a139383-iterated-bell-diagonals} (the sides
agree to eight digits at $\beta=1$, both uncertified); the write proves a
consequence for the labelled array conditional on it (reciprocal note
there).  Open: validated $c$ and $h$, higher corrections, effective constants,
$n/m\to\infty$, real analyticity of $h$.  Kaneiwa, Prellberg and
Dudko--Sauzin are credited; no priority for the parabolic method.  The
independent check found no mathematical error, corrected two sentences
overstating the novelty of Part~II's restated interface, and recomputed
$c=4.4923298971$ (uncertified).  The leading inverses are instances of the
transseries volume's factorial core.  Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{Strict partition chains and their asymptotic expansions}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a005121-strict-partition-chains}
{article.pdf}{batch~106: Report223.zip (bundle Report~223)}
{OEIS A005121 (Lengyel's numbers) counts the strict chains from the least
to the greatest element of the partition lattice $\Pi_n$.  With $L=\log2$
and $f_n=(n!)^2(2L)^{-n}n^{-1-L/3}$ the report proves
$z_n=Cf_n\bigl(\sum_{j\le M}c_j(L)n^{-j}+O(n^{-M-1})\bigr)$ for every fixed
$M$, with $c_j\in\mathbb Q[L]$ ($c_1=L/6$; $c_2$, $c_3$, $c_4$ explicit)
and $C\approx1.0986858055$ (uncertified), directly from the Stirling
recurrence: a factorial majorant, a block-defect kernel expansion and a
weighted first-difference operator of limiting norm $2\log2-1<1$.  The
same argument on the disk $|q-1|\le1/16$ of the length marker gives every
fixed cumulant of the chain length.  It adds a Lambert-$W_0$ inverse with a
triangular recursion, eventual recovery of $n$ from $\log z_n$, and
two-ceiling threshold enclosures.  Against the transseries volume (write):
the core is an instance of its factorial core, not of its Lambert core, the
recursion of its core reversion, the recovery of its staircase rounding;
the enclosure is proved directly, with a second route through the
staircase theorem.  The leading equivalent and $C>0$ (Babai--Lengyel,
1992), the leading mean and the central limit theorem (Van Cutsem--Ycart,
1994) are prior and re-proved; formal higher corrections were announced by
Prellberg (2002), and no priority is claimed for the coefficients.  The
write records that OEIS A005121's formula line ``E.g.f.\ satisfies
$Z(z)=\frac12(Z(e^z-1)-z)$'' (in the entry since 2003) has the wrong sign:
the correct equation is $2Z(z)=z+Z(e^z-1)$; nothing was sent to the OEIS.
A formal Laplace link with the amplitude $I$ of
\dir{a139383-iterated-bell-diagonals}, $C=L^{L/3-1}I(L)/2$, is an open
question; the independent check traced its $1.9\cdot10^{-4}$ numerical gap
to the $(\log n)^2/n$ term of that report's first correction, after which
the agreement is about $10^{-5}$ (not a proof).  Open: certified constants,
dependence on the order, larger marking regions, distributional
refinements, Van Cutsem and Ycart's variance conjecture.  Unrefereed; not
formalized.}

\entry{Subdiagonal and superdiagonal partitions (OEIS A238875 and A238873)}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a238873-diagonal-partitions}
{article.pdf}{batch~107: Two\_Partition\_Conjectures\_Proofs\_and\_Quantitative\_Extensions\_Source.zip (bundle Report~156, base) + Subdiagonal\_Partitions\_All\_Orders\_Expansion\_and\_Integer\_Inverses\_Source.zip (Report~159) + Superdiagonal\_Partitions\_Exact\_Exponential\_Growth\_and\_Inverse\_Source.zip (Report~158) + Superdiagonal\_Partitions\_Airy\_Correction\_and\_Limit\_Shape\_Source.zip (Report~160) + Superdiagonal\_Partitions\_Full\_Prefactor\_and\_Refined\_Inverse\_Source.zip (Report~162)}
{For partitions written $\lambda_1\le\cdots\le\lambda_k$, let $s_r(n)$ count
those with $\lambda_i\le i+r$ ($s_0$ is OEIS A238875, subdiagonal) and $A(n)$
those with $\lambda_i\ge i$ (A238873, superdiagonal).  Part~I proves both
conjectures of Archibald, Blecher, Elizalde and Knopfmacher (Afr.\ Mat.\ 2025;
journal conjectures, not OEIS ones): $0\le P(R\le r)-s_r(n)/p(n)=O(1/\log n)$
uniformly in $r\ge0$ for Dyson's rank $R$, so $s_0(n)/p(n)\to1/2$, and
$\liminf\log A(n)/\sqrt n\ge B_*=\sqrt{\pi^2/3+2\log^22}>\pi/\sqrt3$, so
$q(n)/A(n)\to0$; also a logistic crossover in $r/\sqrt n$ and a Lambert-$W$
inverse.  Part~II expands $s_0(n)/p(n)$ to every fixed order in
$\beta=\pi/\sqrt{6n}$ ($c_0=1/2$, $c_1=-1/8$, $c_2$, $c_3$ explicit), a second
proof at $r=0$ only.  Part~III proves the exact rate
$B\sqrt n-O(n^{1/3})\le\log A(n)\le B\sqrt n+O(1)$,
$B=\sqrt{\pi^2/3+4\log^22}=2.2829\ldots$, replacing Part~I's $B_*$; Part~IV
the Airy term $\log A(n)=B\sqrt n-Dn^{1/6}+o(n^{1/6})$, $D=1.5507\ldots$ (via
Mallein's published killed-walk estimates), and the limit shape; Part~V the
full equivalent $A(n)\sim Kn^{-11/12}e^{B\sqrt n-Dn^{1/6}}$, $K=1.2705\ldots$,
\emph{conditional} on the unrefereed preprint arXiv:2607.27504v1 (Yu-Tian Li)
and labelled so throughout; Parts~III--IV are the unconditional statements.
Merged from five manuscripts in dependency order; six restated proofs are
printed once, with pointers, and the local limits of Parts~IV and~V as two
routes.  The write proves that a partition has choosable initial intervals
iff it is superdiagonal, settling Gus Wiseman's conjecture in A238873,
which no manuscript addresses (and those in A387118, A388711, A387112); the
argument is not new: Cl\'ement Garrot posted it in A387112 on 31~August 2026
(credited).  It also derives the $r=0$ defect
$\beta/4+(7/16-3/(2\pi^2))\beta^2+O(\beta^3)$, answering Part~I's question.
Part~V repairs two published displays (the conjecture paper's Theorem~2.1, a
stray factor $q$ in \v{S}tampach--\v{S}\v{t}ov\'{\i}\v{c}ek).  The independent
check corrected one wording (``decays like'' for an upper bound).  No explicit
constants or onsets, nothing at fixed $r\ge1$ beyond $O(1/\log n)$, no
all-orders expansion of $A(n)$; nothing refuted.  Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{Factorial-weighted Stirling products (OEIS A192563 and A192561)}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a192563-factorial-stirling-products}
{article.pdf}{batch~107: Diagonal\_Poly\_Cauchy\_All\_Orders\_and\_Inverse\_Thresholds\_Source.zip (bundle Report~154, base) + Matched\_Cycles\_Exact\_Certificates\_All\_Orders\_and\_Inverses\_Source.zip (Report~155)}
{Two members of Munarini's 2011 OEIS family of sums of $k!$ times two
Stirling numbers.  Part~I treats A192563,
$a_n=\sum_m c(n,m)(m+1)^n$ with $c$ the unsigned first-kind numbers, the
diagonal of the poly-Cauchy array A344639 of B\'enyi and Ram\'irez: with the
exact saddle $r=\log n-\log\log n-\log\log\log n+o(1)$ it proves an expansion
of $a_n$ to every fixed order in $r/n$ with explicit Gaussian contraction
coefficients, the same for $A_{n,k}$ uniformly for $\alpha\le k/n\le\beta$
(nothing for fixed rows or columns, nor for any regime with $k/n\to0$ or
$k/n\to\infty$, a non-claim the independent check made explicit), threshold brackets from a smooth model
and a nested-logarithm inverse; its re-proof of B\'enyi--Ram\'irez's Stirling
product shows that $a_n$ equals Munarini's defining form for every $n$.
Part~II treats A192561, $b_n=\sum_kk!\,c(n+1,k+1)^2$, as a Gaussian Fock
norm: an exact positive expansion with an explicit tail at every $n\ge1$, a
rational-centre certificate that isolates $b_{10}=1273270076895432$, the same
certificate for finite products with negative real zeros, an expansion to
every fixed order in $1/w$, $w=W_0(en)$, with rational coefficients through
$A_{10}$, and a nested-Lambert inverse with a two-ceiling bracket.  The two
manuscripts use different methods and do not cite each other; they are
merged as one family, read as Fock pairings, with a notation dictionary.
The write adds, for the third member A192562, which neither treats, the
identity $c_n=a_n-nA_{n-1,n}=\sum_mc(n-1,m)((m+2)^n-(m+1)^n)$ for its
terms $c_n$, with proof; its asymptotics stay open.  Part~II's leading inverse is an instance
of the transseries volume's factorial core; no bracket is an instance of its
staircase theorem.  Fixed orders only; no effective constants or onsets, so
neither asymptotic inverse is a rounding rule; the $1/n$-scale correction for
A192561 is not computed; Part~I's novelty is provisional on Yakymiv's 2019
paper, and Part~II claims no priority for its general certificate.  Nothing
refuted or corrected, and the independent check found no error.  The two classical identities behind Part~I's re-proof
are formal in the Lean \dir{Analysis/FabiusFunction} tree; the report's own
statements are not.  Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{Stirling transforms of the Catalan numbers (OEIS A064856 and A086662)}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a064856-stirling-catalan-transforms}
{article.pdf}{batch~107: Report205.zip (bundle Report~205, corrected revision~2, base) + Report207.zip (Report~207)}
{For the Catalan numbers $C_k$, both Stirling transforms,
$\sum_kS(n,k)C_k$ (OEIS A064856) and $\sum_kc(n,k)C_k$ with unsigned
first-kind numbers (A086662), studied from one Catalan density integral.
Part~I (second kind) proves an expansion to every fixed order in
$W(n/4)/n$ with $n!$ kept exact and rational corrections in the Lambert
saddle, the Bell comparison $\log(a_n/B_n)\sim(\log4)n/\log n$, an
all-fixed-order inverse with a two-ceiling enclosure, and that the single
ceiling $\lceil I_0(a_n)\rceil$ is eventually $n+1$; it credits Spiridonov's
2004 Conjecture~3.3, the moment bound $B_k\le M_{2k}\le a_k$, to Bauer and
Golinelli (2001), with no novelty claim.  Part~II (first kind) proves a
resummed expansion with relative error $O_J(n^{-J-1})$, the expansion in
$1/\log n$ with factorially divergent coefficients and an exact
incomplete-gamma completion, explicit and resummed inverses with two
ceilings, a local cycle law with a central limit theorem, the
total-variation distance $\sim3\sqrt{2/\pi}/(8\sqrt{\log n})$ to Ewens$(4)$,
and a boundary Gamma$(3/2)$ law.  Merged from two manuscripts, Part~II
filling the gap Part~I leaves; the shared beta integral is printed in both,
with a pointer.  Revision~1 of Report~205 was never received.  The write
proves that Spiridonov's numerical model $a_k\approx(1.47^k+1)A_k$ is not an
asymptotic (the ratio tends to $0$ for every fixed base $\beta>1$), checks
$B_k\le M_{2k}\le a_k$ on the thirteen listed terms of A094149, classifies
the inverses against the transseries volume, and corrects, with a dated
note, Part~II's sentence that Part~I ``expressly deferred'' the first-kind
transform.  The independent check separated the volume's quadratic part
$h_{\mathrm{vol}}=0$ from Part~I's $h(\rho)$ (notation only).  Fixed truncation
orders only, existential constants and onsets, no single-ceiling rule, no
relative asymptotics for $M_{2k}$ (later manuscripts of the same bundle,
still unplaced, are named as leads).  Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{Growth bounds for maximal families of disjoint partitions}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a068598-disjoint-partition-families}
{article.pdf}{batch~107: Maximal\_Disjoint\_Partition\_Families\_Growth\_and\_Inverse\_Bounds\_Source.zip (bundle Report~163)}
{For $a(n)$, the number of inclusion-maximal families of pairwise disjoint
strict partitions of $n$ (OEIS A068598), proves
$1/5\le\liminf\log a(n)/(n\log n)\le\limsup\le1/4$.  The upper bound
$a(n)\le3^{n-1}n^{\lfloor(n-2)/4\rfloor}$ ($n\ge2$) is elementary and
explicit, from an anchor and free-label encoding and the distinct-parts mass
inequality (Spencer, via Nathanson); the lower constant $1/5$ injects queens
configurations and rests on Luria and Simkin's theorem, while an elementary
construction gives $1/7$.  Also first-passage inverses with constants
between $4$ and $5$ and finite Lambert brackets, without monotonicity.  Two
statements of the OEIS entry are corrected (nothing submitted): Meeussen's
graph comment counts \emph{maximal} cliques (at $n=8$: six vertices,
21 nonempty complete subgraphs, $a(8)=3$), and Beregovsky's fits
$A\exp(Bn^c)$ with $c>1$ (2022, 2026) are not asymptotic: each exceeds the
proved upper bound for $n\ge1215$ resp.\ $n\ge8579$, signs certified in
ball arithmetic at the independent check.  The write adds Simkin's 2023
improvement of the lower bound's linear term (read at abstract level), the
two Lambert roots as instances of the transseries volume's factorial core
and the brackets as analogues only, and an exact free-label identity that
locates the families any proof of the constant $1/4$ would have to count.
The independent check corrected the write's Simkin interval to
$[0.7096,0.7109]$ and one sentence on the block construction.  No limit,
equivalent or expansion; the linear term belongs to a lower bound only; the
monotonicity of $a(n)$ and the count of quadruple decompositions are open.
Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{Odious and evil integer partitions}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a067590-odious-evil-partitions}
{article.pdf}{batch~107: Odious\_and\_Evil\_Partitions\_All\_Orders\_and\_Inverses\_Source.zip (bundle Report~165)}
{For partitions into odious or evil parts (positive integers with odd or
even binary digit sum; OEIS A067590, A067591) and into distinct such parts
(A116492, A116491), proves Kot\v{e}\v{s}ovec's July~2025 conjectures in
A067590 and A116492, $p_-(n)\sim K_-n^{-5/8}e^{\pi\sqrt{n/3}}$ and
$p_{-,1}(n)\sim n^{-3/4}e^{\pi\sqrt{n/6}}/(2\cdot12^{1/4})$, with the evil
counterparts, expansions to every fixed order and a Bessel comparison with
error smaller than every power of $n$; for each fixed multiplicity cap $m$
the odious-to-evil ratio is $m+1+O(n^{-R})$ for every $R$.  The methods are
sectorial Mellin flatness of $\prod_j(1-e^{-2^jw})$, the eta
transformation, uniform arc bounds and a completed Hankel contour; also a
Lambert-$W$ inverse to every fixed order and two-ceiling threshold
enclosures.  The continuation of the Thue--Morse Dirichlet series
(Allouche--Cohen) and the evaluation of $d=D'(0)$ (Allouche) are credited;
the nine printed digits of $K_-=0.22186\ldots$, which the write supported
by two concordant floating evaluations, were enclosed at the independent
check by an interval computation of $\log Q$ (given $d=-\log Q$; a machine
computation, not a formal proof).  The write relates the report to
the Thue--Morse atlas in \dir{Analysis/FabiusFunction}, whose real-axis
flatness and shifted Dirichlet series are formal in Lean there, proving a
bridge $D(s,1/2)=(1-2^s)D(s)$; classifies the inverses as instances of the
transseries volume; and proves that the ratio law is not uniform in
$1\le m\le n$; the independent check made three of its Lean citations
precise (one statement holds only for $\Re s>0$, one needs a range
hypothesis, one step is formal only).  No growing-cap uniformity, no term
beyond all orders, no other certified constants, no onsets, no priority
claim; nothing refuted.
Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{Exact generating function for pop-stacked permutations: poles, differential algebraicity and faster enumeration}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a307030-pop-stacked-permutations}
{article.pdf}{batch~108: Pop\_Stacked\_Permutations\_Exact\_Generating\_Function\_and\_Poles\_Source.zip (bundle Report~164, base) + Pop\_Stacked\_Permutations\_Asymptotics\_and\_Inverses\_Source.zip (bundle Report~161) + Pop\_Stacked\_Permutations\_Run\_Distribution\_Source.zip (bundle Report~166)}
{For pop-stacked permutations (OEIS A307030; by runs A309993), the base,
bundle Report~164, derives from the run-overlap characterization an
explicit exponential generating function, a quotient $P=N/D$ of explicit
entire functions (near $0$, a ratio of $\sin(zr/4)$, $\cos(zr/4)$ terms
with $r=\sqrt{4e^z-1}$).  Every genuine pole is simple, with residue
$-2e^{-2\zeta}(4e^\zeta-1)/(\zeta+2)$; there are infinitely many positive
poles, so $P$ is not D-finite and $(p_n)$ is not P-recursive, as
Claesson, Gu\dh mundsson and Pantone (CGP, arXiv:1908.08910) predicted
numerically; yet $P$ is differentially algebraic (an annihilator of order
at most two, minimality not claimed).  A Riccati equation computes
$p_0,\ldots,p_N$ in $O(N^2)$ arithmetic operations against $O(N^4)$ in the
cited papers (not a bit-complexity claim).  The dominant pole
$\rho=1.1134390417\ldots$ and amplitude
$C=2e^{-2\rho}(4e^\rho-1)/(\rho(\rho+2))=0.6956885490\ldots$ are
rationally certified and confirm CGP's decimals.  Report~161 is a second
route to $p_n=Cn!\rho^{-n}(1+O(q^n))$ by a positive-operator argument
without the formula, with the elementary bound
$(24/23)^{1/4}\le\rho\le2$ and all fixed inverse orders.  Report~166 adds
the bivariate generating function, run mean $\mu n+\beta$
($\mu=0.44214954918886\ldots$) and variance $vn+\gamma$, a central and an
$O(1/n)$ lattice local limit theorem, and an alternate proof of CGP's
Conjecture~2 (fixed-run denominators); it credits a prior proof posted by
Shivam Patel on MathDB (20~August 2026), which the write read and checked:
Patel's proof is first, Report~166's a different one.  The write proves
(Remark~5.1, interval arithmetic) that CGP's printed list of positive poles
(Section~3.3, p.~10) skips $\rho_{12}=5.33887671271979\ldots$, every printed
digit of the other fifteen being right (their repository file lists
$\rho_{12}$), and finds their three printed complex pairs correct to 62--66
decimals, after which each drops two digits; it credits Part~II's
interleaving bound to Asinowski--Banderier--Hackl's Theorem~12 and moves
twelve open items (effective gap and onsets, nonreal poles, a minimal ODE,
bit complexity, certified $\beta,\gamma$, among others) to its questions.
The independent check re-proved Remark~5.1 by its own phase argument,
confirmed Patel's proof step by step and the recurrence against all 450
b-file terms, and corrected the write's description of ABH's Theorem~11
(its statement does include the costs Part~I cites) and of Patel's MathDB
status.  No effective gap, error constants or onsets, no pole-sum
expansion, no certified $\beta,\gamma$; OEIS terms are CC~BY-SA~4.0.
Merged from three manuscripts in batch~108.  Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{Airy logarithmic asymptotics for bounded-indegree labelled DAGs: exact parking reduction and a Lambert-$W$ inverse}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a397711-bounded-indegree-dags}
{article.pdf}{batch~108: Bounded\_Indegree\_DAGs\_Airy\_Asymptotics\_and\_Inverses\_Source.zip (bundle Report~157)}
{For labelled simple DAGs on $[n]$ with every indegree at most two (OEIS
A397711), proves
$\log a_n=2n\log n-n-\kappa n^{1/3}+o(n^{1/3})$ with
$\kappa=3\zeta/2^{1/3}=5.567271244467717\ldots$, $-\zeta$ the largest
zero of the Airy function, and the threshold inverse
$b+\kappa b^{1/3}/(2\log b+1)+o(b^{1/3}/\log b)$ with
$b=y/[2W_0(y/(2\sqrt e))]$, $y=\log x$.  The same argument gives the
$n^{1/3}$ Airy correction, with factor $(c-1)^{2/3}$, for every fixed
indegree bound $c\ge2$.  The route: sink inclusion--exclusion, then Kung
and Yan's generalized parking functions write the count as a Poisson
excursion partition function with potential $q_i=\log(i/(i-1))\sim1/i$,
and geometric blocks transfer Mallein's homogeneous area-tilt bounds to
this singular potential (the same Mallein lemmas as Part~IV of
\dir{a238873-diagonal-partitions}; the Airy zero is that of
\dir{a082161-airy-amplitudes}).  Exact enumeration, generalized parking
and the homogeneous Airy estimates are prior inputs.  The theorems hold
modulo Mallein's Lemmas~2.6--2.7 (arXiv:1307.4496v3), whose statements the
write and the independent check read and whose proofs nobody here
verified: the stated trust boundary.  Exact counts to $n=100$ are
consistent with $o(n^{1/3})$ but cannot confirm $\kappa$ (windowed fits give
$-5.50$ to $-5.54$ against $-\kappa=-5.567$, recorded as no confirmation),
so the finite table is not evidence for the constant; the independent check
withdrew the write's reading that the fits suggest no power prefactor.  The
write also shows that the OEIS entry's bound $g(n-1)^n$ is off by an
exponential factor (nothing submitted).  A logarithmic asymptotic only: no power
prefactor, amplitude, $O(\log n)$ remainder or all-orders expansion, no
uniformity in growing $c$, no certified inverse bracket.  Steinsky's 2003
paper on coding bounded-indegree DAGs was not inspected in full, a
priority limitation the report states.  OEIS terms are CC~BY-SA~4.0.
Reciprocal notes went to \dir{a082161-airy-amplitudes} and
\dir{a238873-diagonal-partitions}.  Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{Asymptotics of assemblies of nested cycles: exact counts and fixed-order marked saddle expansions}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a308338-nested-cycle-assemblies}
{article.pdf}{batch~108: Nested\_Cycle\_Assemblies\_Asymptotics\_and\_Inverses\_Source.zip (bundle Report~167)}
{For $P(z)=\prod_{m\ge1}(1+z^m/m)$ and $F(z,u)=\exp\{u(P(z)-1)\}$, the
labelled counts $a_n=n![z^n]F(z,1)$ are OEIS A308338, and the coefficient
of $u^k$ counts assemblies with $k$ nested-cycle components (A392471;
components A007838).  With $c=e^{-\gamma}$, proves
$a_n\sim Cn!\,n^{c/2-3/4}e^{2\sqrt{cn}}$, $C=e^{c(1/2-\log2)-1}
c^{1/4-c/2}/(2\sqrt\pi)=0.0947779253\ldots$, and a complex-uniform
expansion near $u=1$ to every fixed order in $n^{-1/2}$ with coefficients
polynomial in $\log n$, the first two explicit; a whole-circle estimate
controls the secondary arcs.  For the component count $K_n$, the mean is
$\sqrt{cn}+(c/2)\log n+m_0+\cdots$ and the variance
$\sqrt{cn}/2+(c/2)\log n+v_0+\cdots$, so
$\mathbf E K_n/\sqrt n\to e^{-\gamma/2}=0.7493\ldots$; also a
rounding-safe Lambert-$W$ inverse.  This proves the conjecture of
Riedel's January~2026 note: its boxed statement is
$\mathbf E[X]\sim N\sqrt n$ with $N$ to be determined, and Theorem~7.1
gives $N=e^{-\gamma/2}$.  The note's alternative, $n^M$ with $M$ ``close
to but not equal to'' $1/2$ (numerically $M\ge0.4704567259$), is refuted,
and its $N\approx0.7719351399$ is not the constant (it equals
$\mathbf E K_{4500}/\sqrt{4500}$ to $2.5\cdot10^{-10}$); the write proves
(Remark~7.3) that finite-$n$ ratios overshoot and two-point exponents
undershoot $1/2$ by order $\log n/\sqrt n$, the $(c/2)\log n$ term read as
a power.  Report~167 called Riedel's value a finite-data estimate without
saying the question is settled; the placement record (and an earlier
version of this entry) misquoted the note as conjecturing
$\mathbf E K_n\sim Nn^M$ with fitted $M\approx0.4705$.  The write's
Remark~10.1 records that Erlihson and Granovsky's covariance display
(preprint eq.~(4.54) $=$ published (4.18), both read) gives, at $p=1$,
$C=3$, $u=1$, the negative variance $3/e-18/e^2$ and contradicts
Corollary~7.2 in this model, gives the corrected denominator
$C\,\Gamma(p+2)$ and locates the dropped $C$; the independent check
confirmed the correction against exact finite-$n$ variances at $n=20000$
and found that their (5.72) as printed holds only for $C=1$.  The report
does not use the display.
The Gaussian regime with variance $\sim\sqrt{cn}/2$ and the component
asymptotics $B_m/m!\to e^{-\gamma}$ are prior (Erlihson--Granovsky;
Greene--Knuth).  Fixed orders only: no beyond-all-orders terms,
effective constants, certified $C$ or local limit; the source's five
questions stand, with a sixth from the write (whether Erlihson and
Granovsky's proof yields the corrected displays).  OEIS terms are
CC~BY-SA~4.0.  Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{All-orders asymptotics for square lonesum decomposable matrices}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a299907-lonesum-decomposable-matrices}
{article.pdf}{batch~108: A299907\_All\_Orders\_Matrix\_Asymptotics\_and\_Inversion\_Source.zip (bundle Report~132)}
{For square binary matrices that split, after independent row and column
permutations, into lonesum blocks and zero lines (OEIS A299907;
equivalently induced-$P_5$-free bipartite graphs on two fixed labelled
shores of size $n$), proves from Kamano's exact bivariate generating
function (credited)
$a_n=(n!)^2C\ell^{-2n}n^{-5/4}e^{\beta\sqrt n}(1+\sum_jc_jn^{-j/2})$ to
every fixed order, $\ell=\log2$, $\beta=\sqrt{2/\ell}$,
$C=0.3394739586\ldots$ explicit, $c_1=-0.5317063378\ldots$,
$c_2=-0.1635701301\ldots$, with a Gaussian recipe for every further
coefficient; the proof treats the anisotropic saddle and all
complementary arcs.  Also all-orders inverse threshold brackets that keep
the rounding uncertainty inside the ceiling, the exact sum
$a_n=\sum_kk!B_kS(n+1,k+1)^2$ ($B_k$ from A000262; a second route to the
OEIS double sum) and $a_{n+1}\ge2a_n$.  Ordinary lonesum matrices are
Khera, Lundberg and Melczer's (2021), a different saddle; Khera's 2021
dissertation was not inspected, a stated priority limitation.  Not
claimed: effective remainders or onsets, certified finite-$n$ bounds,
rectangular aspect ratios, a block-number limit law, beyond-all-orders
terms.  The write proves the two structural facts the source states
without proof (each nontrivial component is a connected chain graph; the
forbidden configurations are exactly the induced $P_5$), evaluates the
source's recipe at order three, $c_3=-0.26674650591\ldots$ in closed form
(the independent check added $c_4=1.00342017659\ldots$), classifies the
inverses against the transseries volume, and records that A299906's
example prints $T(5,4)$ as 90446 for 90946 (nothing submitted); the check,
which classified every binary matrix up to $5\times5$, found no error.
Khera's dissertation and the shape of the $c_j$ are open questions.  OEIS
terms are CC~BY-SA~4.0.  Reciprocal notes went to
\dir{a122399-surjection-diagonal} and \dir{a321941-asymptotic-coefficient-integrality}
(whose ``A000262 occurs nowhere else'' became stale).  Unrefereed; not
formalized.}

\entry{Sparse binary matrices with line sums at most two: exact counts and all fixed-order asymptotics}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a197458-line-sum-two-matrices}
{article.pdf}{batch~108: Sparse\_Binary\_Matrices\_All\_Orders\_and\_Inverses\_Source.zip (bundle Report~168)}
{For $n\times n$ binary matrices with at most two ones in every row and
column (OEIS A197458), proves
$a_n\sim(n!)^2e^{2\sqrt{2n}}/(2^{5/4}\pi n^{3/4})$ with an expansion to
every fixed order in $n^{-1/2}$: $c_1=-17\sqrt2/96$, $c_2=6409/9216$,
$c_3$ and $c_4$ explicit, and a finite exact Gaussian-moment rule for every
later coefficient, plus a logarithmic expansion.  The route is the
labelled-component decomposition (isolated vertices, paths, even cycles)
and a modified-Bessel diagonal
$G(t)=P(t)I_0(\sqrt t(2-t)/(1-t))$, which satisfies an order-two ODE and
an integer recurrence; a sectorial Bessel remainder, a whole-circle bound
and off-circle connector estimates give the sharp fixed-order error, and
the ODE recovers $c_1,\ldots,c_4$ as a second route.  Also an explicit
Lambert-$W$ real inverse through its bounded correction and a two-ceiling
enclosure of the integer threshold.  The component generating function is
classical (the Goulden--Jackson exercise cited in A062154 and A062156, not
inspected), as are general bounded-degree enumeration (Caizergues and de
Panafieu; Greenhill, McKay and Wang); this is a specialization and
refinement, with no priority claim.  Fixed orders only: no convergence,
no exponentially small terms, existential remainder constants and
thresholds; rectangular shapes and marked components are left open.  The
write derives the source's generating function in one line from the
A062154 formula, proves the cycles-only ratio to A001499, and extends the
proof to $A_{n+d,n}$ for fixed $d$ (all orders) and to the zero rows and
columns, asymptotically independent Poisson$(1)$ counts; it shows which
inverse steps are instances of the transseries volume.  The independent
check corrected one sentence (the $e_n$ of Remark~7.3(b) are polynomials
in $1/q$, not Laurent polynomials in $q$).  OEIS terms are CC~BY-SA~4.0.
Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{Fixed positive permanent binary matrices (OEIS A089479): asymptotics, canonical block laws and reproducible certificates}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a089479-fixed-permanent-matrices}
{article.pdf}{batch~108: Fixed\_Permanent\_Matrices\_Asymptotics\_and\_Inverses\_Source.zip (bundle Report~184)}
{For each fixed $k\ge1$, $C_{n,k}$ counts labelled binary $n\times n$
matrices with permanent $k$ (the triangle A089479).  Proves
$C_{n,k}=(n!)^2k^{-1}2^{\binom n2}\{\rho^{-n}P_k(n)+O_k(2^{-n})\}$, with
$\rho=1.4880785455\ldots$ the classical root for acyclic digraphs and $P_k$
an effectively computable polynomial of exact degree $\Omega(k)$ (prime
factors with multiplicity) whose positive leading coefficient is an
explicit product of prime amplitudes.  The route: marked perfect
matchings ($kC_{n,k}=n!\,b_{n,k}$ for digraphs with
$\operatorname{per}(I+A)=k$), finite suppressed strongly connected kernels
(circulation bound $m-v\le k-2$), Robinson's component transform (read in
de Panafieu and Dovgal, credited) and a self-contained complex root
isolation.  Also the complete $P_2$, $P_3$, $P_4$ with rationally
certified coefficient intervals, explicit all-$n$ remainder constants
$M_2=1000$, $M_3=3900$, $M_4=170000$, every fixed Stirling and finite
spectral order, smooth and integer threshold inverse bounds, and a
limiting independent prime-block size law with expansions in
exponentially weighted total variation.  The case $k=1$ (the
permanent-one/acyclic-digraph correspondence) is classical.  Not
claimed: growing $k$, explicit correction laws for composite $k$, a
certified inverse solver, worldwide priority; decimal diagnostics are not
certificates, the optional C++ audits are finite, and Robinson's 1995
paper was not retrieved.  The report says that a ``second independent
rational implementation'' reproduces the certified intervals; it is not
in the package, as the write records (a dated note and Question~7).  The
write recomputed the rows $n\le6$, $k\le4$ and every certified constant,
identified the case $k=1$ with A003024's classical asymptotic, and found
Jovovic's ``up to permutation of rows/columns'' right for either action
alone, wrong jointly; the independent check counted all $2^{36}$ binary
$6\times6$ matrices, found no error and named the joint orbits (A003087).
Reciprocal notes went to \dir{a182220-source-boundary} and
\dir{a116379-bounded-identity-trees}.  Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{Asymptotics for binary matrices with zero row and column slopes}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a222959-zero-slope-matrices}
{article.pdf}{batch~108: A222959\_Zero\_Slope\_Matrices\_All\_Orders\_and\_Inverses\_Source.zip (bundle Report~194)}
{For the square diagonal $C_n$ of OEIS A222959, the $n\times n$ binary
matrices whose least-squares regression slope vanishes in every row and
every column, proves, with $S_n=n(n^2-1)/12$ for odd $n$ and
$n(n^2-1)/3$ for even $n$,
$C_n=2^{n^2}e^{-7/5}\sqrt{2/\pi}\,(2/(\pi S_n))^{n-1}
(1+171/(350n)+483051/(245000n^2)+O(n^{-3}))$, and an expansion to every
fixed order with rational, parity-free coefficients given by a finite
Wick-pairing and power-sum prescription.  The main issue is global
localization of a growing-dimensional Fourier integral, settled by a
relative exponential bound outside one central tube through a dense
arithmetic-progression inverse lemma and a defect-core decomposition.
Consequences: the parity ratio $C_{2m}/C_{2m-1}\sim6e^{-3}/(\pi m^3)$,
parity-aware smooth inverses with odd-integer threshold envelopes, a
meet-in-the-middle formula and the palindromic bound
$C_n\ge2^{\lceil n/2\rceil^2}$.  It credits the one-row fair-word counts
(Prodinger; Richomme) and the Barvinok--Hartigan framework.  The
localization constants give only an existential threshold, the known
small counts are strongly preasymptotic, and $c_3$ onwards are not
computed; no practical onset, convergence or absolute priority.  The
package's data notes say the nine square values were recomputed by a C++
meet-in-the-middle count that is not shipped (the Python replay covers
$n\le8$, $n=9$ optionally), as the write records.  The write reproduces
$C_{10}=615667968$ from the b-file the source could not reach, proves
Wiseman's 2023 ``Conjecture'' in A222955 to be Richomme's theorem, and
adds $T(n,k)\ge b_n^{\lceil k/2\rceil}$ with equality iff $k\le6$, so
$C_n\ge b_n^{\lceil n/2\rceil}$, sharper than the palindromic bound; the
independent check recounted 119 cells and corrected one classification
(the smooth inverse is also a formal instance of the transseries volume's
Lambert--$W$ template).  OEIS terms are CC~BY-SA~4.0.  A reciprocal note
went to \dir{a380592-tied-football-seasons}.  Unrefereed; not
formalized.}

\entry{All fixed-order asymptotics for square contingency tables: the density-one model and its controlled inverse}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a110058-square-contingency-tables}
{article.pdf}{batch~108: Report236.zip (bundle Report~236)}
{For labelled $n\times n$ nonnegative integer matrices with every row and
column sum $n$ (OEIS A110058), with Canfield and McKay's leading
equivalent $L_n=e^{1/4}4^{n^2}/((4\pi)^{n-1/2}n^{n-1})$ (credited),
proves that $\log(a_n/L_n)$ has a rational inverse-power expansion to
every fixed order, with first coefficients $-3/2$ and $223/32$ (relative
form $1-3/(2n)+259/(32n^2)$) and every coefficient given by a finite
connected-Wick algorithm.  The proof combines Canfield--McKay's absolute
localization theorem with Isaev's complex cumulant theorem; an
independent formal Gaussian factorization checks all eighteen cumulants
behind the two corrections.  A compact-positive-density extension has
coefficient polynomials in $1/[\lambda(1+\lambda)]$ and gives the
correction factor $\Delta=(67/6+5/(3u))/n+O(n^{-2})$, $u=\lambda(1+\lambda)$.
The report calls this ``an eventual square case'' of the Canfield--McKay
correction-factor conjecture $0<\Delta<2$; it settles one regime
(square tables, density in a fixed compact interval, $n$ large), not the
conjecture (finite sizes, nonsquare tables and vanishing or diverging
densities stay open, as the report says; Greenhill and McKay's sparse
asymptotic for $\Delta$ is prior); the write narrowed the abstract, the
Section~9 title and one sentence, keeping the first wordings, and
tabulates $\Delta(n,n;n,n)\in(0,2)$ for $n\le13$ (consistent, not
proof).  Its uniformity in the density rested on the report's own reading
of the Canfield--McKay localization proof; the write's Lemma~8.2 derives
the uniform superpolynomial bound the proofs use from the statement of
their Theorem~3 alone, and the uniform rate stays the report's reading
(a further question).  Also a two-ceiling enclosure of the inverse
counting function.  Remainder constants are not explicit, there is no
explicit starting index, and the exact computations check identities and
small counts, not finite-size errors; its comparison with Barvinok and
Hartigan's exponent is not a correction.  The independent check re-read
the cited proofs and corrected one classification (the four-term
reversion is also a formal instance of the volume's Lambert--$W$
template).  OEIS terms are CC~BY-SA~4.0.  A reciprocal note went to
\dir{a380592-tied-football-seasons}.  Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{All fixed-order asymptotics for symmetric packed matrices: dimension, trace, collision laws and controlled inverses}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a138178-symmetric-packed-matrices}
{article.pdf}{batch~108: Report238.zip (bundle Report~238)}
{For symmetric nonnegative integer matrices of total entry sum $n$ with no
zero line and ordered, distinguished positions (OEIS A138178), with
$L=\log2$ and $I_n$ the involution numbers, proves
$A_n=e^{L^2/4}I_n/(2L^{n+1})\,(1-L^2/(6\sqrt n)+(-L^2/4+L^4/18)/n
+O(n^{-3/2}))$ and every fixed further order, uniformly for complex
dimension and trace markers near one, through Jovovic's geometric
transform (OEIS, credited; the marked extension is the report's).
Dimension and trace have independent Gaussian limits on the scales
$n^{1/2}$ and $n^{1/4}$; uniformly on compact sets of collision markers,
including zero, the repeated diagonal and off-diagonal cells are
asymptotically independent Poisson($L$) and Poisson($L^2/2$) counts,
independent of the Gaussian pair, with the sharp leading total-variation
distance $e^{-L-L^2/2}L^2(2+L)/\sqrt n$ (the intake re-derived the
constant) and an $O(n^{-1})$ corrected Poisson approximation.  Also an
all-fixed-order Lambert-$W$ inverse with two-ceiling bounds.  The leading
equivalent of the binary companion A135588 and the collision mechanism
are Cameron, Prellberg and Stark's (credited).  It is the symmetric
analogue of the matrix compositions of
\dir{a261781-matrix-compositions} and uses the devices of its Part~IV
(bundle Report~169), but answers none of that report's questions, and
its own source notes say the models differ: a separate report, with a
reciprocal note in \dir{a260700-parabolic-double-cosets} (the same Fubini
pole at $\log2$; \dir{a261781-matrix-compositions}' Part~IV already notes
it).  The write checked all 501 b-file terms, which the source could not
fetch, against its own exact computation and computes
$C_3=-L^2(50L^4+324L^2-1755)/6480=0.11772518\ldots$ (a further question:
one implementation); the independent check, by a different exact route to
$n=2000$, found no error and confirmed $C_3$ numerically at three values of
$L$.  No effective onset, lattice local limit, large deviations or
exponentially small sectors; the trace marker does not cross $v=0$; no
global priority.  Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{Symmetric sign matrices and free degree constraints: leading asymptotics, a critical window and controlled inverses}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a027832-symmetric-sign-matrices}
{article.pdf}{batch~108: Report239.zip (bundle Report~239)}
{For symmetric $n\times n$ matrices with entries $\pm1$ (diagonal
included) and nonnegative row sums (OEIS A027832), an exact encoding as
graphs with one extra vertex of unrestricted degree (a structural fact
Greenhill and McKay state, credited) reduces the count to degree-capped
graphs.  The report proves that freeing a fixed number $f$ of degree caps
near half the order multiplies the all-capped count by
$\Phi(\ell)^{-f}(1+o(1))$, where $\phi(\ell)=\ell\Phi(\ell)$
($\ell=0.5060544689\ldots$), and, uniformly for $f=O(\sqrt M)$, a
finite-saddle formula and an explicit equivalent with a collective
quadratic and a bounded-offset linear correction, by uniform dense
degree-sequence enumeration (McKay--Wormald), relative concentration, an
exponential tilt and balancing of the even degree-sum lattice.  With
McKay, Wanless and Wormald's all-capped asymptotic this gives
$A_n\sim2^{n(n+1)/2}q^nZ_0e^{-\ell^2/2}$ times $\exp(\ell\sqrt n+J/4)$
for even $n$ and $1$ for odd $n$, explicit constants, consistent with
all 17 OEIS terms (ratios $0.9996$ and $0.9982$ at $n=16,17$, an intake
check), plus the precision of a continuous inverse and eventual
two-ceiling threshold bounds.  A leading $1+o(1)$ theorem only, conditional
on the cited dense-enumeration inputs: no all-orders expansion, effective
onset, caps beyond $\sqrt M$ or historical priority (Anderson's chapter
and the unpublished Sillke--Flammenkamp work were not inspected; the
write matched Flammenkamp's table with $A_1,\ldots,A_{16}$).  In one
delivered section two carriage-return bytes replaced the backslash and
``r'' of a roman-font command, so the delivered PDF prints ``meven'' in
eq.~(7.10) and one more display; the write restored the command with a
dated note and removed the file's \texttt{-text} attribute.  The
independent check found no error and corrected two rounded constants of
the README.  OEIS terms are CC~BY-SA~4.0.  Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{Maximum-density kings on cylinders and planes (OEIS A137432, A061593, A061594)}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a137432-maximum-density-kings}
{article.pdf}{batch~109: Rectangular\_Kings\_Aspect\_Ratio\_Transition\_Source.zip (bundle Report~174, base, Part~I) + Cylindrical\_Kings\_All\_Order\_Asymptotics\_and\_Inverses\_Source.zip (Report~173, Part~II) + Planar\_Kings\_Pole\_Bounds\_and\_Inverses\_Source.zip (Report~175, Part~III)}
{Count placements of $hw$ nonattacking kings on a $2h\times2w$ board, one
in each $2\times2$ block: $A(h,w)$ with cyclic columns, $a_n=A(n,n)$ (OEIS
A137432), and $P_h(w)$ on the ordinary board (A061593, A061594 at
$h=2,3$).  Part~I, for $w/h$ in a compact positive interval, gives a
marked-star sum with relative error $O(1/h)$, a free energy with a
transition at $w/h=\log2$, every fixed order above it,
$A=h^wC(\lambda_h)\sum_jc_j(\lambda_h)h^{-j}$, a critical window, three
limit laws, Lambert-$W$ first-crossing brackets and, at fixed height,
squarefree denominators whose irreducible factors have degree at most
$\lfloor h/2\rfloor+1$, with equality at the heights $h=p-3$.  Part~II
proves $a_n=Cn^n(\sum_{j\le M}c_jn^{-j}+O(n^{-M-1}))$ with
Kot\v{e}\v{s}ovec's $C=2e(e-1)^2/(e-2)^2$, exact $c_1,c_2,c_3\in\mathbb Q(e)$
and the ratio expansion $a_{n+1}/(na_n)=e(1+1/(2n)+O(n^{-2}))$, which
proves the A137432 conjecture (Kot\v{e}\v{s}ovec, 2011) that the ratio
tends to $e$.  Part~III bounds the degree and pole order of every factor
of the planar denominators, gives Wilf's dominant term
$(\alpha_hw+\beta_h)(h+1)^w$ with $\alpha_h\ge1$ and a Lambert-$W$ bracket.
