Merged in dependency order: Part~II's main theorem is Part~I's at
$\lambda=1$ (the coefficients agree identically).  Of Kot\v{e}\v{s}ovec's
book conjectures (the book itself not read), Conjectures~1 and~4 are
proved and the upper half of Conjecture~2 on both boards; the write proves
$2(h+1)^w\le A(h,w)\le2^h(h+1)^w$ with its equality cases (so the book's
strict bound for $a_n$ fails exactly at $n=1$) and, reading Part~III's
Theorem~27.2, that the dominant planar factor has exponent exactly two at
every height (Conjecture~3's printed $\lfloor(h+1)/2\rfloor$ contradicts
the exponents on its own page; not settled).  Report~173's fixture names
the OEIS b-file (to $n=384$) but holds $a_{400}$ and $a_{800}$; the
independent check identified its recorded SHA-256 as that of Matsuo's
table and corrected three rounded digits and one range.
Effective constants, growing windows and low-degree multiplicities are
open questions.  A reciprocal note went to
\dir{a201513-sparse-chess-placements} (a different, sparse regime).
Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{Nonattacking bishops: the amplitude and the diagonal corrections (OEIS A002465, A238260)}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a002465-nonattacking-bishops}
{article.pdf}{batch~109: Report219-reproducibility.zip (bundle Report~219)}
{For the number $B_n$ of placements of $n$ nonattacking bishops on the
$n\times n$ board (OEIS A002465), proves
$B_n=b\,\Gamma(n)q^n(\sum_{j\le J}c_j^{(n\bmod2)}n^{-j}+O(n^{-J-1}))$ for
every fixed $J$, with $r\in(1,2)$ the root of $r/(1-e^{-r})=2$, $q=e^r/r$
and the closed form $b=e^r/(2\pi\sqrt{r^2-1})=0.63126\ldots$ of the OEIS
constant A238260 (given there only by digits and a limit definition);
$c_1,c_2$ are parity-free and the parity split first appears at $n^{-3}$,
$c_3^{(1)}-c_3^{(0)}=r^3/(4(r+1))$.  The route is the two colour Ferrers
boards, a Stirling convolution and a two-variable saddle; also a
parity-preserving two-ceiling inverse with a Lambert centre.  The write
checked $b$ against all 105 printed digits of A238260, gives a Lambert form
$b=-1/(\pi w_0\sqrt{(1+w_0)(3+w_0)})$, $w_0=W_0(-2/e^2)$, matches all 376
b-file terms, proves $B_{n+1}\ge2B_n$ ($n\ge1$, so the inverse's onset is
explicit) and moves the angular-only order-4 receipt and the unproved
parity-branch enclosures to its questions; the independent check found no
mathematical error and corrected its description of Santos's Theorem~6.
The exact enumeration and the leading equivalent with numerical $b$ are
prior (Perott, Arshon, Kot\v{e}\v{s}ovec, Santos).  Not a Part of
\dir{a201513-sparse-chess-placements}, whose general theorem assumes a
finite move set; a reciprocal note went there.  Unrefereed; not
formalized.}

\entry{Maximal matchings of complete tripartite graphs (OEIS A297487)}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a297487-tripartite-maximal-matchings}
{article.pdf}{batch~109: Tripartite\_Maximal\_Matchings\_Asymptotics\_and\_Inverses\_Source.zip (bundle Report~176, base, Part~I) + Unequal\_Tripartite\_Matchings\_Transitions\_and\_Inverses\_Source.zip (Report~178, Part~II)}
{A matching of a complete multipartite graph is maximal exactly when its
unmatched vertices lie in one part, so every count is a sum of factorial
ratios.  Part~I treats $A_n$, the maximal matchings of $K_{n,n,n}$ (OEIS
A297487): with $\lambda=\sqrt{n/2}$, an expansion to every fixed order,
uniform in a real marker of unmatched vertices, which gives the OEIS
equivalent $A_n\sim3\sqrt2(2n/e)^{3n/2}e^{\sqrt{n/2}-3/8}$ with four
corrections; the unmatched count is within $O(n^{-1/4})$ in total
variation of a parity-conditioned Poisson$(\lambda)$ law; the probability
of a maximum matching to every fixed order; and a two-ceiling threshold
bracket.  Part~II, an explicit sequel, treats unequal parts: the branches
for strictly triangular proportions, and $K_{2n+d,n,n}$ across the face
$a=b+c$ of the triangle inequality with a uniform moving window at scale
$n^{1/3}$, Gaussian laws, four corrections of the boundary counts
$M(2n,n,n)$ (no OEIS entry) and an exterior Poisson law.  It settles one
instance of Part~I's question on unequal parts; more parts and higher
orders stay open.  The write compared all 100 b-file terms, proved
Howroyd's OEIS program equal to the exact formula, and classified the
inverses (the leading terms are factorial-core instances, the brackets
not staircase instances); the independent check, with brute-force counts
and exact $\log M(2n,n,n)$ to $n=4000$, found no mathematical error and
corrected two attributions of quoted wording.  Unrefereed; not
formalized.}

\entry{Labelled outerplanar graphs to all fixed orders (OEIS A097998, A098000)}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a097998-outerplanar-graphs}
{article.pdf}{batch~109: Labeled\_Outerplanar\_Graphs\_Expansions\_and\_Inverses\_Source.zip (bundle Report~170)}
{For connected and all labelled outerplanar graphs (OEIS A097998,
A098000), proves $c_n=An!\rho^{-n}n^{-5/2}(\sum_{j\le J}d_j(0)n^{-j}+
O(n^{-J-1}))$ and the same for $g_n$ with amplitude $e^\nu A$, uniformly in
a complex component marker, with four explicit corrections, a refined
shifted Poisson law $1+\mathrm{Poisson}(\nu+5\tau/(2n))$ for the number of
components with total-variation error $O(n^{-2})$ (the limit
$1+\mathrm{Poisson}(\nu)$ is prior), and
a two-ceiling inverse with a Lambert start.  It corrects a published
constant: the amplitude $h\approx0.018216$ of Bodirsky, Gim\'enez, Kang and
Noy (2007), Theorem~5.1, is $e^\nu A=0.0080960474\ldots$, the printed
value being $(9/4)e^\nu A$ (exponent and radius right); the write proves
this with a hand bound and an interval enclosure and refutes the
exponent and decimals of their introduction, finds the preprint's
$0.017657$ wrong too and Kang's thesis value $0.008095$ inaccurate, and
observes the same $9/4$ in their Theorem~7.1 (graphs with no $K_{2,3}$
minor).  The independent check confirmed the correction by Richardson
extrapolation of exact counts to sixteen digits, traced the $9/4$ to a
misprinted constant in their Theorem~3.6 ($-(3/2)F_1$ for the $-(2/3)F_1$
of its proof), and found numerically that their series-parallel constants
appear to be $3\sqrt\pi$ too large (an open question, with the cause of
the $9/4$ and a second analytic route).  Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{Planar Eulerian orientations: exact Lambert sectors, logarithmic corrections and inverses (OEIS A324312, A277493, A324314)}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a324312-planar-eulerian-orientations}
{article.pdf}{batch~109: Planar\_Eulerian\_Orientations\_Lambert\_Expansions\_and\_Inverses\_Source.zip (bundle Report~171)}
{For rooted planar Eulerian orientations by edges (OEIS A324312, and
A277493 $=2\cdot$A324312) and quartic ones by vertices (A324314), with
$m=n+2$, $L=\log m$ and $T=\log L+C+\gamma$, proves
$a_n=\kappa_0\mu^m/(m^2L^2)\{\sum_{j\le J}Q_j(T)/L^j+O_J((\log L)^{J+1}/L^{J+1})\}$
with six universal polynomials $Q_0,\ldots,Q_5$, the sharper
$a_n=\kappa_0\mu^m\{h_m-k_m/2+O(m^{-4}L^{-3})\}$ through the coefficients
of exact analytic Lambert models and any fixed number of such sectors, a
relative correction $2/(mL)$ that no logarithmic truncation sees, and
smooth inverse charts with a two-ceiling threshold bracket.  Every count
statement is conditional on Bousquet-M\'elou and Elvey Price's
$\Delta$-continuation (JCTA 2020, Propositions~8.2--8.3), whose proof for
the general family they only indicate; the independent check found the
same authors' later statement (refined preprint arXiv:2503.15046v3,
Section~6.4) that the logarithmic behaviour has been proven for both
weights, so the condition is a refereed proposition with an indicated
proof, not a conjecture.  The write corrected the source's
citation of the continuation ODEs (Section~8.1, not~7, in every version),
reproduced all printed terms by its own exact reversion and classified
the inverses; a written proof of that proposition is its open question.
The 3.0~MB \texttt{exact.json} is not staged (it regenerates
byte-identically; README recipe).  Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{Distinct sums of square roots: quantitative asymptotics and inverse indices (OEIS A397045)}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a397045-square-root-sums}
{article.pdf}{batch~109: Distinct\_Square\_Root\_Sums\_Asymptotics\_and\_Inverses\_Source.zip (bundle Report~172)}
{$a(n)$, the number of distinct values in $[n,n+1)$ of finite sums of
square roots of positive integers (OEIS A397045), equals the number of
vectors $(m_d)$ over the squarefree $d\ge2$ with $\sum m_d\sqrt d<n+1$.
Proves $\log a(n)=K(n+1)^{2/3}+O(n^{1/3})$, $K=(81\zeta(3)/\pi^2)^{1/3}$,
which is the entry's conjecture including the constant (Kohlbecker's
1958 Tauberian theorem already implies the leading law, as the report
says and the write works out), an exact-product saddle formula with
relative error $O(n^{-1/3})$, inverse indices, and, unconditionally, that
no equivalent $Cn^\beta\exp(Kn^{2/3})$ exists, because of a surviving
pole at a zeta zero of least height.  The write refutes, with proof, the
entry's comment that integer radicands are equivalent to squarefree ones
(the integer-radicand count is $3,8,21,46,104$ against
$a(n)=3,7,17,35,76$), proves that $a(n)$ increases strictly, and extends
the table to $n=75$; the independent check confirmed both and corrected
one inference (the non-equivalence is visible from $n=2$, not $n=1$) and
the letters of a cited theorem.  Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{Diagonal multiple alignments: all fixed orders, column counts and inverse thresholds (OEIS A262810, A262809, A316677, A316674)}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a262810-diagonal-alignments}
{article.pdf}{batch~109: Diagonal\_Sequence\_Alignments\_Asymptotics\_and\_Inverses\_Source.zip (bundle Report~177)}
{For the number $a_n$ of binary matrices with $n$ labelled rows, every row
sum $n$ and ordered nonzero columns (OEIS A262810, the diagonal of
A262809), proves $\log(a_n/K_n)=\sum_{j\le J}C_jn^{-2j}+O(n^{-2J-2})$ for
every $J$, with Kot\v{e}\v{s}ovec's equivalent $K_n$ and explicit
$C_1,C_2,C_3$, uniformly in a column mark; the column count has mean
$n^2/(2\log2)+n/4+\cdots$ and a Gaussian limit on scale $n$; substituting
$k=n$ in the classical fixed-$k$ equivalent misses a factor $e^{-1/12}$;
a Lambert-$W$ inverse and a two-ceiling threshold enclosure; and the
nonnegative analogue A316677 by a binomial coupling.  The write quotes the
four entries, checks both b-files by Howroyd's formula and records that
the unmarked relation $2^{n-1}a_n$ is also in Eger (2015), credited there
to Duchi and Sulanke (not read); the independent check confirmed every
number and qualified the write's claim that Howroyd's formula is a
different route (it is the same double sum in the other order).  The
report's README promises a source file not in the archive (disclosed).
Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{Lazy closed walks in growing dimension (OEIS A328716, A328718)}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a328716-lazy-closed-walks}
{article.pdf}{batch~109: Lazy\_Closed\_Walks\_Asymptotics\_and\_Inverses\_Source.zip (bundle Report~179)}
{For the number $a_n$ of $n$-step closed walks in $\mathbb Z^n$ with steps
$0$ and $\pm e_i$ (OEIS A328716, the diagonal of A328718), proves
$a_n=C_\varepsilon(dn)^n\{\sum_{\ell\le M}c_{\varepsilon,\ell}n^{-\ell}+O(n^{-M-1})\}$,
$\varepsilon=(-1)^n$, for every $M$, with Bessel definitions of
Kot\v{e}\v{s}ovec's constants $d$ and $C_\pm$ (so his equivalent) and
explicit $c_{\varepsilon,1},c_{\varepsilon,2},c_{\varepsilon,3}$; an
absolute complex-uniform marked expansion; the zero-step count to every
order about a parity-conditioned Poisson law; occupied axes Gaussian on
scale $\sqrt n$, asymptotically independent of the zero steps; parity
inverse brackets of eventual width at most one; and a compact
proportional extension.  The write proves Kot\v{e}\v{s}ovec's 2019
conjecture in A328718 that row $r$ is asymptotic to
$(2r+1)^{n+r/2}/(2^r(\pi n)^{r/2})$ (a local limit theorem, classical, no
novelty claimed); the independent check re-derived that proof and made
its $1/N$ term, $-3D/16$, explicit (corroboration, not a theorem).  Pin
\texttt{83befe707} exists.  Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\subsection{Asymptotics, records and discrepancy}

\entry{The Exponential Scale of Gregory Sign Thresholds}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/gregory-coefficient-sign-thresholds}
{gregory\_sign\_threshold.pdf}{gregory\_sign\_threshold\_research.zip}
{For $(x/\log(1+x))^m=\sum_n G^{(m)}_nx^n$ and $\mathrm{Gr}(m)$ the least $N$
beyond which $(-1)^{n-1}G^{(m)}_n>0$, proves
$\log\mathrm{Gr}(m)=m+1-\gamma-(\zeta(2)+\zeta(3))/m+C_2/m^2+O(m^{-3})$ with
$C_2$ explicit, plus a finite algorithm for every coefficient of the complete
expansion; in particular $\mathrm{Gr}(m)/e^m\to e^{1-\gamma}$ and
$\mathrm{Gr}(m)/3^m\to0$.  This replaces Conjecture~6.1 of Xu and Zhao
(arXiv:2609.11072v1), whose printed lower bound already conflicts with its own
reported $\mathrm{Gr}(5)=113$; that value is independently certified here.}

\entry{Exact Records in the Square-Root-of-Three Walk}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/sqrt-three-walk-records}
{article.pdf}{sqrt3\_record\_recurrence.zip}
{Proves the four-phase record recurrence for
$S(N)=\sum_{n\le N}(-1)^{\lfloor n\sqrt3\rfloor}$ in the corrected form on
page~7 of Bruin and Fokkink, \emph{On the records and zeros of a deterministic
random walk} (arXiv:2503.11734v2), where it is experimental and attributed to
Arias de Reyna and van de Lune.  Includes minimality of every claimed first
passage, a rational generating function, closed forms, sharp logarithmic
extrema, and an extension to every even integer trace $D\ge4$.}

\entry{Sharp discrepancy for two families of binary substitutions}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/binary-substitution-discrepancy}
{article.pdf}{oeis\_discrepancy\_counterexamples.zip; batch~73: OEIS\_Quadratic\_Morphic\_Words\_Research\_Package.zip; batch~114: binary\_morphic\_fluctuations.zip}
{For the fixed word of $0\mapsto1$, $1\mapsto101010$, the position sequences
A284365 and A284366 both carry a conjectured upper position error below~2, and
both conjectures are false: the first counterexamples are at ranks
$2{,}977{,}771$ and $8{,}933{,}313$, with common error
$(2977771\sqrt{21}-13645857)/2\approx2.00487$ and sharp common upper bound
$(6+\sqrt{21})/5\approx2.11652$.  Proves complete discrepancy limit intervals
for every substitution $0\mapsto1$, $1\mapsto(10)^m$, with infinite
counterexample families and exact recurrences for their indices.  Part~II,
added in batch~73, treats the disjoint family $0\mapsto1$,
$1\mapsto10^a1^b$: the prefix discrepancy is bounded exactly when
$a\le b+1$, with sharp, unattained position-error envelopes from a
four-state Bellman system, and sharp bounds for A284368--A284371.  The
density question of Parts~I and~II stays open.  Part~III (batch~114)
answers Part~II's question on the unbounded regimes, with
$X(N)=\#0-q\,\#1$ over the first $N$ letters: at $a=b+2$,
$\limsup X(N)/\log N=-\liminf X(N)/\log N=(a-1)/(2\log a)$, every value in
between is a limit point, and Part~II's cycle $P_k$ is the first prefix of
weight $k(a-1)$; for $a\ge b+3$ the cluster set of $X(N)/N^\alpha$,
$\alpha=\log q/\log\lambda$, is a closed-form interval
$[-\mathcal C_{a,b},\mathcal C_{a,b}]$ with a continuous anti-periodic
graph-directed phase profile, and at $(a,b)=(8,1)$ the endpoints are
$\pm\sqrt{10}$ and $X(N)^2+5X(N)\le10N$ for every prefix.  For every word
with $a$ zeros and $a-1$ ones beginning with~1 it gives an exact,
coefficientwise positive convolution formula for complete-block prefix
histograms, exact moments, quantitative Gaussian and local limit laws, a
large-deviation principle with explicit pressure, the sharp variance bounds
$(a-1)/a^2\le v\le(a^2-1)/12$, and the zero-height profiles, counted by
$(a-1)2^{a-2}$, A001787$(a-1)$.  The existence of the Gaussian limit
(Paquette--Son) and the invariant-section identity (Solomyak,
Rajabzadeh--Safaee) are credited; the first local (Edgeworth) correction,
only sketched, is an open question.  The independent check of the Part~III
write found eleven places of Part~II where a batch-73 rename had left
Part~I's constant $C$ in place of $\widehat C$, so that the bound (13.9) for
A284369 read $E_0<2.1547$ although $E_0$ reaches $3.6437$; all eleven now
read $\widehat C$, giving the sharp $2+\sqrt3$ of Theorem~12.1, with a dated
note.  Unrefereed; not formalized.}

\entry{Exact eventual maximizers of Rudin--Shapiro autocorrelations}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/rudin-shapiro-autocorrelation-maximizers}
{article.pdf}{rudin\_shapiro\_maximizers.zip}
{For the aperiodic autocorrelation $C_m(k)$ of the level-$m$ Rudin--Shapiro
sequence, classifies the maximizing shift exactly for all large $m$:
$k_m^\ast=(2^{m+1}+(-1)^m)/3$, with $A_m=b_{m-2}$, settling the discrete
uniqueness-and-location conjecture of Tarnu (J.~Approx.\ Theory 287 (2023)
105866), repeated as Conjecture~1.5 of Choi--Tarnu (2025), including the limit
$k_m^\ast/2^m\to2/3$.  The certificate is a rational polytope argument with
exact arithmetic.}

\entry{Apéry arrays and conjectural zeta accelerations}
{generating-functions-and-asymptotics/apery-array-zeta-accelerations}
{article.pdf}{Apery\_OEIS\_Conjectures\_Proofs.zip, apery\_oeis\_conjectures.zip}
{Proves the entire shifted-diagonal $\zeta(3)$ family recorded as conjectural in
OEIS A143007, and both the super- and subdiagonal $\zeta(2)$ families of
A108625, at every nonnegative offset: for all $k\ge0$,
$\zeta(3)=H_k^{(3)}+\sum_{n\ge1}(2n+k)(3n^2+3nk+k^2)/
(n^3(n+k)^3T_{n-1,n+k-1}T_{n,n+k})$.  The main diagonals are the classical
Apéry sequences A005259 and A005258.  Proofs are finite telescoping identities
with rational lattice potentials and explicit limit estimates; no computer test
or unproved conjecture enters.  Adds rational error bounds, numerator and
denominator recurrences, a three-term recurrence along every shifted diagonal,
and sharp fixed-offset asymptotic constants.  Merged from two reports whose
boxed identities were character-identical up to macro names.}

\section{The Jacobian conjecture and Keller maps}

Four reports continuing the formal project \dir{Algebra/JacobianConjecture},
which verifies in Lean and Rocq Alpöge's three-variable counterexample and a
degree-six stable representative in five variables (its stabilization to all
higher dimensions in Lean only); its further degree reductions are SymPy
certificates.  None of the four
reports' own theorems is formalized; each README names the project
declarations it builds on.  Three study the project's three-variable map,
one of them its iteration; the fourth studies a different map, Gao's
five-dimensional~$F_6$.

\entry{The fiber geometry of Gao's Keller map $F_6$}
{jacobian-conjecture/gao-f6-fiber-geometry}
{article.pdf}{ProveIt\_Six\_Sheets\_Research.zip, ProveIt\_Keller\_Fibers\_Research.zip}
{For Gao's five-dimensional six-sheeted Keller map $F_6$
(arXiv:2608.00222v1, \S4.5.1), classifies every geometric fiber: the
cardinalities are exactly $0,1,2,3,4,6$, all seven root-multiplicity
patterns occur, and off the hyperplane $c=0$ the inverse points are the simple
roots of an explicit sextic.  The omitted set is a smooth closed surface
isomorphic to $\mathbb{G}_m\times\mathbb{A}^1$, of codimension three, with the
single effective omitted parameter $(21/32,5/32,25/128)$.  Gives an explicit
finite normalization of rank six, the two-component nonproperness locus, the
discriminant's normalization and local analytic types, global monodromy $S_6$
with two arithmetic certificates, no proper intermediate field, real fiber
cardinalities $\{0,1,2,3,4\}$, and an obstruction to equivalence with a
stabilization of a map in at most three variables.  The map is Gao's; the
fiber theory is the reports'.  Merged from two manuscripts proving the same
core in identical normalizations; the second one's different proofs are kept
as marked second routes.  Not to be confused with the project's formally
verified dimension-five stable map.}

\entry{Arithmetic local--global dichotomies for the three-variable Keller map}
{jacobian-conjecture/arithmetic-local-global-fibers}
{article.pdf}{ProveIt\_Arithmetic\_Local\_Global.zip; batch~58: ProveIt\_Dense\_Integral\_Hasse.zip; batch~64: ProveIt\_Beyond\_Split\_Fibers.zip}
{For the project's three-variable Keller map, gives an explicit infinite
family of integral targets with a preimage in every $\mathbb{Z}_p^3$ but none
in $\mathbb{Z}^3$ (each fiber is three rational points, so these are not
rational Hasse failures); classifies the split integer-root plane $C=2$ by a
gcd condition, with locally soluble parameter density $27/(4\pi^2)$ surviving
the removal of $S$-integral fibers; proves the exact integral 2-adic fiber law
$(21,7,3,1)/32$ for fiber sizes $0,1,2,3$, determined modulo~8; and contrasts
it with a rational Hasse principle over every number field.  The complex
stratification is credited to Gao and the finite-field histograms to an
external note.  An intake remark records an integral triple collision over the
project's own collision value.  Not a new disproof of the Jacobian
conjecture.  Part~II, added in batch~58, answers Part~I's density question:
such failures are Zariski dense in every plane $C=c\ne0$, hence in
$\mathbb{A}^3$, and none lies on $C=0$.  It gives a complete local criterion
for completely split fibers, finds finitely many integral split fibers on
each plane, and proves $\#\mathcal H^{\mathrm{sp}}_c(T)\sim\kappa(c)T^{2/3}$
for the completely split failures in square boxes $|A|,|B|\le T$, with
$\kappa(c)$ an explicit Gamma factor times an Euler product.  Part~III, added
in batch~64, gives a complete local-integral certificate for every target,
nonsplit fibers included, and on $C=2$ counts the integral Hasse failures in
the same boxes: $\frac{27}{2\pi^2}T\log T+O(T)$, almost all of them
nonsplit.}

\entry{All-degree rigidity of a weighted Keller class}
{jacobian-conjecture/weighted-keller-rigidity}
{article.pdf}{ProveIt\_Weighted\_Keller\_Rigidity.zip; batch~44: ProveIt\_Affine\_Weighted\_Keller\_Classification.zip}
{For polynomial maps of weights $(-1,1,2)$ written through $t=xy$ and
$v=x^2z$, classifies the Keller maps in every degree over any field of
characteristic zero: those with $p,q$ affine in $v$ and $r$ affine in $(t,v)$
are an explicit tame family and a two-parameter diagonal orbit of the known
map, so every noninvertible member has coordinate degrees $(7,6,4)$ and
sixteen monomials, with no input degree bound.  Extends to
$r=R(t)+\gamma v$, where the possible maximum degrees of the noninvertible
members are exactly $7$ and every even integer at least $8$, and shows that
with $r=1+t+v$ the fixed degree-seven coefficient scheme is the double point
$\mathbb{Q}[\varepsilon]/(\varepsilon^2)$, with an explicitly obstructed
tangent (a torus times it when the two coefficients of $r$ are freed).  The
classification does not cover unrestricted weighted maps.  Sharpens to all degrees the project's
degree-seven sparsity search; it does not show degree seven minimal in
dimension three.  Part~II, added in batch~44, removes the restriction on the
coefficient of $v$ in $r$: whenever $p,q,r$ are affine in $v$ that coefficient
is constant, so the classification covers the whole affine-in-$z$ class; it
adds dual-number slopes of every degree and a universal second-order
obstruction.}

\entry{Dynamical degrees of the three-variable Keller map}
{jacobian-conjecture/keller-map-dynamical-degrees}
{article.pdf}{ProveIt\_Keller\_Dynamics.zip (batch~70)}
{Studies the project's three-variable Keller map $F$ under iteration, which
neither the project nor the other reports touch.  Over any field of
characteristic other than three, $\deg F^n$ satisfies
$d_{n+2}=6d_{n+1}+d_n$ with $d_0=1$, $d_1=7$ ($1,7,43,265,\dots$), so the
first dynamical degree is $3+\sqrt{10}$; every positive weighted degree of
every iterate is explicit, and every coordinate's downward-completed Newton
polyhedron has exactly two vertices.  Outside characteristic three,
composing with a shear $z\mapsto z+y^2h(xy)$, $h\ne0$ of degree~$m$, gives
first dynamical degree $\bigl(2m+7+\sqrt{4m^2+32m+61}\bigr)/2$; in
characteristic zero these maps are all Keller, noninjective, of generic
degree three and in one tame left--right equivalence class, yet their
dynamical degrees are unbounded.  In characteristic three the second
coordinate is $y$ exactly and the degrees follow separate closed formulas
(first dynamical degree $4$ or $m+3$); in characteristic two the map is
purely inseparable of degree two.  A monomial-dominance
escape theorem gives a forward-invariant complex escape region, and integral
points there have maximal arithmetic degree and are Zariski dense.  No Lean
or Rocq source is supplied; the formalization route is a plan.}

\section{Galois theory and radicals}

Two reports continuing the formal project \dir{Algebra/PolynomialFormulas}
(Lean and Rocq root formulas through degree four, Abel--Ruffini, and Lazard's
quintic solver); none of its theorems is formalized.

\entry{Specialization-safe radical solvers}
{galois-theory-and-radicals/specialization-safe-radical-solvers}
{article.pdf}{ProveIt\_Radical\_Solvers\_Research.zip; batch~52: ProveIt\_Optimal\_Kummer\_Atlases.zip}
{Gives an exact cyclotomic-resultant criterion for the characteristics in which
equal-cardinality subset sums of the roots of an irreducible prime-degree
polynomial can collide, and evaluates it: the bad characteristics are $\{5\}$
for quintic pairs, $\{2,7\}$ for septic pairs and $\{2,7,29\}$ for septic
triples.  Classifies the collisions completely in characteristic~29 (exactly
the cyclic polynomials $(X-\eta)^7-b$) and in characteristic~2 (translated
$Z^7+aZ^3+bZ+d$, realizing the nonsolvable $\mathrm{GL}_3(\mathbb{F}_2)$).
Constructs separating subset invariants in every characteristic, reconstructs
all roots from one coherent $p$-th radical on each Fourier chart once the
intermediate-field invariant data are given (generalizing Lazard's pivot-one
scheme for quintics; a coefficient-only solver is left as further work), realizes every Fourier support by
rational polynomials with full affine Galois group, and shows the Picard group
of the cyclic configuration quotient is $\mathbb{Z}/p$, so no single global
Kummer coordinate exists.  The Picard result has a written proof only.
Part~II, added in batch~52, answers Part~I's question on the least number of
charts: $\mathrm{CH}^*(\mathrm{Conf}_p(\mathbb{A}^d)/C_p)=\mathbb{Z}[t]/(pt,t^{d(p-1)})$,
so every cover by regular Kummer charts, even with nonlinear character
sections and different characters on different charts, needs exactly
$d(p-1)$ charts ($p-1$ for polynomials: four for quintics, six for septics),
and the Fourier charts attain it.  This too is a written proof, by integral
intersection theory; its checker verifies only finite identities.}

\entry{Finite separating ranges for sextic block resolvents}
{galois-theory-and-radicals/sextic-block-resolvent-separators}
{article.pdf}{ProveIt\_Finite\_Sextic\_Separators.zip (batch~41); batch~57: ProveIt\_Three\_Plus\_One\_Resolvents.zip; batch~70: ProveIt\_Zero\_Parameter\_Sextics.zip}
{The project finds parameters separating the sextic pair and triple
block-resolvent descriptors by an unbounded \texttt{Nat.find} search.  This
report proves in writing that on the curve $(t,2t^2)$ one of $t=1,\dots,196$
always separates both families, sharply for arbitrary complex test sets, by
orbit counting.  The bound is not formalized; formalizing it would make that
search bounded, and two further unbounded searches of the project remain.
Part~II, added in batch~57, shows that deciding radical solvability of an
irreducible sextic needs no separating parameter: the sextic is solvable
exactly when its triple resolvent has a base-field root at one parameter
chosen in advance, or its matching resolvent has one at each of three
distinct parameters, because a base-field matching root of a nonsolvable
sextic comes from a Galois-invariant pentad, of which there is at most one,
and occurs at no more than two parameters.  Part~III, added in batch~70,
sharpens this: one compressed matching test at $t=0$ together with one triple
test decides, by an integer certificate that the five values of a pentad
are never all equal and by reciprocal transport; and since a pentad's
points span an affine space of dimension at least two, the polynomial of
false-positive parameters has degree at most one and does not vanish at
zero, so any two distinct matching parameters suffice.  It adds a
denominator-free Pfaffian of order twenty for the constant triple resolvent
and a cubic $15\times15$ matrix polynomial whose determinant is the cube of
the constant matching resolvent.}

\section{Hilbert's tenth problem}

Twelve reports on Diophantine representations of computation that control
their witnesses, beside the formal project
\dir{Computability/HilbertTenthProblem}, which proves MRDP in both
directions in Lean, the universal pair $(58,4)$ and the single-fold unary
exponential representation of Jones and Matiyasevich.  The first three
(canonical certificates; probabilistic, quantum and continuous computation;
liveness beyond halting) continue that project.  The other six, added in
batches~76, 78 and~79 (groups as substrates, signal machines, stochastic and
thermal exactness, quadratic orthant certificates, polynomial witness
histories, smooth finalizers), treat further substrates or witness domains
and use MRDP at most as an imported theorem.  Batch~79 also extended five
of the older reports.  Batch~80 added Parts~V and~VI to the signal-machine
report and replaced the programs of three batch-79 Parts (canonical
certificates Part~XIX, quadratic orthant certificates Part~VI, signal
machines Part~IV) by their authors' corrected code editions, whose
manuscripts are unchanged.  Batch~82 opened
\dir{five-particle-binary-automata} and \dir{fixed-universal-polynomials},
the latter on the programme's own fixed universal polynomial, and extended
the signal-machine report (Parts~VI--VIII) and the group-substrate report
(Part~V); batch~83 opened \dir{periodic-turmite-first-revisits} and added
Part~XX to the canonical certificates and Part~V to the fixed universal
polynomials; batch~88 added Parts~II--IV to the turmite report (a literal
periodic Langton ant and a Diophantine certificate for the fixed machine it
simulates) and Parts~VI--VIII to the fixed universal polynomials (the
candidates below the 84-operation record).  Batch~91 brought the
pipeline's Reports 49--71 (Report~68 was never delivered): Part~XXI of the
canonical certificates (fixed-arity sandpile polynomials), Part~VI of the
group-substrate report (a chronological matrix certificate), Parts~IX--XIII
of the signal-machine report and Part~VII of the five-particle report
(smaller recognition radii); its two OEIS manuscripts became
\dir{a196460-clipping-tables}.  The last three entries below
are batch~82's and~83's new reports.  None of the
reports' own theorems is formalized; each README names the project
declarations it builds on or relates to.

\entry{Canonical Diophantine certificates}
{hilbert-tenth-problem/canonical-diophantine-certificates}
{article.pdf}{Diophantine\_Causal\_Computation.zip, canonical\_diophantine\_certificates.zip, ProveIt\_Linear\_Size\_Diophantine\_Certificates.zip, Witness\_Faithful\_Diophantine\_Compilation.zip, canonical\_trace\_polytopes.zip, diophantine\_counter\_programs.zip (batch~60); batch~61: Causal\_Diophantine\_Queue\_Compilation.zip; batch~62: Names\_to\_Numbers\_Diophantine\_Heaps.zip, order\_free\_diophantine.zip, ProveIt\_Priority\_Pumping\_Diophantine.zip, one\_shared\_fallback\_research.zip; batch~63: Diophantine\_Certificates\_Without\_Histories.zip; batch~78: Exact\_Wiring\_Diophantine\_Report.zip, Topology\_Is\_Not\_Free\_Interaction\_Nets.zip, no\_ghost\_wires.zip, sandpile\_diophantine\_research.zip; batch~79: Positive\_Spectrum\_Diophantine.zip, Spectral\_Guards\_Without\_Time\_Expansion.zip, no\_borrowed\_firings.zip, Compressed\_Queue\_Diophantine\_Research.zip, Eager\_Tree\_Calculus\_Research\_Package.zip; batch~80 (corrected code edition of the last): Eager\_Tree\_Calculus\_Research\_Package\_corrected.zip; batch~83: Literal\_Periodic\_Sandpiles\_and\_Diophantine\_Certificates\_Package.zip, Real\_Exactness\_of\_Binary\_Sandpile\_Certificates\_Package.zip; batch~91: A\_Fixed\_Arity\_Integer\_Certificate\_for\_Binary\_Sandpile\_Stabilization\_Package.zip, Finite\_Legal\_Binary\_Target\_Firing\_in\_Periodic\_Sandpiles\_Package.zip, Repeated\_Legal\_Target\_Firing\_in\_Periodic\_Sandpiles\_Package.zip, Unrestricted\_Finite\_Global\_Sandpile\_Stabilization\_Package.zip (Part~XXI)}
{Diophantine representations that control their witnesses, not only their
existence.  For Petri nets and interval-guarded translations, a convex
quadratic integer polynomial whose natural zeros are in bijection with the
commutation classes of enabled executions of length~$T$ (its real zero set
is a rational polytope with exactly those lattice points), with quartic
second routes, a Cartier--Foata variant for bounded causal height, exact
commutation tests, $\#\mathrm P$-completeness of counting and an
exponential lower bound for normal-form automata.  Single-fold quartics for
multisets and nested repetitions of counter actions independent of the
repetition counts, for ``$p(i)\ge0$ for all $i\le T$'' with $O((d+1)^3)$
witnesses independent of~$T$, for memory logs with $24L$ witnesses, and for
FRACTRAN and scheduled SKI runs.  For cyclic-tag and deletion-tag systems
and fixed-read FIFO networks, certificates with one coordinate per consumed
symbol and one guard slack, with no intermediate queue quantified; faithful
word memory with polynomial insertion at both ends needs two natural
coordinates.  Proves that a single-fold (finite-fold) universal halting
polynomial exists exactly when every enumerable set has such a
representation, an open problem not solved here.  Parts~X--XIII, added in
batch~62 from four manuscripts: quartic certificates for dynamic heaps and
local graph programs, with birth-order normalization of fresh names,
two-stack universality and a proof that perpetual memory safety has no
existential representation; for polynomials nonnegative on the real orthant,
degrees two and three represent exactly the semilinear sets and degree four
every enumerable set, with single-fold quadratics for semilinear sets and
order-free self-assembly compilers; for first-enabled-rule multiset and
FRACTRAN programs the sharp pumping threshold $2H+1$, a uniform single-fold
quartic and c.e.-completeness of eventual periodicity; and conservative
priority reaction networks with one shared fallback, with single-fold trace
and minimum-fuel quartics and a least-reservoir relation whose single-fold
(finite-fold) representability is again equivalent to that of every c.e.\ set.
Part~XIV (batch~63): one quartic per run-length routing topology with exactly
$4n+4m+s$ witnesses and a unique root iff the run terminates, and a fixed
polynomial-time rank function whose single-fold (finite-fold) graph is
equivalent to the corresponding principle for all c.e.\ sets.  Part~XV, added
in batch~78 from three manuscripts: exact port wiring for Lafont's
interaction combinators.  If $M\ne N$ are perfect matchings of $2n$ ports
and $Q$ is a uniformly random one, closing by $Q$ gives different parities of
cyclic wires with probability between $2/5$ and $2/3$, both bounds sharp and
the equality cases classified; bounded six-rule compilers with
$O(C_0+F+T)$ natural coordinates, degree four and one witness per legal
schedule (scheduled histories, not commutation classes), built on a minimal
ten-state boundary calculus, a ten-pattern annihilation kernel and memory
certificates that are ledger variants of Part~V's $24L$ (whose product lemma
two of the manuscripts re-prove; no novelty is claimed for it); and a
canonical permutation-orbit certificate with an implemented fixed-schedule
compiler.  This answers Part~X's question on an interaction-combinator
frontend for bounded scheduled histories; quotienting by independent
schedules stays open.  Part~XVI (batch~78): for abelian sandpiles on a
finite undirected multigraph with a sink, an exact support criterion for
the odometer and cubic integer polynomials with exactly one natural zero
for every input, carrying the odometer, the stable endpoint and canonical
burning ranks instead of a toppling history, with $10n+6E$ coordinates in
the compact form (a directed counterexample fixes the scope); on
$\mathbb Z^d$ with a stable periodic background, an exterior collar that
certifies global halting exactly, with a unique canonical radius that by
Cairns's imported universality theorem has no computable bound; and the
exact minimal eventual period of stabilization along an input ray.  It
answers Part~XIV's question on infinite-background abelian computation for
undirected graphs, and its single-fold equivalence for Cairns's relation is
an instance of Part~IX's.  Batch~79 added a second route inside
Part~XVI, written independently: $12n+16E$ quadratic residuals in $11n+12E$
natural witnesses with exactly one natural zero, carrying the odometer,
whose sum of squares is a quartic with an integral rejection gap, and on
$\mathbb Z^3$ one fixed radius-one normal form with 47 finite-deviation
fields whose field solution exists, uniquely, exactly when the input
stabilizes (neither ledger improves the other).  Part~XVII (batch~79, from
two manuscripts proving one theorem by two routes): complete discrete sign
charts of positive-base exponential polynomials along a loop, verified at
run endpoints only (a row of order $m$ has at most $2m-1$ sign runs,
sharp), with one witness per input in arithmetic augmented by power atoms
$y=b^n$ ($O(D^3)$ witnesses for fixed bases, $O(D^4)$ with the bases as
inputs) or, for an externally fixed bit bound, in an ordinary quartic; the
complete sign-chart graph of $2^n-y$ has a single-fold (finite-fold)
representation exactly when every c.e.\ set has one, which sharpens a
proposition of Part~IX; and $\Re\bigl((3+4i)^n\bigr)$ changes sign with
positive density on every residue class.  Part~XVIII (batch~79): for
reliable FIFO actions along a straight-line trace grammar, a quartic with
exactly $6g+3c+1$ natural witnesses and one zero exactly when the trace
connects the supplied words, quantifying no intermediate queue; exact macro
repetition counts, infinite repeatability as a word equation, a Fine--Wilf
pumping threshold and unique-witness quartics for supplied infinite loops;
its re-proofs of Parts~I and~VI in one coordinate are printed as pointers.
Part~XIX (batch~79): for Barry Jay's Tree Calculus under eager evaluation
and every externally fixed bound $N$ on distinct calls, a quartic with
$3N^2+19N$ natural witnesses whose zeros project to the terminating
applications (memoized proof DAGs, made well-founded by additive call
costs), a canonical refinement with exactly one witness, a strict
bracket-abstraction compiler from weak call-by-value $\lambda$-calculus,
and a literal universal tree of 175 constructor-DAG nodes with an
r.e.-complete halting domain, whose code of about $4.5\cdot10^{10}$ bits
is charged by 175 quadratic equations and never materialized.  Since
batch~80 Part~XIX ships its authors' corrected code edition, whose
evaluator rejects non-exact or negative codes before any state change, the
repair proposed by the Hilbert's-tenth research tree's review; its
manuscript is unchanged.  Part~XX (batch~83, from two manuscripts, the
pipeline's Reports~35 and~36) delivers the universal periodic input loader
that Part~XVI asks for: one explicit stable periodic background on
$\mathbb Z^3$ and a finite loader for every finite two-sided binary tape of
the Neary--Woods machine $U_{15,2}$, with finite total activity exactly when
the machine halts, and finite binary prism certificates whose natural zeros
are the canonical odometers (a re-derivation of Part~XVI's certificate,
marked as such); replacing a product $fg$ by $f(g+k+c)$ makes every
nonnegative real zero natural, with the same summand count, degree, support
and coefficient height, by an argument different from the real gates of
\dir{quadratic-orthant-certificates}, which it credits.  The research tree's
review is printed with its findings, among them a real-algebraic limit on
fixed-arity compression.  Dated notes of batch~88 record that Parts~II--IV
of \dir{periodic-turmite-first-revisits} build the turmite analogue of
Part~XX for the same machine (no shared theorem, and no ordinary-input
loader), and that the candidates below the 84-operation record are
tabulated with their status in \dir{fixed-universal-polynomials}, whose
Parts~VII--VIII refute the first-index deletions and the square/product~82
candidate (one 83-operation candidate there is still unresolved).
Part~XXI (batch~91, the pipeline's Reports 50 and~52--54) answers Part~XX's
question on an integer-only packed interface: fixed-arity positive-integer
polynomials of exact degree~18 on one raw tile-and-patch input, with the
input encoding paid and no real-witness claim, for binary global
stabilization (2{,}566 witnesses), finite legal binary target firing
(3{,}308), repeated target firing (3{,}865) and unrestricted global
stabilization (3{,}262).  The zero sets of the last two are r.e.-complete
conditionally, through Cairns's universality theorem and through Part~XX's
loader respectively, by outside computable reductions, not a paid compiler.
Their witness sets are infinite, so the fixed-arity compression questions
are touched, not answered, and Part~XX's sentence that nothing there is a
fixed-arity universal polynomial is scoped to Part~XX.  Six printed passages
of Cairns's paper that two manuscripts call misprints are checked, and each
printed form is shown to fail; whether the corrected constructions prove
Cairns's theorems stays open.  The power macro is a lemma of
\dir{periodic-turmite-first-revisits} and the containment step the
Jones--Matiyasevich masking formalized in the Hilbert's-tenth project, both
credited; Part~VI of \dir{group-theoretic-substrates} shares both, and an
earlier sentence here that no other theorem is shared is retained, with
its refutation in that report's Remark~1.1.  Gate counts treat integer constants as free and are not
comparable with Part~XX's; the 84-operation record is unchanged.  Merged
from twenty-seven manuscripts;
not a Lean formalization.}

\entry{Diophantine laws of probabilistic, quantum and continuous computation}
{hilbert-tenth-problem/probabilistic-quantum-and-continuous-computation}
{article.pdf}{ProveIt\_Diophantine\_Quantum\_Normalization.zip, quantum\_diophantine\_research.zip, ProveIt\_Diophantine\_Barriers.zip (batch~60); batch~62: Quartic\_Probability\_Landscapes.zip, ProveIt\_Reversible\_Equilibria.zip, Diophantine\_Certificates\_for\_Coherent\_Computation.zip, proveit\_rational\_rotations\_research.zip, dense\_rotations\_research.zip, strict\_contractions\_research.zip, Diophantine\_Contracting\_2Adic.zip, Precision\_Is\_Memory\_Diophantine\_Neural\_Computation.zip; batch~64: Rational\_Tubes\_Integer\_Zeros.zip; batch~76: Infinite\_Quantum\_Runs\_Diophantine\_Certificates.zip}
{One fixed quartic represents the output law of every effective
probabilistic or quantum program as masses of projected roots; exact
normalization budgets with a unique-witness quartic certificate;
normalized two-outcome probabilities are exactly the weakly computable
reals, and conditional comparisons are $\Sigma^0_2$-complete.  Deciding
whether a seven-outcome rational quantum instrument in dimension four has
an impossible outcome word is undecidable (via Neary's matrix-mortality
theorem), with bijective quartics for words of fixed length.  One rational
piecewise-affine map of the cube realizes every disjoint pair of
enumerable sets as exact acceptance and robust rejection, with quartic
safety certificates for a computable critical-noise radius.  Parts~V--VII,
added in batch~62 from eight manuscripts: Boolean circuits compile to
coercive integer quartics of height at most~4 whose real zeros are Boolean,
with uniform Hessian and rounding constants, so that halting probabilities
of fair-coin programs are root counts, component counts or Euler
characteristics; birth--death chains with defect environments have the sharp
spectral gap $(3-2\sqrt2)/4$ and a root mass that is rational exactly for
eventually periodic environments; unique-witness quartics for coherent
contraction trees over $\mathbb{Z}[e^{i\pi/4}]$, and dense rational
$\mathrm{SO}(4)$ gate groups from the rotations $(3\pm4i)/5$, $(3\pm4j)/5$ with
c.e.-complete state transfer or c.e.-complete membership; a fixed
$1/2$-Lipschitz rational piecewise-linear map of $\mathbb{R}^4$ and a
$1/2$-contraction of $\mathbb{Z}_2$ with $\Sigma^0_1$-complete exact
half-space reachability and exact extinction; and unique-witness quadratics
for rational clipped neural networks, whose tied four-layer synthesis is
equivalent to Hilbert's tenth problem over~$\mathbb{Q}$.  Part~VIII (batch~64):
complete integer tube certificates, sums of squares of quadratics with at
most one witness, for reaching open rational targets under rational
polynomial flows, and global mass-action normal forms that carry them to
chemical reachability.  Part~IX, added in batch~76 from one manuscript: for
time-homogeneous rational quantum loops (completely positive,
trace-nonincreasing), the group inverse and Ces\`aro projection as the
unique solution of four linear equations with no rank choice, all stopping
moments by one finite recurrence certified by a Faddeev--LeVerrier circuit,
unique seven-coordinate natural wires extending Part~VI's quartic
compilation to rational inputs, an exact weak-clock resolvent with an
exponential limit law, a geometric clock on the Eisert--M\"uller--Gogolin
instruments giving every device the law $(16/25)(9/25)^n$ while
forbidden-record existence stays undecidable (also in dimension four,
conditional on Part~II), a two-state counterexample to the unrestricted
reading of Lardizabal's rationality conjecture, and no uniform existential
Diophantine graph for computably scheduled one-dimensional loops.  Its three
arguments repeated from Parts~I, II and~V are pointers.  The report now
points to the sibling reports \dir{stochastic-and-thermal-exactness},
which answers Part~VII's structural-subclass question negatively in part
(dated notes), and
\dir{signal-machine-collision-certificates}, which shares no theorem.
A dated note (batch~79) applies the finalizer of
\dir{smooth-diophantine-finalizers} to Part~V's Boolean-circuit quartics,
whose zero sets are singular at every real zero.
Merged from thirteen manuscripts; not a Lean formalization.}

\entry{Beyond halting: recurrence, progress deadlines and Diophantine liveness}
{hilbert-tenth-problem/liveness-beyond-halting}
{article.pdf}{Beyond\_Halting\_Diophantine\_Liveness.zip, Quadratic\_Diophantine\_Dynamics.zip (batch~62); batch~63: quadratic\_infinite\_computation.zip; batch~79: clock\_spectra\_research.zip}
{For computable finitely branching systems, existence of a recurrent run
with a computable progress deadline is $\Sigma^0_3$-complete and equivalent
to a nonempty effectively closed class of accepting schedules, and to an
accepting schedule of hyperimmune-free degree; fixed deadlines are
$\Pi^0_1$, bounded gaps $\Sigma^0_2$, unrestricted recurrence
$\Sigma^1_1$-complete, with strict separations and every c.e.\ degree as an
exact deadline spectrum.  An auxiliary-free degree-two polynomial with
coefficients at most eight gives the transition relation of counter
networks (degree two is minimal); a second, incoming-edge encoding has
height six, a sum-of-squares quartic companion and an exact planar
realization; and a two-stack quartic certifies finite runs meeting
prescribed deadlines.  Part~VI (batch~79) answers in part the report's
question on deadline spectra beyond c.e.\ cones: clock problems are, up to
uniform computable transformations, the upward effectively closed mass
problems of Baire space; every $\Delta^0_2$ degree, and every degree of a
unique path through a recursive tree (among them $\mathbf 0^{(r)}$ and
$\mathbf 0^{(\omega)}$), is a least clock degree of an input of a fixed
two-stack system; unions of incomparable cones have no least degree, and a
least clock degree is hyperarithmetic and contains a self-modulus.  Its
degree-two two-stack certificate is a second route, at lower degree, to the
report's quartic compiler.  Merged from four manuscripts; not a Lean
formalization.}

\entry{Groups as Diophantine substrates}
{hilbert-tenth-problem/group-theoretic-substrates}
{article.pdf}{batch~76: arithmetic\_van\_kampen.zip + three\_commutative\_phases\_research.zip; batch~78: Order\_Is\_Not\_a\_Moment.zip + ProveIt\_Affine\_Matrix\_Diophantine\_Research.zip; batch~82: Universal\_Matrix\_Semigroup\_and\_Diophantine\_Certificates\_Package.zip; batch~91: Chronological\_Diophantine\_Matrix\_Certificates\_Package.zip (Part~VI)}
{Low-degree certificates for computation in groups, all through the integral
Heisenberg group $\mathsf H$.  Part~I: with Sanov's chart (the free group of
rank two as the integral points of $x+t+4xt-yz=0$), quartics whose zeros
are in bijection with padded factorizations and that accept exactly the
words of van Kampen area at most $m$, with $(2s+13)m-4$ witnesses, so that
minimal arity tracks the Dehn function; proof-DAG compression, and exactly
$k+\ell$ product gates for $[a^{2^k},b^{2^\ell}]$, optimal in the stated
calculus; a height bound rests on an imported Cornulier--Tessera lemma.
Part~II: three injective commuting phase homomorphisms into
$\mathsf H^N\times\mathbb Z^m$ whose ordered product realizes every
integral quadratic system fibre-bijectively, hence, with MRDP, three fixed
free abelian subgroups of a Heisenberg power with c.e.-complete product
membership, a proposed, unrefereed negative answer to the three-subgroup
question of K\"onig--Lohrey--Zetzsche and Roman'kov; two-phase
decidability, known for integer exponents and credited, is new only for
natural and mixed exponents.  Part~III (batch~78): for three letters the
realizable pair counts $K_{AB}$ at fixed $K_{AC},K_{BC}$ form a gapless
interval, with a 24-witness quartic single-fold over complete profiles for
$c=2$, holes for four letters, and decidable three-generator submonoid
membership in $\mathsf H^r$ given one multiplicity, a partial answer to a
question of the Hilbert's-tenth programme.  Part~IV (batch~78): the hit set
of an affine unimodular curve in any subgroup of
$\mathrm{SL}_d(\mathbb Z)$ is finite (at most $d-1$ points) or exactly
periodic, with sharp periods and no uniform algorithm; input degree two,
via the repository's quadratic loader (reconstructed and attributed), is
the least that loads a noncomputable c.e.\ set; and an affine abelian
compiler into $\mathrm{SL}_{6g+2}(\mathbb Z)$.  Part~V (batch~82, the
pipeline's Report~32): one fixed positive semigroup of 229 literal
$\mathrm{SL}_4(\mathbb Z)$ matrices whose membership simulates the
Neary--Woods machine $U_{15,2}$ on finite tapes, hence r.e.-complete
conditionally on that simulation chain, with a family of bounded-length
sum-of-squares certificates, a 94-tile certificate and an exact fibre
generating function; it answers Part~IV's question on a complete universal
matrix certificate in part (positive semigroup, bounded length), re-derives
the research tree's $U_{15}$ construction (credited) and prints its machine
table a third time in the collection.  Part~VI (batch~91, the pipeline's
Report~55): one fixed-arity polynomial of exact degree~12 with 41{,}309
positive witnesses and 184{,}016 gates recognizing histories of every
length of the programme's five-register matrix countdown with fixed
contexts, which answers in part Part~V's question on a uniform unbounded
polynomial and Part~IV's on a fixed-arity unbounded checker; the
arbitrary-duration packing it calls missing was supplied the same night by
the research tree by another route (1{,}393 operations, at degree at least
35{,}587), recorded in a dated note, not a retraction.  Its matrix-interface
equivalence and its reading as a family of programs (a Neary--Woods
premise, an unimplemented compiler) are kept as open items, not refuted.
Its power module and containment lemma are shared with Part~XXI of
\dir{canonical-diophantine-certificates}; Remark~1.1 retains and refutes
earlier sentences of both reports that said otherwise.  Built from six
manuscripts; the first five share no theorem but a classical Sanov lemma, the Heisenberg setup printed
once; one Mihailova step is a second route to the probabilistic-quantum
report's.  Not a Lean formalization.}

\entry{Collision geometry is linear: Diophantine certificates for rational signal machines and conservative particle dynamics}
{hilbert-tenth-problem/signal-machine-collision-certificates}
{article.pdf}{batch~78: Collision\_Geometry\_Diophantine\_Signal\_Machines.zip + Signal\_Machine\_Diophantine\_Certificates.zip; batch~79: Conservative\_Signal\_Frontend\_Corrected.zip, Sparse\_Lattice\_Diophantine\_Certificates.zip; batch~80: Sparse\_Lattice\_Diophantine\_Certificates\_corrected.zip (corrected code edition), Three\_Mass\_Reversible\_Computation.zip + Exact\_Targets\_Three\_Mass\_Units.zip, Single\_Unit\_Three\_Mass\_Decidability~(1).zip + Four\_Mass\_Decidability\_Package.zip; batch~82: Timed\_Four\_Mass\_Quartic\_Certificates.zip (Part~VI), Dimension\_Independent\_Particle\_Thresholds\_Package.zip + Sparse\_Orbit\_Geometry\_and\_Exact\_Counting\_Package.zip + Binary\_Planar\_Four\_Particle\_Shuttle\_Package.zip (Part~VII), Unbounded\_Compact\_Clean\_Clocks\_Package.zip + Canonical\_Histories\_and\_Infinite\_Fibers\_Package.zip (Part~VIII); batch~91: A\_Sharp\_Reversible\_Four\_Particle\_Timing\_Threshold\_Package.zip + Uniform\_Timed\_Charts\_for\_Four\_Mass\_Units\_Package.zip (Part~IX), Periodic\_Signal\_Machine\_Collision\_Certificates\_Package.zip + Five\_Signal\_Nonsemialgebraic\_Periodic\_Validity\_Package.zip + Five\_Signal\_Diophantine\_Certificate\_Source.zip + Five\_Signal\_Rational\_Rotation\_Family\_Source.zip (Part~X), Planar\_Signal\_Realization\_Source.zip + Invertible\_Signal\_Returns\_Source.zip + Projective\_signal\_returns\_source\_and\_evidence.zip + Five\_signal\_homogeneous\_realization\_source\_and\_evidence.zip (Part~XI), Five\_signal\_branching\_source\_and\_evidence.zip + Clocked\_signals\_source\_and\_evidence.zip (Part~XII), Bounded\_certificates\_and\_encoded\_gap\_counts\_source.zip + Positive\_POWER\_reductions\_and\_exact\_compiler\_costs\_source.zip + Low\_arity\_Diophantine\_compilers\_sources.zip (Part~XIII); superseded, not printed: Conservative\_Signal\_Diophantine\_Frontend.zip (batch~79), Single\_Unit\_Three\_Mass\_Decidability.zip (batch~80)}
{A rational signal machine computes by collisions of finitely many kinds of
particles moving at fixed rational speeds on a line.  Once a complete finite
collision history is fixed, its realizations are exactly the natural zeros
of a sum of squares of integer affine residuals, of degree two, with one
witness tuple each; typed endpoint conditions on maximal adjacency
intervals exclude unrecorded and omitted simultaneous collisions, and causal
depth controls the denominators.  Part~I eliminates every event coordinate:
the realizing initial gaps form a relatively open rational polyhedral cone
cut out by at most $3(n-1)+(3m+1)E$ conditions, which gives small integral
realizations, an NP upper bound for bounded synthesis, a canonical
first-hit quartic, exact quasi-polynomial seed counts, an exactly solved
first-collision tournament and a four-speed accumulating clock with
denominators $2\cdot3^k$.  Part~II keeps event times and positions as
witnesses on a rational grid, adds halting, proves full column rank (strict
convexity in the witnesses) and bounds the canonical witnesses by
$60^K\max(1,M_0)$ for the four-speed interface of Durand-Lose's universal
machine, which is not transcribed.  Part~I's convex-quadratic barrier is a
special case of Part~XI of \dir{canonical-diophantine-certificates}, printed
as a second route.  No fixed-arity polynomial for unbounded halting is
claimed.  Part~III (batch~79) writes out a fixed reversible,
number-conserving universal signal machine with 114 meta-signals, 445
two-to-two rules and exactly 18 live signals on every encoded input,
compiled from Morita's 15-state, 6-symbol reversible universal Turing
machine through Durand-Lose's conservative stacks; its event dynamics is an
18-dimensional partial piecewise homogeneous integer-linear map, and every
fixed horizon has an explicit canonical quadratic certificate (4{,}196{,}998
auxiliaries per step for the literal machine).  This answers Part~I's
question on an audited universal frontend, except for an ordinary-integer
loader; the manuscript is the corrected edition of an earlier delivery,
which is superseded.  Part~IV (batch~79) compiles a fixed horizon of
one-dimensional partitioned number-conserving lattice dynamics into one
quartic sum of squares with a unique natural witness, corrects a sign in
Morita and Imai's construction, and proves decidability and effective
semilinearity at total mass at most two; since batch~80 it ships its
authors' corrected code edition, whose polynomial terms must be exact
integers (a floating-point false zero is now rejected), the repair proposed
by the Hilbert's-tenth research tree's review.  Parts~V and~VI (batch~80)
find the least conserved mass of an undecidable observation in Part~IV's
weighted model.  Part~V: when many symbols have weight one, every separated
reversible two-counter machine compiles to a globally reversible,
mass-conserving cellular automaton of radius at most four (radius one by
blocking) whose canonical simulation has total mass exactly three; a
universal source machine gives one fixed rule, so three is the least mass
with undecidable pattern occurrence there, and each fixed horizon has a
degree-two certificate with a unique natural witness.  Its declared
extension runs the source forward, then its inverse, then relabels, so that
exact-pair reachability is undecidable at mass three as well, with an exact
first-target clock and a compact certificate.  Part~VI: with at most one
weight-one symbol the timed relation at mass at most three is effectively
Presburger, and exact reachability stays decidable at mass four, where a
binary radius-six shuttle with hit times $k^2+(2d-11)k$ is not Presburger;
so integer-state number-conserving automata need at least five particles,
and since batch~82 the binary five-particle upper bound of
\dir{five-particle-binary-automata} makes five exact for binary rules.
Batch~82 added a third source to Part~VI: for a fixed input of mass at most
four, disjoint half-open charts and a quartic with exactly one natural
witness tuple for each complete timed configuration (four witnesses and
seven squares for the binary shuttle, exact even over the nonnegative
reals).  Part~VII (batch~82, three manuscripts): with at most one
weight-one symbol, exact and translated reachability and pattern occurrence
are decidable from every input of mass at most four on $\mathbb Z^d$, for
every $d$ and without reversibility (at $d=1$ a second proof of Part~VI's
theorem); semilinear visited sets, eventually quasipolynomial box counts of
sharp degree, quadratic clocks, the effect of drift and canonical
first-visit quartics; and a binary radius-six planar shuttle with exact
wedge and drift counts and a one-step quartic.  Its sharp four-versus-five
boundary in every dimension is conditional on two Reports of the
five-particle report.  Part~VIII (batch~82, two manuscripts): for four fixed
nonuniversal three-mass sources, emitted horizon-free polynomials (about 480
to 610 operations, exact degrees 2344 and 1192) with a positive zero
exactly when the cleaned automaton first reaches its exact target; every
accepted fibre is infinite, and after the outer height is made canonical
the outer history is unique and projects bijectively onto an infinite
native fibre, a theorem implied by Part~III of
\dir{fixed-universal-polynomials}; both rest on inherited research-tree
results.  Batch~91 added Parts~IX--XIII from fifteen manuscripts, the
pipeline's Reports 49, 51, 56--67 and~69.  Part~IX: a binary number-conserving rule,
globally reversible with radius at most~90, whose four-particle orbit hits
an anchored word exactly at the times $k^2+(2d-23)k$, so four particles are
the least for a non-eventually-periodic occurrence set under reversibility
too; and, conditional on Part~VI's sources, one finite family of timed
charts for every ordered input of mass at most four, with uniform
stationary-frame Presburger reachability and a single-fold quartic, while
original-frame untimed reachability need not be Presburger.  Part~X: for a
supplied ultimately periodic complete macro with at most four live
signals, infinite validity is a finite formula of quadratic sign tests,
compiled into a quartic with one natural witness tuple; Zeno behaviour and
rational deadlines are decided, and every valid such macro on rational
data has a rational accumulation time (Part~I's accumulation question in
part); with five live signals a half-contracted irrational rotation has a
valid set that no finite sign formula describes, yet explicit
degree-twelve positive-integer certificates exist (81 witnesses and 44
equations for one machine).  Part~XI realizes every invertible rational
planar return, every fixed-centre projective lift and, locally, every
compatible invertible $3\times3$ homogeneous return with five live
signals, and classifies and certifies the strict iteration kernels.
Part~XII compiles every fixed two-counter program into one
number-preserving table with exactly five live signals and an exact
nondestructive branch test (Durand-Lose's five-signal counters credited;
no universality claim), pads every instruction to exactly $30D$ and pays
for native gap initialization inside two degree-twelve power modules.
Part~XIII compresses the certificate of each fixed horizon to three, two
or one positive witnesses, shrinks the power module from 26 to 12 leaves,
classifies when no witness is needed (restated with proof in
\dir{a196460-clipping-tables}) and counts the encoded gap triples exactly;
Report~68, which it cites, was never delivered.  Report~64's strict bound
$25D/18<T<25D/9$ is attained at $a=1$ and is corrected in a remark; the
paid compiler for unbounded histories is absent, so no universal operation,
degree or witness bound changes.  The research programme reviewed the
archives before placement and the written Parts after it
(\texttt{5b9101184}, which corrected five summaries that could be read as
one scalar witness).
The three-mass manuscript re-proves Part~IV's mass-two theorem by the same
argument (printed as a pointer), and the single-unit manuscript reruns
Part~IV's semilinearity architecture one mass higher (marked step by step).  Four large packet and fixture files of Parts~III
and~IV, five rule and certificate exports of Part~V (20.8\,MB), the 70
emitted circuit files of Part~VIII and nine heavy evidence files of
Part~XIII (about 35\,MB) are regenerated rather than stored.
\emph{Parts~I and~II are two independent manuscripts proving one theorem by
two routes, both printed in full; Parts~III and~IV come from two further
manuscripts, and Parts~V and~VI from four more, each Part a manuscript and
a companion built on it (a fifth archive is superseded by its attribution
revision and not printed); batch~82 added six more, a third source of
Part~VI, three for Part~VII and two for Part~VIII, and batch~91 fifteen
more, two to four for each of Parts~IX--XIII, whose restatements of one
another are numbered remarks;}
not a Lean formalization.}

\entry{Stochastic and thermal exactness: exact events in uniformly convergent systems}
{hilbert-tenth-problem/stochastic-and-thermal-exactness}
{article.pdf}{batch~78: ProveIt\_Exact\_Erasure\_Research.zip + ProveIt\_Exact\_Convergence\_Research.zip + Thermal\_Arithmetic\_Diophantine\_Hamiltonians.zip; batch~79: Mixing\_Does\_Not\_Remove\_Arithmetic.zip, Coercive\_Green\_Diophantine.zip + Well\_Conditioned\_Diophantine\_Computation.zip}
{One pattern in five settings: an approximation converges uniformly at a
known rate, while an exact event of the same system is undecidable.
Part~I lifts integer matrix mortality to strictly positive rational
column-stochastic matrices with no common invariant complex line, entries
in any band around $1/n$ and total-variation contraction $\eta^T$; a
product has rank one exactly when the source product vanishes, so exact
erasure is undecidable for at most eight positive $7\times7$ matrices (from
an imported mortality bound) and many-one complete for a nine-state family
with a variable alphabet, with horizon-$T$ quartics having one witness per
erasing word.  Part~II builds a fixed turn-based stochastic game with
discount $1/2$, reward $1/4$ and full-support dyadic rows: value iteration
converges at rate $2^{-n}$, yet exact attainment of the known fixed point
$\tfrac12\mathbf 1$ from a dyadic terminal payoff is c.e.-complete; the
one-player case stays open.  Part~III turns natural arithmetic circuits
into diagonal Hamiltonians $H=(1+\sum_in_i)Q$ of degree at most five whose
zero states biject with the witnesses; compact resolvent and finite thermal
trace are equivalent to finite witness multiplicity and undecidable on a
padded universal family, and a uniformly computable critical fugacity
satisfies $t_*<1$ exactly on halting inputs.  The quadratization,
no-cutoff and finite-fold steps are those of
\dir{canonical-diophantine-certificates}, printed as pointers; defects that
the research tree found in three shipped programs are disclosed, not
patched, and Part~II's two largest certificates are regenerated by its code
rather than stored.  Part~IV (batch~79): every nonzero rational polynomial
$P$ of translation rank $r(P)$ is realized by $s=r(P)+1$ states (optimal)
with commuting, invertible, strictly positive doubly stochastic rational
matrices in any band around $1/s$, acceptance $1/s+\varepsilon q^{|n|}P(n)$
and total-variation contraction $\theta^L$; on a fixed alphabet, with the
input on a rational quadratic B\'ezier curve or a line segment, the exact
hit of the equilibrium value is c.e.-complete, with no computable horizon.
It reuses Part~I's lift, printed as a pointer.  Part~V (batch~79, two
manuscripts proving one theorem by two routes): uniformly coercive
operators $\alpha I-A$ on computable path forests, and on a connected graph
of maximum degree three, with polynomial-time approximable inverses, whose
unique normalized integer Green certificates exist exactly on halting
inputs; the rational Green values are classified by a Pell equation,
finite support and attainment of a strongly convex energy are undecidable,
and membership over $\mathbb Z[S^{-1}]$ is decidable for every finite prime
set $S$.  \emph{Merged from six manuscripts; Parts~I--IV share no
theorem;} a sibling of
\dir{probabilistic-quantum-and-continuous-computation}, not a Lean
formalization.}

\entry{Quadratic orthant certificates: maximal parallelism, timed irreversible races, a fixed Waterfall universal machine, active membranes and reset Petri nets}
{hilbert-tenth-problem/quadratic-orthant-certificates}
{article.pdf}{batch~78: Maximal\_Parallel\_Diophantine.zip + Total\_Quadratic\_Diophantine\_Semantics.zip + Waterfall\_Diophantine\_Certificates.zip; batch~79: Membrane\_Motif\_Research\_Package.zip, Universal\_Membrane\_Research\_Package.zip, Reset\_Petri\_Net\_Certificates.zip; batch~80 (corrected code edition of the last): Reset\_Petri\_Net\_Certificates\_corrected.zip}
{Parts~I--III, V and~VI compile fixed finite systems into integer polynomials
of degree two, nonnegative on the real orthant, whose natural zeros biject
with the semantic objects and whose unsquared products express a condition
that balance equations cannot; Part~IV uses degree four. Parts~I--III sit at the level $D^{+}_2=\mathrm{SL}$
of the degree classification in Part~XI of
\dir{canonical-diophantine-certificates}.  Part~I certifies one maximal
parallel round of a multiset-rewriting system with $d+2m+4H$ natural
auxiliaries, and also histories and exact first halting; the resource-cover
rank $\rho(A)$ gives the sharp counting exponent $m-\rho(A)$, fixed-horizon
counts are eventually quasipolynomial, counting is \#P-complete, the
deadlock of $A+B\to C$ has no convex quadratic, and a program-uniform
certificate needs degree four.
Part~II gives irreversible exclusive-site systems with positive integer
delays a total semantics: one quadratic per catalogue has exactly one
natural zero for every delay vector, recording the earliest-completion
outcome and certified permanent absence, which has no computable grounding
bound.  Part~III proves the exact macrostep behaviour of Iijil's fixed
46-clock Waterfall program for the Neary--Woods universal machine
$U_{15,2}$ and compiles $k$ steps into one quadratic with $35k$ witnesses
and an empty or singleton fibre; its arity grows with~$k$.  Re-proofs and
generalizations of Part~XI's results are printed as second routes; the
research tree's reduction to 107 witnesses at $k=7$ and its independent
finding of the same Neary--Woods erratum are credited.  Iijil's two machine
data files are third-party and not under MIT-0.  Parts~IV--VI were added
in batch~79.  Part~IV certifies one maximally parallel step of
polarizationless noncooperative active membranes with division and
dissolution by a sum of squares of quadratic residuals (degree four, one
polynomial per finite schema), with exact inclusion maximality, an exact
variable and residual ledger, the sharp next-population bound $2^n-1$ and a
four-rule system that defeats identical-subtree sharing.  Part~V gives two
literal universal active-membrane frontends for the Neary--Woods machine
(affine: 528 three-counter instructions and 2{,}544 rules; prime: 8{,}408
two-counter instructions and 34{,}605 rules), both c.e.-complete, the
transcendental density of the prime language, of Turing degree
$\mathbf 0'$, and outcome quadratics whose products are strong selector
gates, so that every zero over the real orthant is natural and every fibre
is empty or a singleton.  Part~VI builds a unit-weight reset Petri net (539
places, 771 transitions, 233 reset arcs from three places) with
c.e.-complete exact-target reachability, exactly one accepting word at each
length of an arithmetic progression and a canonical certificate with $2813h$
witnesses, natural over the nonnegative reals; it re-derives Part~V's
program layer without citing it, which is printed once.  Seventeen heavy
schemas and traces of Parts~V and~VI are regenerated rather than stored.
Since batch~80 Part~VI ships its authors' corrected code edition, whose
net builder rejects unknown places and non-exact or negative counts and
whose peak compiler requires Boolean flags, the repair proposed by the Hilbert's-tenth research tree's
review; nets, schemas, witnesses and counts are unchanged.  \emph{Merged from six manuscripts: the first three
share no theorem, and Parts~IV and~V are complementary;} not a Lean
formalization.}

\entry{Polynomial witness histories: unique linear certificates with finite polynomial unknowns}
{hilbert-tenth-problem/polynomial-witness-histories}
{article.pdf}{batch~79: unique\_polynomial\_histories.zip + one\_coordinate\_certificates.zip + Linear\_Boundary\_Transport\_Research.zip}
{Certificates whose unknowns are a fixed number of finite polynomials rather
than integers: identities linear in them (in Part~II of degree two, every
product through one shared stride), a clock that makes the whole witness
unique with $0/1$ coefficients, and no computable input-only bound on its
degree or support.  Part~I compiles cellular computation over $\N[X,Y]$
with $s^3+s+5$ polynomial unknowns and $3s+3$ affine-linear rows (or
$3\lceil\log_2 s\rceil+6$ unit-coefficient rows, or nine weighted rows),
with a unique Boolean-coefficient witness exactly at a first singleton
acceptance, exact degrees, a $\Sigma^0_1$-complete fixed matrix and a
single quadratic.  Part~II answers Part~I's question on one coordinate
negatively: coefficient-bounded univariate linear systems over $\N[X]$ are
decided by a finite-state procedure, so no universal univariate linear
compiler has computably bounded witness heights; eleven degree-two
identities with the self-calibrating stride $S=X^{m+2h+2}$ keep the full
unique Boolean history, in bijection with Part~I's witnesses.  Part~III,
independent of both: linear boundary transport uniform over every nonzero
commutative ring, a compiler with $q+d$ linear identities in $q+2d$ sorted
polynomial unknowns, a row-tagged single equation, an exact
integer/rational energy gap and a Hilbert-space range theorem.  A fixed
number of polynomial unknowns is not a fixed number of integer unknowns: no
ordinary fixed-arity representation is claimed, the no-bound results are
the bounded-search argument of Part~IX of
\dir{canonical-diophantine-certificates}, and undecidability of linear
equations over polynomial semirings is older work (Narendran).  Three
expanded-polynomial exports of 1.0--1.6\,MB are regenerated by the shipped
code rather than stored.  \emph{Merged from three manuscripts, one
answering another;} not a Lean formalization.}

\entry{Smooth everywhere, universal on the integers: smooth Diophantine finalizers}
{hilbert-tenth-problem/smooth-diophantine-finalizers}
{article.pdf}{batch~79: Smooth\_Quartic\_Diophantine\_Certificates.zip}
{For integer polynomials $f_i$ of degree at most two with homogeneous
quadratic lifts $q_i(x,t)$, the finalizer
$F=\sum_i q_i(x,t)^2+(tu-1)^2+g(z_1)+g(z_2)+2g(z_3)$, $g(z)=2z^2-z$, is a
quartic in five more variables whose natural zeros are in bijection with
those of the system and whose integer zeros are exactly two sign-related
copies of its integer zeros, while its affine scheme is smooth over
$\mathbb Z$ with geometrically integral fibres, certified by an explicit
integral B\'ezout identity $AF+\sum_v B_vF_v=1$ of product degree at most
seven.  It has a nonnegative point over $\mathbb Z[1/2]$, points over every
$\mathbb Z_p$ and modulo every integer, rational points dense in its real
locus and exactly two real path components, so no local obstruction
remains.  With first weight one, universal smoothness holds exactly for
total guard weight 4 or~16, and three guards (five auxiliaries) are optimal
within positive weight-four guard families; also a bounded counter-program
frontend with a unique natural witness, c.e.-completeness on the compiled
class with no computable bound on the least integer witness, and
power-of-two padding to higher degree.  General smooth-variety
undecidability is Poonen's; the counter adapter and the quartic
normalization are those of \dir{canonical-diophantine-certificates},
credited; no finite-fold or single-fold MRDP theorem is claimed.  A dated
note in \dir{probabilistic-quantum-and-continuous-computation} applies the
finalizer to that report's Boolean-circuit quartics.  From one manuscript;
not a Lean formalization.}

\entry{Five-particle binary automata: reversible universality, a literal universal source, clocks, lazy evaluation and quartic certificates}
{hilbert-tenth-problem/five-particle-binary-automata}
{article.pdf}{batch~82: Reversible\_Binary\_Five\_Particle\_Package.zip (base), five-particle-binary-portable.zip, Literal\_Universal\_Reversible\_Source\_Package.zip, Reversible\_Startup\_Optimization\_Package.zip, Cellular\_Clock\_Domination\_Package.zip, Exact\_Lazy\_Reversible\_CA\_Evaluator\_Package.zip, Event\_Budgeted\_Quartic\_Certificates\_Package.zip, Two\_Parallel\_Conservative\_Involutions\_Package.zip, Sparse\_Parallel\_Particle\_Evaluation\_Package.zip, Canonical\_Parallel\_Quartic\_Certificates\_Package.zip; batch~91: Two\_scale\_recognition\_radius\_sources.zip, Shorter\_exactness\_windows\_sources.zip (Part~VII)}
{Twelve ``Research Reports'' (14--20, 26--28, 70, 71) of one pipeline, on one object,
that continue the signal-machine report above.  Part~I: every
finite partial-injective guarded two-counter machine compiles to a globally
reversible, number-conserving binary cellular automaton of explicit radius
with five-particle encodings and an anchored halt word, and every
deterministic two-counter program to a binary conservative one; with that
report's mass-four decidability, five is the exact particle threshold for
binary and for globally reversible binary number-conserving automata
(answering its threshold question), with fixed-horizon certificates of
degree at most four and a literal instance from the 8{,}408-row source of
\dir{quadratic-orthant-certificates}, whose source lemmas are re-derived
(second presentation).  Part~II prints the universal reversible two-counter
source that Part~I left existential (122{,}622 controls, 141{,}561 rows),
with positive return and periodicity r.e.-complete on its clean family and
quartic certificates.  Part~III: history-tag swaps that give empty startup
in 138 source steps and first-encounter optimality among static
orientations, lifted to the cellular clock.  Part~IV: a lazy evaluator equal
on every finite support to the frozen ordered product of about
$2.7\cdot10^{11}$ involutions.  Part~V: event-budgeted quartics with exactly
one natural witness for a fixed source, mass, horizon and budget.
Part~VI: two full-shift number-conserving involutions that agree with the
ordered rule on admissible states, a sparse evaluator and fixed-horizon
quartics for the new rule; their arithmetic layer re-proves lemmas of
\dir{canonical-diophantine-certificates} and of the signal-machine report,
credited.  Part~VII (batch~91, Reports~70 and~71) answers Report~26's first
question with two further full-shift extensions of the same admissible
dynamics: a two-scale isolation lemma gives the sufficient radius
$108D+149+3J$ (55{,}027{,}013 for the universal ledger), with a
four-particle input separating the new rule from Report~26's and the
injective number-conserving binary rules of radius two shown to be exactly
the five shifts; triple exactness lowers the radius to $76D+105+3J$
($76D+104+3J$ for the same rule; 38{,}722{,}713 and 38{,}722{,}712), so the
earlier tiny-source comparison, 978 against 540, becomes 581 and~409.  The
earlier evaluator and certificates do not transfer to the new rules, and
the true or minimal radii, Report~70's reliance on the undelivered
Report~68 and an independent audit of the main proofs are open items.  No intrinsic universality, minimal radius, unbounded or
fixed-arity representation, executed universal startup or novelty is
claimed; the threshold corollaries rest on the signal-machine report's
lower bound.  Heavy regenerable tables, sources and traces (over
200\,MB) are rebuilt by the shipped code rather than stored.  The Hilbert's-tenth research tree
reviewed seven of the first ten Reports, and Reports~70 and~71 before and
after the write (without re-auditing their proofs), without finding a
defect; not a Lean formalization.}

\entry{Fixed universal polynomials: a literal Grill instance, exact degree laws, native witness fibers and index restoration}
{hilbert-tenth-problem/fixed-universal-polynomials}
