{article.pdf}{batch~82: Fixed\_Universal\_Grill\_Polynomial\_Package~(1).zip (base), Native\_Grill\_Exact\_Degree\_Laws\_Package.zip, Entire\_Native\_Witness\_Fiber\_Package.zip, Failure\_of\_Positive\_Index\_Restoration\_Package.zip, Counterexample\_Height\_Expansions\_and\_Rotation\_Discrepancy\_Package.zip; batch~83: Exact\_Negative\_Index\_Obstruction\_for\_Positive\_Diophantine\_Interfaces\_Package.zip; batch~88: Nonredundant\_Main\_Comparisons\_in\_Positive\_Diophantine\_Certificates\_Package.zip (Part~VI), Failure\_of\_First\_Index\_Deletion\_Package.zip (Part~VII), Structural\_Obstructions\_for\_the\_Free\_Coefficient\_83\_Candidate\_Package.zip, Full\_Positive\_Collapse\_of\_the\_Square\_Product\_82\_Candidate\_Package.zip, Exact\_Certificate\_Height\_and\_an\_Even\_Rank\_Obstruction\_Package.zip (Part~VIII); superseded, not printed: Fixed\_Universal\_Grill\_Polynomial\_Package.zip (batch~82)}
{Eleven ``Research Reports'' (23 revision~1, 24, 25, 33, 34, 37, 39, 41, 43,
45, 46) on the
Hilbert's-tenth programme's own fixed universal polynomial and its native
arithmetic block, built on its research artifacts at pinned commits.
Part~I: one literal 397{,}488-phase Grill program and a complete circuit of
3{,}600{,}546 operations with 797{,}135 positive witnesses, whose language
theorem is conditional on pinned imports and whose exact total degree
69{,}339{,}973 is unconditional algebra.  Part~II: exact degrees $63N-4$,
$12N+10$ and $87N+16$ for every run table of the native Grill template.
Part~III: the entire positive fiber of the native component at fixed ports
and a two-term height law with a $\zeta(1/2)$ second term, conditional on
pinned native soundness and completeness.  Part~IV: the programme's proposed
positive index restoration fails for one compiler child, which has
infinitely many positive zeros with negative restored index at every input;
one canonical family of them is counted, $N(B)\sim\kappa\log\log B$, with a
rotation discrepancy and a Kesten obstruction.  Part~V (batch~83): for the
two interfaces left open there, an exact necessary-and-sufficient
arithmetic predicate for a full positive zero with negative restored index,
with positive reconstruction of every witness; whether the predicate ever
passes stays open.  Part~VI (batch~88): on explicit thinned scale
progressions every condition of that predicate but one congruence holds
infinitely often while the congruence fails, so the main projection and
the main norm are nonredundant on positive tuples, and every odd-index hit
of the congruence needs a Jacobi symbol~$-1$ (its analytic existence proof
was not recertified by the programme).  Part~VII: deleting the
first-index quotient from the tree's 85- and 86-operation polynomials
gives 81- and 82-operation candidates with full positive zeros at every
input and every genuine compiler, so with the empty-set compiler they
accept a rejected input.
Part~VIII, three sources on the one- and two-operation reductions of the
84-operation polynomial: structural constraints on every full positive
zero of the free-coefficient~83 candidate (which the research tree has
since refuted), infinitely many positive zeros of the square/product~82
candidate at every input (a second construction of a research-tree
theorem), and the exact height of the displayed certificates with an
even-rank obstruction.  ``82'' and ``83'' name several polynomials; a
front-matter table lists every candidate below the record with its status.
A dated note of batch~91 records that the r.e.-complete fixed sandpile
polynomials of Part~XXI of \dir{canonical-diophantine-certificates} are
not ordinary-input universal polynomials and do not bear on the record.
Results that re-derive research-tree constructions are
credited there; Report~23's first edition is superseded by its
revision; a 61\,MB circuit and a 1.6\,MB program are rebuilt by the shipped
code; two third-party papers are not redistributed.  No new MRDP theorem,
optimality or operation record is claimed, and no Part lowers the
84-operation record; the programme reviewed every
source but certifies only parts of some; not a Lean formalization.}

\entry{Periodic turmites: first revisits, a literal Langton ant and its Diophantine certificate}
{hilbert-tenth-problem/periodic-turmite-first-revisits}
{article.pdf}{batch~83: Polynomial\_First\_Revisit\_and\_Exact\_Pattern\_Queries\_for\_Turmites\_Package.zip (Part~I); batch~88: Literal\_Periodic\_Langton\_Ant\_and\_Visit\_Boundary\_Package.zip (Part~II), Arithmetic\_Initialization\_for\_a\_Periodic\_Langton\_Ant\_Package.zip, Complete\_Positive\_Certificate\_for\_the\_Literal\_Periodic\_Ant\_Package.zip (Part~III), Exact\_Structured\_Construction\_of\_the\_Fixed\_Ant\_Coefficients\_Package.zip, Exact\_Fusion\_of\_the\_Ant\_Background\_Polynomials\_Package.zip (Part~IV)}
{For an explicit cyclic L/R turmite on a periodic colour tile with finitely
many binary-encoded defects and a binary start, the first repeated lattice
position is decidable in polynomial bit time: the algorithm returns the
exact first repeated arrival with a witness, or a no-revisit certificate
with a periodic tail, through an affine lane calculus with an exact
intersection primitive.  It also compiles finite unions of head-relative
observation clauses into an exact first hit (no polynomial bound claimed
for the Boolean expansion; a Presburger route through Cooper's algorithm,
which is formalized in \dir{Logic/PresburgerArithmetic}, is a second,
unexecuted proof), and proves that no undecidable language reduces to
globally one-visit runs read by such observations.  This is the negative
side of two research-tree notes on ant and toggle-router universality;
turmite universality itself is third-party.  Part~I claims nothing after
the first revisit, no loader or universal atlas, no novelty.  Parts~II--IV
(batch~88, Research Reports 40, 42, 44, 47 and~48) answer its open
question on a literal two-visit interface: one fixed doubly periodic
binary background, a fixed
start and a finite loader make the binary RL ant (Langton's ant) simulate
the fixed fifteen-state Neary--Woods machine $U_{15}$, visiting every cell
at most twice, with halting read off a residue-and-heading port; with
Part~I this gives a sharp one-versus-two-visit boundary.  A fixed
polynomial system initializes the board from two positive sentinel
integers, and one fixed polynomial $F_2$ with 465 positive witnesses and
exact degree 2{,}304{,}000 recognizes halting of $U_{15}$ on the two
sentinel-decoded side words (Report~44, a recovered edition).  Its source
uses 2{,}307{,}457 operations with prescribed coefficients.  When all
coefficients are built from the literals 1 and~3, Reports~47 and~48 reduce
the dense-prefix construction to 14{,}658{,}934 operations; later programme
packets reduce that count further.  $F_2$ recognizes a fixed
r.e.-complete language only through the published $U_{15}$ encoding: it is
not an ordinary-input loader for the programme's input convention and does
not bear on its 84-operation record.  Seven primitive cell maps are
numerical data taken from the figure sources of Maldonado, Gajardo,
Hellouin de Menibus and Moreira, credited and not covered by MIT-0; 27
heavy regenerable files were excluded with recipes.  A dated note of
batch~91 records that Part~III's fifteen-equation power system recurs in
Part~XXI of \dir{canonical-diophantine-certificates}, Part~VI of
\dir{group-theoretic-substrates} and Parts~X--XIII of
\dir{signal-machine-collision-certificates}, whose Report~67 shrinks it.
From six manuscripts,
each reviewed by the programme without a defect (it certified only what it
read); not a Lean formalization.}
